Add RequestSettingsRepository.update(requestId, transform) doing read-modify-write and make both VMs use it for their own field only.
9) Tests: unit tests with the injected delay (instant, deterministic) for FlowPoller caps/cancel/Retry-After, runIf/alwaysRun in the runner, CLI parity, RequestSettings round-trip incl. backup/workspace.json, and a widget test for the Flow section.

CORRECTED NUMBERS (honest): 8 uses/week is optimistic; use about 3 min x 4-5/week (async jobs, Odoo queued jobs, post-deploy wait, plus run-if/always-run in collection runs). Wall-clock time is not saved, attention is (the developer stops babysitting the Send button). Still worthwhile and it closes a real Postman/Bruno/Hurl gap without scripting.
- **Risks:** 1) Prod-lock hole: ProductionGuard.checkRun counts requests, not attempts, and checkSend confirms once. A polled POST/PUT/DELETE on a production environment could send up to maxTries writes after a single confirmation. Mitigation: count maxTries in the confirm text ("may repeat up to 12 times"), default poll cap lower for write methods, and a clear warning in the Flow editor. Do NOT hard-block POST: Odoo 19 JSON-2 uses POST for every call including read-only search_read, so a GET-only rule would exclude the Odoo persona.
2) Data-loss race on settingsJson between RequestSettingsViewModel and the new Flow VM (see design item 8). Without the read-modify-write fix, saving a Settings override can erase the Flow or vice versa.
3) Back-compat: a workspace.json/backup with a `flow` key opened by an older PostPilot is silently dropped (RequestSettings.fromJson ignores unknown keys, doc canonicalisation rebuilds known keys) and the next push from the older app removes it. Acceptable pre-release, but note it; no backup version bump is needed.
4) Behavioural changes to existing code: CollectionRunnerService constructor and its tests change; the runner stop logic (early return -> stopped flag) and _onDone stoppedOnFailure inference break with alwaysRun and skipped results unless the service reports the stop explicitly; skipped must be added to summary/JSON/CSV/CLI reporters, or counts and exit codes drift (CLI bail/exit code must treat skipped as non-failing, as it already does).
5) Polling side effects: History spam unless recordHistory is added; console log 200-entry cap churned by a long poll; extractors must run only on the final attempt. Hammering a third-party API is a politeness risk, so enforce min interval 1 s, honour Retry-After, hard caps.
6) Platform: no dart:io needed, works on web/mobile. Web: browsers throttle timers in background tabs (about 1/min after a few minutes hidden), so a long poll slows when the tab is hidden; mobile: Dart timers pause when the app is backgrounded, so polling is foreground-only. Web CORS is unchanged (each attempt is a normal request). Say this in the UI helper text.
7) Legal/ToS/store policy/security: none material. No code execution, no new secrets or permissions, no background services; `expected` values in the until-condition travel in workspace.json exactly like existing assertion values (same secret-exposure profile; the local-secrets splitter handles only the known secret-looking fields).
8) Scope creep: skip exporting flow to Postman/Insomnia (no equivalent without scripts), skip run-if and on-failure on manual Send, skip a Send dropdown. Keeping to this keeps the effort at M rather than L.
- **Strongest objection:** The claimed 3 min x 8 uses/week = 24 min is inflated. (1) Frequency: a typical backend, Flutter or Odoo dev hits a genuinely async endpoint (report export, KYC or payment status, import job) only 1-2 times a week. Odoo JSON-2 calls are synchronous, so Odoo queue-job polling is rare, and Flutter devs mostly call plain REST. 8 polling sessions a week is not typical. (2) Per-use saving: re-clicking Send 5-10 times takes about 2-3 s per click, so a whole poll costs 30-60 s of clicking. The real cost is attention, not minutes, and 3 min per use is generous. (3) Run-if and per-step on-failure/always-run only matter inside runner flows with cleanup steps. That is a niche QA pattern, and the same result comes from ordering requests or the existing global stopOnFailure. Adding three controls to every request Tests tab also works against the 'clean UI' requirement. (4) Alternatives: in CI or CLI, a one-line shell loop or Hurl's retry already does this. In PostPilot today the runner has a fixed Duration delay (collection_run_options.dart, max 10 min) plus stopOnFailure, which already covers the 'wait N seconds' case. I could not check market demand beyond that: the WebSearch budget was exhausted, so the competitor evidence (Hurl retry, Postman setNextRequest, Bruno sleep) is taken from the candidate's own list and I did not re-verify it. The one defensible slice is 'repeat until assertion passes' on a runner step. That slice is a real gap for QA chains such as create job, poll until done, then extract id. It replaces a flaky fixed delay, but it is a small S-sized addition, not an M-sized Flow section. Skip or shrink the full candidate.
- **Platform notes:** No platform limits (cancel token and delay primitives exist). Hard caps on tries/total time. Polled GETs must not trigger the production write confirmation on every attempt; a polled POST confirms once and is off by default.

### 6. Odoo create/write payload from a record, plus x2many command builder
- **Score 2.4** | effort M | ~6 min/week saved | persona odoo | lenses: odoo-daily | skeptic votes 2/3
- **Problem today:** To create a sale.order with lines through the API the dev reads an existing record and hand-edits the output: deletes id/create_date/write_uid and every readonly/computed field, turns many2one pairs [7,'Name'] into plain ints, reads each order line separately, strips it and wraps it as [0,0,{...}], writes many2many as [6,0,[ids]]. Wrong command tuples give silent no-ops or obscure errors.
- **What PostPilot would do:** Explorer row menu and the JSON tree menu (for /json/2/ responses): 'Create payload from this record'. PostPilot reads the record, uses fields_get to keep only writable fields, flattens many2one to ids, expands one2many children (depth 1-2) into (0,0,vals) and many2many into (6,0,ids), shows an include/exclude tree and 'Save as request' (create or write). A body-editor popover writes correct tuples when the cursor is inside a one2many/many2many value (create, update, delete, unlink, link, clear, replace).
- **Where it lives:** Odoo Studio Explorer row action and a context-menu entry in the existing JSON tree explorer for Odoo responses; the tuple popover is a palette command, not a toolbar button.
- **Competitors:** Evidence check, with caveats:
1. The Odoo forum thread is real and shows 7,386 views and no accepted answer. It is about adding many2many items from C# via XML-RPC, and the page does not discuss command tuples. It supports "x2many through the external API is a pain" only loosely.
2. The Cybrosys Command class blog is real. It says that before Odoo 14 devs used cryptic tuples like (0,0,vals) or (4,id), and that Command.create/update/delete/link/unlink replaced them. It is a fair source for the tuple confusion, but it is about server-side Python, not JSON-2.
3. The openerp-proxy link is real but does not contain command-tuple builders. It has convert_to_write(), which turns a read record into write/create values, and a convert_x2many docstring that lists the commands. It is a precedent for the record-to-write-payload idea in a Python library, not a GUI competitor.

Competing or cheaper alternatives the candidate should address:
- Odoo's own server-side ORM copy_data(). It is public, so it may be callable as POST /json/2/<model>/copy_data. It returns create-ready vals with one2many as create commands. That is how "Duplicate" works. This is from memory of the Odoo source and was not verified, because the web-search budget was exhausted. It skips copy=False fields and is not selective, so PostPilot's include/exclude tree still adds value.
- Odoo's UI Duplicate and Studio.
- Postman and Insomnia have no Odoo-aware payload builder; they require hand-editing.

Scope advice:
- The strongest part is "record -> writable-only create/write payload with many2one flattened and x2many expanded, then Save as request". That is a real time saver (8 min x 3/week is plausible).
- The weakest part is the body-editor tuple popover as a palette command. It is more gimmicky and could be cut or shipped as a small second step.
- A cheap variant is a "Create payload" button next to "Search" in the Explorer, reusing the existing fields_get and addRequest plumbing.
- Keep the hand-off honest: one2many children need an extra read per line model, so depth 1 is enough.
- **Design in this codebase:** VERDICT: feasible without a rewrite, but only if the cursor-aware tuple popover is cut. Keep the payload builder (the core value) plus a standalone command builder.

WHAT EXISTS THAT FITS (read in lib/):
- features/odoo/data/odoo_client.dart: OdooClient.call(connection, OdooCall) already does fields_get and read over Dio and works on web (the CORS caveat is already documented in the Studio view model).
- features/odoo/domain/entities/odoo_model_info.dart: OdooModelInfo/OdooField already parse fields_get (type, relation, readonly, store, required).
- features/odoo/domain/services/odoo_json2.dart: OdooCall/OdooJson2.draft builds the request. create takes {vals_list:[...]} and write takes ids plus {vals}.
- OdooStudioViewModel.addRequest + CreateOdooWorkspaceUseCase.addRequest save a draft into a collection. odoo_convert_tab.dart already has the collection dropdown + "Add as request" + CodeBlock pattern to copy.
- ToolDialog/ToolTabs, CodeBlock, InfoBanner and BusyLabel cover all the UI. Stacking a dialog on a dialog is already done (_ExtractDialog in explore_tab.dart).
- odoo_dart_generator.dart already uses the writable rule "readonly && !required => skip", so there is precedent for the heuristic.

DESIGN (all in features/odoo):
1. domain/services/odoo_payload_builder.dart: pure Dart, no I/O, unit-testable like test/odoo/odoo_logic_test.dart.
   - Input: a record map, its OdooModelInfo, and optional child records keyed by field name (each with its own OdooModelInfo).
   - Output: a tree of PayloadNode(field, type, included, reason, value) plus the final vals Map.
   - Default rules: drop id, create_*/write_*, display_name, binary fields, non-stored fields, readonly&&!required fields, mail-mixin fields (message_*, activity_*, website_message_ids), and names matching /password|token|secret|api_?key/. Omit empty `false` values on non-boolean fields (toggle).
   - many2one [id,name] or int => int. many2many => [6,0,[ids]]. one2many => [[0,0,childVals],...] with the inverse field removed. In write mode use (1,id,vals) for existing lines.
   - Add OdooCommand helpers: create, update, delete, unlink, link, clear, replace (0,1,2,3,4,5,6). Output JSON arrays, which the ORM accepts like tuples.
2. Small changes to existing code:
   - OdooField gets relationField, and loadFields' attributes list gets 'relation_field'. I confirmed in odoo master (fields_relational.py, One2many) that `_description_relation_field = property(attrgetter('inverse_name'))`, so fields_get exposes it as relation_field.
   - OdooStudioViewModel.runSearch must always force 'id' into fields, because the user can untick id today.
3. data/odoo_record_reader.dart, using OdooClient: fields_get(model) + read(ids=[id]) + for each o2m field (depth 1 default, max 2) fields_get(comodel) + read(child ids).
   - Cap it at about 12 calls and 200 child rows.
   - Skip mail.*/ir.attachment comodels. If a branch fails (access error), parse it with the existing OdooErrorParser and show a per-branch warning instead of failing the whole payload.
   - Pass load to read, but also accept both [id,name] and plain int (flatten defensively).
4. presentation/view_models/odoo_payload_view_model.dart: new registerFactory in core/di/injector.dart (test/di_wiring_test.dart needs a line). It takes OdooClient + EnvironmentRepository + CreateOdooWorkspaceUseCase, and resolves the connection from the active environment (same logic as loadFromActiveEnvironment; extract it so it is not duplicated).
5. presentation/widgets/odoo_payload_dialog.dart, a ToolDialog: left side is a CheckboxListTile include/exclude tree (like the Explorer field list), right side is a live CodeBlock preview, footer has a create/write toggle, Copy JSON, and collection + "Add as request". Saving goes through addRequest and nothing is ever sent. Optional offline mode: paste a record plus a fields_get response (as the Explorer already allows), which also helps on web without CORS.
6. Entry points:
   a. Explorer: a trailing "..." cell (PopupMenuButton, works on touch) on each DataRow in odoo_explorer_tab.dart. This is S.
   b. Response tools: there is NO context menu in ExploreTab, only a selection + an action bar (Copy path / Use as variable / Add test). Show one "Odoo payload" button there only when the selected node is a Map with an int `id` AND vm.request.url matches /json/2/<model>/. `_selectedValue` already exists in that State. Alternative: a conditional "Odoo" tab (ToolTabs rebuilds its DefaultTabController when the tab count changes after vm.load(); I did not test that).
   c. Palette: add PaletteItem 'odoo.payload' that opens Odoo Studio on the new tab (initialTab), consistent with the other odoo.* items.
7. x2many "Commands" helper as a new tab or small dialog in Odoo Studio, not in the body editor: pick the operation, enter ids/vals, see the JSON array and the Python Command.xxx equivalent, copy. This is S and has zero coupling.

CUT THE CURSOR-AWARE POPOVER (this part is not clean):
- BodyEditor's _rawController is private to its State. A palette run(BuildContext) has no handle on it, and opening the palette moves focus away from the editor.
- It would need a new tolerant "JSON path at offset" scanner that survives {{variables}} (JsonBodyFormat's scanner has no offset/path model), a registry coupling command_palette to request_builder, and a fields_get lookup inside the editor using the request's resolved variables.
- That is L on its own and fragile. Fallback if wanted later: a button in the BodyEditor toolbar, shown only for /json/2/ create|write URLs, that opens the command dialog and inserts text at the controller selection (M-, no cursor-path parsing).

PLATFORM: pure Dart + Dio, no dart:io, no conditional imports needed. Desktop works fully. Web works only if the Odoo server allows CORS; if the user already got the response in the web build, reads to the same server work too. On a phone the ToolDialog goes full-screen and the tree is a scrollable list. If clipboard/offline paste is wanted, ClipboardData is already used everywhere.

EFFORT CORRECTION: M (1-2 weeks) for builder + reader + dialog + explorer/response entry points + commands tab + tests. With the cursor popover it becomes L. I keep minutesSaved 8 as plausible for a nested sale.order, but usesPerWeek 3 is optimistic; 2-3 is more honest.
- **Risks:** 1. PII/secret leakage via Git sync (the main risk). "Save as request" writes real record data (names, emails, phones, prices, bank numbers) into the workspace JSON, which PostPilot's Git sync can push to GitHub, possibly a public repo. The existing local-only-secrets protection covers environment secrets, not request bodies. Mitigations: a visible InfoBanner ("contains data read from <server>"); a "blank personal fields" option (emails, phones, names, addresses, acc_number); a name denylist for password/token/secret/api_key; binary fields off by default; warn when the active environment is the production-flagged one.
2. Ids are database-specific. Flattened many2one/many2many ids (partner_id=7, tax_ids=[3]) are only valid in the source database, so a payload read from prod and sent to staging silently links the wrong records. Needs a clear banner. Optionally offer {{var}} placeholders for chosen ids later.
3. "Writable" detection is heuristic. fields_get exposes readonly/store/required/relation/relation_field but NOT copy, default, compute or inverse, so computed fields with an inverse and some readonly+required fields are misclassified. That is why the include/exclude tree and the live preview are mandatory, and the UI must say "review before sending". Command semantics (0,1,2,3,4,5,6) are stable in Odoo 17-19, and JSON arrays are accepted like tuples.
4. Cost and traffic: depth 2 on big models (account.move with lines, mrp, stock.picking) means many fields_get/read calls and 8 MB max responses. Needs the call/row caps, a progress label (BusyLabel) and per-branch graceful failure. Child models can also deny read access to the API key.
5. No ToS, licensing or store-policy risk. It is the user's own Odoo, with the user's own API key, through the public JSON-2 API (already used by Odoo Studio), and it does not scrape or redistribute anything. Security is fine as long as the dialog never sends create/write itself (reads only); sending later goes through the normal sender, so the PROD safety lock still applies.
6. Scope risk: the cursor-aware body-editor popover as written would add cross-feature coupling and a new tolerant JSON scanner, so keep it out of v1.
7. Minor UI risk: a conditional button in the Response tools action bar adds a little clutter; limit it to Odoo /json/2/ record selections. The Explorer must force `id` into search_read fields.
- **Strongest objection:** As scoped (M effort, 8 min x 3 per week = 24 min) the saving is inflated. Four reasons.

1. Frequency is overstated. An Odoo/Flutter dev writes a nested create payload once per integration, per model, while building a feature. After that it is saved as a request and reused, and PostPilot already saves requests. Over a year that averages about 1-1.5 fresh nested payloads per week, with many zero weeks. Copy-a-record, strip fields, wrap one level of lines is about 3-6 minutes for a typical sale.order with a few lines, not 8. A fair figure is about 1.5 x 4-5 min, roughly 6-8 min/week.

2. The tuple popover is the weakest part. There are only 7 commands. Anyone who writes x2many payloads weekly memorises (0,0,vals), (4,id) and (6,0,ids) within a few uses. The forum thread's 7,386 views show a documentation gap for newcomers, not a weekly time sink for the target dev. Evidence of demand is thin: the cybrosys blog and the openerp_proxy helper are explainers and a Python helper, not proof that people lose real time.

3. Existing alternatives already cover most of it.
   - PostPilot's own AI assistant (natural language to request) can emit correct command tuples if it is given fields_get context. A small prompt or context tweak gets most of the tuple value for almost no effort.
   - Odoo's native workaround is to create sale.order, then create sale.order.line records separately with order_id. This avoids tuples entirely and is common in JSON-2 integrations.
   - The existing Create template, `create {vals_list:[{'name':'New record'}]}` in odoo_json2.dart line 92, is a weak placeholder. The real gap is a good skeleton, not clone-a-record.

4. Clone-a-record is also semantically fragile. A copied record carries production data: names, prices, partner ids and company-specific values. The dev must still hand-edit it. Create does not run onchange, so the clone can differ from what the UI would have produced. Depth 1-2 expansion with an include/exclude tree is a lot of UI (M effort) for a feature used a few times per project.

Painful/error-prone case: one real gotcha is that (6,0,ids) on write silently replaces all existing links, where (4,id) would add one. A tuple popover would help there, but write-time mistakes on many2many are rare and are usually caught in dev.

What is worth keeping (S effort, about 5 min x 2 per week):
- Replace the 'New record' Create template with a fields_get-driven skeleton. Include required fields, plus writable stored non-computed fields as optional, with typed placeholders: 0 for many2one, [] for x2many, a first selection value, and so on.
- Show the x2many tuple shapes as inline hints or comments in that skeleton.
- Optionally pull in default_get values.

This covers about 70% of the value at about 20% of the effort. Skip the record-clone depth expansion and the separate tuple popover.
- **Platform notes:** Pure Dart; reads only, no side effects until the user saves the request. Needs fields_get and read rights on the models involved. Command tuples behave the same through JSON-2.

### 7. Find and replace across the workspace, plus 'Rename variable everywhere'
- **Score 2.4** | effort M | ~6 min/week saved | persona all | lenses: productivity-ux | skeptic votes 2/3
- **Problem today:** There is only a sidebar name filter (request names, not URLs/bodies) and Ctrl+F inside a response. When the staging host changes, /api/v1 becomes /api/v2, a field is renamed in 40 JSON bodies, or {{baseUrl}} becomes {{apiUrl}}, the dev opens every request, edits, saves and hopes nothing was missed (also in extractors, assertions, auth, environments): about 20-40 s per request.
- **What PostPilot would do:** Ctrl+Shift+F opens Find in workspace: text or regex, scopes (URL, headers, params, body, auth, extractors/assertions, environment values, saved examples). Hits are grouped by collection/folder with per-hit checkboxes and a diff preview; 'Replace selected' applies in one DB transaction with 'Undo last replace' (snapshot of touched rows). The hover card on any {{variable}} gets 'Rename everywhere' (all uses, the env/collection/global key and extractors that save into it). Doubles as fast global search ('which request uses this id?').
- **Where it lives:** Palette commands 'Find in workspace' / 'Replace in workspace' (Ctrl+Shift+F) opening one dialog; 'Rename everywhere' on the variable hover card and environment rows; collection context menu 'Find in this collection'.
- **Partly exists already:** PARTIAL, only the read-only search half exists. (1) lib/features/command_palette/presentation/palette_items.dart (requests(), ~line 190-211) and domain/services/palette_search.dart: the Ctrl+Shift+P palette already finds requests by name, method, URL, raw body and GraphQL query, using a literal case-insensitive substring match with a 2-character minimum (README line 88 says "by name, URL or body"). It has NO regex, NO headers/params/auth/extractors/assertions/environment values/saved examples scope, no per-hit grouping or preview, and nothing can be replaced. (2) lib/features/collections/presentation/view_models/collections_view_model.dart (searchQuery, _matchesQuery, ~lines 84-121): the sidebar filter matches names only (collection, folder, request). (3) lib/features/request_builder/presentation/view_models/variable_scope.dart and widgets/variables/variable_hover_card.dart: undefined {{variables}} are coloured red and the hover card shows value and scope, but it is display-only (wrapped in IgnorePointer), so it has no actions. Where-used or usage lists for variables do not exist. (4) Repository/DAO layer: renames exist only for collection, folder, request and environment names (collections_view_model.dart, environments_dao.dart renameEnvironment). A variable key can be edited, but nothing updates {{refs}} in requests, extractors or assertions. Grep for find-and-replace, replaceAll over the workspace, rename variable and findUsages found nothing relevant; the only replaceAll hits are string utilities in codegen, docs and git code. The README and bin/ have no such feature. So the Replace, Rename-everywhere, regex, multi-scope, diff-preview and Undo parts are genuinely new.
- **Competitors:** Postman (desktop) ships Find and Replace across a workspace (Ctrl+Shift+F, scope-selectable, can replace). Verified in the Postman docs at learning.postman.com/docs/getting-started/navigating-postman/. It is a Postman desktop-app feature (the docs say so); the page I fetched does not mention web. The Postman community thread 26972 confirms users rely on it to rename an environment variable everywhere. They report that replacements in URLs did not take effect until the request was re-saved, and that colleagues did not see the changes. That gives PostPilot a concrete angle: apply in one transaction, refresh open tabs, show the Git push diff, and offer Undo. Correction to the evidence list: Hoppscotch issue #5572 (open, filed 2025-11-11) is a top-bar search request for substring matching of endpoint names, not a find-and-replace request. It supports the 'search is weak' pain but not 'replace', so do not cite it as replace evidence. The sidebar-name-only claim and the palette claim above are verified in code. I did not check Insomnia or Bruno.
- **Design in this codebase:** VERDICT: feasible with the current architecture, no rewrite, no schema change. Effort M (about 1.5 weeks) if undo stays in memory. It splits cleanly into three shippable slices: (1) read-only Find in workspace, about 3-4 days; (2) Replace with diff preview and undo, about 4-5 days; (3) Rename variable everywhere, about 3 days.

WHY IT FITS (verified in lib/)
- All state is local Drift/SQLite on every platform: requests (url, headersJson, queryParamsJson, bodyText, formFieldsJson, urlEncodedFieldsJson, graphqlQuery, graphqlVariables, authConfigJson), request_scripts (assertionsJson, extractorsJson), response_examples, environment_variables, global_variables, collection_variables, collection_auth, entity_docs. There is no dart:io or network dependency, so web, mobile and desktop behave the same. No conditional imports are needed.
- Git "dirty" tracking needs no work, which corrects the candidate's platformNotes. GitStatusUseCase diffs a snapshot read from the DB (LocalCollectionStoreImpl.readSnapshot) against the stored base, so any DB write shows up in the Git push preview automatically. WorkplaceViewModel mirrors workplaceDataChanges() to workspace.json with an 800 ms debounce, so one transaction produces one file write.
- Precedent for a data class that uses AppDatabase directly and runs one _db.transaction: LocalCollectionStoreImpl. Precedent for the dialog, planner and use-case pattern: OpenApiRefreshDialog + OpenApiRefreshPlanner (pure domain) + RefreshOpenApiUseCase. A diff-list UI exists in push_preview_dialog.dart (ToolSection, change rows).
- Palette registration is one PaletteItem line in PaletteItems.tools(). The tab-refresh helper reopenReplacedRequests(shell, changedIds, deletedIds) already exists in features/git_sync/presentation/.

DESIGN
New folder lib/features/workspace_search/ with domain, data and presentation.
1. domain/ (pure Dart, unit-testable):
   - TextSlot = (table, rowId, column, jsonPath inside the column, label, ownerRequestId, collectionId, isSecret, text).
   - TextMatcher: literal or regex, case, whole word, and $1/$& expansion in the replacement. The regex is compiled once and invalid input is reported as an error.
   - ReplacePlan: hits grouped by collection > folder > request > field, each with a before/after snippet and a selected flag.
   - VariableRenamePlanner: reuses AppConstants.variablePattern, which is exactly {{[\w.$-]+}} with no spaces. It rewrites the token {{old}} to {{new}} in every slot. It also renames the key of environment_variables, collection_variables and global_variables rows, and the "key" field inside extractorsJson. It rejects a new name that does not match the pattern, skips $-dynamic names, and warns when the new name already exists in a scope.
2. data/ WorkspaceTextRepositoryImpl(AppDatabase):
   - readSlots(scopes, collectionId?) does bulk selects per table. Do NOT reuse CollectionLoader.loadAll(), which does N+1 findById through stream .first.
   - Slots address JSON columns by decoded leaf (headers[i].key, headers[i].value, authConfig.bearerToken, extractors[i].key, ...). The repository decodes the column, replaces the leaf, and re-encodes it. A raw SQL REPLACE over JSON text would corrupt quotes and newlines.
   - applyEdits(edits) runs in ONE _db.transaction. Each edit carries expectedOld and is applied compare-and-swap, so a row edited meanwhile is skipped and reported. It returns a ReplaceReceipt that holds the before and after values per touched slot.
   - undo(receipt) is also compare-and-swap, and restores a slot only if its current value still equals the replaced value.
   - Receipt kept in memory for the session. Persisting it would need a new table or a SettingEntries JSON blob. Avoid a new table: the AppDatabase migration note says addColumn crashes the drift web worker, and createTable is the only proven path.
3. presentation/: WorkspaceSearchViewModel (ChangeNotifier, factory in injector.dart like OpenApiRefresh) and WorkspaceSearchDialog built on ToolDialog (the icon/title chip, ToolSection, InfoBanner and BusyLabel from core/widgets).
   - Layout: query + replace fields, case/word/regex toggles, scope chips (URL, params, headers, body, auth, tests, examples, environments and variables, docs), a grouped hit list with checkboxes and an inline before/after diff, and a footer with "Replace N selected" and "Undo last replace".
   - On phone widths ToolDialog already goes full screen; stack the diff columns when narrow.
   - Render with ListView.builder and cap hits at about 2,000 with a "refine your search" note.
   - Clicking a hit calls ShellViewModel.selectRequest(id), or opens EnvironmentsManagerDialog.show / CollectionVariablesDialog.show for variable hits.
4. After apply, call reopenReplacedRequests(shell, touchedRequestIds, const []) for every request whose url, headers, body, auth, scripts or examples changed. This is MANDATORY: RequestBuilderViewModel loads the request once and every later edit re-saves the whole in-memory snapshot (_update -> saveRequest), and RequestScriptsViewModel behaves the same for extractors and assertions. An open tab would silently revert the replace on its next keystroke.
5. Wiring:
   - injector.dart: register the repository and use case in _registerDevTools().
   - Palette: add 'Find in workspace' and 'Replace in workspace' to PaletteItems.tools(), plus 'Rename variable everywhere...' with a variable picker fed by ListVariablesUseCase.
   - Shortcut: add AppShortcut.findInWorkspace (Ctrl/Cmd+Shift+F, browserReserved: true so it becomes Alt on web) with an optional ShortcutHandlers callback, like openCommandPalette.
   - Collection PopupMenu in collections_sidebar.dart: one new item 'Find in this collection...' that pre-scopes the dialog.

UI PLACEMENT CORRECTION. VariableHoverCard is deliberately IgnorePointer and transient (it hides on MouseRegion exit), so a clickable "Rename everywhere" button cannot live on it without breaking its design. Put the entry instead in (a) a contextMenuBuilder on VariableTextFormField that shows "Rename variable everywhere" when the caret sits inside a {{token}} (works with right-click and mobile long-press), (b) the overflow menu of rows in environments_manager_dialog.dart and collection_variables_dialog.dart, and (c) the palette command.

CORRECTED PREMISE. The candidate says there is "only a sidebar name filter". The command palette (PaletteItems.requests) already matches request URL, raw body and GraphQL query through hiddenText (low weight, 2+ characters). It does not cover headers, auth, extractors, assertions, environment values or examples, and has no regex or replace. So the global search half of the pitch overlaps partly; the differentiator is replace and rename. Do not add a second search UI inside the palette.

REALISTIC NUMBERS. About 15-20 min per replace or rename event (about 40 requests x 25 s), but such events are spiky, roughly 1 per week. Plus about 3 quick lookups per week at about 1 min each. That is about 12 min x 1/week for replace/rename, not 12 x 2.

TESTS. Matcher and planner as pure unit tests. Repository tests on an in-memory AppDatabase.forTesting (precedent in test/git_sync/local_collection_store_test.dart): JSON round-trip with quotes/newlines, CAS skip, undo conflict, rename across environments, globals and extractors. One widget test for the dialog, and one for reopenReplacedRequests being invoked.
- **Risks:** 1) Stale open tabs (HIGH if missed). The request editor and the Tests tab keep an in-memory snapshot and re-save the whole thing on the next edit. Without reopenReplacedRequests on every touched request id (including ones changed only via scripts or examples), the replace is silently undone. Needs an explicit test.
2) JSON column corruption. Never do SQL or string REPLACE on headersJson, queryParamsJson, authConfigJson, assertionsJson, extractorsJson, formFieldsJson or urlEncodedFieldsJson. Decode, replace string leaves, re-encode. Replacing in bodyText, graphqlQuery and url as plain text is safe.
3) Secrets. Slots flagged secret must be excluded from the default scope and their snippets masked, like the hover card. Use the existing SecretNames/SecretFields helpers in git_sync/domain/services (auth credential keys, secret-looking headers/params/form fields, environment and global rows with isSecret). Replacing inside secrets is opt-in via a checkbox. The result list and diff must never print secret values. No network, no ToS or store-policy issue: it is entirely local. Postman documents the same Ctrl+Shift+F feature, so only the concept is shared; do not copy Postman's UI or assets.
4) User regex and large text. Dart has no regex timeout and web has no isolate. A catastrophic pattern can freeze the UI. Mitigate by compiling once, validating, yielding between rows (await Future.delayed(Duration.zero)), and skipping slots over about 1 MB (large saved examples). Accept the residual risk: it is the user's own data and own regex.
5) Variable rename semantics. Collisions when the new name already exists in some scope; the same name defined in several environments, so it must be renamed in all of them (offer scope checkboxes); {{old}} used inside other variables' values; extractors store the key without braces; $dynamic names must be excluded; new names must match [\w.$-]+. Show a full preview and require confirmation.
6) Undo is session-only and compare-and-swap. If a row was edited after the replace, that slot is reported as a conflict and not restored. Safety nets for linked collections are the Git discard and the existing Backup export; suggest "Back up first" in the confirm step. Do not add a new table: the AppDatabase migration comments say addColumn crashes the drift web worker, and only createTable is known to work.
7) Performance on web (wasm SQLite, single thread). Bulk selects only, a hit cap, a lazy list. Do not use CollectionLoader.loadAll() (N+1).
8) Scope creep. Hover-card button and per-hit diff are the costly UI parts; shipping Find + Replace first and Rename second keeps each step reviewable. History table and response bodies in history should stay out of scope (blobs, no value).
9) Overlap note: the command palette already matches URL and body; keep Find in workspace as a separate dialog to avoid two competing search UIs.
- **Strongest objection:** The claimed 2 uses/week x 12 min is not realistic. Most of the scenarios in the pitch are already handled by a variable or by an existing PostPilot feature.

1. Host and version changes. A host change or an /api/v1 -> /api/v2 bump is one edit of {{baseUrl}} in the environment. If the host is hardcoded in 40 requests, the real chore is "extract the host into a variable", not a replace. That is a different, one-time feature that belongs to import and smart-paste.
2. Field renames. A field renamed across 40 JSON bodies is a backend contract change. PostPilot's 'Update from OpenAPI' with diff already handles that, safely and with review. A blind regex replace over bodies is the riskier tool for the job.
3. Variable renames. Renaming {{baseUrl}} to {{apiUrl}} happens maybe once a month, mostly during early workspace setup. It is not weekly.
4. Odoo. The JSON-RPC to JSON-2 migration is a one-time job, and the RPC converter already covers it. Odoo devs keep one {{baseUrl}} and {{db}}/{{apiKey}} per environment.
5. Real frequency. A bulk replace is roughly one use every 2-3 weeks per developer, about 12 min each, so about 4-5 min/week. Global lookup ('which request uses this id?') is maybe 1 use/week at about 1 min. Total is about 6 min/week, not 24.
6. Evidence is weak. Postman does ship Find and Replace (docs confirm it exists, desktop app only), so it is table stakes, but that is not evidence of weekly time savings. Hoppscotch #5572 (opened 2025-11-11) is only a request for substring matching in request-name search. It is not about replace and shows no visible reactions or comments. The Postman community thread was not re-verified, because the web search budget was exhausted.
7. Bruno and similar tools are file-based, so users reach for VS Code. In PostPilot the data lives in Drift and is not greppable, so the gap is real, just rare.

It does not clearly remove a painful chore. It is error-prone only when it happens, and it rarely happens.

Recommendation: do not build the full find-and-replace with snapshot undo and rename-everywhere as a headline feature (M effort). Ship the cheap part instead, a read-only 'Find in workspace' (URL, header, body and variable usage search, effort S). Pair it with 'Extract hardcoded host/token into variable' after cURL, Postman or smart-paste import. That second feature is the version of this idea that fixes a real recurring pain. Optionally add 'Rename variable' only as a small action on the environment row.
- **Platform notes:** Pure Drift/SQLite work, all platforms including web. Must mark touched entities dirty so Git Push preview shows them and refresh open tabs after apply. Do not search or replace inside local-only secret values unless the user ticks a box.

### 8. One-click CI workflow and scheduled monitor (GitHub Actions file, failure alerts, CLI parity)
- **Score 2.0** | effort M | ~5 min/week saved | persona qa | lenses: competitors, qa-testing, observability-debug, data-flows | skeptic votes 2/3
- **Problem today:** Running the collection on every push or every N minutes means hand-writing a GitHub Actions/GitLab file: install Dart or fetch the CLI, map each local-only secret to POSTPILOT_VAR_<name>, upload a junit report, wire an alert (25-45 min per project), so 'run the smoke folder before release' stays a manual 3-5 min chore and overnight breakage goes unnoticed. The CLI also lacks --data, --iterations, --retry, HTML/markdown report and step summary compared with Bruno/Hurl.
- **What PostPilot would do:** Collection Git menu 'Run in CI / Automate...': pick collection, environment, trigger (push/PR, nightly, every N min) and alert target (Slack/Discord/Teams/ntfy webhook or GitHub issue). PostPilot lists exactly which secrets to add (from local-only variables), shows the YAML diff and commits .github/workflows/postpilot.yml via the existing GitHub connection; GitLab/cron/Task Scheduler are copy-only. A 'Monitor' tab runs the collection on a timer while the desktop app is open and notifies only on failure, with slow/shape-drift thresholds. CLI gains --data csv|json, --iterations, --retry N, --report html|markdown and automatically writes GITHUB_STEP_SUMMARY and ::error annotations.
- **Where it lives:** Collection context menu/Git panel 'Run in CI...' (small wizard dialog), small Monitor dialog with last-20-runs sparkline, palette command, CLI subcommand 'postpilot ci init --provider github' and 'postpilot monitor'.
- **Partly exists already:** The raw ingredients exist; the automation on top of them does not.

EXISTS:
- CLI `run/list/mcp` in `lib/features/cli/cli_main.dart`, with `--env/--collection/--folder/--var/--bail/--timeout/--delay/--insecure/--report console|junit|json/--out`.
- `POSTPILOT_VAR_<name>` env-var secrets and `workspace.local.json` (cli_main.dart lines 31-33, 89-92).
- JUnit/JSON reporters (`lib/features/cli/reporters.dart`).
- README lines 97-105 document the CI usage.
- GUI collection runner already has iterations and CSV/JSON data rows: `collection_run_options.dart`, `run_data_parser.dart` (plain Dart with package:csv, so it is portable to the CLI), `collection_run_report.dart` (JSON/CSV export), `collection_runner_dialog.dart`.
- The Git layer can already write arbitrary paths. `GitHostClient.commit(..., changes: Map<path, text?>)` in `lib/features/git_sync/domain/repositories/git_host_client.dart` is not workspace-only, so committing `.github/workflows/postpilot.yml` needs no new plumbing.

DOES NOT EXIST (grepped lib/, bin/, README):
- No workflow/YAML generator and no 'Run in CI' wizard.
- No scheduler or monitor: there is no `Timer.periodic` run loop in lib.
- No Slack/Discord/Teams/ntfy webhook sender. The only webhook mention is a secret masker regex in `secret_masker.dart`.
- CLI has no `--data`, `--iterations`, `--retry`, HTML or markdown report, `GITHUB_STEP_SUMMARY` or `::error` annotations (`_Args._valued` is only env/collection/folder/timeout/delay/report/out).
- No retry anywhere.
- No `ci` or `monitor` subcommand.
- `.github/workflows/build-and-release.yml` builds only the desktop app and has no `dart compile exe` step, so there is no downloadable CLI binary today.

The candidate's own claim that cli_main exposes only run/list/mcp and README documents only junit is correct.

WEB CHECK:
- Postman monitors with schedule and email/Slack/Teams failure alerts: confirmed.
- Bruno `--csv-file-path` and `--iteration-count`: confirmed.
- Postman CLI GitHub Action URL: the page describes a hand-configured pre-built action. It does not describe a generator, so the 'config generator' wording is slightly off.
- Bruno `--reporter-html` was not on the fetched page; the follow-up search failed because the search budget is spent.
- Hurl and Apidog URLs were not re-fetched.

CAVEATS FOR THE OWNER:
- The time saving is inflated. Writing the workflow is a one-time 25-45 min job per project, not 4 uses a week. A fair figure is about 30 min per project, 1-2 projects a month.
- The bundle is three features: the wizard, the monitor and CLI parity. The monitor only runs while the desktop app is open.
- The strongest, cheapest core is `postpilot ci init`, the Git-menu wizard, a downloadable CLI binary, and `--data/--iterations/--retry/--report markdown` plus the step summary.
- Port the existing `RunDataParser` for the CLI data options.
- The in-app timer monitor is the weakest part and could be cut or deferred.

All paths are under C:/Users/Md Manzurul Islam/OneDrive/Desktop/claude/postpilot/.
- **Competitors:** Postman Monitors: scheduled runs with email/Slack/Teams failure alerts (learning.postman.com/docs/monitoring-your-api/setting-up-monitor, confirmed). Postman CLI GitHub Action (confirmed as a hand-written action, not a generator). Bruno bru CLI with --csv-file-path and --iteration-count (confirmed at docs.usebruno.com/bru-cli/runCollection); its --reporter-html was not confirmed. Apidog scheduled tasks and Hurl --retry/--report-html were not re-verified. None of these auto-commit a CI workflow file to the user's repo from the desktop app, so that part is genuinely differentiating.
- **Design in this codebase:** VERDICT: feasible with the existing architecture, but only if scoped down. Effort is M (about 2 weeks) for the cut-down version below. The full candidate as written (CI wizard + CLI parity + in-app Monitor + webhook alerts + release pipeline) is L (3-5 weeks) and I would split it. The idea is also less turnkey than the candidate claims: it has 4 factual gaps (see CORRECTIONS).

CORRECTIONS TO THE CANDIDATE (verified in code or by running dart)
1. The CLI binary prerequisite is wrong. `dart compile exe bin/postpilot.dart` FAILS in this repo: "'dart compile' does not support build hooks, use 'dart build' instead. Packages with build hooks: objective_c, sqlite3." `dart build cli -t bin/postpilot.dart -o <dir>` WORKS on Dart 3.12.0: it produced bundle/bin/postpilot.exe (7.5 MB) plus bundle/lib/sqlite3.dll (1.7 MB, an unneeded native asset), about 8.9 MB in total, and `postpilot.exe --version` printed "postpilot 1.0.0". So the release asset must be a tar.gz/zip of the whole bundle, not one exe. A new `build-cli` job in C:/Users/Md Manzurul Islam/OneDrive/Desktop/claude/postpilot/.github/workflows/build-and-release.yml (flutter-action, `flutter pub get`, `dart build cli`, sha256 file, upload to the release) is needed. `dart build` does not cross-compile, so one runner per OS; linux-x64 is enough for CI. The CLI import closure is 51 files with no Flutter, drift or dio imports, so the source is fine; only the packaging is the issue. Today the release workflow publishes no CLI at all, and the README only documents `dart run bin/postpilot.dart`, which needs a checkout of the PostPilot source plus Flutter in CI. This is the real blocker for the feature.
2. "Existing GitHub connection" is two different things. (a) Workplace sync (lib/features/workplace) pushes ONE `workspace.json` at the repo root via the contents API, with a per-workplace PAT in WorkplaceTokenStore (classic token, `repo` scope, header `Authorization: token ...`). This is the file the CLI reads (+ workspace.local.json). (b) git_sync (lib/features/git_sync) syncs per-collection docs (`collection.json`, `*.request.json`, `_folder.json`) with a global token; the CLI cannot read that layout (no loader). So the CI generator must target workplace-connected repos (`postpilot run workspace.json`). Supporting git_sync repos would need a new CLI loader over RepoLayout.fromFiles (a pure-Dart service), which is extra, so defer it.
3. The PROD lock is not honoured by the CLI. ProductionGuard is UI-only (collection_runner_dialog.dart, request_builder_page.dart) and works by showing a confirm dialog, so a cron or unattended run cannot use it. `postpilot run --env Production` currently sends POST/PUT/DELETE with no check. ProductionDetector (lib/features/safety/domain/services/production_detector.dart) is pure Dart and reusable.
4. Secret mapping is incomplete. `POSTPILOT_VAR_<name>` only feeds variables. SecretSplitter also blanks literal auth fields (keys `cauth/<collection>/<field>`, `rauth/<collection>/<request>/<field>`), and the CLI cannot receive those, so a request with a typed-in bearer token gets 401 in CI. Also GitHub secret names allow only [A-Z0-9_], and the same variable name in Staging and Production collapses to one POSTPILOT_VAR_ name.

DESIGN (cut-down M scope)
A. CLI parity (S, about 3-5 days, all in lib/features/cli/, unit tests in test/cli/cli_test.dart with the fake CliSend):
 - `--data file.csv|json` and `--iterations N`: reuse the pure-Dart RunDataParser and CollectionRunOptions from lib/features/request_builder/domain/services/. Feed each row as top-priority `overrides` in RunState.
 - `--retry N`, `--report markdown|html` (new functions in reporters.dart).
 - When GITHUB_ACTIONS=true: print `::error` annotations. When GITHUB_STEP_SUMMARY is set: append the markdown report.
 - `POSTPILOT_LOCAL_SECRETS` env var (the content of workspace.local.json, base64) fed to the existing WorkspaceRunner.parse(localSecrets:). This is one GitHub secret instead of N, and it covers auth-field secrets.
 - `--allow-prod-writes`, default off: when the env name is production-like (ProductionDetector), skip or fail data-changing requests.
 - Mask assertion `actual` and extracted values (SecretMasker.maskMessage, plus `::add-mask::` for extracted tokens) in CI output.
B. CI generator (M), new folder lib/features/ci_automation/ in the existing domain/data/presentation style:
 - domain/services/ci_workflow_builder.dart (pure Dart string builder, byte-stable, unit-testable): outputs YAML for GitHub Actions. It uses `permissions: contents: read`, a job per env (or GitHub Environments so one secret name resolves per environment), a pinned CLI download URL + sha256 check, `postpilot run workspace.json --env X --report junit`, an artifact upload, an `if: failure()` webhook step (Slack/Discord/Teams/ntfy body built with jq --arg or env, never `${{ }}` inside `run:`), and triggers `schedule` + `workflow_dispatch` + optional `push` on workspace.json. For PRs, same-repo only; never `pull_request_target`.
 - domain/services/ci_secret_planner.dart: reads SecretSplitter.split(...).secrets keys to list exactly which GitHub secrets to create and flags auth-field secrets.
 - data/workflow_committer.dart: reuses GitHubHttp (lib/features/git_sync/data/github/github_http.dart) with a small GitCredentialsStore adapter over WorkplaceTokenStore, doing PUT /contents/.github/workflows/postpilot.yml (the pattern already exists in WorkplaceRepositoryImpl._pushToRemoteGit). It maps the missing-`workflow`-scope failure to a clear message, because the existing _formatGitDioError would say "ensure repo scope". Works on web: api.github.com is already used from the web build.
 - presentation: CiViewModel as a factory in core/di/injector.dart `_registerDevTools`; ci_dialog.dart as a ToolDialog + ToolTabs (Workflow | Secrets | Alerts); a PaletteItem 'ci.workflow' in PaletteItems.tools().
 - UI placement correction: the collection context menu (collections_sidebar.dart) already has about 20 entries, so do not add one there. Put the entry in the workplace sidebar header menu (workplace_sidebar_header.dart, next to push/pull) plus the palette. Ship "Copy / download YAML" first, "Commit to repo" second. Copy-first avoids the token-scope friction.
C. In-app Monitor: DEFER (extra M, desktop-only). Reasons: no timer or scheduler infrastructure, and no tray or local-notification package in pubspec.yaml, so the only alerts are an in-app indicator plus a webhook. Web timers are throttled in background tabs and mobile suspends, so it must be gated to desktop (conditional import like mock_server_engine_io/stub). CollectionRunnerService always uses the ACTIVE environment (BuildVariableResolverUseCase), so running a chosen env means switching the user's UI, which is wrong. Instead build a snapshot with BackupService.snapshot() and run the same pure-Dart WorkspaceRunner with a CliSend adapter over ApiClient, which also gives CI parity. Avoid a Drift table (schemaVersion 4 means a migration plus build_runner, and C: has about 3 GB free); keep the config as JSON in SharedPreferences and the webhook URL in flutter_secure_storage, like SecureAiSettingsStore. The scheduled GitHub workflow already covers "overnight breakage" without any new runtime.

REALISTIC NUMBERS: setup is a one-off 25-45 min per project (not weekly). Weekly gain is about 3-4 automated smoke runs x 3-4 min, so about 10-15 min per week for a developer who really uses CI, and near 0 for one who does not. Persona is qa/backend, not all.
- **Risks:** 1. Release engineering (biggest effort risk): the CLI has to ship as a `dart build cli` bundle (exe + sqlite3 native asset) built per OS. The release workflow runs on every push to main with auto-numbered tags, so the generated YAML must pin an explicit tag + sha256, and only tags built after the new job exist carry the asset. The CLI's cliVersion is '1.0.0' while the app is 1.0.4+3: align them. I could not check that Dart cross-compiles for other targets (web search budget ran out), so assume one runner per OS.
2. Security, secret exfiltration: the CLI runs whatever workspace.json says with secrets in env, so a PR that edits a request URL can leak them. Mitigate: no pull_request_target, same-repo PRs only, `permissions: contents: read`, secrets only via env, no `${{ github.event.* }}` in run steps, pin actions by SHA and the CLI by sha256.
3. Security, log leaks: RunReporters.line prints raw assertion `actual` (response data) unmasked. On public repos this lands in logs and step summaries, and GitHub only auto-masks registered secrets, not tokens returned by a login call. Mask in CI mode.
4. Production safety: the CLI has no PROD guard today. A nightly cron with `--env Production` would POST/DELETE against prod. Default read-only for production-named envs and require an explicit `--allow-prod-writes`; the wizard should warn.
5. Token scope: writing .github/workflows needs the classic `workflow` scope (or a fine-grained "Workflows: write" permission). Most users' existing `repo`-only token will be rejected, so they must create a new one, and the existing error mapping would mislead. Keep copy/download as the default path and never store a second token silently. I could not re-verify this with a search; it matches GitHub's documented scope list (docs.github.com, OAuth scopes: `workflow`).
6. GitHub Actions limits and terms (not re-verified, check before shipping): the shortest cron interval is 5 minutes and runs are often delayed; scheduled workflows in public repos are disabled after about 60 days without repo activity; private repos consume the free-minute quota, and a 5-minute cron can use roughly 8,600 minutes a month. Default to nightly or hourly, and warn below 15 minutes. Actions terms restrict use unrelated to building/testing the project, so a pure uptime pinger could be flagged. Frame it as API smoke tests.
7. Webhooks: Slack/Discord/Teams URLs and ntfy topics are secrets. Store them only in GitHub secrets or secure storage, never in workspace.json or the repo (SecretMasker already recognises Slack webhooks). Send only request names, status and masked URLs, never bodies. An ntfy.sh topic is public to anyone who knows the name. From the web build, Slack webhooks fail on CORS, so any app-side alert is desktop-only.
8. Store policy: no risk, because the app only generates text and commits a file and never downloads or runs code on iOS or Android. The in-app Monitor would be refused on iOS and Android background limits, so it is desktop-only.
9. Semantics: a `push` trigger on the collection repo fires on collection edits, not on backend releases (the collection repo is usually separate from the code repo), so also emit a `workflow_call`/`repository_dispatch` snippet for backend pipelines. GitLab, cron and Task Scheduler stay copy-only.
10. Scope creep: Monitor "shape drift" needs response bodies and schema compare, but the runner discards bodies after the scripts run, so do not promise it in v1.
- **Strongest objection:** The claimed 4 uses/week x 5 min is wrong. The CI wizard is a once-per-project chore, not a weekly one. A dev who starts about 1 new CI-enabled project a month saves roughly 25 of the 25-45 setup minutes, which is about 6 min/week at best. A README snippet or a 'Copy workflow YAML' button gets most of that for a few days of work. (My WebSearch budget ran out, so I could not check Postman CLI's generator or GitHub cron limits online. The points below come from reading the code and from general knowledge.)

1. The hard prerequisite does not exist yet. cli_main.dart only offers run/list/mcp, and build-and-release.yml builds no CLI binary. A generated workflow in a user's repo has nothing to download, and PostPilot is not on pub.dev. Until a binary is released, the wizard would generate a broken file. That release is a separate, small chore.

2. The Monitor tab does not solve the stated pain. It runs only while the desktop app is open, and web and mobile suspend timers. 'Overnight breakage goes unnoticed' is exactly the case it misses. Free tools already cover uptime and alerting: Uptime Kuma, UptimeRobot, Checkly, and Postman or Apidog monitors. The scheduled GitHub Actions cron is the only real monitor, and it has its own limits: it is not exact, and it can be disabled after long repo inactivity.

3. Reachability. GitHub-hosted runners cannot reach VPN-only or IP-allowlisted staging APIs. Many Odoo and internal backend setups are like this. They would need a self-hosted runner, which this wizard does not set up. Secrets stay a manual step anyway.

4. The commit step needs a token with Workflows: write. Asking users to paste a broader PAT just to save a copy-paste is a poor trade for a security-conscious audience.

5. 'Run the smoke folder before release' is already one click in the existing runner (about 1 min, not 3-5). The 'manual chore' this removes is smaller than claimed.

6. The CLI parity items (--data, --iterations, --retry, html/markdown report, GITHUB_STEP_SUMMARY and ::error annotations) are the most useful part, and QA would use them. They are separate small CLI features, not a CI wizard. They should be judged on their own: each is S effort and worth a few minutes a week for a QA user.

Verdict: do not build the wizard plus Monitor bundle as pitched. If anything, do two small things. First, publish a CLI binary in the release workflow and add a 'Copy GitHub Actions YAML' button, with the secrets list taken from the local-only variables. Second, add step-summary and markdown report output to the CLI.
- **Platform notes:** YAML generation and GitHub commit work on every platform. Real prerequisite: a downloadable CLI binary (add 'dart compile exe bin/postpilot.dart' to .github/workflows/build-and-release.yml). Writing under .github/workflows needs a token with Workflows: write (ask only at this step); setting repo secrets stays manual. In-app scheduler works only while the desktop app runs (web/mobile suspend timers); production monitors stay GET/HEAD and honour the PROD lock.

### 9. Suggest tests from this response (deterministic, with stability probe)
- **Score 2.0** | effort M | ~5 min/week saved | persona all | lenses: qa-testing | skeptic votes 2/3
- **Problem today:** After sending a new endpoint the dev adds assertions row by row (type, JSON path, expected value): about 8 rows and 4 min per request, so tests stay thin or are skipped. Hard-coded values that change per call (ids, timestamps, tokens) make tests flaky the next day. The existing Ask AI test suggestion needs an Anthropic key, looks at one response and caps at 8 checks.
- **What PostPilot would do:** A 'Suggest tests' button in the request Tests tab (and 'Add smoke tests to every request that has a saved example' in the collection menu) reads the last response and proposes a checkbox list: status, Content-Type, response time (observed x3), one 'matches JSON Schema' assertion from the existing schema infer, array non-empty, and exact-value checks only for fields that stayed identical across 2 probe sends (GET/HEAD only, never against a production-locked env). Dev reviews and clicks 'Add selected'. Offline; Ask AI can optionally add business rules on top.
- **Where it lives:** Request Tests tab (button above the assertion list, enabled when a response exists), collection context menu 'Add smoke tests', palette command. Nothing added to request/response panes.
- **Partly exists already:** The building blocks exist, but the core of the candidate (deterministic bulk suggestion, stability probe, collection-wide smoke tests) does not.

What exists:
- lib/features/ai_assistant/presentation/ai_tab.dart + lib/features/ai_assistant/domain/ai_tasks.dart: "Suggest tests" in the Ask AI response-tools tab. It needs an Anthropic key, reads a single response, caps at 8 items (parseAssertions breaks at 8), has no review checkboxes (shows a list, then "Add N tests" adds all), and explicitly skips jsonSchema. The candidate's claims about AI are accurate.
- lib/features/response_tools/presentation/widgets/schema_tab.dart: "Infer from N responses" builds a JSON Schema from the current response plus history (compareSources), and "Save as test" adds one jsonSchema assertion. So the 'matches JSON Schema' item is already one click, but only from the response-tools Schema tab.
- lib/features/response_tools/presentation/widgets/explore_tab.dart (_assert): per-node "add test" (jsonPathEquals for scalars, jsonPathExists otherwise). This is manual, one path at a time.
- lib/features/response_tools/presentation/widgets/compare_tab.dart has a hard-coded volatile-key set (updated_at, request_id, ...) used only for diffing, not for tests.
- lib/features/scripting/domain/entities/assertion_entity.dart: AssertionType has statusEquals, statusIn2xx, bodyContains, jsonPathEquals, jsonPathExists, headerEquals, headerExists, responseTimeBelowMs, jsonSchema. There is no type-is, array-length or regex assertion, as the candidate says.
- lib/features/scripting/presentation/widgets/request_tests_tab.dart: only the assertion editor (manual rows) and extractors. There is no suggest button.

Not found anywhere in lib/, bin/ or README.md (grep for suggest/smoke/probe/stability/volatile/generate tests/bulk assertions):
- A deterministic, offline "suggest tests" that bundles status + Content-Type + time x3 + schema + array non-empty + stable exact values into a reviewable checkbox list.
- A stability probe (re-send twice, keep only fields that stay identical, GET/HEAD only, skip prod-locked env). The only "probe" in the code is the DNS/TCP/TLS network_probe for the timing tab.
- Any collection-menu "Add smoke tests" (collections_sidebar.dart popup has Run, Variables, Auth, Docs, Git sync, OpenAPI refresh, Dart API, Mock server, Export... but nothing for tests), palette command (palette_items.dart has no test/assert entries) or CLI subcommand (lib/features/cli has no generate/suggest).

Two small issues: the "OpenAPI test generator" the candidate says it shares code with does not exist in the code (README line 89 only mentions schema check as a saved test), so that note is speculative. The "saved example" source for bulk smoke tests does exist indirectly (mock server uses saved examples, see lib/features/mock_server), but I did not verify a per-request saved-example store that the bulk action could read.
- **Competitors:** Postman Postbot / Agent Mode: confirmed. The Postman docs page (learning.postman.com/docs/agent-mode/overview/) lists writing and updating tests as a capability, and the dev.to article describes Postbot generating "happy path" tests from a response. Apidog AI test case generation with accept/reject review: confirmed on the cited page. Verify (VerifyTests/Verify): the README has dedicated Guids and Dates scrubbing docs, which supports the volatile-value point loosely (the fetch summary said scrubbing is configurable, not automatic, so treat this one as supportive but not exact). All three tools are AI- or cloud-based, which supports the offline deterministic angle. Postman and Apidog do not appear to do a stability probe that drops volatile fields, which is the most original piece.
- **Design in this codebase:** VERDICT: feasible without a rewrite; nearly every piece already exists. Corrected effort is M (about 8-11 working days) for the full candidate (generator, dialog, probe, bulk, palette). It is S (3-4 days) if v1 uses only the 9 existing assertion types and takes its stability signal from history instead of new sends.

WHAT EXISTS AND CAN BE REUSED
- AssertionEntity/AssertionType in lib/features/scripting/domain/entities/assertion_entity.dart, with AssertionEvaluator, ScriptsJsonCodec and RequestScriptsRepository. Assertions are stored as plain JSON rows, and the CLI (lib/features/cli/workspace_runner.dart) and MCP use the same evaluator. Any new assertion type therefore works headless for free.
- JsonSchemaTools.infer(samples) and validate() in lib/features/response_tools/domain/services/json_schema_tools.dart. The scripting evaluator already imports it, so no new dependency direction.
- ResponseHistory (lib/features/response_tools/domain/services/response_history.dart): the last 8 responses per request, kept in memory and recorded by ResponseViewer on every send. It is a free stability signal with ZERO extra network calls. Saved ResponseExampleEntity rows are a second sample source.
- SecretNames.looksSecretKey (lib/features/git_sync/domain/services/secret_names.dart) and SecretMasker, to keep secrets out of generated checks.
- ToolDialog (lib/core/widgets/tool_dialog.dart), which fills the screen on phones, plus BusyLabel for the loading button.
- CollectionLoader and ResponseExampleRepository, used the same way BuildApiLayerUseCase and BuildMockRoutesUseCase already use them.
- ProductionGuard, which holds the production check. Only a small public accessor is missing.

PROPOSED STRUCTURE (inside the scripting feature, no new top-level feature needed)
1. Domain, pure Dart, works on web and mobile (no dart:io):
   - scripting/domain/services/test_suggester.dart.
   - Input: a list of response samples (newest first), the request method, and a hint for whether the response looks like Odoo.
   - Output: List<SuggestedTest> {AssertionEntity, label, group, defaultChecked, reason}.
   - Rules:
     * Status: statusEquals with the observed code, not statusIn2xx.
     * Content-Type: headerEquals or headerExists.
     * Response time: limit = max(1000 ms, 3 x the slowest observed). Unchecked by default, because time checks are the number one source of flaky tests.
     * Structure: for an array, assert non-empty with a minimal per-path schema. For each field present in ALL samples, assert the type. Infer from ALL history and example samples, not only the current one.
     * Exact value: only for fields identical across at least 2 samples, and only for short, enum-like scalars (bool, small int, short string). Never for secret-looking keys (SecretNames.looksSecretKey), JWT-like or high-entropy strings, emails, or long strings.
   - Generated JSON paths must round-trip through JsonPathResolver, which does not support a ']' inside a key. Use ["a.b"] bracket form for keys containing dots, and skip fields whose keys contain ']'.
   - Skip array[0] exact checks (order is brittle).
2. Probe, only when history plus examples hold fewer than 2 samples:
   - scripting/domain/usecases/probe_response_stability_usecase.dart. It calls SendRequestUseCase 2 times. That use case does NOT run extractors, so no variable side effects.
   - Add an optional recordHistory flag, default true, to SendRequestUseCase.call so probes do not pollute History.
   - Add a public Future<String?> activeProductionName() to ProductionGuard (it wraps the private _activeProductionName). Refuse to probe when it is non-null.
   - Allow only GET/HEAD, plus an allowlist of Odoo read methods on /json/2/<model>/<method>: search_read, read, search_count, fields_get, name_search, read_group, search. Odoo uses POST for everything, so a GET/HEAD-only rule would exclude the Odoo persona entirely.
   - Explicit button: "Check stability (sends 2 more GET requests to host X)". Never automatic.
3. UI:
   - request_tests_tab.dart gets a "Suggest tests" button above the assertion list. Disabled with a tooltip when there is no response. Read the response from context.read<RequestBuilderViewModel>().response, falling back to ResponseHistory.of(requestId).firstOrNull. RequestBuilderViewModel.load() nulls response on a tab switch, so the fallback is needed.
   - New scripting/presentation/widgets/suggest_tests_dialog.dart in ToolDialog: a grouped checkbox list (Status, Headers, Time, Structure, Values) with a reason caption per row, a footerLeading status line, and an "Add selected (N)" action.
   - "Add selected" calls RequestScriptsViewModel.updateAssertions([...existing, ...picked]), deduped on (type, path, expected). Do not use ResponseToolsViewModel.addAssertion here.
   - Banner when the observed status is 4xx/5xx: "tests below assert the failure"; preselect only the status check.
   - Warn and disable when response.truncated is true (see CollectionRunResult.truncatedBodyHint).
   - Collection menu item "Add smoke tests..." in collections_sidebar.dart, plus a palette item in PaletteItems.tools(). The bulk use case reads CollectionLoader plus ResponseExampleRepository only, so it is fully offline and sends nothing. It only touches requests that have saved examples and currently zero assertions, shows a preview dialog first, and preserves extractorsJson when saving (use the current.copyWith pattern).
   - A per-request palette command needs a new ShellViewModel register/unregister pair like registerBodySearch. Optional.
4. Assertion types: the three proposed types (JSON path type is X, array length >= N, regex) are all expressible today as a jsonSchema assertion with a path (type, minItems, pattern), so v1 needs no enum change. The cost is poor readability: every row is named "$path matches the JSON Schema". Adding one "JSON path type is" type (and optionally "array length at least") is worth it for review readability. It touches AssertionEntity.name and the evaluator (exhaustive switches), the editor hint, the AI testsSystem prompt and parseAssertions, plus tests in test/assertion_evaluator_test.dart (45 references already). Estimate 1-2 days.
5. Tests: the generator is a pure function, so unit-test it with fixture JSON (Odoo search_read with false and [id,name] pairs, nullable fields, volatile ids and timestamps, a secret-keyed field, truncated body) in test/scripting or test/dev_tools.
6. Ask AI optional layer: reuse AiTab/AiTasks.testsSystem unchanged. Do not merge the two flows in v1.

PLATFORM NOTES
- Generator, dialog and bulk use case are pure Dart plus the Drift repositories, so they work on web, mobile and desktop.
- Probe re-sends go through the same ApiClient/Dio as Send. If Send worked on web (CORS passed), the probe works, so there is no new CORS surface. No conditional imports are needed.
- On web, browsers expose only CORS-safelisted response headers. Content-Type is safelisted, but other generated header checks would be unreliable. Restrict generated header checks to Content-Type on web, or when the header was actually observed.
- CLI/MCP get the new checks for free through the shared evaluator.
- **Risks:** SECURITY / PRIVACY (the one that must be designed in)
- Assertion rows are committed to Git verbatim. doc_values.dart and request_doc_mapper.dart pass tests.assertions through with no secret scrubbing; secret handling covers headers, query, form and body, not assertions. A generated "access_token equals eyJ..." or an exact email, name or phone value would be pushed to the user's GitHub repo, which may be shared or public. Mitigation: never emit exact-value checks for SecretNames.looksSecretKey keys, JWT-like or high-entropy strings, emails, or long strings. Keep exact-value rows unchecked by default. Consider also running SecretMasker over the expected text.

PROBE RISKS
- Extra requests can hit rate limits or quotas, or trigger billing, and some GET endpoints have side effects. Mitigation: opt-in only, show the host and the number of requests, GET/HEAD plus the Odoo read allowlist only, refuse while the production lock detects a production environment, and prefer history and examples so most users never probe.
- ProductionGuard.checkSend only warns for POST/PUT/PATCH/DELETE. The candidate's "never against a production-locked env" therefore needs the new activeProductionName() accessor. Note that if the user turned the production lock off in Safety prefs, the probe is allowed. That is acceptable.
- Probes also appear in the Console log (LoggingApiClient). Pass recordHistory=false to keep History clean.
- If the request uses dynamic variables ({{$random...}}, {{$timestamp}}), each probe resolves them differently. Fields then look volatile, which errs on the safe side.

FLAKINESS / CORRECTNESS
- Inferring from ONE sample is wrong in two ways. A null-valued field infers type "null" and fails the next time it is populated. Odoo returns false for empty char and many2one fields, so an empty string field infers as boolean and fails once it has a value. Mitigation: infer from all history and examples, skip type constraints on null and false values for Odoo-looking responses, and mark required only for keys present in every sample.
- Response-time assertions are noisy and slower on CI or the CLI. Keep them unchecked by default, with a floor of at least 1000 ms.
- A truncated response (size cap) yields invalid or partial tests, so block generation. An error response (4xx/5xx) would be encoded as the expected behaviour, so warn.

COMPATIBILITY
- ScriptsJsonCodec.decodeAssertions falls back to statusIn2xx for an unknown type name. A workspace synced via Git or Backup from a newer build and opened in an older build would silently turn a new "type is X" row into "Status is 2xx" (a wrong but passing check). Mitigation: either avoid new enum values in v1 (use jsonSchema plus path), or add a forward-compatible fallback (a disabled or unsupported marker) in the same change.

STATE / RACE (existing latent issue the bulk action would worsen)
- RequestScriptsViewModel loads once and rewrites BOTH lists on every edit without watching the DB. A bulk "Add smoke tests" that writes through the repository while that request's Tests tab is mounted can be overwritten on the next edit there. Mitigation: have the VM subscribe to RequestScriptsRepository.watch(requestId) (the stream already exists), and restrict the bulk action to requests with no assertions. The in-tab button writes through the VM itself, so it is not affected.
- Bulk-adding assertions to many requests makes a large Git push diff. This is fine because the push preview exists, but mention it in the dialog.

LEGAL / ToS / STORE POLICY
- No concerns: pure local logic, no new third-party endpoint, no remote code, no new permission. The optional Ask AI path is already opt-in with the user's own key and masking. Re-sending a user's own request to the host they already target is user-initiated and negligible.

SCOPE HONESTY
- The text claims the new types are "shared with the OpenAPI test generator". No such generator exists in lib/ today. If a spec-based generator is wanted, that is a separate candidate; do not count its savings here.
- 4 min saved x 6 uses per week is plausible for the in-tab button. Generated rows still need a review pass, so about 3 min net is more honest, and the collection-wide bulk action is a rarer use (about 1 per week).
- **Strongest objection:** The claimed 4 min per request is inflated, because PostPilot already removes most of the manual work. I checked the code. (1) The Schema tab (schema_tab.dart:73) already adds a whole-body "Matches JSON Schema" assertion in one click, and that single assertion covers the 8-row structural checking. (2) The Explore/JSON tree (explore_tab.dart:134) already turns a clicked node into a jsonPathEquals or jsonPathExists assertion. (3) Status is one row. So the real flow today is about 3 clicks and 30 to 60 seconds. This candidate would save about 1 to 1.5 minutes per use, not 4. At 2 to 4 uses a week that is about 3 to 6 minutes, not 24. The uses figure is also high. A backend dev ships a new endpoint a few times a week at most. A Flutter dev mostly consumes existing endpoints. An Odoo dev mostly calls stock models. Many of them skip assertions in an interactive client on purpose, because exploratory calls do not need regression tests. That is a rational choice, not a chore failure. The stability probe is the claimed differentiator, and it is weak. Two sends seconds apart show a field did not change per call. They do not show it will not change tomorrow. Counts, list contents, names, pagination totals, status flags and updated_at on idle rows all pass the probe and then break the day after, so the probe gives false confidence about the flakiness the problem statement complains about. A cheaper rule-based filter (skip UUIDs, ISO dates, JWTs, ids and anything named *_at, token or nonce) catches the same volatile fields without re-sending. The re-sends also carry risk. Some GETs are rate-limited, metered or single-use-token flows, and some badly designed APIs mutate on GET. "Response time observed x3" is itself a flaky-test generator, since it depends on network, cold start and CI versus laptop. Postbot and Apidog do ship this feature, but I could not find independent evidence that it is used weekly. The web search budget was exhausted, so demand is unverified and Postman's own docs only prove the feature exists. What is worth keeping is the bulk action, "add smoke tests (status + schema + content-type) to every request that has a saved example". Run once per imported collection it saves about 30 minutes, or about 2 minutes a week amortised. It would be stronger as part of an OpenAPI-driven "generate tests for all operations" feature (status and schema from the spec, no probing) than as a probe-based response analyser. Drop the probe and make exact-value checks opt-in, unchecked by default.
- **Platform notes:** Pure Dart, all platforms. Current assertion set is equals/exists/contains/time/jsonSchema; adding 3 small types (JSON path type is X, array length >= N, regex match) makes generated tests more precise and is shared with the OpenAPI test generator. Probe re-sends on web are subject to CORS.

### 10. Dart test generator: fixtures, http_mock_adapter, DTO round-trip and mocktail tests
- **Score 2.0** | effort M | ~5 min/week saved | persona flutter | lenses: flutter-daily, ai-agentic | skeptic votes 2/3
- **Problem today:** After the API layer exists (generated or hand-written) the Flutter dev still writes tests by hand: copy response JSON into test/fixtures (and strip PII), set up DioAdapter/http_mock_adapter, happy-path and error-path tests (401/404/422/timeout), fromJson/toJson round-trip, mocktail repository and use-case tests. About 10-30 min per endpoint of boilerplate, so it is skipped; fixtures also go stale when the API changes.
- **What PostPilot would do:** A third Dart Studio tab 'Tests' beside 'Models' and 'API layer'. Pick a collection and Generate: fixtures from saved examples or the last response (through SecretMasker, optional anonymizer), data-source tests with http_mock_adapter (2xx example returns the DTO, 4xx/5xx example asserts the typed DioException), model round-trip tests, repository/use-case tests with mocktail, optionally an integration_test smoke replaying status/JSON-path assertions against staging. Optional 'Add edge cases' asks Claude with the schema only (never real values). 'Check fixtures against live' (also CLI) flags shape drift for contract-test use. Then Copy all / Save to folder.
- **Where it lives:** Dart Studio dialog, new 'Tests' tab (same Copy all / Save to folder footer); 'Check fixtures' as a CLI subcommand for CI.
- **Competitors:** Evidence checked. http_mock_adapter 0.6.1, 313k downloads, 185 likes, 'mocking package for Dio intended to be used in tests' (pub.dev page confirmed). The Flutter mocking cookbook (https://docs.flutter.dev/cookbook/testing/unit/mocking.md) confirms the fully manual workflow (add mockito and build_runner, hand-write when/thenAnswer, one test per condition). The aso.dev page does discuss fixed fixture data per state (loading, empty, error), but it is about golden tests, not API fixtures, so it is weak evidence. Keploy (https://keploy.io/docs/running-keploy/api-test-generator/) generates API-level end-to-end tests from OpenAPI, Postman collections, curl or traffic; its docs name no output language and no Dart or Flutter support. That makes it a competitor for API tests only, which PostPilot's runner assertions already cover, not for Dart unit tests with http_mock_adapter or mocktail. Postman's AI helper (Postbot) writes JavaScript tests, not Dart (recalled from general knowledge, not fetched). I found no Dart-specific generator for fixtures plus http_mock_adapter plus mocktail, but the web search quota was exhausted, so I could not run further searches to confirm absence. Treat 'no direct competitor' as likely, not proven. The 20 min x 1.5 per week estimate is plausible but unverified; the author already says it is net of review.
- **Design in this codebase:** VERDICT: feasible without a rewrite, if scoped to the core. Effort is M (1-2 weeks) for fixtures + data-source tests + DTO round-trip + opt-in mocktail repository/use-case tests.
