Add the CLI drift check, AI edge cases and integration_test as the candidate describes and it becomes L, so cut them (see 'Scope cuts').

WHAT I CHECKED IN THE CODE
- lib/features/dart_codegen/domain/services/api_layer_generator.dart is a pure-Dart string generator with no UI, DB or dart:io, so a tests generator in the same folder works on web, desktop and mobile. All names are built inside it: the data source is '${group}RemoteDataSource', the use case is '${Pascal(methodName)}UseCase' with '...Params', and the DTO class names come from _model(), which renames on collision. The operation data lives in private classes (_Operation, _Param). A separate test generator that re-derives these names will drift from the real generated code on the first collision or rename.
- build_api_layer_usecase.dart already reads a collection through CollectionLoader and ResponseExampleRepository and maps it to ApiSpecRequest. It only picks the newest 2xx example. ResponseExampleEntity does carry statusCode and headers, so saved 4xx/5xx examples can drive the error-path tests.
- GeneratedFilesView (list, Copy all, Save to folder) and writeFilesToFolder (conditional import, io vs stub) are reusable as-is. Web gets Copy all only.
- The DI pattern is registerFactory for the view model plus a use case in _registerDevTools. test/di_wiring_test.dart and test/dev_tools/dev_tools_dialogs_test.dart list those registrations by hand, so any new type must be added there too.
- SecretMasker.maskBody exists but is regex-based and replaces values with the string '••••••'.
- workspace.json, which the CLI and Git sync use, contains no saved response examples (the cli/ and workplace/ code never mentions them). Only backup and the DB hold examples.

DESIGN
1. Refactor the generator, about 1 day. Make _Operation and _Param public (ApiOperation, ApiParam). Add ApiLayerGenerator.plan(...), which returns groups, operations, the model file path and class name per operation, and the use-case and params names. generate() becomes plan + emit, and the existing test/dart_codegen/api_layer_generator_test.dart must stay green. The test generator then consumes the same plan, so names cannot drift.
2. Add lib/features/dart_codegen/domain/services/api_tests_generator.dart. It emits: test/helpers/fixture_reader.dart; test/fixtures/<feature>/<op>.json; test/features/<feature>/data/datasources/<snake>_remote_data_source_test.dart; test/features/<feature>/data/models/<model>_test.dart; and, behind an opt-in toggle, repository and use-case tests with mocktail.
   - Data-source tests: Dio(BaseOptions(baseUrl: ...)) plus DioAdapter(dio: dio). onGet/onPost take the concrete path (path params filled with sample strings), queryParameters, and data from the fixture. Happy path asserts the DTO. Error paths use synthetic 401/404/422/500 replies and expect isA<DioException>. That is correct for the current generated code, because it has no failure mapping and Dio throws on non-2xx. Timeout uses DioExceptionType.connectionTimeout.
   - Round-trip tests: compare jsonDecode(jsonEncode(model)) with an idempotent second pass (fromJson(toJson(fromJson(f)))). Do NOT compare to the raw fixture: DateTime.parse('...Z').toIso8601String() adds '.000', so the naive equality test fails for every date field. Using jsonEncode also covers json_serializable, whose nested toJson returns objects (no explicitToJson) and freezed.
   - Fixture reading: use File('test/fixtures/x.json').readAsStringSync() in the VM-run tests. Alternatively emit const raw strings in a fixtures.dart, which is simpler to copy-paste and has no path problems.
   - GraphQL and FormData bodies: match with a wildcard (verify the exact Matchers.any form against http_mock_adapter 0.6.1) or skip with an InfoBanner note. Operations without an example get a note ('N operations skipped: no saved example') rather than a fake fixture.
   - Notes banner, like the existing dio note: add dev_dependencies http_mock_adapter, mocktail and test; run build_runner first for the json_serializable and freezed styles.
3. Add lib/features/dart_codegen/domain/services/fixture_sanitizer.dart. Walk the decoded JSON (not a regex over text) and replace values type-safely. Use SecretMasker.isSensitiveName for keys, plus the known-token patterns, which need exposing publicly (_knownToken is private). Replace with 'redacted' or a same-type stand-in, and keep ISO dates and numbers. The optional email/phone/name anonymizer is best-effort and must be labelled as such. Reason: running SecretMasker.maskBody on a fixture turns "token_expires_at" or "session_expires" into '••••••', and DateTime.parse then throws in the generated test.
4. UI: do not add a third tab. Tests need the same package name, model style and domain-layer options as the API layer, and the pane's view model is a per-pane factory instance, so a separate tab would duplicate those options and let them mismatch. Add a FilterChip 'Include tests' (plus 'Mocktail repository/use-case tests', default off, and an 'Anonymize' toggle) to ApiLayerPane/ApiLayerViewModel. Add a palette entry 'Dart Studio: generate tests' (dart.tests) that opens initialTab: 1 with tests pre-enabled, in palette_items.dart. No new use case or DI type is required: BuildApiLayerUseCase gains a loadSpec() or plan() method, and ApiLayerViewModel calls the tests generator. The result is fewer DI changes and fewer test-wiring edits.
5. Writer: add a skipExisting option to writeFilesToFolder (io and stub), used for test/ and fixtures/ paths. Today it overwrites silently, and generated tests are exactly the files developers edit afterwards. Report 'N written, M skipped' in the snackbar.
6. Quality gate in PostPilot's own CI: one tagged test that generates layer + tests for the sample collection into a temp package and runs dart analyze (and flutter test where available). Without it, string-contains assertions cannot show that the emitted tests compile or pass. A generated test that fails on first run is worse than none. Keep it CI-only: the local C: drive is nearly full.

SCOPE CUTS (changes to the candidate)
- Drop the integration_test smoke replay: `postpilot run --report junit` already replays status and JSON-path assertions against staging in CI. Generated write calls (POST/DELETE) against staging would also be unsafe, because the ProductionGuard does not exist in generated code.
- Defer 'Check fixtures against live' to a separate, later candidate. workspace.json has no examples, so the CLI would need a generated manifest (fixture file to collection/request name) plus new dart:io-only code in cli_main.dart (the command set is hard-coded to run/list/mcp), plus a shape-diff service. That is its own M.
- AI 'Add edge cases' is optional v2. The infrastructure exists (AiClient already sends the kIsWeb browser-access header), but it needs a new AiTasks prompt and a validator that edge-case tests compile. Send schema only, never fixture values.
- Present the mocktail repository/use-case tests as optional. The generated repository and use cases are one-line pass-throughs, so those tests only verify delegation. The real value is the data-source and DTO tests. Use concrete arguments instead of any() to avoid registerFallbackValue boilerplate.

REALISTIC NUMBERS: about 15 min saved per run (net of reading and fixing the output) x about 1 run per week. The candidate's 20 x 1.5 is slightly optimistic. It is still a real saving, because one run covers a whole collection.
- **Risks:** 1) Data leak into the user's repo (main risk). Fixtures embed real values from saved examples, which may be prod data with PII or secrets, and they get committed. Today the generator emits only shapes, never values, so this is a new exposure. Mitigate with the type-safe FixtureSanitizer plus a visible warning banner ('review fixtures before committing'), anonymizer default on, and never use an example whose 'x-postpilot-truncated' header is set (truncated JSON is invalid anyway). SecretMasker covers credentials, not PII, so 'strips PII' must be promised as best-effort.
2) Generated tests that do not compile or fail make the feature worse than useless. Pitfalls: date round-trip (see design), type-breaking masks, query/header/body matching in http_mock_adapter (a route that omits queryParameters will not match a request that sends them), FormData/GraphQL bodies, dynamic responses with no example, and build_runner-dependent styles. The CI compile gate is a hard requirement, not a nice-to-have. Pin and state http_mock_adapter ^0.6.x and dio ^5 in the notes banner, since the mock API has changed between versions.
3) Tautology. Tests built from the same example that produced the DTO pass by construction on day one. Their value only appears when the fixture is refreshed or the DTO is hand-edited. Say so in the UI hint to avoid overselling.
4) Silent overwrite of user-edited test files on Save to folder. Fix with skipExisting.
5) Name drift between the layer and the tests if the generator is not refactored to share one plan. This is the main reason the refactor comes first.
6) Legal, ToS and store policy: no issue. It is pure local string generation, and http_mock_adapter and mocktail are MIT packages that are only referenced by name in generated code, not bundled. The AI edge-case step reuses the existing bring-your-own-key client and sends no user values. No new platform permission or store-review concern.
7) Platform: generation and Copy all work on web. Save to folder is desktop/mobile only (existing canWriteFilesToFolder gate). No new dart:io in lib/ except through the existing conditional writer. A CLI drift check, if added later, is dart:io-only and desktop/CI only.
- **Strongest objection:** The claimed 20 min x 1.5/week = 30 min/week does not survive scrutiny. Four problems.

1. The usage frequency is inflated. The Dart Studio API layer is generated per collection, once per feature or service, not per endpoint per week. A Flutter dev adds a new backend integration every few weeks. A realistic rate is 0.3-0.5 runs per week. Even at 8 endpoints per run and net-of-review savings of about 12-15 min, that is roughly 5 min/week. This is a one-shot generator, not a daily tool.

2. The IDE already does this faster. Copilot, Cursor and Claude Code can write "mocktail tests for this repository" or "http_mock_adapter tests for this data source" in under a minute. They read the dev's actual, usually edited, code and project conventions. PostPilot can only emit tests that match its own generator's frozen layout. Once the dev edits the generated layer (and they always do), the generated tests stop matching it. They cannot be regenerated without losing changes. The "Add edge cases" option via Claude is the same thing an IDE assistant already does, with less context.

3. Most of the generated tests have low value. The generated layer is mechanical: data source calls Dio, DTO maps, repository delegates, use case delegates. Testing that boilerplate (round-trip, "repository calls the data source") is the kind of coverage devs skip on purpose, because it catches almost no bugs. Generating it faster does not change the skip decision. The 10-30 min/endpoint figure counts effort the dev would not have spent anyway.

4. The part that does matter is already covered. Shape drift between fixtures and the live API is already handled by JSON Schema assertions, the collection runner, the headless CLI with junit output, response diff, and "Update from OpenAPI". A separate "check fixtures against live" is mostly a second route to the same contract check. The only truly new pieces are small: masked fixture file export (about 1 min saved per endpoint) and the generator-matched test skeleton.

Verification limits: I could not run web searches (the session budget was exhausted), so I did not independently check demand evidence or the cited URLs. I did confirm in code that api_layer_generator.dart has no test generation today. The refutation rests on reasoning about usage frequency and on IDE-AI substitutes, not on new web evidence.

If anything is kept, shrink it to an S-effort "Save response as masked fixture file" command plus reuse of the existing CLI assertions for drift. Do not build a full Tests tab. Even that saves only a few minutes per week.
- **Platform notes:** Pure string generation, works on web (Copy all; Save to folder is desktop/mobile; live fixture check is CLI/desktop). Output must match class names and layout of the existing api_layer_generator. Generated tests still need a human read (estimate is net of review). Examples saved from prod must pass the secret masker before becoming fixtures.

### 11. Run as roles: identity matrix (admin/user/anonymous/portal) with expected-access assertions and cross-tenant swap
- **Score 2.0** | effort M | ~5 min/week saved | persona qa | lenses: qa-testing, security-auth | skeptic votes 2/3
- **Problem today:** To check 'does a normal user get 403 on admin endpoints, anonymous 401, can tenant B read tenant A's order' the dev pastes the admin token, runs the folder, pastes a user token, runs again and compares status codes by eye (about 32 manual sends for 4 users x 8 requests, ~25 min). Odoo devs do this after every ir.model.access.csv, record-rule or group change. Because it is tedious it is rarely done and broken access ships.
- **What PostPilot would do:** Define 'Identities' once (name plus variable overrides such as apiKey/bearer, incl. anonymous; values in local-only secrets). 'Run as roles' on a folder reuses the runner's data-row mechanism: one pass per identity, shows a request x identity grid (status 200/403/404/empty list). The dev ticks expected access once; later runs flag only changed cells ('portal user can now DELETE res.partner'). Horizontal mode: extractors capture ids owned by identity A, identity B replays them and must get 403/404. The grid can be saved as assertions for runner and CLI. Odoo preset: one API key per user, per model a read-only has_access matrix via 'Test access for this model'.
- **Where it lives:** Collection Runner dialog ('Run as...' multi-select and a Matrix results view), Identities section in the Environments manager, folder context menu 'Run as roles...', Odoo Studio shortcut. CLI: --as admin,user,anon --expect expected-access.json.
- **Partly exists already:** The multi-pass half already exists as the data-driven Collection Runner. In lib/features/request_builder/domain/services/collection_run_options.dart, `dataRows` makes one pass per row. run_data_parser.dart parses pasted CSV or JSON rows. collection_runner_service.dart loops iterations and passes each row as `dataVariables`. Those are the top-priority variables (build_variable_resolver_usecase.dart). collection_runner_dialog.dart has a "Data (optional)" box and groups results under "Iteration N" headers. collection_run_report.dart exports per-iteration JSON and CSV. So a developer can already emulate roles by pasting rows such as `role,token` and writing the auth header as `{{token}}`. Nothing else in the candidate exists. Grep over lib/, bin/ and README.md finds no matches for identity, role matrix, run-as, expected access, BOLA or cross-tenant. What is missing: (1) named Identities with local-only secrets. (2) Anonymous or auth-stripped passes: a data row can only override variables, so an empty token still sends `Bearer ` instead of no auth, and a request with its own explicit auth cannot be swapped. (3) The request x identity grid. Today's view is a flat list per iteration with no side-by-side comparison. (4) Expected-access baselines and "only changed cells" diffing. There is no snapshot, baseline or expected-status feature anywhere. (5) The horizontal cross-tenant replay: identity A's extracted ids replayed by identity B. Extractors and assertions exist, but their variables chain forward through the run in one shared scope, not per identity. (6) CLI support. cli_main.dart has no --data, --iterations or --as option, so the headless CLI cannot do data-driven runs at all. (7) The Odoo "Test access for this model" matrix. odoo_rpc_converter.dart only mentions has_access in a conversion note, and no Odoo Studio screen probes access.

Tighten before building:
- The grid with baselines is the real novelty. The "identities" part is mostly named data rows plus an auth-strip option, so build it on the existing runner and not as a parallel mechanism.
- Honest numbers: roughly 10 min saved is plausible, but 2 uses/week is optimistic. Most developers will do this about once a week, after access or record-rule changes.
- The Odoo `has_access` probe over JSON-2 is unverified, so keep the `search_count` read-probe fallback.
- The Postman thread cited as evidence is about generating OAuth2 tokens per role without manual steps. It does show the multi-role pain, but it is not a feature request for this grid.
- **Competitors:** Verified by fetching the pages. Burp Suite AuthMatrix (https://portswigger.net/bappstore/30d8ee9f40c041b0bfec67441aad158e) builds tables of users, roles and requests and runs every combination with a colour-coded result. Burp Autorize (https://github.com/PortSwigger/autorize) replays a privileged user's traffic as low-privilege and unauthenticated contexts and marks each as Bypassed, Enforced or Requires Configuration. Both are security-pentest plugins, not API clients. The Postman community thread (https://community.postman.com/t/generating-oauth2-0-token-with-granttype-code-without-manual-intervention/4676) shows users want to test APIs per role, and that the only route is to change credentials by hand or iterate a data file. I could not confirm that Postman, Bruno, Insomnia or Hoppscotch lack a built-in role matrix, because the web-search budget ran out. The only comparable feature I know of in them is Postman-style data-file iteration, which PostPilot already has. The OWASP API Top 10 2023 and Odoo 19 security links in the candidate were not re-fetched. They are well-known pages and the claims about them are plausible.
- **Design in this codebase:** VERDICT: feasible without a rewrite. The run engine already does one pass per data row, with the row as the top-priority variable scope for both the send and the scripts that follow (CollectionRunOptions.dataRows, BuildVariableResolverUseCase, PrepareRequestUseCase, RunRequestScriptsUseCase). An identity is therefore a named variable overlay. Corrected effort: M (about 1.5-2 weeks) for a vertical matrix (identities x requests). The candidate as written, with cross-tenant swap, matrix saved as assertions and an Odoo has_access preset, is L.

WHERE IT LIVES
- New folder lib/features/access_matrix/.
- domain/ holds pure Dart with no Flutter import: RunIdentity, AccessClassifier (response to cell class), AccessExpectations (JSON codec), MatrixDiff.
- Pure domain is required because lib/features/cli/workspace_runner.dart is Flutter-free and is a SECOND, separate runner. The app uses the Drift-bound CollectionRunnerService. The CLI uses a snapshot-based WorkspaceRunner. Classifier and diff code must be shared, but the run loop is written twice.
- presentation/ holds AccessMatrixDialog (built on ToolDialog, ~800x640) and its view model.
- Entry points: collection and folder context menu 'Run as roles...', and one PaletteItem in PaletteItems.tools() (id 'access.matrix').
- Do NOT bolt this into CollectionRunnerDialog. It is a plain Dialog, not ToolDialog, and it is collection-scoped only. There is no folder runner in the app (only the CLI has --folder).
- Later: an Odoo Studio shortcut.

IDENTITIES: REUSE ENVIRONMENTS AS OVERLAYS (key design decision)
- An identity is an existing environment whose variables are layered on top of the active environment, passed as the pass's data variables.
- Why: environments already ride the existing machinery. Secrets are already split out by SecretSplitter ('env/<name>/<var>', local-only), and backup and workspace.json already carry them. The CLI already loads environments by name. The Odoo Studio 'save as environment' flow already creates one environment per API key.
- A new Drift table would touch AppDatabase tables, clearWorkplaceData, workplaceDataChanges, BackupCodec (plus a version bump), SecretSplitter and workspace_diff. It must be a new table because Migrator.addColumn crashes the drift web worker in this project (see the comment in app_database.dart). It also needs build_runner.
- Add a built-in synthetic 'Anonymous' identity that is not stored.
- Optional later: a first-class identity table, once the v1 shape is proven.

CORE CHANGES NEEDED (all small)
1. CollectionRunOptions gets an optional list of identities (name, variables, anonymous). The iteration count equals the identity count. CollectionRunResult and RunIteration carry the identity name.
2. Anonymous cannot be a variable override. Odoo and most APIs put the credential in header rows or in auth.
   - Add a pure function: ApiRequestEntity -> entity with auth set to AuthType.none, the Authorization, Cookie and api-key header rows removed, and the apiKeyName query param removed. ApiRequestEntity.copyWith already supports this.
   - Call it in CollectionRunnerService._sendOne before SendRequestUseCase, and in the CLI before the spec build.
   - Real override cases beyond variables: OAuth2 tokens are cached directly on RequestAuth rather than held in a variable, so an identity that must swap them needs an auth override, not just variables.
3. The run drops the response body (_withoutBody) after the scripts run, so an empty list cannot be told from data afterwards.
   - Compute a small 'shape' in _sendOne before the body is dropped: status class, JSON list length or empty, and OdooErrorParser exception name (AccessError, Unauthorized, Forbidden).
   - Record-rule denials in Odoo return 200 with an empty list, not 403, so this is required for the 403 vs empty vs 404 distinction.
4. Cookie isolation, a real correctness bug if skipped.
   - DI registers ONE shared StrictCookieJar, injected into DioApiClient and LenientCookieManager. The same jar backs the Cookies dialog.
   - A session cookie set during the admin pass would be sent in the anonymous or user pass.
   - Add ApiRequestOptions.useCookies (default true), honored through options.extra in LenientCookieManager.loadCookies and saveCookies. Matrix runs set it false.
   - Matrix runs should also set followRedirects false. A session-based app redirects an unauthenticated user to /login, and with the default (true) that becomes a 200 HTML page that looks allowed. Treat a 3xx to login, or an HTML login page, as 'denied'.
   - The CLI is already isolated: no jar, and a fresh HttpClient per request.
5. Extractor side effects. RunRequestScriptsUseCase writes extracted values into the ACTIVE environment in the DB, so identity B's extractors clobber identity A's values.
   - Add a runExtractors flag to RunRequestScriptsParams, set false for non-owner passes.
   - This flag is also the foundation for the later horizontal (cross-tenant) mode, which is a separate phase. It needs a setup vs probe designation per request: the owner identity runs setup requests and extractors once, and the others replay the probes. Do not ship it in v1.
6. Folder scope: add a folderId filter (including descendants) to requestsIn. Folders have parentFolderId.

EXPECTED-ACCESS DATA
- A pure JSON document keyed by 'folder/path/name' (reuse SecretSplitter's '#2' duplicate-name rule) mapped to identity -> allow | deny | empty | any.
- In the app, store it in SettingEntries KV (no migration), keyed by collection name, with Export and Import buttons. A committed file is what the CLI reads (--expect).
- Do not put it into per-request assertions. AssertionEntity has no identity condition, and changing ScriptsJsonCodec ripples into Git request docs, the backup codec and the CLI.
- The CLI adds a --as admin,user,anon option. It loops identities with a fresh RunState whose overrides are the identity's variables, which already work as top-priority overrides there. It also adds --expect and --allow-writes. Exit code 1 when a cell differs from the expectation. Secrets come from workspace.local.json or POSTPILOT_VAR_*.

SAFETY ENFORCEMENT (must not reuse the current guard as-is)
- ProductionGuard is name-based (prod/live/...), method-based (POST/PUT/PATCH/DELETE), silenceable per session, and only enforced by the dialog, not by the service or the CLI.
- Matrix runs deliberately send unauthorized writes, and a vulnerable endpoint actually executes them. So enforce in the service: default 'safe requests only' (GET/HEAD/OPTIONS), writes only by explicit opt-in. Show the count as N writes x M identities. On a prod-named environment, refuse writes outright.
- Odoo problem: every JSON-2 call is a POST, so the guard treats everything as a write. The matrix needs a path-based read-only classifier for Odoo (search_read, read, search_count, fields_get, name_search, has_access are safe; create, write, unlink are not).

ODOO SPECIFICS
- Odoo workspace requests carry 'Authorization: bearer {{odooApiKey}}' as header rows (OdooJson2.headers, CreateOdooWorkspaceUseCase). The environment overlay of odooApiKey therefore works with zero auth-override code.
- Anonymous is meaningless for JSON-2, because the key is mandatory and the answer is always 401. The Odoo value is the per-user key matrix.
- has_access column: odoo_rpc_converter.dart already notes that check_access_rights was removed in favour of has_access(operation). I could NOT verify that it is callable over JSON-2 on a model-level (empty ids) call. The docs fetch was truncated and the web search budget was exhausted. Treat it as unverified.
- Fallback for the read column: a search_count probe (403 = no read ACL).

UI AND PLATFORM
- The matrix is a horizontally and vertically scrollable DataTable (request rows x identity columns, cell = status plus shape chip, changed cells highlighted).
- ToolDialog already goes full-screen on a phone, so mobile works but is desktop-first.
- Web: CORS is the blocker. A 401/403 from a server without CORS headers on error paths surfaces as a network error, not a status, so the cell would show 'blocked', not 403. Odoo has no CORS by default.
- dart:io is not needed. The matrix goes through ApiClient (Dio) and works on desktop and mobile. The CLI part is dart:io-only and desktop/CI-only, as it already is.

TESTS: the harness already exists. test/collection_runner_test.dart has fakes for the repositories and the sender, and test/cli/cli_test.dart covers the CLI.
- **Risks:** LEGAL / TOS / STORE POLICY: low. This is testing the developer's own APIs with their own credentials, the same category as Burp AuthMatrix/Autorize and Postman collections. There is no third-party service, no code download, no store-policy trigger. Add one line in the dialog: only test systems you own or are authorised to test.

SECURITY RISKS (acceptable, but need mitigations)
1. Credentials. Admin-level tokens would sit in the Drift DB in plaintext, the same as every existing isSecret environment variable. The backup file is plaintext ('sensitive: true'). The Console log (features/console) has no masking, and 4 identities multiply the exposure. Mitigations: keep identity variables secret-flagged so SecretSplitter keeps them out of Git pushes, and run results and exports through SecretMasker.
2. Destructive replay. When access control is broken, the 'forbidden' write really executes. The mitigations are in the design section: service-level enforcement, a safe-only default, refusal on prod-named environments, the N x M count, and an Odoo path-based read-only classifier. The existing guard is not enough because it is name-heuristic, silenceable and dialog-only.
3. Lockout and WAF. Repeated invalid or anonymous requests can trip account lockout, fail2ban or rate limiting. Add a default inter-request delay and a cap.

CORRECTNESS RISKS
4. Shared cookie jar contamination (must fix, see core change 4). Without it, 'anonymous' silently sends the admin session and the matrix reports false 'allowed' results.
5. Redirect-to-login looks like 200 unless followRedirects is off. GraphQL returns 200 with an errors[] array. Record-rule denials are 200 with an empty list. The classifier is heuristic. Show the raw status plus shape in every cell and let the user override a cell's class.
6. Extractors mutate the active environment in the DB. The runExtractors:false flag handles non-owner passes. The runner already has this side effect today.
7. Renaming a request orphans its expectation. Flag it as 'new/unknown request' instead of silently passing.

SCOPE RISKS
8. Horizontal cross-tenant mode (A creates, B replays) needs a setup/probe designation and a two-phase run, roughly another M. It is not in v1.
9. Environment-as-identity clutters the environment selector. Use a naming convention such as 'as: admin' and filter those entries out of the environment selector. A proper identity table can come later.
10. Two run loops (app service vs CLI WorkspaceRunner) can drift. Keep all logic that decides outcomes in the shared pure domain classes.

PLATFORM: web is unreliable because of CORS (see design). Mobile works through Dio but the grid UX is desktop-first. The CLI has no cookies and no Digest or OAuth2 support; the WorkspaceRunner skips those auth types, so identities that need them are skipped in CLI runs.

UNVERIFIED: whether has_access is callable over Odoo 19 JSON-2 without ids (docs fetch truncated, search budget exhausted); the Odoo search_count fallback is the safe path. The candidate's evidence URLs were not re-verified.
- **Strongest objection:** The 25 minutes of manual work is overstated, because PostPilot's runner already does most of the job. RunDataParser accepts a CSV or JSON array of rows, and each row overrides variables for one full pass. CollectionRunReport keeps one RunIteration per row and exports JSON or CSV with each request's status. So today the dev can paste 4 rows (name, token) and run the folder once. That is 1 run, not 32 sends with token swapping, and the status codes come back per iteration.

What the feature adds is a request-by-identity pivot grid, saved expected-access baselines, a cross-tenant id swap, and an Odoo has_access preset. The pivot grid is a view over data the runner already produces. The baseline and swap are real extras, but for most developers they are a niche, infrequent chore.

Frequency is also overstated. "After every ir.model.access.csv or record-rule change" is not twice a week for a typical developer. It is closer to once per feature or sprint, and most devs skip it entirely. That is the "rarely done" the candidate itself admits, and a nicer UI does not turn a rarely done chore into a weekly one.

The setup cost eats the gain. Each Odoo identity needs its own API key created through the UI, and those keys can expire. Each user and group fixture has to exist on the instance. Tokens for admin, user, portal and tenant B have to be minted and kept current. Odoo devs also have faster, CI-friendly alternatives: TransactionCase tests with with_user, plus the built-in debug-mode "impersonate" and the access rights and record rule views. For other backends, the cheap workaround is already in PostPilot: one environment per identity, plus the CLI loop `for e in admin user anon; do postpilot run ws.json --env $e --report junit; done`.

Horizontal and BOLA replay (identity A's ids replayed as identity B) is the strongest part. Even there it needs per-request ownership knowledge, and it duplicates tools built for it (Burp AuthMatrix and Autorize, which run inside a proxy over captured traffic). Their audience is pentesters, not the app developers PostPilot targets.

Honest value: about 8 minutes saved per use, 0.5 to 1 use per week for a typical developer, so about 5 minutes a week. For a dedicated QA or security tester it might reach 10 to 15. The 10 min x 2/week claim is optimistic by about 4x.

Recommendation: do not build the M-effort feature as specified. If anything, ship a thin S-effort slice on top of the existing data rows. That slice is a "pivot by data row" matrix view in the runner results (request x row, status per cell), plus an optional per-row expected-status column that fails a cell on mismatch. It would capture most of the real value at a fraction of the cost.
- **Platform notes:** Pure HTTP replay, all platforms. Treat 403 vs empty list vs 404 as different cells (record rules often return empty lists). Tokens per identity are local-only secrets, never pushed. Runs against prod must be read-only unless confirmed (existing lock). Odoo has_access over JSON-2 must be verified on the target instance; fall back to a search_count read probe for the read column.

### 12. Persistent, searchable response history with 'edit and re-send' and HAR export
- **Score 2.0** | effort M | ~5 min/week saved | persona all | lenses: observability-debug | skeptic votes 2/3
- **Problem today:** History stores only method, URL, status and duration; response_history.dart says bodies are deliberately not stored and keeps only the last 8 per request in memory. 'What did this endpoint return yesterday before the deploy?' or 'which call returned order SO0042?' means re-sending (data may have changed) or digging through logs. Evidence for a Jira/GitHub issue is hand-copied text.
- **What PostPilot would do:** Opt-in setting 'Keep response bodies in history' stores the last N (default 20) responses per request in the local Drift DB, size-capped (e.g. 256 KB), secrets redacted, never Git-synced, off for PROD by default. The History dialog gains full-text search over URL and body, filters (env, status, slower than 2x that request's median), 'Compare with current' (existing Compare tab), 'Edit and re-send' with the exact request as sent, and 'Export HAR' for selected rows. Slow calls get a small badge.
- **Where it lives:** Extends the existing History dialog (Ctrl+H) and the Compare tab's source dropdown; one settings toggle. Nothing added to request/response views.
- **Partly exists already:** The candidate's description of today's code is accurate: the persistent, searchable, re-sendable history is mostly missing.

What exists:
- lib/features/history/domain/entities/history_entry_entity.dart holds only id, method, url, statusCode, durationMs and sentAt.
- lib/features/history/presentation/widgets/history_dialog.dart (Ctrl+H) is a plain list of the 200 newest rows (HistoryDao.watchRecent). It has no search, no filters and no slow badge. Tapping a row only opens it in the builder.
- lib/features/history/presentation/view_models/history_view_model.dart: openInBuilder restores method and URL only. Its doc comment says headers and body are not restored. So "edit and re-send exactly as sent" does not exist.
- lib/features/response_tools/domain/services/response_history.dart keeps the last 8 responses per request in memory (the session only). compare_tab.dart and response_tools_view_model.dart (compareSources) use these as "Previous response" / "Earlier response (n)" sources. This is the in-session version of the candidate's "Compare with current".
- Saved response examples (response_examples_table.dart, response_examples_tab.dart) already persist response bodies, but only when the user saves one by hand, and they are also Compare sources. That is a manual, durable baseline, not automatic history.
- lib/features/response_tools/domain/services/bug_report_builder.dart and share_tab.dart already build masked share text for the current response, using SecretMasker. This partly covers the "hand-copied evidence for Jira" pain.
- HAR import exists (har_parser.dart, import_har_usecase.dart, import_format_detector.dart). There is no HAR export anywhere in lib/ or bin/. The only export code is export_collection_dialog.dart and openapi_exporter.dart.
- A repo-wide grep found no median, percentile or "slower than" logic.
- bin/ and README.md have nothing on history beyond a one-line "Request history & replay" folder note.

Details that change the effort:
- lib/core/database/tables/history_table.dart already has a nullable responseBody blob and requestId column, but nothing writes them. HistoryRepositoryImpl.record writes responseHeadersJson only, and its signature has no requestId. SendRequestUseCase.call (send_request_usecase.dart) is the single place to hook in.
- There are no columns for the request as sent (headers and body). "Edit and re-send" therefore needs a new snapshot table, or new columns.
- app_database.dart documents that Migrator.addColumn crashes the drift web worker. The change must be done with createTable and a schemaVersion bump to 5, which makes it somewhat riskier than a simple "M".
- Per-request retention and the median-based slow badge both need requestId to be recorded.
- The current history table is never pruned (only the display is capped at 200). A body-storing history needs real retention.

Evidence check:
- Bruno's History page (docs.usebruno.com/get-started/history) exists. It describes a log of executed requests and does not clearly say it stores bodies.
- The Insomnia docs URL (docs.insomnia.rest/insomnia/responses) now returns a 308 redirect to developer.konghq.com/insomnia/. I could not re-verify that page's content. Insomnia's per-request response history is a long-standing feature, but cite the new Kong docs location.
- The HAR-handoff link is a Flipsnack help page about Chrome, which is weak evidence. A better source is the HAR 1.2 spec or Chrome DevTools "Save all as HAR".
- WebSearch was unavailable (budget exhausted), so I did no broader competitor sweep.

Verdict: pass is true. Persisted bodies, full-text search, filters, request-snapshot re-send and HAR export are new, and HAR export closes the existing HAR-import round trip. Two things to trim before accepting. First, "Compare with current" and last-N compare already exist in memory, so only the persistence of that is new. Second, 4 min x 6 uses per week is slightly generous. The honest core is full-text search plus edit-and-re-send plus HAR export.
- **Competitors:** Insomnia: per-request response history, plus HAR export of requests (docs moved to developer.konghq.com/insomnia/, exact page not re-verified). Bruno: request History view (docs.usebruno.com/get-started/history, verified to exist; unclear whether it stores bodies). Postman: History tab and saved responses/examples; it has no HAR export, so the HAR round trip would be a differentiator.
- **Design in this codebase:** VERDICT: feasible without a rewrite on desktop, web and mobile. It is mostly pure Dart + Drift, with no dart:io and no CORS exposure. Effort is M (about 8-12 working days). It could ship in two slices: (1) snapshots + search + Compare sources, then (2) edit-and-resend + HAR.

WHAT THE CODE ALREADY GIVES US
- The single send chokepoint is SendRequestUseCase.call. It already records history there, so there is one place to hook.
- history_entries already has requestId (never filled), responseHeadersJson (filled) and responseBody blob (unused). The table is close to ready, but the request template, environment name and flags are missing.
- Reusable pieces: ProductionDetector, SafetyPrefs, SecretMasker, HarParser (import side), downloadFile (conditional imports for io/web/stub), RequestJsonCodec, ToolDialog, the palette 'app.history' entry and the AppShortcut.openHistory binding. The Compare tab's CompareSource(label, body) is trivial to extend. schema_tab also reads compareSources, so more stored samples improve schema inference for free.

DESIGN
1. DB (core/database): add table HistorySnapshots(id, historyEntryId FK->history_entries cascade, requestId nullable, environmentName, isProduction, requestJson (the template, via RequestJsonCodec), resolvedMethod/resolvedUrl/resolvedRequestHeadersJson/resolvedBody (masked, for HAR only), responseStatus, responseHeadersJson, bodyText, contentType, bodyBytes, truncated, sentAt) plus indexes on requestId and sentAt. Bump schemaVersion 4 -> 5 with m.createTable + m.createIndex only. Do NOT use addColumn or edit history_entries: app_database.dart documents that addColumn crashes the drift web worker, and createTable is the proven path. Regenerate app_database.g.dart and history_dao.g.dart with build_runner. Add test/database/schema_v5_test.dart in the style of schema_v4_test.dart. Add delete(historySnapshots) to clearWorkplaceData.
2. Do not change HistoryRepository.record. Five test fakes implement it with an explicit signature, and a changed signature breaks them. Adding NEW methods is safe, because every fake declares noSuchMethod. Add recordWithSnapshot(...) (one Drift transaction: insert the entry with requestId, then the snapshot), watchFiltered({query, env, statusClass, slowOnly, limit}), snapshotFor(id), pruneSnapshots() and clearSnapshots() to the interface in features/history/domain. Implement them in HistoryRepositoryImpl and HistoryDao. The HistoryDao list query already selects list columns only. Keep that. Add a LEFT JOIN and an EXISTS flag, and put the body LIKE inside SQLite. Skip FTS: LIKE over a capped table is fine.
3. Recorder service (features/history/domain/services/response_snapshot_recorder.dart). Inputs: SafetyPrefs (extend it with keepResponseBodies=false, keepBodiesInProduction=false, maxBodyKb, perRequestCount), EnvironmentRepository.watchActive().first (as ProductionGuard does) and ProductionDetector. Policy: skip if the toggle is off; skip prod unless allowed; skip non-textual content types; if the body is over the cap, store metadata only with a 'too large' flag (a truncated JSON body would break Compare). Pass it to SendRequestUseCase as an optional named constructor parameter and add an optional keepInHistory=true argument to call(). CollectionRunnerService passes keepInHistory:false (see risks). Write the snapshot fire-and-forget (unawaited, with a catch) so a send is never slowed or broken by it. Prune after insert (per request N, plus a global byte cap).
4. UI. Convert HistoryDialog from the fixed 640x480 plain Dialog to ToolDialog (icon, title, search field in headerActions, filter chips Env/Status/Slow, footer 'Export HAR' and 'Clear stored bodies'). It then becomes full-screen on phones for free. Add a row detail view using the existing ResponseBodyView/ResponseBodyFormatter widgets. HistoryViewModel switches its subscription to watchFiltered after a 250 ms debounce; the default (no query) path keeps using watchRecent. Compute the 'slower than 2x median' badge in the view model from the loaded rows, grouped by requestId. Add a toggle and a 'Clear stored bodies' button to the existing Settings > Safety pane (safety_settings_pane.dart). Add a palette command 'Search history' and extend the 'app.history' palette item. Nothing is added to the request/response views.
5. Compare: ResponseToolsViewModel.load() is already async. Load the last N snapshots there and append them to compareSources. Drop the duplicates of the in-memory ResponseHistory.
6. Edit and re-send: apply the snapshot's requestJson template through requestRepository.createRequest + saveRequest into the ORIGINAL request's collection (look it up through requestId; fall back to the first collection), then ShellViewModel.selectRequest(id). Tabs only hold saved request ids, so there is no ephemeral tab to use. Variables re-resolve against the CURRENT environment, so it is not byte-exact (see risks).
7. HAR export: a pure-Dart HarWriter in features/import_export/domain/services, next to har_parser.dart, written as a HAR 1.2 log (creator, entries, timings{send:0, wait:duration, receive:0}). It uses the masked resolved fields and goes out through downloadFile(). Add a round-trip test against HarParser (test/har_import_test.dart is the model). Mobile writes to the app documents directory, same as every other export today.

RISKS AND CORRECTIONS TO THE CANDIDATE
- 'Edit and re-send with the exact request as sent' cannot be exact without storing resolved secrets. The resolved Authorization header and body are what PrepareRequestUseCase produces. History deliberately stores the TEMPLATE URL (comment in send_request_usecase.dart) so secrets never reach History. Store the template for re-send. Store a masked resolved copy only for display and HAR (HAR needs absolute URLs, not {{baseUrl}}). Label the re-send honestly as 'uses the current environment'.
- Redaction level is the one real design decision. SecretMasker.maskBody is written for generated docs and over-masks. A JSON string under a field called key, token, session or auth becomes the mask, which hurts Odoo bodies such as ir.config_parameter. It also causes false diffs in Compare, because the live side is unmasked. Recommendation: at rest, mask only known token shapes (JWT, sk_live, ghp_, AWS keys) plus Set-Cookie and Authorization header values. Run full SecretMasker at the egress points (HAR export, share/bug report, AI), which is how BugReportBuilder, ai_tasks and the MCP server already work. The device-local DB already holds raw secrets and raw saved examples, so this adds no new class of at-rest risk. Opt-in and prod-off stay as the candidate proposed. A benchmark of maskBody on a 256-512 KB body is still needed. It is about 6 regex passes on the UI thread, especially on web, so run it deferred and after the response is shown.
- Collection runner shares the send path. Without keepInHistory:false, a run of up to 1000 passes would write 1000 snapshots, so this flag is mandatory.
- Existing issues to fix while in there: history_entries is never pruned (the 200 limit applies only to reads); responseHeadersJson already stores Set-Cookie unredacted every send; requestId is never filled; failed sends (timeouts, connection errors) throw before history.record, so they are not recorded; History is not cleared on workplace switch, and with bodies that would leak project A's responses into project B (hence the clearWorkplaceData change above).
- Existing HistoryViewModel.openInBuilder creates a new request in collections.first on every tap. It would pollute the Git-synced workspace.json. The replay path must create in the original collection, with a clear name such as '<name> (replay)'.
- Size caps: default 512 KB per body (Odoo search_read often exceeds 256 KB), a global byte cap about 64 MB native and about 16 MB web (drift docs: IndexedDB stores chunks; the smaller web cap is prudence, not a verified limit), per-request N=20.
- Search: SQLite LIKE is case-insensitive for ASCII only; fine for IDs like SO0042.
- Legal/ToS/store policy: no concern. HAR is an open format, the data stays on device, nothing is collected, and there is no new network surface. Only the privacy hygiene above applies.
- Evidence note: the Insomnia docs URL now 308-redirects to developer.konghq.com. The Bruno History page confirms request tracking but does not say response bodies are stored, so treat it as weak evidence. Insomnia's per-request response history is the real precedent. The minutes-saved figures were not assessed (feasibility lens only).
- **Strongest objection:** The candidate bundles four features, and only one has real value.

1. In-session compare already exists. ResponseHistory keeps the last 8 responses per request, and the Compare tab reads it. For "what changed since my last call" while debugging, the developer already has the answer. The new part is persistence across app restarts. That is useful but narrow, roughly 1-2 times per week for a QA or backend developer, not 6.

2. Full-text search over stored bodies ("which call returned SO0042") is rare. Developers usually look this up in Odoo itself, in the DB, or in server logs, and those are authoritative. History search over a capped 256 KB, 20-per-request window will often not contain the record. I estimate under 0.5 uses per week.

3. HAR export is niche. HAR is a browser-trace format. A teammate reading a Jira ticket wants the request, the response and a curl command, and PostPilot already has share/bug-report text and copy-as-cURL. Nobody on the receiving end opens a HAR of API calls. HAR import exists, so the round trip is neat but not time-saving.

4. "Edit and re-send exactly as sent" duplicates keeping the tab open. It would only add value if request headers and body were stored too. That means storing secrets and PII, so it needs opt-in, redaction, quota, and a clear-bodies command. Reliable redaction of Odoo and API bodies is hard, so the real effort is closer to L than M. At the moment HistoryEntryEntity holds only method, URL, status, duration and time, and openInBuilder rebuilds a request from method and URL alone.

5. It is opt-in and off for PROD. Most users will never turn it on, so the average saving is below the per-user figure.

Realistic numbers: about 2 uses per week at about 3 minutes each, which is 5-6 minutes per week. A heavy QA or regression debugger might reach 10-15, but a typical developer will not. Competitor parity (Insomnia, Bruno) shows the feature exists, not that it saves meaningful time. I could not run extra web searches for demand evidence because the session's search budget was used up. This verdict rests on reading history_entry_entity.dart, response_history.dart, history_dialog.dart and the HAR import code.

If anything is kept, shrink it to an S-sized item: persist the last N responses per request across restarts (non-PROD, size-capped, opt-in), feeding the existing Compare tab. Drop search, HAR export and edit-and-re-send. Alternatively, store the request as sent (headers and body, secrets redacted) so a History entry restores the exact request instead of just method and URL. Either version is a quality-of-life improvement, not a time-automation feature.
- **Platform notes:** All platforms; web storage is IndexedDB so apply a per-workspace quota. Bodies can hold PII/tokens: keep opt-in, add 'Clear stored bodies'. HAR export is a pure JSON write; HAR import already exists, so this closes the round trip.

### 13. Trace-ID follower and redirect chain in the Timing tab
- **Score 2.0** | effort S | ~2 min/week saved | persona backend | lenses: observability-debug | skeptic votes 2/3
- **Problem today:** After a 500 the dev opens Headers, finds X-Request-Id or traceparent, copies it, opens Grafana/Kibana/Jaeger/Datadog, pastes it and sets the time range (~2 min); QA pastes a screenshot and backend asks 'which request id?'. For redirects (http to https, trailing-slash 301 turning POST into GET, login loops, Authorization dropped cross-origin) the dev reruns curl -L -v to see hops.
- **What PostPilot would do:** PostPilot detects trace headers (traceparent, X-Request-Id, X-Correlation-Id, X-B3-TraceId, x-amzn-trace-id, cf-ray) and shows a 'Trace' row in the Timing tab with Copy and 'Open in...' built from a per-environment link template (e.g. https://grafana.corp/explore?...{{traceId}}). Optionally it injects a W3C traceparent / X-Request-Id on each send, and includes the id in Share/bug-report text. The same tab lists each redirect hop (status, Location, ms, Set-Cookie, method change 301/302 POST to GET, warning when Authorization was stripped).
- **Where it lives:** Section in the existing Timing tab of Response tools, one 'Trace link template' field in Environment settings, palette command 'Copy trace id of last response'.
- **Competitors:** Evidence I could verify: the k6 tracing page (https://grafana.com/docs/k6/latest/javascript-api/k6-experimental/tracing/) does say k6 injects a W3C traceparent header so traces can be found in Tempo. This is a good precedent for injection, but it is a load-test tool, not an API client. The W3C Trace Context link is the standard spec, which I did not fetch.

Evidence I could not verify: the Insomnia cited URL (docs.insomnia.rest/insomnia/responses) now returns a 308 to developer.konghq.com/insomnia/, and my guess at the new path (/insomnia/responses/) returned 404. Replace it with the current Insomnia docs URL before the claim is used. Separately, from memory and unchecked this session, Postman Console and Insomnia Timeline show redirect hops and headers, so the redirect view is parity with the incumbents rather than a differentiator. My WebSearch budget was exhausted, so I could not confirm that Postman or Bruno has a trace-id link-out. I know of no mainstream API client with a per-environment "open trace in Grafana/Kibana" link, but I did not verify that.

Candidate weaknesses to weigh:
- It bundles two unrelated features, trace-id follower and redirect chain, so splitting them may be cleaner.
- Auto-injecting traceparent on every send touches request building and the production lock, so it should be opt-in.
- The 2 min x 6 per week estimate is plausible but only for teams that already have a tracing backend.
- **Design in this codebase:** FEASIBLE with no rewrite. Verdict on effort: S (about 5-8 working days) only if the per-environment 'Trace link template' is a reserved environment variable instead of a new column. As written (a dedicated Environment field) it would be M (see Risks).

What the code already gives us (verified in lib/):
- lib/core/network/dio_api_client.dart: _sendFollowingRedirects already walks every hop and already computes the method change (_redirectsAsGet), the credential drop (_keepsCredentials strips Authorization/Cookie cross-host) and the Location target. None of it is returned: ApiHttpResponse (lib/core/network/api_http_response.dart) and ApiResponseEntity have no hop list or final URL. The candidate's claim is correct.
- The Response tools dialog (response_tools_dialog.dart) uses ToolDialog and ToolTabs. TimingTab already receives ResponseToolsViewModel (has data.response, request). It already calls locator<BuildVariableResolverUseCase>()(collectionId), so it can read the active environment's variables without new plumbing.
- ResponseHistory (in memory, keyed by request id) plus ShellViewModel.selectedRequestId let a palette item find 'the last response of the active tab'.
- Environments and their variables already travel through Git workspace JSON and Backup (secret_splitter.dart handles env/<name>/<var> with the secret flag), so a reserved non-secret variable gets team sync and backup for free.
- url_launcher is already a dependency (launchUrl with LaunchMode.externalApplication used in simple_markdown.dart and git_operation_view_model.dart). SecretMasker.maskUrl/maskValue already exists for Share text.
- Test harness for hops exists: test/settings/dio_api_client_test.dart has _FakeAdapter and a _redirect() helper.

Concrete design:
1. RedirectHop model: new lib/core/network/redirect_hop.dart (plain Dart so the CLI build can share it). Fields: status, fromUrl, toUrl (resolved Location), methodBefore/After, ms for that hop (Stopwatch lap taken around each _dio.request, which returns at headers because responseType is stream), setCookieNames (names only), droppedAuthorization (true only when the outgoing headers really held Authorization/Cookie and _keepsCredentials was false). Add optional `redirects = const []` to ApiHttpResponse and ApiResponseEntity (non-breaking: every existing const constructor and the ~15 test fakes keep compiling). Fill it in _sendFollowingRedirects and copy it in SendRequestUseCase._toEntity. collection_runner_service._withoutBody may drop it (memory).
2. Trace detection: pure Dart lib/features/response_tools/domain/services/trace_headers.dart. TraceHeaders.find(Map headers) returns ordered refs {kind, headerName, id}: traceparent (extract the 32-hex trace-id, ignore span id and flags), b3 and x-b3-traceid, x-amzn-trace-id (parse Root=1-...), x-datadog-trace-id, x-request-id, x-correlation-id, request-id, x-trace-id, cf-ray. Case-insensitive. Unit-testable like json_tree/quick_decoder.
3. UI: inside the existing TimingTab only (no 10th tab; the strip already has 9). Two new sections under the connection bars: 'Trace' (one row per detected id: Copy button, 'Open in...' button) and 'Redirect chain' (one row per hop: 301 POST->GET, Location, ms, cookie names, an amber InfoBanner when Authorization was dropped). Hide each section when empty. Rename the tab label to 'Timing' still, with a small dot only if hops exist. Use the existing InfoBanner, BusyLabel and theme tokens; no changes to the request or response views.
4. Link template: reserved variable `traceLink` (a full https URL with {traceId} and optionally {from}/{to}) read with resolver.lookup('traceLink') (env > collection > global). If missing, the Trace section shows one hint with an 'Add traceLink to the active environment' button that calls EnvironmentRepository.upsertVariable. No Drift change, no workspace/backup change.
5. Palette: new PaletteItems.response(...) list added in shell_page._openPalette (it already has context.read<ShellViewModel>()): 'Copy trace id of last response', visible only when ResponseHistory.of(selectedRequestId).firstOrNull has a detectable id.
6. Opt-in send injection (the half that really works): SendRequestUseCase is 'the single place a request leaves the app', so add the header there after PrepareRequestUseCase, not in RequestSpecBuilder (keeps test/code_snippet_matches_send_test.dart and snippets deterministic). New AppSettings bool `sendTraceparent` (default false; stored in setting_entries JSON via SettingsJson.boolOr, no migration), a toggle beside 'Send no-cache header' in general_settings_pane.dart (disabled on web like the redirect options), field in EffectiveRequestOptions, generator in trace_headers.dart (Random.secure, 00-<32hex>-<16hex>-01, never all zeros, injectable Random for tests). Do not overwrite a user-set traceparent/X-Request-Id; reuse the same headers map for the Digest retry. Return the id as ApiResponseEntity.sentTraceId so the Timing tab and Share text show it even when the server does not echo it.
7. Share: BugReportBuilder gets an optional traceId line; ShareTab passes it (it currently lists request.headers of the saved request, so an injected header would otherwise be missing; response headers are already included when the Headers chip is on).
8. Optional later: CLI (workspace_runner builds its own CliRequest) can print the id in junit using the same pure service.

Tests to add: TraceHeaders (each header kind, malformed traceparent, case), DioApiClient hop list (reuse _FakeAdapter: 301 POST->GET, cross-host Authorization drop, same-host keep, Set-Cookie names only, followRedirects off gives no hops), SendRequestUseCase injection (off by default, no overwrite, same id on Digest retry), settings model JSON round trip.

Platform notes: trace row, copy and open work on desktop, mobile and web. Hops are desktop and mobile only: on web the browser follows redirects and DioApiClient never sees a 3xx, so hops are empty (optional: Dio's realUri on web can at least reveal 'final URL differs'). On web a response header is readable only if the server lists it in Access-Control-Expose-Headers, so say that in the empty state. Injection must stay off on web: a custom traceparent/X-Request-Id request header forces a CORS preflight and can break calls to servers that do not allow it. Nothing needs dart:io outside the existing adapter files, so no new conditional imports.

Honest value check: W3C Trace Context (fetched) defines only the request headers traceparent/tracestate; there is no standard response header, so many servers return nothing to detect (Rails/Envoy/nginx x-request-id and Cloudflare cf-ray are the common ones). The deterministic win is client-side injection plus the link, which is exactly what k6 does (page fetched, confirms it injects W3C traceparent for Tempo). Odoo and Flutter-only users get little from this: persona stays backend (and qa). 2 min x 4-6/week for trace plus about 1-2 min x 1-2/week for hops is realistic; do not claim more.
- **Risks:** 1. Schema trap: a dedicated 'Trace link template' field on Environments needs a new Drift column (schema v5). lib/core/database/app_database.dart documents that Migrator.addColumn reproducibly crashes the drift web worker here, and the field would also need entity, DAO, backup_codec, workspace JSON, secret_splitter and restore changes. That turns S into M and risks web. Mitigation: reserved environment variable `traceLink` (zero schema change, auto-synced and backed up).
2. Link template is attacker-controllable once it arrives through Git sync (a teammate or a hostile repo can set it). Only the trace id is substituted, so data exposure is small, but: allow http/https only (SafeLink.isAllowed also permits mailto: and #, so write a stricter check), show the target host on the button tooltip before opening, percent-encode the id, and do NOT expand {{otherVars}} inside the template (read it raw with resolver.lookup, substitute only built-in placeholders) so a template like https://evil/?k={{apiKey}} cannot exfiltrate local secrets, whether the variable is secret or not. Opening a link is not a request, so the PROD guard does not apply.
3. Redirect data privacy: Location URLs can carry OAuth codes, presigned-URL signatures or one-time tokens, and Set-Cookie carries session values. Keep hops in memory only (same lifetime as ResponseHistory, never persisted to History/Drift/examples), show cookie NAMES only, and run URLs through SecretMasker.maskUrl for any copy, Share or bug-report output. The Console (ApiCallObserver) should not be given raw hop URLs without the same masking.
4. Injection side effects (opt-in, default off): adds a harmless random header to every host including third parties; a few strict gateways or WAFs reject unknown headers; flags 01 (sampled) forces sampling on head-based samplers, which raises trace volume on PROD, so label the setting accordingly. SigV4 is fine because the header is added after signing and is not in SignedHeaders. Never reuse ids across sends.
5. Detection ambiguity: cf-ray is a Cloudflare ray id, not an id for the team's own Grafana/Tempo; x-amzn-trace-id needs Root= parsing; x-datadog-trace-id is decimal; x-request-id is not a W3C trace-id. Show every candidate as its own row with the header name, rank traceparent > b3 > amzn > x-request-id > x-correlation-id > cf-ray, and do not auto-pick for the link button.
6. Small gaps: a Digest 401 retry replaces the response, so hops of the first attempt are lost (acceptable, document). The Response tools button is disabled for non-textual bodies, so a redirect chain ending in a binary download is not reachable from the toolbar (optional: allow the dialog for a response that has hops). Existing tests that compare AppSettings/EffectiveRequestOptions JSON or equality (test/settings/settings_models_test.dart, settings_dialog_test.dart) need the new field added. Per project convention, do not run dart format over the repo; keep edits local.
7. Evidence hygiene: the candidate's Insomnia URL (docs.insomnia.rest/insomnia/responses) now 308-redirects to developer.konghq.com, so cite the new location or drop it. W3C and k6 URLs checked and valid. No legal, ToS or store-policy risk found: no new permissions, no bundled vendor logos or SDKs, an open spec, and url_launcher is already shipped.
- **Strongest objection:** The claimed 12 min/week (2 min x 6 uses) does not hold up.

Trace link (the main half)
- Copying X-Request-Id from the Headers tab takes about 5 seconds, not 2 minutes. The template link only saves the paste into Grafana/Kibana/Jaeger and picking the time range, which is roughly 20-40 seconds per lookup.
- It only helps backend devs whose company runs a trace backend and whose API echoes a trace ID in the response. Odoo has no trace IDs by default. Odoo devs grep server logs or use Odoo.sh logs, so for them it saves 0. Flutter devs usually do not own the observability stack. Their only gain is a request id pasted into the bug-report text, which is a few seconds.
- A realistic backend dev with tracing hits "500 -> find the trace" 2-4 times a week at about 0.5 min each, so about 1-2 min/week for that subset. Averaged over all personas it is below 1 min.
- The optional traceparent / X-Request-Id injection is niche and can clash with an existing OTel or gateway setup. It adds no measurable time saved.

Redirect chain (the other half)
- Redirect surprises (301 turning POST into GET, Authorization dropped on a cross-origin hop) are real but rare, maybe once every few weeks.
- Today's alternatives are already cheap. `curl -L -v` is one line (about 30 seconds). PostPilot already has a per-request and global "follow redirects" setting, so turning it off shows the 3xx with its Location header. Insomnia and Postman already show redirect hops in their timeline and console, so this is parity rather than an edge.
- Even counting only the occasions it is needed, that is about 0.5-1 min/week.

Net: about 2 min/week for one typical developer. It is not a weekly habit and it is not a painful or error-prone chore. It is a nice diagnostic, not a time-saver.

Salvage: the redirect hop list is almost free (S effort, since `_sendFollowingRedirects` in lib/core/network/dio_api_client.dart already walks each hop). It could ship as a small polish item, a hop list in the Timing tab shown only when a redirect happened. It should not be pitched as an automation feature, and the environment trace-link template and traceparent injection are not worth building for this goal.
- **Platform notes:** Trace chips work everywhere. Redirect hops available on desktop/mobile only (browser owns redirects on web; Timing tab already shows a similar notice). On web, custom headers are readable only if exposed via Access-Control-Expose-Headers.

### 14. MCP toolkit: let Claude Code/Cursor create requests, assertions and examples, with a server-side production lock
- **Score 1.6** | effort L | ~8 min/week saved | persona all | lenses: competitors, ai-agentic | skeptic votes 2/3
- **Problem today:** A dev finishes an endpoint with an AI agent, then switches to the API client to add the request, body, assertions and example by hand and re-run it (about 4-6 min per endpoint), or pastes responses into the chat after hand-deleting tokens. PostPilot's MCP server exposes only list_requests, list_environments, run_request, run_collection: no bodies, saved examples or assertions in the output, nothing that creates or edits. Nothing under lib/features/cli references the production guard, so an agent could send a POST to Production unprompted.
- **What PostPilot would do:** Add MCP tools: get_request (full, masked), create_or_update_request, set_assertions/set_extractors, save_example (from the last run), update_from_openapi, last_failure (failing outcome plus upstream chain), get_response_shape / generate_dart (runs the existing Dart Studio generators), odoo_fields(model), set_variable (secrets go to local file, never echoed). Writes are off unless --allow-write, default to a dry-run diff of workspace.json shown in an 'AI changes' review list, and any non-GET against a production-flagged environment is refused unless the user confirms. Ship a ready agent skill file and a 'Copy config for Claude Code/Cursor' button.
- **Where it lives:** No new screen: one Settings pane 'Connect an AI agent' (copy-paste MCP command, skill file, 'AI changes' review list). The open desktop app reloads the workspace on file change (watcher plus existing conflict guard).
- **Partly exists already:** An MCP server already exists, so only the extension is new. lib/features/cli/mcp_server.dart (McpServer, started by `postpilot mcp` in lib/features/cli/cli_main.dart; README line ~96-105 documents `claude mcp add postpilot ...`) exposes exactly four tools: list_requests, list_environments, run_request, run_collection. run_request returns status, timing, masked headers/body, assertion results and savedVariables through SecretMasker. It is read/run only: no request body, saved examples or assertion definitions in any output, and nothing that creates or edits a request, assertion, extractor, example or variable. The production-guard claim is accurate: grep for safety/production under lib/features/cli finds only a description string in mcp_server.dart. ProductionGuard (lib/features/safety/domain/services/production_guard.dart) depends on EnvironmentRepository and SafetyPrefs (app DB and prefs), so it is not reusable as is. ProductionDetector (production_detector.dart) is pure Dart and imports only HttpMethod, so porting it to the CLI is cheap. WorkspaceRunner.runRequest sends whatever the agent asks, so an agent can POST to a Production-named environment unprompted. Also new: no agent skill file or 'Copy config' button (only the README one-liner), no --allow-write, no 'AI changes' review list. Caveat on feasibility: the proposal's 'open app reloads on file change' is not existing code. WorkplaceViewModel (lib/features/workplace/presentation/view_models/workplace_view_model.dart) treats the Drift database as the live copy and workspace.json as a debounced autosave mirror, with no file watcher, so a CLI edit to workspace.json while the app is open would be overwritten by the next autosave. The existing conflict guard is the Git remote-SHA one, not a local-file one. So the write half is closer to a two-way file sync (M, possibly L) and not the 'M' as stated. Cheaper first slice: richer read tools (get_request, examples, assertions, last_failure), the production-write refusal in run_request/run_collection, the skill file and the config-copy button.
- **Competitors:** Evidence checked and accurate. Postman MCP server (glama.ai listing): Minimal and Full (100+ tools) sets, create/update collections, requests and response examples, run collections, supports Claude Code and Cursor. Postman Jan 2026 blog: MCP server runs collections and generates code. Yaak v2026.6.0 changelog lists 'A Yaak skill for coding agents'. Apidog MCP server: read-only, feeds API specs to Cursor/VS Code. pub.dev dart_mcp_server: official Dart team MCP server (analysis, tests, hot reload, widget inspection), complementary and not an API client. So agent-writable API clients are already a competitor norm (Postman), and PostPilot is behind on write tools. The server-side production lock for agent writes is a differentiator, since none of the checked sources mention an equivalent.
- **Design in this codebase:** VERDICT: feasible without a rewrite, but the candidate's write path and shipping story are wrong in three ways. Build it in two slices. (Root: C:/Users/Md Manzurul Islam/OneDrive/Desktop/claude/postpilot.)

WHAT I VERIFIED IN THE CODE
- The CLI import closure (lib/features/cli/*, 50 files) is pure Dart (only package:crypto). Generators (dart_codegen/domain/services/*), odoo_json2/odoo_model_info, openapi_parser + openapi_refresh_planner, scripts_json_codec, SecretMasker, SecretSplitter, WorkspaceDiff, ProductionDetector are all Flutter-free and reusable as is. The flutter-coupled ones are BackupService, RefreshOpenApiUseCase and the import usecases (they import package:flutter compute and repositories); do not touch those from the CLI.
- No shipped binary exists today. `dart compile exe bin/postpilot.dart` FAILS ("'dart compile' does not support build hooks": sqlite3 and objective_c are in the package graph). `dart build cli` WORKS: I ran it (Dart 3.12, 4.8 s) and got bundle/bin/postpilot.exe (7.5 MB, `--version` works) plus an unneeded lib/sqlite3.dll, 8.9 MB total. The release workflow (.github/workflows/build-and-release.yml) only builds the Flutter bundles, and bin/ plus lib/features/cli/ are still untracked in git. So the 'Copy config for Claude Code/Cursor' button has nothing to point at until CI builds and bundles the CLI per OS (extra step in each build job, add to installer.iss, Windows zip, macOS .app Resources, Linux bundle). The Flutter exe cannot double as the CLI (Windows runner is a GUI-subsystem exe, no stdio), so it must be a separate binary.
- The candidate's 'open app reloads on file change (watcher plus existing conflict guard)' does not exist. The database (Drift) is the live copy and workspace.json is only a debounced mirror (WorkplaceViewModel: autosave 800 ms, also on app pause). There is no file watcher anywhere in lib/. If the MCP process edits workspace.json while the app is open, the next autosave silently overwrites the agent's changes. The only 'reload from file' path is _replaceDatabase = clearWorkplaceData + restore, which gives every row a new id and closes the open request (shellViewModel.closeRequest()). Using that for each agent edit would wipe the user's tabs. The existing conflict guard (SyncEngine/ThreeWayMerger) is Git-remote-only and per-uid, not for local file edits.
- The production lock is name-based (ProductionDetector.isProduction, pure Dart). The extra words live in SafetyPrefs (shared_preferences, per device), not in workspace.json, so the CLI cannot see them. ProductionGuard itself needs EnvironmentRepository and SafetyPrefs, so it cannot be ported; only the detector can.
- Existing run_request already lets the agent pass `variables` that win over every layer (RunState overrides). An agent can set baseUrl to any host and the request goes out with the resolved Authorization header: a secret-exfiltration path that exists TODAY. Adding create/update tools makes it worse.

DESIGN
Slice A (read-only + safety + shipping, about 1.5 weeks, desktop):
1. lib/features/cli/mcp/ (new folder, split mcp_server.dart into one file per tool group, pure Dart): get_request (SecretMasker.maskValue/maskBody on headers, URL, body, auth shows type only), last_failure (keep the last N RequestOutcomes in the McpServer session; upstream chain = static scan of {{vars}} against extractor keys of earlier requests), get_response_shape + generate_dart (infer schema/models from the last outcome's body, call DartModelGenerator/ApiLayerGenerator, return text, no file writes), odoo_fields(model) (OdooJson2 call fields_get through the existing CliSend + OdooModelInfo.parse), update_from_openapi in DRY-RUN only (OpenApiParser + OpenApiRefreshPlanner.plan over ExistingRequest built from BackupRequest; returns the plan). Add tool annotations (readOnlyHint etc.) to tools/list.
2. Safety choke point inside WorkspaceRunner.runRequest just before `send` (so run_request, run_collection and the headless CLI share it): new ProductionPolicy {refuse | allow} in RunOptions. MCP defaults to refuse, so any data-changing request in a production-named environment returns an error telling the agent to ask the human to run it in the app. `postpilot run` for CI stays unchanged unless --forbid-prod-writes is passed. Prod words come from repeatable `--prod-word` flags in the registered MCP command (the Settings pane copies the user's SafetyPrefs words into the command). Extend ProductionDetector with changesDataFor(method, url, body): Odoo JSON-2 read methods (search_read, read, search_count, fields_get, name_search) and GraphQL `query` operations count as reads. Otherwise every Odoo read on prod is refused because JSON-2 is always POST; the app's own prompt would benefit too.
3. Exfil fix: secrets (env/global isSecret, SecretMasker.isSensitiveName keys, auth fields) are only substituted when the final URL origin is in an allowlist computed once at server start from the on-disk, human-written workspace (every environment's resolved base URLs plus saved request origins). Otherwise error. This also blocks the `variables` override attack.
4. Ship: CI step `dart build cli -o ...` per OS, bundle next to the app, installer.iss entry. Skill file (Claude Code: .claude/skills/postpilot/SKILL.md; Cursor: rule file, I did not verify Cursor skill support) is a plain asset.
5. UI: new section in lib/features/settings/presentation/widgets/settings_dialog.dart (_Section enum, same SettingRow style as SafetySettingsPane): 'Connect an AI agent', hidden when !repository.usesRealFolders (web) and on mobile. It shows the command built from Platform.resolvedExecutable's folder + the active workplace's folderPath, plus Copy buttons for `claude mcp add ...` and the .cursor/mcp.json snippet. No new screen, no change to request/response views.

Slice B (writes, about 2 weeks):
- MCP never edits workspace.json. Write tools (create_or_update_request, set_assertions, set_extractors, save_example, set_variable non-secret only) apply to an in-memory copy of the BackupSnapshot (the agent's run_request sees them immediately) and ALSO emit one op file per change into <workplaceFolder>/.postpilot/agent-inbox/<id>.json via tmp+rename (safe with two agents running). An op addresses its target by collection + folder path + name (the file has no db ids), carries a fingerprint of the target as the agent last read it, and the new value.
- App side: AgentInboxService (feature folder lib/features/agent_inbox/{data,domain,presentation}, conditional import io/stub exactly like workplace_storage_factory_io/_web.dart). It polls every ~2 s (OneDrive and sandbox folders make File.watch unreliable) and applies APPROVED ops through the existing repositories (RequestRepository.createRequest/saveRequest, RequestScriptsRepository, ResponseExampleRepository, EnvironmentRepository), the same calls RefreshOpenApiUseCase already makes. The user's autosave then mirrors them to workspace.json as usual, tabs and ids are untouched. A stale fingerprint (user edited that request meanwhile) marks the op 'conflict' instead of applying. The 'AI changes' review list is a ToolDialog (lib/core/widgets/tool_dialog.dart) opened from a command-palette entry 'Review AI changes' plus a count badge; the per-op diff is rendered with WorkspaceDiff.compare on before/after JSON. Results are written back (applied/rejected/conflict) so a `list_changes` tool can tell the agent what happened.
- Agent-created or agent-edited requests stay 'unapproved' in the MCP process: run_request on them runs with secret substitution disabled (or is refused) until the human approves them in the review list. This closes the 'create request to evil.example with {{apiKey}}' hole. Reject literal credentials in written headers/auth/body (SecretMasker.isSensitiveName with a non-{{var}} value): the agent must use {{variable}} references. save_example runs the body through SecretMasker.maskBody because examples are committed to Git and feed the mock server.
- Drop set_variable for secrets: the user would have to paste the secret into the chat, so the value enters the model provider's context. Humans enter secrets in the app (they go to workspace.local.json via SecretSplitter).
- Tests: extend test/cli/cli_test.dart style (fake CliSend) for each tool, the policy, the origin allowlist and op apply/conflict logic.
- **Risks:** 1. Secret exfiltration (HIGH, already present): `variables` overrides in run_request plus any agent-writable URL/header can send {{secrets}} to an attacker host, and a prompt-injected API response can steer the agent to do it. Needs the origin allowlist and 'unapproved until reviewed' rules above; without them do not ship write tools.
2. The 'server-side production lock' is a guard against mistakes and injected tool calls, not a sandbox. Claude Code/Cursor have shell and file access to the same folder, so they could edit any config file or curl prod directly. Keep the prod words and policy in the registered MCP command flags (set by the human), never in a file inside the workspace folder, and state this plainly in the Settings pane copy. 'User confirms' has no UI over stdio: Claude Code does support MCP elicitation (checked the docs), but PostPilot's server speaks 2024-11-05 and its serve loop is strictly request-reply, so elicitation needs protocol negotiation plus routing of client responses to pending requests. Default must be hard refuse; elicitation is a later add-on, and Cursor support is unverified.
