3. Data loss (HIGH if done as in the candidate): writing workspace.json under a running app is overwritten by autosave, and the only reload path destroys tab state. Inbox plus apply-through-repositories is required; this is what moves effort from M to L.
4. Prod-detection by environment name only, and the extra words are per device: a customer env named 'eu-west' is not protected in the CLI unless --prod-word is passed. POST-only APIs (Odoo JSON-2, GraphQL) need the read-method classification or Odoo users get every prod read refused.
5. Distribution: no shipped CLI today (dart compile exe fails; dart build cli works). The bundle drags an unneeded sqlite3.dll through a build hook that downloads a prebuilt binary in CI. Extra signing/notarization surface on macOS (the .app is sandboxed, Release.entitlements has app-sandbox; the inbox only works for folders the app can already read/write, such as the default workplace dir inside the container, so the pane must show the real path) and an installer.iss change on Windows. Desktop only: web has no stdio/dart:io and mobile has no way to run an MCP process, so hide the pane there.
6. Cross-process races: two agents plus the app. Handled with per-op files and fingerprints, not with a single shared file.
7. Context/stdout hygiene: MCP stdout must carry only JSON-RPC; reused generators must never print. Large outputs (generate_dart, response bodies) need clipping like the existing 20 000 char clip.
8. Legal/ToS/store: low. MCP is an open protocol; naming Claude Code/Cursor on a button is nominative use (no logos). No store-policy issue because the feature is hidden on web and mobile and the app does not call any AI vendor for it (the user's own agent does). Responses returned to the agent leave the machine to the model provider, so the existing masking must cover every new tool, and the pane should say so.
9. Value check: the read tools overlap what an agent already does with curl; the unique value is writing assertions/examples/requests into the shared workspace and the saved-example masking, so slice B is what earns the 5 min x 6/week, while slice A alone is closer to 2 min x 4/week.
- **Strongest objection:** The claimed 5 min x 6/week = 30 min does not hold up. Four points.

1. The 4-6 min manual baseline is inflated. PostPilot already has smart-paste cURL, OpenAPI import and "Update from OpenAPI". The agent already prints a working cURL, so creating the request takes about 1 min. For backends that publish OpenAPI (FastAPI, NestJS, Spring), request creation is already automated. The only real remaining manual step is 2-3 assertions, about 2 min. Reviewing an "AI changes" list costs about 1 min, so the net saving is roughly 2-3 min per use.

2. The debug-loop half is already covered. The existing run_request returns status, failures, tests and the masked response body, up to 20k characters, through SecretMasker. The "paste responses into chat after hand-deleting tokens" problem is therefore mostly solved today. last_failure with an upstream chain, get_request, odoo_fields and generate_dart would each save well under a minute, a few times a week.

3. Usage frequency is overstated. A dev needs to ship about 6 new or changed endpoints a week through an agent to reach 6 uses.
   - Backend devs ship roughly 2-3.
   - Flutter devs mostly consume endpoints, and the agent already curls them.
   - Odoo devs mostly call generic JSON-2 model endpoints and rarely create endpoints.
   - Only the subset already working in Claude Code or Cursor benefits.
   - Realistic frequency is about 3 uses/week x 2.5 min, about 8 min/week for an agent-heavy dev and about 3-4 min/week for the average dev.

4. The cost is underestimated. Write tools are needed only because the desktop app owns the data in Drift and workspace.json is a single-file export. Neither the CLI nor the app has a file-watcher or merge path today (I grepped lib/ and found no workspace.json watch code), so an app<->CLI two-writer reload plus conflict guard plus an "AI changes" review UI is closer to L than M. Yaak and Bruno get agent support cheaply (Yaak shipped a skill) because their collections are plain files the agent edits directly; PostPilot's cost comes from its architecture, not from user demand.

The one solid part is the safety gap, and it is a bug, not a time-saver. I confirmed that mcp_server.dart exposes exactly 4 tools and that nothing under lib/features/cli references ProductionGuard or ProductionDetector. An agent can therefore send a POST or DELETE to a production environment through run_request or run_collection with no confirmation. That fix is small (S): refuse non-GET calls on a production-flagged environment unless the user passes an explicit flag. It should be done now as hardening of the existing MCP server, outside this feature-ranking.

Recommendation: drop the full toolkit from the "saves developer time" list. Do the production guard as a separate S safety fix. At most add a skill file plus a couple of read-only tools later. Defer write tools until real users ask. I could not collect fresh demand evidence because the WebSearch budget was exhausted; the Yaak skill release is the only external datapoint I confirmed, and it supports "a skill file is enough", not "write tools are needed".
- **Platform notes:** stdio MCP runs as the dart:io CLI, so desktop OS only. Concurrent edits by the app and the CLI need the same conflict-safe merge path as Git sync. Port ProductionGuard/ProductionDetector from lib/features/safety into the shared runner. Never return secret values; run all output through SecretMasker.

### 15. Smart HAR import: noisy recording to a clean API collection (Flutter and Odoo aware)
- **Score 1.6** | effort M | ~4 min/week saved | persona odoo | lenses: competitors | skeptic votes 2/3
- **Problem today:** To learn which calls an app or the Odoo web UI makes, devs record traffic. Importing the HAR makes one request per entry named 'METHOD /path' with hard-coded host and tokens: images, JS bundles, analytics and duplicates come along, and every Odoo UI call is POST /web/dataset/call_kw/... with a JSON-RPC envelope. Cleaning 60-200 entries takes 10-20 min.
- **What PostPilot would do:** The Import dialog gets a cleanup preview ('212 entries -> 14 API calls'): keep XHR/fetch/JSON, drop assets and known trackers, de-duplicate by method+path+body shape, group into folders by path prefix, replace host with {{baseUrl}} and create the environment, move Authorization/cookie/API-key values to local-only secret variables, name requests by operation. Odoo call_kw calls are named model.method and offered 'Convert to JSON-2' via the existing converter. Flutter DevTools can export its Network tab as HAR, so a real app session becomes a collection without a proxy.
- **Where it lives:** Preview step of the existing Import dialog (checkbox list plus summary line) and palette command 'Import HAR'. No new screen.
- **Partly exists already:** A basic, no-cleanup HAR import already exists. Files: lib/features/import_export/domain/services/har_parser.dart, lib/features/import_export/domain/usecases/import_har_usecase.dart, lib/features/import_export/presentation/import_any_dialog.dart (the generic paste-and-import dialog), lib/features/import_export/domain/services/import_format_detector.dart, and the 'Import…' palette item in lib/features/command_palette/presentation/palette_items.dart (subtitle already lists HAR).

What it does today: HarParser.parse turns EVERY log.entries[].request into one request named 'METHOD /path'. It skips only entries with a non-http scheme, an unsupported method, or a CORS preflight. It drops transport headers (Host, Content-Length and similar) and merges the HTTP/2 split Cookie headers. The URL stays absolute, with the host hard-coded. All requests land flat in one new collection named 'HAR import - host'.

What does NOT exist (grep over lib/ confirms):
- No filter for static assets, XHR/fetch or JSON-only entries, and no tracker list.
- No de-duplication.
- No folder grouping by path prefix.
- No host-to-{{baseUrl}} replacement and no environment creation. OpenAPI import does set a baseUrl collection variable (import_openapi_usecase.dart, openapi_parser.dart), but HAR does not.
- No moving of Authorization/cookie/API-key values to variables at import time. Only the Git push path already blanks such header values (SecretFields.stripDoc in git_sync/domain/services/secret_fields.dart), so the replayable token is simply lost on push rather than turned into a local-only variable.
- No preview or checkbox step. ImportAnyDialog is a single paste TextField (maxLines 12) with a format dropdown. There is also no file picker, which matters because real HAR files are several MB.
- The Odoo converter (OdooRpcConverter in lib/features/odoo/domain/services/odoo_rpc_converter.dart, which already handles /web/dataset/call_kw and execute_kw) is only reachable from the Odoo Studio 'Migrate RPC' tab. Nothing in the HAR path calls it, and no import step names requests model.method.

Verdict: the importer exists, but the cleanup, grouping, variable extraction, secret handling and Odoo conversion that make up the candidate's value are all new. It should be pitched as 'HAR import v2 / cleanup preview', not a new importer, and it needs a file-picker or drag-drop alongside the paste box.

Realism notes:
- 15 min x 1.5 per week is plausible but at the high end. The wider personas are Flutter (DevTools HAR export) and web QA, so persona 'all' is more honest than 'odoo'.
- The 'secrets to local-only variables' step overlaps the existing push-time blanking. Its genuine new value is that requests keep working after import and sync.
- **Competitors:** Evidence claims checked by fetching the pages. Flutter DevTools 2.38.0 release notes (docs.flutter.cn/tools/devtools/release-notes/release-notes-2.38.0.md) do say DevTools added HAR export of network requests. Yaak changelog 2026.7.0 (yaak.app/changelog/2026.7.0) does say each OpenAPI `servers` entry becomes its own sub-environment with baseUrl and credentials. That is a competitor doing host-to-environment for OpenAPI, not for HAR, and the changelog does not mention HAR import at all. The Odoo 19 external API docs link was not re-fetched. Insomnia and Postman also import HAR as a flat list, but that was not re-verified: the web search budget (200/200) was exhausted, so I could not confirm whether Bruno, Hoppscotch, Insomnia or Postman filter or de-duplicate on import. I found no evidence of a competitor doing HAR cleanup plus Odoo call_kw conversion.
- **Design in this codebase:** VERDICT: feasible without a rewrite. Every building block exists, but the candidate's "S" is optimistic: M (1-2 weeks) is the honest size.

WHAT I VERIFIED IN THE CODE
- har_parser.dart: pure Dart, runs in compute(). It produces ImportedRequest only, keeping no response status, response mime or timing. It skips only non-http URLs and CORS preflights, and does no dedup, asset filter or variable lifting. The candidate's claim holds. test/har_import_test.dart plus the in-memory fakes (support/in_memory_import_export_fakes.dart) already exist, so the new logic is easy to unit test.
- ImportedCollection already carries folders (ImportedFolder), collection variables and collection auth. ImportedCollectionWriter writes them with rollback on failure.
- AddStarterTemplateUseCase (writer + EnvironmentRepository) is the exact precedent: write a collection, create an environment, upsert variables with isSecret. ImportInsomniaUseCase also creates environments and rolls them back.
- OdooRpcConverter._fromJson already accepts the /web/dataset/call_kw envelope ({params:{model,method,args,kwargs}}) and moves context into OdooCall.context. OdooJson2.headers()/draft() plus OdooVars (odooUrl, odooDb, odooApiKey) give the target shape. CreateOdooWorkspaceUseCase.createEnvironment can create the Odoo environment.
- Cross-feature domain imports already have precedent (templates imports odoo and import_export), so no new layering problem.
- SecretNames.looksSecretKey/isSecretHeader/isSecretQuery and SecretText are reusable for detecting tokens in headers, query strings and JSON bodies.
- ToolDialog, BusyLabel, PaletteItems.tools() (one line to register) and the ChangeNotifier ViewModel pattern are all in place.

PROPOSED DESIGN (all under lib/features/import_export unless noted)
1. Parser: refactor har_parser.dart to also emit a HarEntry. It carries the old ImportedRequest data plus responseStatus, responseMime, _resourceType (Chrome only), startedDateTime and pageref. ParsedHar.requests stays, so the existing tests keep passing.
2. domain/services/har_cleaner.dart: a pure Dart service, no Flutter imports, so the CLI can use it later.
   - Output is a HarCleanupPlan: a list of candidates, each with keep/drop and a reason (static asset, tracker, duplicate of #n, preflight, UI-internal), plus the extracted variables.
   - Classification uses _resourceType when present, otherwise the response mime and URL extension. Firefox and DevTools HARs lack _resourceType, so the fallback is mandatory.
   - Dedup key is method + normalised path (numeric/UUID segments become :id) + a JSON body shape hash. GraphQL is named and keyed by operationName.
   - Folders come from path prefix. The host becomes {{baseUrl}}; extra hosts become {{authUrl}} etc.
   - The tracker list is a short hand-written const list. Do not copy EasyList or EasyPrivacy (CC BY-SA/GPL licensing).
3. Secrets: reuse SecretNames. Move the Authorization bearer to ONE collection-level RequestAuth (bearerToken '{{token}}'), and requests keep AuthType.inherit. This is cleaner than rewriting every header. Cookie and API-key headers, secret query params and secret JSON body values become env variables with isSecret=true and '{{...}}' placeholders. This needs a small new "lift" function in SecretText: the existing code only blanks values, it does not extract them.
4. Odoo adapter in features/odoo/domain/services (e.g. odoo_har_adapter.dart), called by the cleaner through a small rewriter interface or direct import (the templates precedent allows it).
   - Detect Odoo by /web/dataset/call_kw/ in paths.
   - Name requests model.method, run OdooRpcConverter, and emit JSON-2 requests with OdooJson2.headers() and {{odooUrl}}.
   - Create the environment through CreateOdooWorkspaceUseCase.createEnvironment with a blank API key.
   - Drop UI-only endpoints with no JSON-2 equivalent (/web/webclient/load_menus, /web/action/load, /bus/*, /mail/*, /web/image).
   - The converter's _signatures table lacks the methods the web client calls most: web_save, onchange, web_read_group, read_progress_bar, web_resequence and action_* buttons. Today they fall into the "arg0 / rename me" branch. Extend the table (about 1 day) or the "Convert to JSON-2" promise is half-true.
5. domain/usecases/import_har_cleaned_usecase.dart: (text, options) -> ImportSummary. It follows the AddStarterTemplateUseCase and ImportInsomniaUseCase pattern: writer.write(collection), create the environment, upsert variables, and roll both back on error. Keep ImportHarUseCase as the "import as-is" path.
6. UI: a new HarCleanupDialog + HarCleanupViewModel built on ToolDialog (the current ImportAnyDialog is a plain AlertDialog, too small for a checkbox list).
   - Summary line "212 entries -> 14 API calls".
   - A checkbox list with reason chips.
   - Toggles: {{baseUrl}}, move secrets, dedupe, Odoo convert.
   - Footer: Import with BusyLabel.
   - Entry points: ImportAnyDialog hands off to it when the detected format is HAR, and a palette item (PaletteItems.tools()) 'Import HAR: clean up a recording'. It fills the phone full-screen automatically.
7. Environment naming: the safety lock detects production by environment NAME only (ProductionDetector). Do not auto-activate the new environment, and let the user name it. Offer to name it "<host> (prod)" when the host is not localhost, so the red PROD lock still protects replays of a recorded production session.

EFFORT BREAKDOWN (about 8-10 working days)
- Parser refactor: 0.5 day.
- Cleaner, classification, dedup and naming: 3 days.
- Odoo adapter and converter table: 1.5 days.
- Secret lifting and writer use case with rollback: 1 day.
- Dialog and ViewModel: 2 days.
- File loading: 1 day.
- Tests: 1.5 days.

ARCHITECTURE FIT: clean Provider/ChangeNotifier ViewModel, UseCase, existing repositories and DI registration in injector.dart (one registerLazySingleton plus one registerFactory). No DB schema change, no new feature folder.

MINUTES ESTIMATE: the candidate's 15 min x 1.5/week is a bit generous. 10-12 min x ~1 per week is more realistic for a dev who reverse-engineers an app or the Odoo web UI.

EVIDENCE CHECKED
- Flutter DevTools 2.38.0 note: "Added support to export network requests as a HAR file". The page does not say whether bodies are included.
- Chrome docs (developer.chrome.com/docs/devtools/network/reference): "Export HAR (sanitized)" and "Copy all as HAR (sanitized)" exclude Cookie, Set-Cookie and Authorization headers by default. A sanitized HAR therefore has NO auth, so the importer must create the {{token}} variable anyway and say so.
- I could not retrieve the Odoo 19 migration section text, so I did not confirm the exact removal date of /jsonrpc and /xmlrpc. Do not quote one in the UI.
- **Risks:** 1. BIGGEST GAP, NOT IN THE CANDIDATE: the import dialog is paste-only. There is no file_picker, no drag-and-drop and no file-read code anywhere in lib (pubspec has none; only downloadFile exists for saving).
 - Real HAR files are 5-200 MB (response bodies, base64 images). A multi-MB paste into the 12-line TextField will jank or freeze Flutter, and Flutter DevTools only offers file export.
 - Fix: add a "Load file" button. The cheapest option is the file_picker package (all platforms, withData: true), with a web-only fallback via package:web, which is already a dependency (see core/utils/file_download_web.dart for the conditional-import pattern). Also add a "Paste from clipboard" button that bypasses the TextField (Chrome "Copy all as HAR").
 - Linux needs zenity/kdialog for file_picker. A new native plugin adds build weight on the already tight C: drive.
2. Memory: jsonDecode of a 100+ MB HAR can use hundreds of MB, and on web compute() runs on the main thread. Mitigate with a size cap (for example refuse above ~150 MB with a "re-export without content" hint) and by dropping response.content early.
3. Secret handling is the main safety point, and it is a net improvement. The current HAR import stores literal Authorization and Cookie values in request headers. SecretSplitter (workspace.json to workspace.local.json) covers only env/global secrets, secret-named collection variables and auth fields, NOT request headers. git_sync's SecretFields.stripDoc does blank headers, but I did not verify which Git path the UI uses by default. Lifting tokens to isSecret environment variables and '{{...}}' placeholders is safe on both paths, because template-only values are kept and secret environment variables go to the local file. Gaps to test: tokens inside URL paths, non-JSON bodies, CSRF tokens, and values that SecretNames does not flag (a heuristic, so the preview must show what was moved and let the user undo it).
4. Heuristic false positives (a real API on a tracker-like host, a dedup that merges two different calls). The checkbox preview with reason chips is the mitigation and must be mandatory, not optional. Keep an "import everything as-is" path.
5. Odoo UI calls authenticate by session cookie, while JSON-2 needs an API key and the X-Odoo-Database header. Converted requests only work after the user creates an API key and fills odooDb. Say so in the dialog. UI-internal methods (onchange, web_save) are noisy, so default them to "dropped" or "needs review".
6. Legal, ToS and store policy: low. The feature only reads a file the user already holds. There is no capture, proxy or MITM, which is where iOS/Play policy trouble would start. Replaying a recorded session against a third-party service is the user's responsibility. HAR contains PII, so never log or upload the file. The AI assistant must not receive it unmasked (the existing credential masking applies).
7. Web: parsing, cleanup and import work everywhere. Replaying imported requests on web still hits CORS (existing limitation, not new). Odoo JSON-2 from a browser origin also needs the server's CORS config.
8. Value: moderate and niche (about 1 use per week, many developers just use "Copy as cURL" for a few requests). A good fourth or fifth candidate rather than top three, but cheap because it extends existing code.
- **Strongest objection:** The claimed 15 min x 1.5 per week does not hold up. (1) Frequency is inflated. A full-session HAR import is an occasional reverse-engineering job, for example mapping an undocumented third-party API or a one-off Odoo UI flow. It is not a weekly habit. Backend devs already have OpenAPI or source code, and Flutter devs know their own Dio calls. A realistic rate is about 0.3-0.5 per week, so about 2-4 min/week even if the 10-15 min saved per use is accepted. (2) The common Odoo case is one or two calls, not 200. A dev wants to know what the Confirm button calls (call_kw on sale.order with method button_confirm). They copy that single request as cURL from the browser's network tab. The existing smart-paste cURL plus the existing RPC-to-JSON-2 converter (odoo_rpc_converter.dart) already cover that in seconds. The 60-200 entry noisy-recording scenario is the rarer one. (3) Browsers already do much of the filtering. Chrome and Firefox have a Fetch/XHR filter, and a dev can delete or keep only a few rows before saving. The 10-20 min cleanup figure assumes the dev imports everything raw and then prunes it inside PostPilot. That is the worst way to do it, and a dev who does it once usually learns not to. (4) HarParser already exists and works. It handles cookie merging, transport headers, preflight skipping and multipart. What is missing is a thin cleanup layer: asset filter, dedup, host to {{baseUrl}}, and secrets moved to local-only variables. The auto-folder grouping and auto-naming are cosmetic for a collection the dev will use once. The Flutter DevTools HAR angle is a stretch, because few Flutter devs export a HAR just to rebuild a collection they could write in minutes. (4b) I could not find demand evidence. The web-search budget was exhausted, and the candidate's cited URLs are about DevTools and Yaak changelogs, not about developers complaining about HAR cleanup. Salvage: one real point is safety. Importing a HAR drops live Authorization headers and cookies into a workspace that Git sync can push. That is an error-prone risk, but it is a hardening fix to the existing importer (strip or move secrets to local-only variables, warn before push), not a time-saving feature. Fold it into a security backlog item and do not rank it as a time-saver.
- **Platform notes:** Pure Dart, all platforms. HAR files contain cookies and tokens: strip or move to local-only variables before anything is saved or synced and warn before Git push. Odoo conversion depends on the existing RPC to JSON-2 converter.

### 16. Odoo request checker: validate a JSON-2 body against the live schema, plus field autocomplete
- **Score 1.6** | effort M | ~4 min/week saved | persona odoo | lenses: odoo-daily | skeptic votes 2/3
- **Problem today:** An Odoo dev writes fields, domain, order and vals by hand. A typo, a renamed field (groups_id became group_ids, product_uom became product_uom_id in 19), a wrong selection key, a many2one sent as [id,'Name'], a write to a readonly/computed field or a missing required field only shows up after a round trip as a 400/500 with a Python traceback; the fix loop (open fields_get in another tab, search, retype, resend) repeats dozens of times per integration.
- **What PostPilot would do:** Odoo Studio already caches fields_get. A Check action parses the open request (model and method from the /json/2/<model>/<method> URL, then the body) and lists problems before sending: unknown field with 'did you mean', wrong value type, selection key not allowed (shows valid keys), required fields missing on create (minus default_get), readonly/non-stored field in vals/domain/order, and a small version-rename table. For Odoo JSON-2 URLs the JSON body editor also offers field-name autocomplete from the cached schema.
- **Where it lives:** New 'Check' tab in Odoo Studio plus palette command 'Odoo: check current request'; autocomplete appears inside the existing body editor only for /json/2/ URLs.
- **Partly exists already:** The user-facing feature does not exist: no code anywhere in lib/, bin/ or the README checks a request body against a model schema before sending, and the body editor has no Odoo field autocomplete. Building blocks exist, but none touch the request:

1. Schema model. lib/features/odoo/domain/entities/odoo_model_info.dart (OdooModelInfo.parse) already parses fields_get into OdooField with type, required, readonly, stored, relation, selection pairs and help. It also accepts the legacy JSON-RPC envelope. This is exactly the data a checker needs.

2. "Cache" is weaker than the candidate says. lib/features/odoo/presentation/view_models/odoo_studio_view_model.dart holds ONE model's `info` in memory. The VM is registered as a factory in lib/core/di/injector.dart and disposed when the dialog closes (odoo_studio_dialog.dart). There is no per-model or per-environment persistence, so the checker needs its own persisted multi-model schema cache. It also needs a fetch for the model parsed from the request URL, plus a paste path (useFieldsJson exists).

3. Autocomplete exists only in Odoo Studio. lib/features/odoo/presentation/widgets/odoo_domain_tab.dart offers field-name Autocomplete from `_info`, and OdooOperators.forType in odoo_domain.dart gives per-type operators. Neither is wired to the request body or the request builder. odoo_explorer_tab.dart has model-name Autocomplete.

4. Post-response only. lib/features/odoo/domain/services/odoo_error_parser.dart plus widgets/odoo_error_banner.dart (shown from request_builder_page.dart:459) explain the error after the round trip. An "Invalid field" message gets the hint "Run fields_get to list the real ones". The banner has no did-you-mean and no schema lookup, which is the manual loop the candidate wants to remove.

5. Request bodies are only checked for JSON syntax. lib/features/request_builder/presentation/widgets/json_body_format.dart does this, and body_editor.dart is a plain VariableTextFormField with a comment calling it an MVP. It has no completion infrastructure, so in-editor autocomplete would be a real build, not a small add-on.

6. No rename table, no "did you mean", no Levenshtein or fuzzy matching, and no selection-key, required-on-create or readonly-in-vals check anywhere in lib/features/odoo. The only mention of /json/2/ in code is odoo_json2.dart, which builds URLs and does not parse them back. The AI tasks (ai_tasks.dart) only describe the URL format in a prompt.

Review notes for the owner:
- The Check tab, the palette command and the did-you-mean, wrong-type, selection-key, required-minus-default_get and readonly/non-stored checks are all new. Effort is M or lower because OdooModelInfo already carries the needed flags.
- Drop or defer the body-editor autocomplete, since it is the L-sized part and the Check list gives most of the value.
- Do not hard-code a rename table. The v19 renames are model-specific: the community gotchas page I fetched says product.line uses product_uom_id while stock.move still uses product_uom. A static table would give false positives. Rely on live fields_get plus did-you-mean.
- Checking "the open request" from inside the Odoo Studio dialog needs a way to read the active request and resolve {{odooUrl}} and {{odooApiKey}} from the environment. The Studio VM already has loadFromActiveEnvironment. A pre-send inline chip next to the existing OdooErrorBanner might fit better than a tab.
- **Competitors:** Closest implementation: odoo-mcp-19 (https://github.com/AlanOgic/odoo-mcp-19). In locked mode its validator fetches fields_get for the target model with a 60s LRU cache and rejects payloads that reference non-existent fields or write to readonly fields. It reads the schema first so field names are not guessed (147 commits). It is an MCP server for AI agents, not an interactive API client with did-you-mean and body autocomplete. I could not confirm that no Odoo-aware Postman-style client does this: the web search budget ran out, so that part is unchecked. Evidence checks: the odoo-data-flow page says "One of the most time-consuming tasks in an Odoo-to-Odoo migration is identifying which fields have been added, removed, or renamed" (confirmed, but it is about migrations, not API calls). The gittea gotchas page confirms groups_id to group_ids and the model-specific product_uom_id split, and it is low authority. The Odoo 19 external_api page fetched only as a table of contents, so the claim that /doc does not validate payloads is unverified from that fetch.
- **Design in this codebase:** VERDICT: feasible without a rewrite; corrected effort M (about 10-12 working days) for the Check only. The field-name autocomplete should be split out as a later S/M item, or dropped.

WHAT I FOUND IN THE CODE (corrections to the candidate's assumptions)
1. "Odoo Studio already caches fields_get" is only partly true. OdooStudioViewModel is a registerFactory, so it is new per dialog and disposed on close. It keeps ONE OdooModelInfo in memory (`info`), and loadFields() replaces it. There is no persisted, multi-model, per-server cache. A schema store has to be built, and it is the main new piece.
2. A "Check tab in Odoo Studio" is the wrong place. The Studio has its own connection form, which can differ from the open request, and a modal tab cannot edit the request. The right precedent is GraphqlExplorerDialog: BodyEditor opens it with the request's URL and headers, and an `onUse` callback writes back through widget.onChanged. Build `OdooCheckDialog` (ToolDialog, width ~1000) the same way, with `onApplyBody(String newBodyText)`.
3. Reuse the request's own connection. PrepareRequestUseCase is the single path from request to wire. It returns the resolved URL, headers (including the auth helper's Authorization header, X-Odoo-Database and any variables) and the exact ApiRequestOptions (SSL verify, proxy, timeout). Fetch fields_get from the request's own resolved origin with those headers and `prepared.options.toApiOptions()`. This needs no connect form. It also fixes a gap: the existing OdooClient uses default options, so it ignores per-request SSL and proxy settings and fails against self-signed dev Odoo servers. PrepareRequestUseCase is not in the GetIt injector today (SendRequestUseCase and GenerateCodeSnippetUseCase each build their own), so register it as a lazySingleton.
4. The request body is resolved and parsed lazily. Take the resolved body from spec.bodyBytes. Replace leftover unresolved `{{x}}` placeholders with an "unknown value" sentinel, so no type check is run on them.
5. The body editor is a plain TextField (VariableTextFormField over VariableTextEditingController), not a code editor. A caret-anchored suggestion popup is not supported, so in-editor autocomplete is a separate chunk of work, not a free add-on.

DESIGN (all inside lib/features/odoo, layered like the rest of the app)
- domain/entities/odoo_model_info.dart: add `sortable` and `searchable` (default true) to OdooField, plus toJson/fromJson for OdooModelInfo so it can be cached. Request these attributes in fields_get, and add a schemaVersion to the cached blob so a wider attribute set invalidates old cache.
- domain/repositories/odoo_schema_repository.dart (interface) and data/odoo_schema_repository_impl.dart. Store JSON in the EXISTING SettingEntries key-value table through SettingsDao, with keys like `odoo.schema|<origin>|<db>|<model>`. This needs NO new Drift table, no migration and no build_runner run. That matters because the C: drive is at 100% (2 GB free). The DAO only needs a prefix-delete method added to its class body (for a "Clear schema cache" action), which needs no regeneration. It works on web via drift wasm. I checked that nothing but SettingsRepositoryImpl reads settingEntries, and BackupService, the git workspace.json and clearWorkplaceData never touch it, so the schema cache does not leak into Git or backups.
- domain/services/odoo_schema_service.dart (data call uses the injected ApiClient). It exposes `Future<OdooModelInfo?> modelFor(PreparedRequest, String model, {bool refresh})`. It reads the cache, fetches on a miss, and stores the result with a fetchedAt time. HARD INVARIANT: it may only ever POST `fields_get` and `default_get`, enforced by a whitelist in code, so it can never fire a write at production. It calls ApiClient directly, so ProductionGuard is not triggered; that is correct for read-only calls. It sends the credentials only to the same origin as the request. A paste-fields_get fallback reuses the Explorer's existing paste flow for web/CORS.
- domain/services/odoo_request_checker.dart: pure Dart with no Flutter imports, so the CLI can reuse it later. It does three things.
  a) A small position-aware tolerant JSON scanner. It is new, because the existing _JsonScanner is private and only reformats, and jsonDecode gives no offsets. It records line, column and span per key and value, which is needed for useful messages and for "Apply fix".
  b) A signature table for about 12 generic ORM methods: create/vals_list, write/vals, read, search, search_read, search_count, name_search, default_get, fields_get, unlink, copy, plus formatted_read_group. It checks unknown or misspelled parameter names, and a missing `ids` on recordset methods. Custom methods such as action_confirm cannot be checked and are skipped.
  c) Rules, with a severity per finding. ERROR: unknown field with a did-you-mean suggestion (reuse FuzzyMatcher or add a tiny Levenshtein), a selection key not in the list, and the wrong shape for many2one, many2many or one2many commands ([0,0,{..}], [6,0,[..]]), recursing into the comodel with a depth limit of 2. WARNING: a required field missing on create (minus default_get, which is a second read-only call), a readonly or non-stored field in vals, and datetime in 'T'/'Z' ISO form. Domain: validate the first path segment strictly and follow relations only through relational fields. Skip date-part suffixes such as .month_number, `properties` fields, and `any`/`not any` sub-domains. Order: field exists and sortable.
  Drop the version-rename table. Fuzzy did-you-mean already maps groups_id to group_ids and product_uom to product_uom_id, and a hand-kept table is a maintenance burden with weak sourcing.
- Staleness: show the cache age and a "Refresh schema" button. When a field comes back unknown from a cached schema, refetch once automatically before reporting, which removes most of the false positives that appear after a module upgrade.
- presentation/view_models/odoo_check_view_model.dart: a ChangeNotifier registered as a registerFactory. It holds findings, the busy label and any error (as the Studio view model does).
- presentation/widgets/odoo_check_dialog.dart: a ToolDialog with a findings list grouped by severity (line:col, message, "did you mean X", valid selection keys), an "Apply fix" button per rename, and a "Re-check" button. After a fix the dialog stays open.

UI PLACEMENT (keeps the request and response views clean)
1. A small "Check Odoo request" button in BodyEditor's JSON toolbar next to Beautify/Minify. It renders ONLY when the URL path matches /json/2/<model>/<method>. It reads the URL through context.read<RequestBuilderViewModel>() with the same try/catch ProviderNotFound pattern that _exploreSchema already uses.
2. OdooErrorBanner (already shown only when Odoo returns an error) gets a "Check request against schema" action for the Unknown field and Unknown parameter cases. It needs a new callback that RequestBuilderPage passes in. This directly serves the fix loop that costs the time, with no extra clutter.
3. Palette item 'odoo.check' in PaletteItems.tools(). The palette runs in the shell context, so use the existing ShellViewModel registerSender and registerBodySearch pattern. Add `registerOdooCheck(requestId, callback)`, which the active RequestBuilderPage registers and unregisters.
4. Optional: Studio's loadFields writes through to the schema repository so the Explorer and the Domain builder share the cache.

EFFORT BREAKDOWN
- Schema repository, service and cache: ~2 days.
- Tolerant scanner with spans: ~1.5 days.
- Checker rules and signature table: ~3-4 days.
- Dialog, view model and three entry points: ~2-3 days.
- Tests and fixtures (recorded fields_get for res.partner and sale.order, DI wiring test): ~2 days.
- Total about 10-12 days, so M.
- Field autocomplete: +4-6 days (S/M). It needs a "where am I in this JSON at this offset" function over incomplete text and a docked suggestion-chip strip under the editor, because there is no caret popup. Recommend deferring it, or making it opt-in, to honour the clean-UI rule.

PLATFORMS
- Pure Dart; no dart:io and no conditional imports are needed for the checker or the store.
- Web: the live fields_get call needs CORS on the Odoo server (X-Odoo-Database and Authorization trigger a preflight); fall back to pasting fields_get.
- Desktop and mobile: fine.
- **Risks:** 1. FALSE POSITIVES are the real product risk, not legal or security. They come from:
- Fields hidden by `groups` for the API user (they look unknown, though the same key could not write them either).
- Stale cache after a module upgrade, mitigated by an automatic refetch on unknown.
- Required fields that create() or a default fills (`required` is only a hint even with default_get).
- readonly is a UI-only flag on plain stored fields, so a "readonly" finding must be a hint. A non-stored computed field without an inverse is silently ignored by write, which is a good warning, but fields_get does not expose `inverse`.
- Dotted domain paths, date-part suffixes, `properties` fields and custom create() overrides.
Mitigation: separate ERROR from WARNING, never block sending, and show how to dismiss.

2. ODOO 19 SEMANTICS MUST BE VERIFIED, not assumed. I reasoned from memory of the ORM that the datetime 'T' form and a JSON [id,'Name'] for a many2one both fail (the latter is silently nulled, not an error), but none of the rules has been verified against Odoo 19 source in this task. Before shipping each rule, check it against Odoo 19 source and a throwaway Odoo 19 instance, and record fixtures (fields_get JSON plus real error bodies) as golden tests. Until a rule is confirmed, ship it as a WARNING.

3. SECURITY: low.
- The only new network calls are read-only fields_get and default_get, sent to the request's own origin with the request's own credentials. Enforce the method whitelist in code so the checker can never send create, write or unlink.
- The schema cache holds the customer's custom model and field names (x_studio_*), stored locally in SQLite. It stays out of Git and backups. Provide "Clear schema cache" and show the cache age.
- Never log or echo the API key (the console log already masks it).

4. LEGAL / TOS / STORE POLICY: none material.
- It uses only the documented JSON-2 endpoints on the user's own server.
- No new OS permission and no dynamic code, so no store issue.
- If any renamed-field list is kept, derive it from Odoo's own release notes, upgrade scripts or tests rather than copying community repos, whose licences are unclear (the evidence link is a low-authority personal Gitea page).

5. SCOPE CREEP: do not bundle autocomplete into the first release (no caret popup in a plain TextField).

6. OTHER:
- The checker only covers the generic ORM methods and /json/2/ URLs, not custom methods and not legacy /jsonrpc.
- The 3 min x 10/week estimate depends on the false-positive rate being low.
- Disk: C: is 100% full (about 2 GB free); the design deliberately avoids a new Drift table and codegen for this reason.
- **Strongest objection:** The claimed 3 min x 10 uses = 30 min/week does not survive scrutiny. (WebSearch budget was exhausted, so this is based on reading the PostPilot code and my knowledge of Odoo's ORM, not new web evidence.)

1. The round trip is not the pain the candidate describes. Odoo already names the bad field in its error ("Invalid field 'x' on model 'y'"), and PostPilot's OdooErrorParser already shows an "Unknown field" banner for it. A resend costs about a second; the real cost is looking up the right name, and that is 30-60 seconds, not 3 minutes.

2. Domains are already covered. The visual domain builder in odoo_domain_tab.dart already uses Autocomplete over the cached fields. The "Update from OpenAPI"-style tooling, the 10 ready-made requests per model, the AI assistant and the Dart client generator (typed classes from fields_get) mean Flutter+Odoo developers rarely hand-write bodies, and when they do, the first draft usually starts from a generated request.

3. The "readonly or non-stored field in vals" check is the least trustworthy part. In the Odoo ORM, `readonly=True` is mostly a UI flag. A stored, non-computed readonly field (state, many others) can be written through the API without error, so flagging it would produce many false positives. The case that actually fails silently, a computed field with no inverse, cannot be read from fields_get because it does not expose the inverse. A checker that cries wolf gets ignored.

4. "Required fields missing minus default_get" is approximate. It needs an extra default_get call, and ignores required-by-constraint and company-dependent defaults, so it will give both false alarms and false assurance.

5. The version-rename table duplicates the live-schema "did you mean" check (group_ids vs groups_id is a small edit distance) and goes stale every release. Cut it.

6. Usage is overstated. Most Odoo developers spend their week writing Python modules. Hand-writing JSON-2 bodies happens in integration-building weeks, about 3-6 new bodies, and not at all in other weeks. Averaged, that is about 4 uses x ~1 min = about 4 min/week. The 10-15 min/week figure is only reachable in a peak integration week.

7. Cost and clutter. Field autocomplete inside the existing JSON editor, a new Check tab and schema-cache freshness handling is an M-to-L effort that touches the request editor the owner wants to keep clean.

What to build instead (S effort, about 80% of the value): extend the existing Odoo error banner so that on an "Invalid field"/unknown-key error with a cached schema it shows "did you mean X?" and a one-click replace in the body. It is reactive, so it has no false positives. Optionally add field-name autocomplete only for /json/2/ bodies. Cache the `searchable` and `sortable` attributes (OdooField does not store them yet) to improve domain and order hints. Drop the Check tab, the rename table and the readonly rule.
- **Platform notes:** Pure Dart, works offline from a cached or pasted fields_get. Live fetch from the web build needs CORS on the Odoo server (desktop/mobile fine). Needs a read-only API user that may call fields_get on the model.

### 17. Odoo smart references: write an XML-ID or name, PostPilot resolves the id (portable across databases)
- **Score 1.6** | effort M | ~4 min/week saved | persona odoo | lenses: pain-points, odoo-daily | skeptic votes 2/3
- **Problem today:** JSON-2 bodies and domains need numeric ids for many2one fields (country_id, currency_id, company_id, journal_id, taxes, stages), and ids differ per database. For each one the dev sends an extra name_search or ir.model.data search_read, pastes the id, and repeats on staging/prod; a request saved with journal_id 3 silently hits the wrong record elsewhere. The old XML-ID helper is private (_xmlid_lookup) and not callable remotely.
- **What PostPilot would do:** Type {{odoo:base.us}} (XML-ID) or {{odoo:res.country?name=United States}} (name lookup) anywhere in a URL, body or domain. Before send PostPilot resolves it against the connected environment (ir.model.data search_read or name_search), caches per connection, and the variable hover card shows the resolved id. A 'Pick record...' action on many2one fields in Odoo Studio, and 'Make portable' on a selected numeric id (reads ir.model.data for model+res_id), insert the token. A collection-level 'Make ids portable' scan proposes replacements for every *_id key with a preview. Runner and CLI resolve tokens the same way.
- **Where it lives:** Part of the existing {{variable}} system (autocomplete, hover card) plus a 'Pick record' / 'Make portable' action in Odoo Studio's Explorer tab; palette commands. No new dialog.
- **Competitors:** No dedicated Odoo API client with this feature was found in the evidence gathered here. A web search for competitors was not possible because the search budget was exhausted. Postman and Bruno can do it only by hand through pre-request scripts. Odoo's own import/load() flow uses external ids (`field/id` columns), which confirms the pain point but is a data-import tool, not an API-request tool.
- **Design in this codebase:** VERDICT: feasible without a rewrite, if v1 is cut to "XML-ID tokens + Explorer XML-ID column". M (about 1-1.5 weeks) for that core. The two other parts of the pitch are not M; see Scope cuts.

WHAT THE CODE LOOKS LIKE (verified in lib/)
- PrepareRequestUseCase.call is already async, and it is the single path for send, code snippets and the app's collection runner. RequestSpecBuilder.build and VariableResolver.resolve are synchronous. The same split already exists for OAuth2: the token is fetched ahead of time and build() only applies the cached value. So the async step goes in front of build(), and build() stays untouched.
- VariableResolver.layered(scopes) exposes a public `scopes` list. A pre-resolved map can be prepended as the top scope with no resolver change.
- The CLI does NOT use PrepareRequestUseCase. WorkspaceRunner.runRequest calls RequestSpecBuilder.build directly and sends through CliSend. The MCP server runs through WorkspaceRunner, so it gets the CLI behaviour for free.
- Odoo calls already exist as pure-Dart OdooCall plus OdooClient(ApiClient). The Odoo workspace stores odooUrl, odooDb and odooApiKey as environment variables.
- Syntax blocker: AppConstants.variablePattern is `[\w.$-]+`, so `{{xmlid:base.us}}` (colon) is not a token today. A literal `{{odoo:res.country?name=United States}}` can never match. The pattern is duplicated in 4 more places (see Design).

DESIGN
1. Token syntax. Add `:` to the name class in AppConstants.variablePattern and in the `_placeholder` regex in json_body_format.dart (line 30). Without the second change, Beautify rejects `"country_id": {{xmlid:base.us}}` with "Invalid {{variable}} placeholder". Two more places need a check: variable_text_controller.dart and variable_text_form_field.dart (colour and hover use the shared pattern), and run_data_parser.dart and extractor_entity.dart (they validate variable names with matchAsPrefix; broadening only allows more names). Token form is `{{xmlid:module.name}}`. The user writes it unquoted, so the body stays valid JSON and the id goes in as a number.

2. New pure-Dart domain service, lib/features/odoo/domain/services/odoo_ref_resolver.dart (no Flutter, so the CLI can import it).
   - `Future<Map<String,String>> resolve(Set<String> tokens, OdooTarget target)`.
   - OdooTarget = origin + database + bearer key, taken from the request being prepared (resolved spec.url origin, `Authorization` and `X-Odoo-Database` headers), NOT from the odooUrl env var. Credentials therefore only ever go to the server the request itself targets, and it works for hand-written requests.
   - The transport is a typedef `Future<Object?> Function(OdooCall)`. The app adapter wraps OdooClient/ApiClient. The CLI adapter wraps CliSend. This is the one seam that keeps both builds on the same code.
   - Lookup: one batched `ir.model.data` search_read per run, `domain: [['module','=',m],['name','=',n]]` OR-ed, `fields: ['module','name','model','res_id']`. Group tokens so one request serves many tokens.
   - Session cache keyed (origin, db, xmlid) with a 10-minute TTL. It lives in a lazy singleton, so Send, Runner and snippets share it.

3. Discovery pass, so no field list can drift out of sync with the builder. Run `_specBuilder.build(request, recordingResolver)` once, where the top scope is a MapBase whose `operator[]` records any key starting `xmlid:` and returns '0'. This also catches tokens nested inside variable values, which a regex over the request fields would miss. If no tokens were recorded, skip everything (zero cost for non-Odoo users). Otherwise resolve, then build for real with the resolved map as the top scope. Add this to PrepareRequestUseCase behind a small injected collaborator, `OdooRefResolver?` (null in tests and the CLI default), the same pattern as the optional `_settings`.

4. Strictness. Send and the app runner are strict: any unresolved token throws InvalidRequestException (message such as "{{xmlid:base.zz}} not found on mycompany.odoo.com"). RequestBuilderViewModel._describeError already shows InvalidRequestException messages verbatim. Never send the literal token or a guessed id. Lookup transport failures must be wrapped in InvalidRequestException too, otherwise the UI says "Request timed out". The code-snippet use case is lenient: it leaves the token in place if the lookup fails, and it may do a read-only lookup because the user asked for the snippet. Add a `strictRefs` flag on PrepareRequestUseCase.

5. CLI/MCP. In WorkspaceRunner.runRequest, between build and send, run the same discovery and resolve with the CliSend-backed transport. Unresolved tokens set RequestOutcome.error. The existing "URL still contains {{...}}" check only covers the URL, so a body-token check is needed here too.

6. UI placement, no new dialog.
   - Colour: VariableScope.isDefined returns true for any well-formed `xmlid:` token, so it is not painted red.
   - Hover: VariableScope.describe is sync, but VariableTextFormField._show is already async. Add a `describeRef` that reads the cache or does one lookup using the active environment's odooUrl/odooApiKey from the scope's own variable map. Add `VariableSource.reference` to VariableInfo.
   - Explorer tab (odoo_explorer_tab.dart): after search_read, one extra batched ir.model.data lookup adds an "XML-ID" column to the existing DataTable; clicking a cell copies `{{xmlid:...}}`. Cells show "-" when the record has no XML-ID. A tiny OdooStudioViewModel.lookupXmlIds(model, ids) method uses the existing _call/_run machinery. A palette item in palette_items.dart opens Studio on the Explorer tab (initialTab already exists).
   - Connect tab: "Test connection" can also probe ir.model.data readability, to answer the ACL question below with a green or amber line.
   - DI: register OdooRefResolver as a lazy singleton next to OdooClient in injector.dart and pass it into PrepareRequestUseCase. There are 3 construction sites (SendRequestUseCase, GenerateCodeSnippetUseCase, direct), plus di_wiring_test.

7. Platforms. Pure ApiClient/Dio code, no dart:io in the resolver. Web works if the Odoo server allows CORS, same as every existing Odoo Studio call. Mobile is unaffected. The only dart:io is the existing CLI sender.

SCOPE CUTS I RECOMMEND (the pitch's M hides these)
- "Collection-level Make ids portable" scan: NOT M. It needs URL parsing to get the model, fields_get per model to get the field's relation, and then understanding Odoo value shapes: many2many commands like [[6,0,[ids]]], [[4,id]], [[0,0,{...}]], domain triples, and vals_list. After that comes a reverse lookup per id and a rewrite preview. That is a separate L (2-3 weeks) and the most error-prone part, since a wrong rewrite silently changes behaviour. Defer it.
- Name-lookup token `{{odoo:res.country?name=United States}}`: spaces, `?` and `=` fall outside the token pattern, and a name match is non-unique and language-dependent. Defer to v2 as `{{ref:model|name}}` with a "must match exactly one record, else error" rule (extra S-M). The same Explorer 'pick' flow can offer it for records with no XML-ID.
- The pitch's "autocomplete" does not exist: VariableTextFormField has no suggestion popup. Skip it; the Explorer click-to-copy replaces it.
- GraphqlExplorerViewModel and RealtimeViewModel call BuildVariableResolverUseCase directly, so tokens will not resolve there. Fine for Odoo (JSON-2 only).

CORRECTED NUMBERS
Core v1 = M. Add roughly 3-5 days for the v2 name token, and a separate L for the bulk rewrite.

REALISTIC VALUE (honest)
Only records created by data files have XML-IDs: countries, currencies, uom, groups, stages, payment terms, base.main_company, chart-of-accounts journals and taxes (company-prefixed like `account.1_...`, so portable only while company ids match). Customer-created partners, products and custom journals have none. For typical Odoo integration work that is closer to 4 uses per week at about 3 minutes, not 8. Still worth building for master-data-heavy flows. It also turns a silent wrong-record bug into a loud, early failure.

TESTS (the codebase already has the harness)
- Unit tests for the resolver with a fake transport: batching, cache, TTL, token-not-found, malformed token, non-int res_id.
- A test extending code_snippet_matches_send_test.
- WorkspaceRunner tests with a fake CliSend.
- A variable_resolver_test regression for the new `:` in the name class.
- A json_body_format test for the new placeholder.
- **Risks:** LEGAL / STORE / TOS: none material. It uses the documented public JSON-2 API with the user's own API key, read-only. No new OS permissions and nothing for the App Store or Play Store to review. JSON-2 availability depends on the customer's Odoo plan, which is already true of all of Odoo Studio. I could not re-verify Odoo ACL details this session (web search budget exhausted), so the ACL point below is unverified.

SECURITY
- Credential scope: this is the one thing that must be right. Resolve against the origin of the request being sent (spec.url) with that request's own Authorization header. Never take the target from a token's text or from some other env var. Then a poisoned Git-synced workspace.json can't make a lookup call a different host with the user's key. A malicious request URL already gets the key sent to it today, so no new exfiltration surface is added.
- Read-only by construction: the resolver hard-codes `ir.model.data` + `search_read` (and later `name_search`) and builds domains as JSON values (never string concatenation). Validate module and name with `^[\w.-]+$`. Require res_id to be an int. Nothing a token says can reach write, unlink or any other method.
- No silent fallbacks: unresolved, duplicate or wrong-model results must fail the send. (module, name) is unique in ir.model.data, so XML-IDs cannot be ambiguous; name lookups can, which is another reason to defer them.

CORRECTNESS RISKS
1. Odoo ACL on `ir.model.data` for a minimal API user is UNVERIFIED. If an integration user cannot read it, every token fails, and a name-lookup fallback cannot apply to XML-ID tokens (no model known). Mitigate with the Test connection probe and a clear error ("this API user cannot read external identifiers; use an admin key for lookups or paste ids"). Verify against a real Odoo 19 before committing.
2. Stale ids: cache TTL 10 minutes, cleared on environment switch. A recreated record could resolve to its old id inside the TTL window. Low impact. Expose "refresh" in the hover card if needed.
3. Pattern change blast radius: allowing `:` in `{{...}}` names makes strings like `{{foo:bar}}` (Handlebars-style text in raw bodies) count as variables and show red. Low, but it needs a test run over variable_resolver_test (29 uses) and the openapi_exporter regex users.
4. The discovery build runs the signers (JWT/AWS) once with dummy values. Harmless, but keep it side-effect free and don't log it.
5. History and Console: the lookups go through the logging ApiClient and will appear in the Console panel (good for transparency); check that Authorization is masked there. They do not go to History, because OdooClient does not record to it.
6. Behaviour change: a request with an unresolvable token now fails before the network. This is intended.

RELATED EXISTING BUG (not part of this candidate, found while reading)
ProductionDetector.changesData() keys on HTTP method, and JSON-2 uses POST for everything. So on an environment whose name looks like production, every Odoo read (search_read, fields_get, name_search) triggers the "this changes data" confirmation. This will annoy exactly the Odoo users this feature targets. A fix would classify JSON-2 calls by method name (search_read/read/search_count/fields_get/name_search/default_get = read) in ProductionGuard.checkSend. It is worth fixing before shipping any Odoo-heavy feature, but I did not flag it as a separate task.

EFFORT SUMMARY
Core (token + async resolver + CLI/MCP + hover + Explorer column + tests): M. Bulk "make portable" scan: L, defer. Name-lookup tokens: S-M, defer to v2.
- **Strongest objection:** The claimed 24 min/week (8 uses x 3 min) does not hold up. 1) The headline premise is wrong. Odoo's own forum thread (the candidate's first evidence link) says the public wrapper ir.model.data.check_object_reference(module, xml_id) is callable over RPC from v13 to v15+. It returns (model, id), and I could not confirm it still exists in Odoo 19. So XML-ID resolution is one existing call, not an unsolved gap. 2) A single lookup is already cheap in PostPilot. Odoo Studio ships a 'Search by name' name_search template per model, so one lookup is about 30-60 s, not 3 min. Most devs look an id up once per database and save it, so 8 lookups a week is too many for one dev. 3) Portability already has a workaround. A dev can define journal_sales_id or country_us_id as an environment variable per environment, or chain a name_search request with an extractor in the runner. Setting that up once costs about as much as the lookup the token would replace, and the dev only does it for the 3-6 ids that matter. 4) The cross-database chore is real but rare. It matters when a Git-shared workspace is promoted from dev to staging to prod, which is a few times a month, not weekly. PostPilot's prod lock and per-environment variables already reduce the silent-wrong-record risk. 5) Cost and risk are high for the gain. Hidden network calls before every send (async prepare_request, CLI, runner, cache invalidation), skewed timing breakdowns, name matches that may not be unique, and a minimal API user that may be unable to read ir.model.data. That is M effort for roughly 4 min/week. A much smaller version (S effort) would keep most of the value: a 'Pick record' button in Odoo Studio that runs name_search and inserts the id or saves it as an environment variable.
- **Platform notes:** JSON-2 HTTP only; web needs CORS on Odoo. Reading ir.model.data may need elevated rights for a minimal API user: fall back to name_search/code lookup and say so. Resolution must become an async step in prepare_request and the CLI. Name lookups must verify a unique match.

### 18. Lifecycle flow generator: chained create -> read -> update -> delete from OpenAPI or an Odoo model
- **Score 1.6** | effort M | ~4 min/week saved | persona all | lenses: pain-points, ai-agentic, data-flows | skeptic votes 2/3
- **Problem today:** 'Update from OpenAPI' and the 10 ready-made Odoo requests produce independent requests with hard-coded values (ids [1], body 'New record'). To test one resource's lifecycle the dev wires it by hand: pick the JSON path of the created id, name a variable, add an extractor, type {{id}} into the next three requests, add assertions and the delete step (12-30 min per resource, redone when the spec changes).
- **What PostPilot would do:** Folder menu 'Generate lifecycle flow'. Deterministic phase: pair producer (POST/create) with consumers (GET/PUT/PATCH/DELETE by id) the way RESTler/Schemathesis infer links, create the id extractor, replace ids with {{id}}, build sample bodies with dynamic variables, add status and response-schema assertions, and register the delete as cleanup. For Odoo it chains create/read/write/search_read/unlink (create returns a list of ids) using fields_get to find required fields and reuse an existing related record via search_read limit 1. Result is an ordered, editable 'Flows' folder with a preview before write; if the id field cannot be inferred it asks the dev to click it once. After 'Update from OpenAPI' only missing steps are added. Optional negative cases later.
- **Where it lives:** Collection/folder context menu, checkbox 'Also generate flows' in the OpenAPI import dialog, 'Chain them' option in the Odoo Explorer's ready-made requests step; palette command.
- **Competitors:** Confirmed: RESTler (github.com/microsoft/restler-fuzzer) infers producer-consumer dependencies from the OpenAPI definition. Schemathesis stateful testing (schemathesis.readthedocs.io/en/stable/guides/stateful-testing/) chains POST /users -> GET/DELETE /users/{userId} via OpenAPI links, discovers them from the schema, and learns more from Location headers (the page shows '4 inferred'). Both are fuzzers/CLI tools; they do not generate an editable GUI collection with extractors and cleanup, which is the gap here. Not verified: the Apidog docs URL (docs.apidog.com/project-544525/doc-599311) and the dev.to Postman Flow vs Apidog URL both returned HTTP 404, and the claimed 'Schemathesis v4.22.4 on 2026-07-08' is not on the fetched page. The web search budget was exhausted, so I could not check whether Apidog or Postman auto-generate chained scenarios. Replace those two URLs before showing the evidence. The Schemathesis and RESTler evidence is enough to justify the idea. Realism notes: the Odoo path is the strongest and most deterministic part, because create returns an id list and fields_get gives required fields and relations. The generic OpenAPI inference is heuristic, so the preview step is essential. 15 min x 1.5/week is plausible. Odoo devs would use it more often than backend devs, so persona could be narrowed to odoo/backend/qa.
- **Design in this codebase:** VERDICT: feasible on the current architecture with no rewrite. Effort is M, corrected to about 2-3 weeks. The original M holds only if "update adds only missing steps" and the AI self-fix loop are cut. The best first slice is Odoo-only, about S+ (4-6 days), because OdooModelInfo already parses required/readonly/stored/relation/selection and the explorer view-model already holds the loaded model.

WHAT ALREADY FITS (read in code)
- Extractors, assertions and the chaining mechanism exist. ExtractorEntity is jsonPath or header into an environment or global variable. The assertion type jsonSchema runs JsonSchemaTools.validate, which supports local $ref, allOf, anyOf, oneOf, pattern and format.
- JsonPathResolver handles a root-array path like "[0]" and "$". So Odoo create returning [42] can be extracted with the path "[0]".
- OpenApiParser already emits path params as {{petId}}, with '-' turned into '_'. A consumer URL therefore needs no rewriting: name the extractor key after the path param, namespaced (see risks).
- Raw bodies are resolved by plain string substitution (RequestSpecBuilder._buildBody). An unquoted [{{id}}] inside ids is valid after resolution.
- Persistence: createRequest + saveRequest + RequestScriptsRepository.save(RequestScriptsEntity(assertionsJson, extractorsJson)), encoded with ScriptsJsonCodec. BackupService already does exactly this. Git sync (local_collection_store_impl) and the CLI (WorkspaceRunner._runScripts) already carry scripts. Generated flows therefore sync via Git and run in CI with no extra work.
- The CLI keeps extracted values in memory (RunState.environment), so CI needs no active environment and has no ID persistence problem. It also already has --folder.
- UI kit and entry points: ToolDialog and ToolTabs. PaletteItems.tools() is a single list, so a palette command is one line.

PROPOSED STRUCTURE
- New feature folder lib/features/flow_generator/ (kept out of import_export, which is already 3.9k lines).
  - domain/services/
    - lifecycle_inferrer.dart (pure Dart). Input: a list of operations. Output: a FlowPlan, an ordered list of steps (name, method, url, headers, body, extractors, assertions, optional cleanup marker).
    - sample_body_builder.dart (constraint-aware).
    - response_schema_builder.dart (inlines the $ref closure into a self-contained schema with components, normalises nullable, drops readOnly and discriminator).
    - odoo_flow_builder.dart (uses OdooModelInfo and OdooJson2.draft/headers).
  - domain/usecases/apply_flow_plan_usecase.dart. Creates the folder and requests in order, then saves the scripts. It takes CollectionRepository, RequestRepository and RequestScriptsRepository directly. Do not widen ImportedRequest/ImportedCollectionWriter. Writing in creation order is what the app uses as run order: the app never assigns order_index (see sibling_order.dart), ties are broken by row id.
  - presentation/flow_generator_dialog.dart and its view-model. One ToolDialog: pick source (collection, spec, or Odoo model), a preview list of steps with MethodBadge (the same pattern as OpenApiRefreshDialog), then Apply.
- OpenAPI input, two tiers:
  1. v1: infer from the requests already in a collection (OpenApiRefreshPlanner.keyOf/normalizePath already normalise {id}, {{id}}, :id). This works for Postman and cURL imports too. Use saved ResponseExamples plus JsonSchemaTools.infer to find the id path and build response schemas.
  2. v1.5: spec-driven. Extend OpenApiParser with an additive operations index exposing operationId, tags, path params, the 2xx response schema and the request-body schema. These are currently discarded and the helpers are private, so this is the single largest change, about 250 lines. Pasted text or download is already supported by the refresh dialog. The generator must not rely on the spec being stored, because it is not. Extract the spec URL/paste field from the refresh dialog into a shared widget instead of copying about 100 lines.
- Odoo input: a "Create lifecycle flow" button on OdooExplorerTab, reusing _vm.info and chosenFields. It reuses the model already loaded in OdooStudioViewModel; fields_get can also be pasted for offline use.
  - Steps: required many2one -> search_read limit 1 on the relation, extract [0].id; create with vals_list; read with ids; write; verify read; unlink.
  - Sample values by type: selection takes the first value; skip readonly and computed (stored == false) fields.
  - Odoo create via JSON-2 returns a list of ids, so the extractor path is "[0]".
- Placement: palette command ("Generate test flow from collection / Odoo model"), the Explorer button, and one entry in the tag-folder menu (6 items today). Do NOT add to the collection menu, which already has about 18 entries. An optional checkbox in ImportOpenApiDialog calls the same use case after import.
- DI: register the use case and view-model in injector.dart. The test/di_wiring_test.dart pattern requires a matching entry.

CHANGES TO EXISTING CODE THE FEATURE NEEDS (small, but not in the original estimate)
1. Folder-scoped run in the GUI runner (S). CollectionRunnerDialog/CollectionRunnerViewModel/CollectionRunnerService.requestsIn work on a whole collection (all requests, in creation order). A flow folder would otherwise run together with every plain import, including the hand-made DELETE with an unresolved id. Either add an optional folderId (with descendants) or generate flows into their own collection. The CLI already supports --folder.
2. Production guard in the CLI (S). ProductionGuard lives only in the runner dialog and the request page. "postpilot run --env production" and the MCP server would execute create + delete flows with no prompt. ProductionDetector is pure Dart and reusable: refuse by default and add --allow-prod.
3. Cleanup on failure. With the default stopOnFailure=false the final delete still runs. With "stop on failure" or --bail it is skipped and test records are orphaned. Document this, and optionally mark cleanup steps (the tag "cleanup" in BackupNotes needs no schema change).
4. Active environment. The GUI extractor writes to the active environment and fails with "No active environment" otherwise. The generator dialog must check for one first, or default to the global scope. An optional later hardening is an in-memory "run" scope. It is not needed for v1.

WEB, DESKTOP AND MOBILE
Generation is pure Dart on the existing parser, yaml, drift and OdooModelInfo, with no dart:io and no conditional imports needed. compute() is a synchronous fallback on web. Running a flow uses the normal ApiClient, so it has the same CORS limits as any send on web. Odoo needs CORS enabled on the server, and OdooStudioViewModel._describe already explains this to the user. The CLI part is dart:io only, as it is today.

TESTS TO ADD
- Inference: nested paths, plural/singular, no-id response, put vs patch.
- Body builder: constraints.
- Schema inlining with cyclic $ref.
- Odoo builder: required many2one, selection, readonly fields skipped.
- Apply use case, using the in-memory drift database pattern already in test/.
- di_wiring entry.
- Runner folder scope.
- CLI prod refusal.
- **Risks:** TECHNICAL RISKS (ordered by severity)
1. Variable collision causing a destructive delete (HIGH, easy to prevent). If the extractor key is the bare path param ("petId", "id") and the dev already has an environment or global variable of the same name pointing at a real record, a failed create makes the update and delete steps hit that real record. Always namespace keys (flow_pet_id, flow_res_partner_id). Never reuse generic names like id.
2. Valid-body generation is the weakest link. The existing parser (_stringExample, 0, true) ignores enum, min/max length, pattern and format constraints, so a create against a strict API returns 400/422 and the flow fails at step 1. Mitigation: prefer example/default/enum-first, add minimum/length handling, always show a preview and keep the bodies editable. Say honestly in the UI that this is "a starting point that works on permissive APIs and needs one edit on strict ones". Do not claim zero-touch.
3. Dynamic variables are redrawn on every occurrence (DynamicVariables doc: "two occurrences in one request differ"). A value created with {{$randomEmail}} cannot be asserted in a later read with the same token. Use a literal marker for fields that are asserted. Use dynamic variables only for uniqueness (email, timestamp suffix) and do not assert them. Alternatively extract the field from the create response (REST only; Odoo create returns just ids).
4. Run order depends on creation order. There is no reorder UI and order_index is never assigned. Steps cannot be inserted into the middle later, so the proposal's "after Update from OpenAPI only missing steps are added" is not implementable without a new RequestRepository.setOrder. Drop it for v1. Offer "regenerate as a new flow folder" instead.
5. Odoo side effects on a real database. Creating and deleting records burns sequences, which matters for gapless invoice numbering and for sale.order names. Creating res.users sends invitation emails, and mail.* and automation rules fire. Odoo's unlink fails on posted or confirmed documents, leaving orphans. Ship a model denylist or warning (account.move, res.users, mail.*, ir.*, res.company, stock.*, payment.*) and a banner: "run against a test database". Mark records with a "[PostPilot flow]" name prefix so leftovers can be found.
6. Inference is heuristic. Path nesting, id naming and responses without a schema (many specs have none) mean the id path may not be found. The proposal's "click it once" would need a new response picker. Cheaper v1: a prefilled text field for the JSON path (id, data.id, _id) and an unresolved-step warning in the preview. Limit v1 to depth 1-2 resource chains.
7. Response-schema assertions can fail on specs that drift from reality, and this will look like a failure of the generator. OpenAPI 3.0 nullable, readOnly, discriminator and Swagger 2 file types are not JSON Schema and must be normalised. Offer a "generate schema assertions" toggle, off by default or clearly labelled.
8. Cross-version assertion decoding: ScriptsJsonCodec decodes an unknown assertion type as statusIn2xx (orElse). Do not add a new AssertionType (for example status-4xx) for the "read after delete" check without a migration plan, or an older client synced through Git will silently flip the meaning. Use statusEquals with the code the spec declares, or omit that step. I could not verify what HTTP status Odoo 19 JSON-2 returns for a missing record: the odoo.com documentation page returned only navigation content to the fetch tool and the web-search budget for this session was exhausted. The code's own odoo_error_parser maps MissingError by exception name, not by status, so do not hard-code a status for Odoo.
9. Extractors write to a persistent environment, so every run leaves flow_* values in it. They are visible in environment exports and Git workspaces, which is noise but not harmful. An in-memory "run" scope would fix it and is optional.
10. Effort claims: the 1.5 uses/week x 15 min estimate is optimistic. A realistic figure is about 10-12 min x 0.5-1 per week for backend/QA, and Odoo developers only wire a flow when they add a custom model. Value is highest for QA onboarding a new API and for Odoo custom modules.

LEGAL / STORE / SECURITY
- Legal and ToS: no new exposure found. The feature generates local requests from a spec or a model. It copies no code from RESTler (MIT) or Schemathesis; only the producer-consumer idea is reused, and ideas are not protected. Odoo's API terms were not verified online (see risk 8). Practically, creating records on a customer's production Odoo is an operational risk, not a legal one (see risk 5).
- Store policy: the feature adds no new capability class. It sends requests to user-chosen servers, as the app does today. No permissions, no downloaded executable code, no credentials involved. Generation needs no network access beyond the existing spec download.
- Security: it automates write and delete calls. Keep the existing safeguards. GUI prod lock already covers the runner (checkRun counts write methods). Add the CLI and MCP prod refusal (change 2). Never auto-run on generation: generate, preview and save only, and let the user press Run. Mask secrets as the runner already does (SecretMasker). Do not store the Odoo API key in generated requests (the existing {{odooApiKey}} variable pattern is already correct).
- **Strongest objection:** The saving is overstated in size and frequency, and the work is bigger than the M estimate.

1. The manual baseline is lower than the candidate's 12-30 min. PostPilot already has extractors, the {{var}} resolver, dynamic variables (core/utils/dynamic_variables.dart) and a runner that chains forward. Wiring one resource by hand is 1 extractor (e.g. `$[0]` -> recordId for Odoo create) plus swapping `[1]` or the path id for {{recordId}} in 3 requests. That is about 4-8 min. The extra minutes the candidate counts are assertions and valid bodies, and a deterministic generator will not get those right.

2. Skeleton bodies are rejected by real servers. openapi_parser.dart already builds schema skeletons with placeholder values, so the 'sample bodies' half exists today. The remaining gap is business validity: unique fields, enums, FK references, state rules. RESTler and Schemathesis can treat 4xx as acceptable because they are fuzzers. A functional lifecycle test cannot, so the dev edits each generated body anyway. For Odoo, fields_get plus default_get plus a search_read limit-1 FK works for simple models. It is brittle for res.partner-style or accounting and sale models with constraints and onchange defaults. Realistic net saving is about 3-6 min per resource, not 15.

3. It is not a weekly habit. A lifecycle flow is built once per resource or spec, then reused. An Odoo dev touches a few models over a project. A backend dev who owns the API normally keeps integration tests in code (pytest, Jest, RestAssured), where they run in CI. They also have `schemathesis run <spec>` as a one-liner. A Flutter dev mostly consumes the API and PostPilot's Dart Studio API-layer generator is already their automation. Realistic usage is about 0.5-1 per week, bursty, not 1.5 steady.

4. The 'register delete as cleanup' step needs a feature that does not exist. CollectionRunnerService is a flat loop over the collection's requests, with no teardown phase. `stopOnFailure` returns early, so a failed update leaves the created record behind. Without a cleanup or finally step, a generated flow leaves junk rows in staging or Odoo databases whenever it fails mid-chain. Adding that step plus preview/merge UI, producer-consumer inference, incremental merge with openapi_refresh_planner and Odoo required-field logic makes this L, not M.

5. The evidence supports the idea in fuzzing and QA tooling (RESTler, Schemathesis, Apidog), used mostly in CI by security and QA teams. It does not show demand among developers who use an interactive client. I could not run extra demand searches because the WebSearch budget was exhausted, so this rests on code reading and judgment.

What is worth keeping is a tiny slice, not this feature. odoo_json2.dart `templatesFor()` hard-codes `ids: [1]`, so the generated 'Delete' request is a foot-gun that unlinks record 1, and 'Read' and 'Update' hit an id that may not exist. Making create extract the new id into {{recordId}} and having read/write/unlink use it is S (about a day). It removes a real error-prone chore. Propose that as a small improvement, not as the 'lifecycle flow generator'.
- **Platform notes:** Pure Dart on top of openapi_parser and openapi_refresh_planner; works offline and on all platforms. Dependency inference is heuristic (path nesting, *_id naming) so always preview. An AI tool-use loop that runs and self-fixes the collection is a later L-size add-on, not part of this estimate. Writes run only against non-production environments.

### 19. Compare across environments: same request or folder on two targets, diff only what matters
- **Score 1.6** | effort M | ~4 min/week saved | persona all | lenses: backend-daily, observability-debug | skeptic votes 2/3
- **Problem today:** 'Works on staging, fails on prod' and 'did my refactor change the output' both end with: switch environment, send, copy the body, switch, send, paste both into a diff site and hand-filter ids/timestamps (about 6 min each; 20 min for a 10-request folder). PostPilot's Compare tab only compares against the previous response of the same request in the same environment. Odoo 17/18 to 19 migrations repeat this per migrated call (old RPC vs JSON-2).
- **What PostPilot would do:** Right-click a request, folder or collection -> 'Compare across environments...'. Pick env A and B (or old RPC call vs its JSON-2 conversion); PostPilot sends both (GET/HEAD only by default, writes need opt-in and go through the PROD lock) and shows one table: status A vs B, latency A vs B, body-diff summary (keys added/removed/type changed) with the existing 'ignore volatile' rules. Clicking a row opens the existing JSON diff; Export table as Markdown for a PR.
