- **Where it lives:** Compare tab source dropdown ('Compare with another environment') for a single request; folder/collection context menu and palette command for the report; CLI 'postpilot diff --env-a staging --env-b prod'.
- **Partly exists already:** The single-request slice is largely there already, so the claim "only compares against the previous response in the same environment" is wrong. ResponseHistory (lib/features/response_tools/domain/services/response_history.dart) is keyed by requestId only, and ResponseViewer._remember (lib/features/request_builder/presentation/widgets/response_viewer.dart:119) records every response. ResponseToolsViewModel.compareSources (response_tools_view_model.dart:87-104) offers "Previous response" and saved examples. If you send on env A, switch env, send on env B and open Compare, you already get a structural diff via JsonDiff.compare (json_diff.dart) with an "Ignore volatile fields" chip (compare_tab.dart:20, which has no id keys). Missing: no environment label on a source, no "send on the other env for me", no status/latency columns. A multi-request or folder diff does not exist: collection_runner_dialog.dart and cli_main.dart run one environment only, and cli_main.dart accepts only run, list and mcp (no diff subcommand). The MCP server (mcp_server.dart) has run_request with an environment argument but no diff tool. There is no Markdown export of a comparison and no shape-only diff mode (JsonDiff matches lists by index and compares values, so staging vs prod data would be noisy). odoo_convert_tab.dart only converts RPC to JSON-2 text and never executes both legs. Verdict: pass, but narrow the pitch. The real new value is the folder/collection cross-env report, `postpilot diff`, a shape-only mode, and the Markdown table. The "one request, two envs" slice saves about 1-2 clicks, not 8 min, because the previous-response compare already covers it. Realistic estimate: roughly 3 min/use for single requests and 10-15 min/use for a folder report.
- **Competitors:** Evidence checked. Confirmed: Speedscale compares request/response pairs across datasets, with staging vs production use cases (docs.speedscale.com/guides/compare-rrpairs). momus-diff (docs.rs/momus-diff) runs one test plan on two environments and reports body, status and header differences, which is the closest direct analogue of the proposed CLI/report. Weak: the Diffy link is a thehackernews.com search-label page for a 2015 article about Twitter's Diffy, so it is not 2025-2026 evidence; cite github.com/opendiffy/diffy instead if needed. Not verified: the Odoo 20.0 docs page (the fetch was truncated, so the deprecation claim was not confirmed). Web search quota was exhausted, so I could not check whether Postman, Bruno or Insomnia ship an environment-compare feature.
- **Design in this codebase:** VERDICT: feasible without a rewrite, but the candidate as written has 4 flaws to fix. Effort: slice 1 (one request, Compare tab) is S+ (about 4-5 days, because of the plumbing below). The folder/collection report plus CLI 'diff' brings the total to M (about 2 weeks).

CODEBASE FACTS CHECKED
- The environment is a global 'active' flag. BuildVariableResolverUseCase reads only EnvironmentRepository.getActiveVariables(). Nothing can send 'as env B' today.
- SendRequestUseCase -> PrepareRequestUseCase is the single path out of the app. It already takes dataVariables, the top-priority scope.
- EnvironmentRepository.watchVariables(envId) already exists, so no interface change or fake breakage is needed.
- RunRequestScriptsUseCase writes extractor values into the ACTIVE environment in the DB.
- ProductionGuard only checks the ACTIVE environment's name.
- Compare tab: sources are static and synchronous (CompareSource(label, body)). _volatile is a private const inside compare_tab.dart. The diff list is inline in that widget.
- JsonDiff is a value diff, and lists are compared by index.
- JsonDiff, JsonPaths, JsonSchemaTools and ResponseReader are pure Dart. The CLI's WorkspaceRunner already has per-run in-memory RunState with environment selection by name.

CORRECTIONS TO THE CANDIDATE
1. 'GET/HEAD only by default' would exclude every Odoo call. Odoo JSON-2 is POST /json/2/<model>/<method> for everything, including search_read. The existing prod lock also treats every Odoo POST as a write (ProductionDetector.changesData). Add a small read-only classifier, 'SendSafety.isReadOnly(method, url, body)':
   - GET, HEAD, OPTIONS;
   - Odoo JSON-2 path with a method in {search_read, search, search_count, read, name_search, fields_get, default_get, read_group, formatted_read_group, web_search_read, web_read, exists};
   - GraphQL body that is a 'query' and not a 'mutation'.
   Put it in lib/features/safety/domain/services. It also fixes the existing false 'writes to prod' prompts on Odoo reads.
2. Different environments hold different DATA, so a value diff (JsonDiff.compare) will be almost all noise. Add a 'Structure only' mode that diffs shapes (key sets, types, nullability). Cheapest route: JsonDiff.compare(JsonSchemaTools.infer([a]), JsonSchemaTools.infer([b])), or a roughly 60-line JsonShape walker. 'Type changed' is derived from runtimeType, because JsonChangeKind has no such value. Default to Structure for cross-environment comparisons, with a value-diff toggle.
3. The Odoo 'old RPC vs JSON-2' leg is not an environment compare. JSON-2 does not exist on 17/18, so it is two DIFFERENT requests. It also needs JSON-RPC envelope unwrapping ({"result": ...}). PostPilot has no XML-RPC sender or XML parser, so the XML-RPC leg is a separate L feature. Cut both from this candidate. The Odoo use that works now is two Odoo 19 databases through the {{odooUrl}}/{{odooDb}}/{{odooApiKey}} environments. Mark 'old RPC vs JSON-2' as a later 'compare two requests' mode.
4. Chained variables: do NOT run extractors through RunRequestScriptsUseCase, because that would overwrite the user's active environment. Keep an in-memory overlay map per side and pass it as dataVariables. Fill it with the pure ExtractorValueResolver, the same way WorkspaceRunner._runScripts does. Assertions are not needed here.

DESIGN (new folder lib/features/env_compare/)
- Plumbing, about 1 day:
  - Add optional 'int? environmentId' to BuildVariableResolverUseCase, PrepareRequestUseCase and SendRequestUseCase. When set, use watchVariables(id).first instead of getActiveVariables.
  - Add 'recordHistory: true' to SendRequestUseCase so two sends do not double-log History.
  - Add 'ProductionGuard.checkSendTo(envName, method, requestName)'. Never honour 'silence for session' for cross-environment writes.
  - Never flip the active environment: it is racy, flashes the PROD pill and persists.
- domain/: EnvCompareService, plus ShapeDiff or JsonShape, SendSafety use, EnvCompareReport (Markdown/JSON) and VolatileFields (moved out of compare_tab). EnvCompareService.run(requests, envA, envB, {allowWrites}) streams one row per request. Each row has status A/B, ms A/B, size A/B, diff summary counts and bodies kept in memory only. Pure Dart so the CLI can reuse it.
  - Sends are interleaved A,B per request with a small delay.
  - Stop on 429 or on cancel.
  - Writes are skipped unless allowWrites is on, both targets are non-prod, and there is an explicit confirm.
- presentation/: EnvCompareDialog built on ToolDialog, with a ChangeNotifier view model registered in the injector like CollectionRunnerViewModel. It has two env dropdowns, a scope label, a 'include writes' checkbox, and a results table. On width < 600 the table becomes a card list. Clicking a row opens the existing diff view.
- Compare tab (slice 1): extract the inline diff list into a shared JsonDiffView widget. Add a lazy async source 'Send to <env>…' (loading state in the dropdown) and a 'Structure only' chip beside 'Ignore volatile fields'. ResponseToolsViewModel gets the environment list plus a call to the new service. The Response tools dialog does not know which environment produced the current response, so side A is labelled 'Current response' and side B 'Sent to <env> now'. The full-report dialog re-sends both sides, so its A and B are always known. No new tab is added, which keeps the 9-tab dialog unchanged.
- Entry points: collection menu and folder menu item 'Compare across environments…', which needs descendant folder ids (the sidebar's folder menu exists, and CollectionLoader gives the folders). Palette item 'env.compare' in PaletteItems.tools(), where the dialog shows a collection picker when no collection is given.
- CLI: 'postpilot diff <workspace.json> --env-a X --env-b Y [--collection --folder --structure --ignore k1,k2 --report console|json|markdown]'. It uses two RunStates and WorkspaceRunner.runRequest, which already returns responseBody and status. Exit code 1 on a structural or status difference, so it works as a CI gate. Optionally one MCP tool 'compare_environments'.
- Export: Markdown table with status, latency, size and structural diffs by default.

PLATFORM NOTES
- Domain code has no dart:io, so web and mobile are fine. On web, CORS must allow the PostPilot origin on BOTH targets, and the browser owns cookies. The CLI uses dart:io, separately.
- Markdown export uses the existing conditional-import downloadFile or the clipboard.
- On phones, ToolDialog goes full-screen.
- **Risks:** 1. Safety (highest risk): sending the same request to production. Reads are acceptable. Writes need these controls:
   - skipped by default;
   - an opt-in that is refused when either target is prod-named, unless the full production confirm dialog passes;
   - no session silencing.
   The existing ProductionGuard checks only the active environment and honours the user pref 'confirmProductionWrites=false', so the cross-environment path needs its own guard method and must not reuse the silence switch. The read-only classifier must fail closed. An unknown POST counts as a write. GET endpoints with side effects (tracking, 'mark as read') still exist, so the dialog should show the list of requests it will send before running.
2. Privacy: prod response bodies may hold PII or secrets. The Markdown and JSON exports and any PR paste must be structure-only by default (paths, types, status, ms, size), with values opt-in. URLs must go through SecretMasker.maskUrl. Bodies stay in memory only, capped by the existing maxResponseSize setting, and are dropped when the dialog closes (the CollectionRunnerService '_withoutBody' pattern).
3. Correctness traps:
   - OAuth2/Digest: the token is stored on the request or collection, not per environment, so side B may 401. Show this as a note rather than a failure of the compare.
   - Shared CookieJar: on the same host (multi-database Odoo, differing only by the X-Odoo-Database header), a cookie from side A could reach side B. JSON-2 uses a bearer key so this is low risk, but state it in the dialog.
   - Folder runs that depend on extractor output will not have those variables on side B unless the overlay is filled, so run the chain in order.
   - Lists compared by index give false changes when ordering differs. The structure-only mode avoids this.
4. Plumbing risk: adding environmentId and recordHistory parameters touches 3 use cases and their tests (send_request_usecase_test, collection_runner_test, production_guard_test, di_wiring_test). Parameters are optional with defaults, so this is a mechanical fix, not a rewrite. Do not change the EnvironmentRepository interface, so the fakes keep compiling.
5. Rate limits and load: sequential sends with a delay, stop on 429, and respect the per-request timeout. Hitting prod twice per request is acceptable for reads only.
6. Legal and store policy: none found. This sends the user's own requests to the user's own endpoints, so it adds no new ToS or store exposure. The competitors named (Diffy, Speedscale) are separate products, with no code or branding reuse. The candidate's Hacker News 'search/label' evidence URL is a tag listing, not a documentation page, so cite the Diffy GitHub README instead.
7. Value check: 8 min x 3 uses per week is on the high side for one developer. 5-6 min x 2-3 per week is more honest. It is still a good candidate, mainly through the CLI gate and the shape diff.
- **Strongest objection:** The core premise is false. The candidate says Compare only diffs against the previous response "in the same environment". In the code it does not. ResponseHistory (response_history.dart) is a Map<int, List<ApiResponseEntity>> keyed by requestId only. CompareTab reads vm.compareSources, which takes the last 8 responses of that request whatever environment they came from. The existing flow for one request is therefore: send on staging, switch env, send on prod, open Compare, tick "Ignore volatile fields". That is about 20-30 seconds and 4-5 clicks, not "6 minutes of copying into a diff site". The single-request slice is roughly 80% built. What is missing is a label showing which environment each response came from, plus a "send in other env" shortcut. That is an hours-sized tweak to the Compare tab, not a headline feature.

The folder/collection report is the only genuinely new part. It is still weak for three reasons.

1. Frequency is overstated. Cross-environment parity checks happen at deploys, incidents and release verification, about once a week or less for a typical dev, not 3 times a week. Many teams have no prod read access from the API client, or no safe way to call prod, so the dev-to-prod leg often cannot be run at all.

2. The body diff is mostly noise. Staging and prod hold different data. JsonDiff matches list items by position and reports every differing leaf as 'changed'. A GET /users on both targets would light up almost every row. The useful output is only the structural part: keys added/removed, type changed, status, latency. That part is not built. It needs a new schema-level diff mode, not just a wrapper around JsonDiff.compare. Odoo.sh is the exception, since staging is a copy of prod data. That is a narrow niche.

3. Regression detection already has a home. The assertions, JSON Schema checks, collection runner and `postpilot run --report junit` already tell a team whether staging and prod both pass the same contract, and this runs in CI. The diff report covers only exploratory unknown-unknowns.

The Odoo 17/18 to 19 argument is one-off migration work, not weekly. Comparing old RPC with JSON-2 is also a different feature in disguise. It needs an RPC executor, and the two response envelopes differ (JSON-RPC wraps in result, JSON-2 does not). It is blocked on web for XML-RPC, as the candidate admits.

The evidence is thin. The Diffy link is a thehackernews.com label-search page, not a source. Diffy, Speedscale and momus-diff are CI or traffic-replay tools for platform teams. None of them shows that individual developers want this inside an API client. I could not run new web searches (the session search budget was exhausted), so I judged mainly from the code.

Honest numbers. Single request: about 0.25 minute saved per use after the existing flow, 1-2 uses a week, so about 0.5 minute. Folder report: about 4 minutes saved per use (not 20) at about 0.5-1 uses a week, so about 2-4 minutes. Total is about 4 minutes a week, well under the 10-minute bar, and it removes no painful or error-prone chore.

Salvage as a small task instead of a feature. Add the environment name to the Compare source labels (for example 'Previous response · staging · 200'). Add a 'Send in <other env> and compare' button in the Compare tab. Add a structural-only mode (ignore values, show only keys and types). Defer the folder report and the CLI diff until real demand shows up.
- **Platform notes:** Pure Dart, all platforms (CORS applies to both targets on web). First slice (one request, two environments) is S; folder report is the follow-up. Sequential sends with a small delay; for writes require both targets to be non-prod plus explicit confirm. XML-RPC leg of the Odoo comparison is blocked by CORS on web.

### 20. Odoo schema and environment diff: staging vs prod, Odoo 18 vs 19, with impact on saved requests
- **Score 1.6** | effort M | ~4 min/week saved | persona odoo | lenses: odoo-daily, docs-collab | skeptic votes 2/3
- **Problem today:** After an Odoo upgrade or module release (18 to 19 renamed groups_id, tax_id, product_uom...) the dev finds broken JSON-2 calls one by one by sending each saved request. 'Works on staging, fails on prod' is usually different module versions, a custom x_ field, a system parameter or a disabled cron; the dev clicks through Apps, Technical > Fields and Parameters in two windows or exports ir.model.fields to CSV and diffs them. Odoo's Database Diff Tool is a paid app installed inside the database.
- **What PostPilot would do:** In Odoo Studio, 'Save schema snapshot' stores fields_get for every model the collection's /json/2/ requests use (schema only, shared via Git). 'Compare' picks two existing PostPilot environments (or a snapshot) and checks: fields of chosen models (added, removed, renamed-looking, type changed, newly required, selection values), installed modules and versions, custom ir.model.fields, ir.config_parameter (masked), ir.cron, companies. Results name exactly which saved requests (URL, body keys, domains) and generated Dart models use a changed field, with 'Rename field in N requests'. Sections the key cannot read show 'no access' instead of aborting; Copy as Markdown for the PR.
- **Where it lives:** New 'Schema drift / Compare' tab in Odoo Studio, palette command, and CLI 'postpilot odoo diff --a staging --b prod --checks modules,fields:sale.order' for a pre-release CI step.
- **Competitors:** Verified by fetching the pages. (1) Odoo Database Diff Tool, https://apps.odoo.com/apps/modules/18.0/database_diff_tool : a PAID third-party Odoo 18 app, installed inside a database. It compares modules (installation state), models, fields (type, relation, attributes), menus and views, and writes a stored read-only report. It needs credentials for both sides and reaches the remote database over XML-RPC, which is being deprecated in 19 and removed later. It has no knowledge of API clients, saved requests or Dart models, so there is no JSON-2 or client-side competitor. (2) Manual workflow, https://odoodataflow.readthedocs.io/en/latest/guides/field_mapping_workflow.html : confirmed, the guide exports ir.model.fields from source and destination with 'fluvo export' and diffs the two CSVs to find added, removed and renamed fields. (3) 18-to-19 renames, https://www.cybrosys.com/blog/how-to-migrate-custom-modules-from-odoo-18-to-odoo-19 : confirmed, it lists groups_id to group_ids, tax_id to tax_ids and product_uom to product_uom_id (plus factor to relative_factor and the UoM category change). (4) https://www.odoo.com/documentation/20.0/developer/reference/external_rpc_api.html : the page shows a danger notice that XML-RPC and JSON-RPC are being phased out and links to the External JSON-2 API, but the fetch truncated the notice, so the exact 'removed in Odoo 20' wording was NOT confirmed. The web search budget was exhausted (200/200), so no further competitor search was possible, and I found no Postman, Bruno, Insomnia or Hoppscotch feature for Odoo schema diffing. That is absence of evidence only, not proof. Honest scoring note: 15 min per use x 1.5 uses/week is plausible but at the upper end, since upgrades and release checks are bursty (many uses around an upgrade, near zero otherwise). Per-developer steady state is closer to 10 min x 1/week, and the value is highest in a CI pre-release check."
- **Design in this codebase:** VERDICT: feasible with the existing architecture, no rewrite. It must be descoped in four places (below). Corrected effort is M (about 2-3 weeks including CLI and rename), not more, only if Git-native snapshot sync is dropped.

WHAT EXISTS AND CAN BE REUSED (read in lib/):
- OdooClient (features/odoo/data/odoo_client.dart) takes an OdooConnection and an OdooCall and returns OdooResult, already with the Odoo error parser and an 8 MB cap. It depends only on ApiClient and ApiHttpResponse, both Flutter-free (api_http_response.dart says the CLI build uses it).
- OdooModelInfo.parse (odoo_model_info.dart) already reads fields_get into type/required/relation/selection/readonly/store, which is exactly what a differ needs. OdooJson2, OdooVars (odooUrl/odooDb/odooApiKey), OdooDomain.fromList (walks DomainLeaf.field) and OdooErrorParser are pure Dart.
- CollectionLoader.loadAll() returns every saved request with url and raw body. RequestRepository.saveRequest(copyWith) can rewrite one.
- ToolDialog/ToolTabs/ToolSection/InfoBanner/BusyLabel, a factory view model registered in core/di/injector.dart (_registerDevTools), palette items with OdooStudioDialog.show(initialTab: N).
- CLI is pure Dart (bin/postpilot.dart -> features/cli/cli_main.dart) and already reads workspace.json environments, merging per-environment secrets from workspace.local.json via SecretSplitter (key env/<name>/odooApiKey).

DESIGN (all in lib/features/odoo, no new package; crypto is already a dependency):
1. domain/entities/odoo_schema_snapshot.dart: models->OdooModelInfo, modules [{name, version, state}], custom ir.model.fields, cron [{name, active, interval}], companies, param keys plus a salted SHA-256 prefix of the value (never the value). JSON codec with format id 'postpilot-odoo-schema' v1, no URL, no key, no database name.
2. domain/services/odoo_schema_differ.dart (pure): per-field added/removed/type changed/newly required/selection values added or removed/relation changed/stored changed; rename candidates only by a deterministic rule (removed and added field with the same type, relation and label); modules by version/state; params by key and hash; cron; companies. Every section is Result{ok | noAccess(reason) | error}.
3. domain/services/odoo_request_field_scanner.dart (pure): input is a neutral List<ScanRequest{collection, folder, name, url, bodyText}> so the app (from CollectionLoader) and the CLI (from BackupSnapshot) feed the same code. It takes the model and method from /json/2/<model>/<method> and field usages from body.fields, domain leaves (first segment of a dotted path), vals/vals_list keys, order, and context default_*. It must tolerate {{var}} in the body (JSON-parse first, quoted-token regex fallback) and lists requests with an unresolved model as 'unknown'.
4. domain/usecases/take_odoo_snapshot_usecase.dart: a read-only allowlist of calls (fields_get, search_read, search_count, context_get), concurrency of 4, per-section try/catch so ir.module.module, ir.config_parameter and ir.cron 403s become 'no access'. The server version comes from the base module's latest_version.
5. domain/usecases/compare_odoo_usecase.dart: snapshots A and B, then diff, then impact (diff x scanner). Plus an optional rename_field_in_requests_usecase.dart that shows a preview of every request body change and only then writes through RequestRepository.
6. presentation/view_models/odoo_compare_view_model.dart (a factory in _registerDevTools: OdooClient, EnvironmentRepository, CollectionLoader, RequestRepository) with progress, cancel and partial results. Do NOT reuse OdooStudioViewModel: its single _run/isBusy cannot do progress or partial sections.
7. presentation/widgets/odoo_compare_tab.dart as a 6th ToolTab 'Compare' (icon difference/compare_arrows) in odoo_studio_dialog.dart (the TabBar is already scrollable). Pick A and B from two environments or 'pasted snapshot', models default to those used by the open collection's requests, a results list grouped by Breaking / Changed / Info with impacted requests under each row, then Copy as Markdown and Save snapshot. Palette item 'odoo.compare' in palette_items.dart (initialTab: 5). The request/response views stay untouched.
8. CLI: features/cli/odoo_diff_command.dart plus a ~25 line CliApiClient implements ApiClient over the existing CliSend/sendWithDartIo, so OdooClient is reused unchanged. In cli_main.dart add 'odoo' to the command set (today {'run','list','mcp'}) and the options --a --b --checks --fail-on breaking|any --format md|json. Exit codes follow the existing 0/1/2.

CORRECTIONS TO THE CANDIDATE:
(a) 'Odoo 18 vs 19' is not possible live through the current client. OdooClient only speaks JSON-2 (/json/2/...), which the app's own Connect tab says exists on Odoo 19+ (I could not fetch the Odoo doc body to re-confirm; web search budget was exhausted). Odoo 18 has only /jsonrpc and /xmlrpc/2, which need a login, a uid and execute_kw, and the environment holds no login (only url, db, apiKey). So v1 = 19 vs 19 (staging vs prod, branch DBs, minor upgrades) plus an imported or pasted snapshot for the older side. A legacy-transport adapter (JSON-RPC execute_kw + a new odooLogin env variable) is a clean optional v2 (+4-5 days) that reuses what odoo_rpc_converter already knows.
(b) 'generated Dart models use a changed field' cannot be traced: OdooDartGenerator.generate() returns GeneratedFile objects shown in the dialog and nothing is persisted. Replace it with 'models affected: Regenerate Dart class' (open the Dart tab on that model). The honest impact list is saved requests only.
(c) 'Shared via Git' cannot hook into the sync: RepoLayout maps only collection.json, _folder.json and *.request.json, and a new doc kind would touch SyncEngine, the three-way merger and the secret masking (L, not worth it). Make the snapshot a plain JSON file that the team commits itself, and the CLI takes --a path/to/snapshot.json. There is no file_picker dependency in pubspec (imports here are paste-based), so in the app: Copy plus the existing downloadFile() for export and a paste dialog for import (the same pattern as the Explorer's 'Paste a fields_get response').
(d) Do not add a method to EnvironmentRepository: 9 test fakes (test/*.dart plus in_memory_import_export_fakes.dart) implement it, so a new abstract member breaks them all. Read other environments with watchAll().first and watchVariables(id).first, which are already in the interface.

PLATFORMS: desktop/mobile work through DioApiClient/dart:io. Web works only if BOTH Odoo servers allow CORS (the Authorization header forces a preflight); reuse OdooStudioViewModel._describe's existing message and point to the CLI/desktop. No dart:io import is needed in the feature (downloadFile already uses conditional imports); the CLI part lives under features/cli, which is already dart:io-only.

TESTS: pure-Dart units for the differ, the scanner (including {{var}} bodies, dotted paths, vals_list) and the Markdown report, a fake-ApiClient test of the snapshot use case (403 -> 'no access'), the CLI via the existing runCli(sender: ...) seam, and a line in test/di_wiring_test.dart.
- **Risks:** LEGAL/ToS/STORE: low. It only calls the customer's own Odoo servers with the customer's own API keys, read-only; no Odoo code or data model is copied; the paid 'Database Diff Tool' is a separate third-party app, so there is no IP overlap. No store-policy issue (no downloaded or executed code, no new permission). The word 'Odoo' is already used in the app.

SECURITY (the real risks, all mitigable):
1. A production key is used from a dev machine. Enforce a hard read-only allowlist of methods in the use case (fields_get, search_read, search_count, context_get), never route through the request sender, and test it. ProductionGuard guards the request sender, not OdooClient, which is fine for reads, but show the same red PROD pill on the compare header when side A or B is a prod-named environment.
2. ir.config_parameter holds real secrets (database.secret, mail/OAuth/payment keys). Never store or show raw values: key names plus a salted-hash 'differs' flag only, a name denylist (secret, password, token, key, uuid), and exclude the section from Markdown/PR text by default. Do not persist the salt in the snapshot, so a committed file cannot be dictionary-attacked.
3. The snapshot file and the Markdown for a PR leak internal module names, custom x_ models and company names. Add no URL/db/key to it, mark it in the file itself the way BackupCodec writes its 'sensitive' notice, and show a warning on Copy as Markdown.
4. The in-app Console log (LoggingApiClient -> RequestConsoleLog) has no header masking (grep of features/console found none) and will record ~20-40 calls with the bearer header per compare. This is the same exposure as the existing Explorer, but bigger. Either mask Authorization in the log or run compare through an unlogged ApiClient.
5. CLI/CI keys: POSTPILOT_VAR_odooApiKey is a single global override, so it cannot carry two different keys (staging and prod). Locally workspace.local.json has both (env/<name>/odooApiKey). For CI add --key-a/--key-b or POSTPILOT_ODOO_KEY_A/_B, otherwise the feature will silently send the same key to both sides.

CORRECTNESS RISKS:
- fields_get returns different fields depending on the key user's groups (fields with groups=). Two keys with different roles produce false 'removed field' noise. Show a standing caveat banner and do not label anything 'breaking' when the key identity differs; ideally compare a field-count of a model both users can read as a sanity line.
- ir.module.module, ir.config_parameter and ir.cron usually need an admin key (group_system); fields_get, companies and the module list are usually readable. Sections must degrade to 'no access', never abort (already in the design), and the CLI exit code must ignore no-access sections unless --checks names them.
- 'Renamed-looking' is a heuristic (same type/relation/label) and can be wrong; label it 'possible rename', and 'Rename field in N requests' must preview every body change before writing, because PostPilot has no undo for DB edits.
- Scanner limits: requests whose URL or body is variable-driven ({{model}}), raw non-JSON bodies, GraphQL and legacy /jsonrpc bodies yield 'unknown usage', not a false 'not affected'. Say so in the UI.
- fields_get of large models (sale.order, account.move) can be 300 KB-1 MB each, and the response cap is 8 MB per call. Fine, but bound concurrency and offer cancel.

PRODUCT/SCOPE RISK: 1.5 uses/week x 15 min is optimistic as a yearly average. The use is bursty (upgrade or release weeks, then little), so a realistic average is about 0.5-1/week at 20-30 min each, about 10-25 min/week. Still worth building because the saving is large exactly at the painful moment, and the CLI/CI step gives an unattended pre-release gate. Drop Git-native sync and the generated-Dart claim from the pitch, and hold the legacy Odoo 18 transport as v2 so v1 stays at M.
- **Strongest objection:** The claimed 15 min x 1.5 uses/week (22 min/week) does not survive scrutiny, for four reasons.

1. The Odoo 18 vs 19 half cannot work with PostPilot's own transport. JSON-2 (/json/2/) only exists from Odoo 19. PostPilot's own Odoo connect tab says "Odoo 19 and later expose the External JSON-2 API". A saved JSON-2 request or fields_get call cannot be sent to an Odoo 18 server. A real 18-vs-19 diff would need a second legacy JSON-RPC/XML-RPC client, which is more effort and goes against the product direction of migrating away from RPC. It would also run once per upgrade, about once a year. Only staging vs prod, both on 19, is feasible, and that is a much rarer problem.

2. The "find broken calls one by one" pain is already handled. The collection runner with assertions and reports can run the whole collection against an environment in one click. The OdooErrorParser banner already turns "unexpected keyword argument" and invalid-field errors into a plain-language hint. Running the collection on the new-version environment lists every broken saved request in about 1 minute. A schema diff adds only a "which field was renamed" label, and that label is mostly already in Odoo's error text. There is no real fix to automate beyond a find-and-replace.

3. The frequency is overstated. Real staging-vs-prod drift (a custom x_ field or a module version mismatch) hits an Odoo API dev a few times a year. When it does, the quick check is Apps in the UI, or one fields_get call that the existing Explorer tab already makes. A realistic rate is about 0.3 uses/week at 10-12 min, which is about 4 min/week. Odoo.sh stagings are also cloned from prod, so drift is smaller there.

4. Most of the proposed sections are out of reach for the people who would use them.
   - ir.config_parameter and ir.cron normally need a system-admin key, and developers rarely hold an admin API key on prod. Those sections would often show "no access".
   - Installed modules, by contrast, are readable by internal users, so the claim that ir.module.module needs an admin key is probably wrong.
   - A diff of modules, parameters, crons and companies is DB-admin tooling, not API-client work. It makes the Odoo Studio feature wide but shallow.

The CLI CI step ('postpilot odoo diff') is a real one-time-setup win, but only for a small team that has CI, two live environments and a prod key.

If any piece is worth keeping, it is a narrow, cheap version (S effort) that does not need the 18/19 or admin-only parts. It would diff fields_get for the models the collection already uses, between two existing environments, and flag which saved requests reference a missing field. Even so, I would rank it below the high-frequency candidates.

Web evidence: my web-search budget was exhausted, so I could not re-verify the candidate's URLs (the Database Diff Tool page, the odoodataflow field-mapping guide, the Cybrosys migration blog). The 19-only JSON-2 fact comes from PostPilot's own UI text and the candidate's own Odoo 20 deprecation note.
- **Platform notes:** All platforms; web needs CORS on both Odoo servers (CLI avoids that). Read-only: fields_get and companies work for a normal user, ir.module.module / ir.config_parameter / ir.cron usually need an admin key (show partial results). Mask parameter values and never write any parameter.

### 21. Odoo record watcher: what did that button, cron or import change?
- **Score 1.6** | effort M | ~4 min/week saved | persona odoo | lenses: observability-debug | skeptic votes 2/3
- **Problem today:** After clicking a button, running a cron/automation rule or importing a file, an Odoo dev needs to know which records and fields changed. View Metadata shows only create/write date and user, chatter tracks only tracking=True fields, and OCA auditlog needs a server-side install (not possible on SaaS or locked-down prod). Workarounds: _logger lines, Postgres, or search_read before/after and eyeballing 80 fields.
- **What PostPilot would do:** New 'Watch' tab in Odoo Studio: pick a model and optional domain (existing visual domain builder), click 'Snapshot', do the action in Odoo, click 'Diff now' to see created, deleted and changed records with field-level before/after plus write_uid/write_date (reusing JsonDiff). A 'Live feed' toggle polls search_read with write_date > last every 3-5 s. 'Save as request' turns a changed record into a ready write call.
- **Where it lives:** New tab in Odoo Studio next to Explorer/Convert, reusing the connection and domain builder.
- **Partly exists already:** No snapshot, watch, poll or change-tracking exists anywhere in lib/, bin/ or README.md. Grep for snapshot/poll/Timer.periodic/write_date/write_uid/get_metadata finds only unrelated hits (git_sync SyncSnapshot, cli BackupSnapshot, and compare_tab.dart line 20 listing 'write_date' as a volatile key to ignore). lib/features/odoo has 5 Studio tabs (Connect, Explorer, Domain builder, Migrate RPC, Dart model; odoo_studio_dialog.dart lines 46-50). Partial building blocks only: (1) odoo_explorer_tab.dart plus OdooStudioViewModel.runSearch (odoo_studio_view_model.dart line 185) already run ONE search_read with a domain, chosen stored fields and a limit of 5/20/50/100, and show rows, but nothing is stored for later comparison. (2) The existing Compare tab (response_tools/presentation/widgets/compare_tab.dart, response_history.dart keeps the last 8 responses per saved request in memory, json_diff.dart) can be used as a manual before/after workaround if the dev saved a search_read request, ran it, did the action, and ran it again. That workaround is weak: JsonDiff matches list items by POSITION, not by id (json_diff.dart), so a created or deleted record shifts every later row and produces noise; it reports paths like $[3].state with no id label, has no created/deleted classification, and the Compare tab ignores write_date by default. So the field-level diff engine exists, but id-keyed record diff, snapshot capture, created/deleted detection, write_uid attribution, live polling and 'save as request' are all new.
- **Competitors:** OCA auditlog (https://github.com/OCA/server-tools/tree/17.0/auditlog): VERIFIED. It tracks create/read/write/delete but needs server-side installation plus per-model rules under Settings/Technical/Audit/Rules, so it is not an option on Odoo.sh/SaaS or locked-down prod. Odoo App 'Form Field Change Timeline' (https://apps.odoo.com/apps/modules/19.0/form_field_change_timeline): VERIFIED to exist as a paid third-party module (about $1.95 to $9.99), but it only shows who/when/old/new for tracked fields inside the form via the existing chatter tracking. The candidate's wording 'shows only who changed which field' is fair, but it is not an external, client-side before/after diff tool, so it does not compete directly. Odoo 19 External JSON-2 doc URL (https://www.odoo.com/documentation/19.0/developer/reference/external_api.html): the page exists and lists 'External JSON-2 API' in its table of contents, but my fetch returned only the nav index, so I could NOT confirm from it that search_read, write_date and write_uid are described. Treat that evidence line as plausible, not verified. No client-side competitor found: I did not find a Postman/Insomnia/Bruno feature that does id-keyed snapshot diff of Odoo records, and the extra web search was not possible because the search budget ran out. Real design caveats to keep in the candidate: (a) search_read skips archived records (active_test), so archiving looks like a delete; (b) records that leave or enter the domain also look deleted or created, so the diff should say 'left the domain' not 'deleted' unless a second read by id (search_read [('id','in',ids)] with active_test=false) confirms it; (c) snapshot of a wide model with all stored fields can hit OdooClient's 8 MB maxResponseBytes (odoo_client.dart), so require a domain plus limit and let the dev pick fields; (d) computed stored fields and write_date itself will make records look changed after unrelated recomputes, so offer an ignore-fields list; (e) a deletion cannot be seen by the write_date live-feed poll, only by an id-set compare. Estimate of 10 min x 3 per week is plausible for Odoo devs debugging buttons, crons and imports, maybe slightly generous for the median dev; effort M is fair because the id-keyed diff must be written (JsonDiff cannot be reused as is for lists).
- **Design in this codebase:** VERDICT: feasible without a rewrite. Odoo Studio already has everything the feature needs except the diff logic and a session-lived state holder. It needs no dart:io and no conditional imports, so it runs on desktop, mobile and web (web only with the CORS limit Odoo Studio already has). Corrected effort is M (about 8-10 working days): S for Snapshot + Diff alone, plus about 3 days for a guarded live feed.

WHAT IS REUSED (verified in lib/):
- OdooClient.call(connection, OdooCall) in features/odoo/data/odoo_client.dart. OdooCall already supports `context`, `params`, `ids`.
- OdooStudioViewModel already holds `connection`, `model`, `info` (OdooModelInfo with per-field type/stored/relation), `domainText` and `chosenFields`. The Domain tab already writes `domainText`, so the Watch tab needs no model or domain picker of its own.
- ToolTabs/ToolTab in core/widgets/tool_dialog.dart. Add one ToolTab to odoo_studio_dialog.dart.
- OdooJson2.draft + addRequest() for "Save as request". OdooDomain.parseText/toList for the domain. InfoBanner, EmptyHint, BusyLabel and the wide/narrow LayoutBuilder pattern (>= 760) from the Explorer tab. ProductionDetector.isProduction() for the prod warning.
- Entry point: palette_items.dart gets one new item. Add the tab at the END (index 5) so the existing initialTab 1-4 palette entries do not shift.

FOLDER AND FILES (all under lib/features/odoo/):
1. domain/entities/odoo_snapshot.dart: OdooSnapshot (model, domain, fieldNames, takenAt, Map<int, Map<String,Object?>> records, truncated flag) and OdooRecordChange/OdooFieldChange value classes.
2. domain/services/odoo_snapshot_diff.dart: pure Dart `compare(before, after, info)`, matched by record id, so it is unit-testable in test/odoo/ next to odoo_logic_test.dart.
   - Do NOT use plain JsonDiff for everything. It matches lists by position and has no idea about Odoo shapes: many2one `[id, name]` would show as two changed cells, and x2many id lists would show a shifted-index mess.
   - Use a field-type-aware layer: scalars by equality (false equals empty), many2one by id, x2many as added/removed id sets. JsonDiff is only the fallback for json/properties fields.
   - write_date and write_uid are shown as record metadata, not as "changed fields", because they always change.
3. data/odoo_snapshot_loader.dart: wraps OdooClient.
   - Fetch with keyset paging (`order: 'id asc'` plus `['id','>',lastId]`), 500 rows per page, hard cap about 5000 records, then a clear "narrow the domain" message.
   - OdooClient caps one response at 8 MB and returns json == null if cut off, so paging is mandatory.
   - Field list = stored fields only, excluding binary/image/html by default. A binary field on res.partner would blow up the payload.
   - Deleted vs left-domain/archived: ids that vanished get one follow-up `search_read [['id','in',missing]]` with `context: {'active_test': false}`. Absent means deleted; present means archived or no longer matches the domain. Without this, archiving a record would be reported as a delete.
4. presentation/view_models/odoo_watch_view_model.dart: its own ChangeNotifier. Do not grow OdooStudioViewModel (it is already 214 lines), and its `_run` blocks on isBusy and notifies on every call, which would make the buttons flicker during polling.
   - Register the snapshot holder as a lazySingleton (in memory only). OdooStudioViewModel is a registerFactory disposed with the dialog, so a snapshot kept there would be lost when the dialog closes. The most PostPilot-native flow is snapshot, close the dialog, send the write request from a collection, reopen, Diff now.
   - Add a line to test/di_wiring_test.dart (_wire<T>()).
5. presentation/widgets/odoo_watch_tab.dart: header "Watching res.partner · domain · N fields", Snapshot and Diff now buttons, count chips like Compare tab's _Count (created/changed/deleted/left domain), a master list of changed records only (ListView.builder, never render the snapshot in a DataTable), a detail pane with field before/after and write_uid/write_date. Narrow screens stack the panes. A filter "only changes by write_uid = me" cheaply cuts the noise on busy servers.

LIVE FEED (phase 2, guarded). Do not build it as `write_date > last` polling (see risks). Better design:
- Re-read the snapshot's ids with `search_read [['id','in',ids]]` (primary-key lookup, cheap for a few hundred records), plus `search [['id','>',maxId]]` for creates.
- Schedule the next poll only after the previous one finishes (no overlapping timers). Minimum interval 5 s, 10 s when ProductionDetector says the active environment is production. Auto-stop after about 10 min and on dispose/app pause. Back off on 429/5xx.
- Deletes are only detected on Diff now or an occasional sweep.

CORE CHANGE NEEDED: add a `quiet`/`log` flag to ApiRequestOptions and have LoggingApiClient skip the observer when it is set. Otherwise live polling fills RequestConsoleLog (capped at 200 entries) and pushes the user's real requests out of the Console in about 15 minutes. It also calls notifyListeners twice per poll. This keeps core/network free of feature imports.

TESTS: pure diff tests (many2one, x2many, false-vs-empty, archived-vs-deleted, truncation) plus a loader test with a fake ApiClient (the repo already has several, e.g. test/collection_runner_test.dart).

SCOPE ADVICE: ship Snapshot + Diff first. Treat the live feed as phase 2. Drop "Save as request" or cut it down to "copy write body" (see risks).

SIDE FINDING for the orchestrator: ProductionGuard.checkSend is method-based, and every JSON-2 call is POST. So every saved Odoo search_read/read request already triggers the "changes data" prod confirmation. A JSON-2-aware guard that treats read-only methods (/json/2/<model>/search_read, read, search_count, fields_get...) as non-writes would be a small, separate, genuinely time-saving fix for Odoo users on prod.
- **Risks:** TECHNICAL RISKS IN THE CANDIDATE AS WRITTEN (fixable in design):
1. "Live feed polls write_date > last" is wrong in two ways. (a) In Odoo, write_date is set from cr.now(), the TRANSACTION START time. I confirmed in odoo/sql_db.py (18.0) that Cursor.now() returns the transaction's timestamp, cached until commit or rollback. A long cron or import that starts at t and commits at t+60s stamps its records with t, so a `write_date > cursor` poll permanently misses them. I recall `_write` using cr.now() but did not see that line in the fetched source. (b) write_date has no index: the field definition in odoo/models.py has no index=True. On big tables (account.move.line, stock.move) each poll is a sequential scan on the customer's production database every 3-5 s. Mitigation: poll by primary key as in the design above, or use a >= 90 s overlap window plus local dedupe. Take the cursor from server-side write_date values, never the device clock.
2. "Reusing JsonDiff" only partly holds. Lists are matched by position, so many2one tuples and x2many id lists come out noisy or misleading. A type-aware layer is needed (counted in the M estimate).
3. "Deletes by comparing id sets" is ambiguous. Archived records (active_test) and records that no longer match the domain also vanish. The follow-up resolve query handles it.
4. The 8 MB response cap in OdooClient truncates JSON and makes `json` null for large snapshots. Paging and a record cap are required.
5. Visibility is limited by the API key user's access rights and record rules, and by company context. Changes to records the key cannot read, or in other companies, will not show. The UI should say this ("shows what this API user can see"). Also say that the diff includes every user's and cron's writes in the window, not only the clicked button. The write_uid filter helps. The candidate already admits it cannot attribute a cron or automation rule.
6. Timers are throttled when a browser tab is in the background, and stop when a mobile app is suspended. Snapshot/Diff is unaffected, but the live feed is unreliable there. Stop it on pause.

SECURITY / PRIVACY:
- A snapshot can hold personal data (res.partner, hr.employee, account.move). Keep it in memory only. Never write it to Drift, backup export, workspace.json or Git sync, and clear it with an explicit "Clear snapshot" button and on app exit. A unit test should assert it is not serialisable into the workspace.
- "Save as request" with real before/after values would put customer data into a collection request body. That is persisted in Drift and pushed to GitHub by Git sync. Recommendation: default to field names with placeholders, or show an explicit warning before including values. Keep the {{odooUrl}}/{{odooApiKey}} template variables that OdooJson2.draft already uses, so the API key is never embedded.
- The Console log stores only metadata (no bodies, no Authorization header, only a header count), so it does not leak record data. The "Ask AI" feature must not be pointed at diff output automatically. That would send Odoo record data to the Anthropic API, so it needs an explicit opt-in if added.

PRODUCTION LOAD / ToS: reads only, and OdooClient bypasses ProductionGuard, so the candidate's claim that the PROD write lock is not triggered is correct. Guard against load instead: require a domain or limit, default the live feed off, enforce the minimum interval, and warn on production environments. I could not retrieve the body of the Odoo 19 External API page this session (WebFetch returned only the table of contents, and the JSON-2 URL I tried returned 404), and the web search budget was exhausted, so I did not verify rate limits or which Odoo plans (Online Custom vs Standard/Free, Odoo.sh, on-premise) allow the JSON-2 API. I recall that Odoo Online restricts external API access to the Custom plan, which would limit the feature's audience but is the same limit the rest of Odoo Studio already has. No evidence of a ToS prohibition on read polling, but treat this as unverified. There is no store-policy issue: no code download, no background service, no private APIs.

OVERALL: acceptable risk, provided the live feed is phase 2 and built as PK polling with the quiet-logging flag. The core Snapshot + Diff flow is low risk and useful on its own."
- **Strongest objection:** The main pain (eyeballing 80 fields before and after) is already solved in PostPilot. Response tools has a Compare tab backed by JsonDiff, and ResponseHistory keeps the last 8 responses per request. Today an Odoo dev can save one search_read or read request for record 42, send it, click the button in Odoo, send it again, and read the field-level diff in Compare. That is about 3 clicks. The "Diff now" step would only repeat it in a new tab.

What is genuinely new is small:
(1) Created and deleted detection by record id. Compare diffs arrays by index, so an insert or delete shifts everything. An id-keyed array diff in JsonDiff is a days-long fix and would not need a new tab.
(2) Polling "Live feed", which is a nice-to-have.

The use case is also weaker than claimed:
- A real button, cron or import usually touches many models (sale.order, stock.move, account.move and others). A snapshot needs a model and domain chosen in advance, so it misses most of the side effects. The discovery case ("what did this touch anywhere") needs a different feature, a write_date > T scan across models, and that shows no before values.
- Odoo devs who want that discovery have cheaper tools on dev, staging and Odoo.sh. These are the debug_sql log, psql with a write_date filter, odoo shell, and the OCA auditlog module. The audience that has none of these is mostly SaaS and functional consultants, not PostPilot's developer target.
- Frequency is closer to 1 or 2 times a week for the subset of Odoo devs using JSON-2. Net saving over the existing Compare workflow is about 2 to 4 minutes per use. Across all PostPilot personas the average is near 0.

I could not run a web search for demand evidence because the search budget was exhausted. This verdict rests on the code reading (response_history.dart, compare_tab.dart) and on how Odoo devs normally debug.

Recommendation: do not build the Watch tab. Fold the id-keyed diff into Compare as an S-sized change instead.
- **Platform notes:** Plain JSON-2 over HTTP, all platforms (web needs CORS on Odoo). Cannot say which automation or cron caused a write (only write_uid and time); deletes are found by comparing id sets; polling adds load, so require a domain, a limit and a minimum interval on prod. Reads only, so the PROD write lock is not triggered.

### 22. Secret Guard and workspace health check with one-click fixes and a pre-push gate
- **Score 1.6** | effort M | ~4 min/week saved | persona all | lenses: security-auth, productivity-ux | skeptic votes 2/3
- **Problem today:** Git push strips secrets by NAME only (SecretNames), so a PAT in X-Custom, a Slack token in a URL path, an AWS key in a raw body or a real access_token in a saved example (used by the mock server) is not caught; value patterns exist in SecretMasker but are not applied to sync. After a Postman/HAR import the dev hunts through dozens of requests replacing literals with {{var}}. Collections also rot: undefined/unused variables, duplicated requests, hard-coded hosts, keys missing in prod. Audit costs ~15 min and secrets are only noticed after they leak.
- **What PostPilot would do:** A scan runs automatically on import, in the Git push preview and before export/share, and on demand ('Check workspace health'). It checks names AND values (provider prefixes, JWTs, URL userinfo, high-entropy strings) in URLs, headers, bodies, auth, extractors and saved examples, plus undefined/unused variables, duplicate method+URL+body, repeated hard-coded hosts, env keys missing in some environments and Odoo checks (missing X-Odoo-Database, legacy /jsonrpc). 'Fix all' replaces each literal with {{generated_name}} stored as a local-only variable, with per-finding fixes (create variable, replace host with {{baseUrl}}, delete duplicates). High-confidence findings block the push unless acknowledged; allow-list for false positives.
- **Where it lives:** Finding strip inside the existing Git push preview and the import result dialog, collection context menu 'Check health...' and palette command; CLI 'postpilot scan|lint workspace.json --fail-on high' for pre-commit/CI.
- **Partly exists already:** PARTIAL building blocks exist, the product itself does not. EXISTS: (1) Name-based stripping on Git sync: lib/features/git_sync/domain/services/secret_names.dart, secret_text.dart, secret_fields.dart (auth keys, secret-named headers/query/form/JSON keys, URL userinfo; applied in sync_engine.dart and local_collection_store_impl.dart). (2) Value-pattern detection (JWT, Stripe, GitHub, GitLab, AWS, Slack, Google, webhooks) only in lib/features/documentation/domain/services/secret_masker.dart, used for generated docs, bug report, AI prompts, MCP/CLI output masking and error messages, never for sync and never as a finding list. (3) Workplace push: lib/features/workplace/presentation/widgets/push_to_git.dart shows a 'Push secret values to Git?' warning, but workplace_view_model.dart secretsInWorkspace() only lists env/global variables flagged isSecret by the user; lib/features/workplace/domain/services/secret_splitter.dart only moves env/global/collection-var/auth fields to workspace.local.json. Request headers, URLs, raw bodies and saved response examples in workspace.json are not stripped or scanned at all on that path, so the candidate's gap is even bigger than it states. (4) Inline red 'undefined' token + hover card per field: request_builder/presentation/view_models/variable_scope.dart and widgets/variables/variable_hover_card.dart; CLI run errors on an unresolved {{var}} in the URL only at run time (cli/workspace_runner.dart:242). (5) Export dialogs only print a static 'check for secrets' caption (import_export/presentation/export_collection_dialog.dart:132-138). NOT FOUND anywhere in lib/, bin/ or README: any scan on import (HAR/Postman/Insomnia importers keep literal tokens and hosts as-is, no {{baseUrl}} extraction), any finding list in push_preview_dialog.dart/push_preview.dart, a pre-push block with allow-list, 'replace literal with {{var}}' fix-all, workspace-wide undefined/unused variable report, duplicate method+URL+body detection, repeated hard-coded host detection, env-key drift across environments, Odoo lint (missing X-Odoo-Database, legacy /jsonrpc usage), or a CLI scan/lint subcommand (bin/postpilot.dart -> cli/cli_main.dart only has run, list, mcp).
- **Competitors:** Evidence checked and accurate: Truffle Security blog (about 40,000 public Postman workspaces scanned, 1,689 live unique credentials, 183 secret types); GitGuardian State of Secrets Sprawl 2026 via The Hacker News (29 million new hardcoded secrets on public GitHub in 2025, +34% YoY). Postman Local Secret Protection (learning.postman.com .../secret-scanner/) detects secrets before cloud/Git sync and lets the user store in Vault, keep plaintext or remove; its docs do not describe auto-replacing literals with variable references, so the one-click 'literal to {{var}} as local-only variable' fix is a genuine differentiator. Postman Agent Mode 'Collection cleanup' recipe covers duplicate requests and folder restructuring only (AI prompt-driven, not deterministic offline rules, not unused/undefined variables or secrets). Postman CLI governance lints API specs, not collections (claimed, not re-fetched). Generic overlap: gitleaks, TruffleHog and GitGuardian ggshield pre-commit scanners work on raw files, not on a request model with variable-aware fixes. Caveats for scoping: the real time savings come from the secret scan on import/push plus the fix-to-variable action (value-pattern reuse of SecretMasker regexes makes that S-M effort); duplicate/hard-coded-host/env-drift/Odoo lint rules are cheap add-ons but individually low-minutes, so ship them as a second phase. Keep entropy-based detection conservative to avoid false-positive fatigue, and the pre-push block needs the allow-list from day one.
- **Design in this codebase:** VERDICT: feasible without a rewrite. The two ingredients already exist as pure Dart (SecretMasker value patterns, SecretNames name logic), the push-preview and import hook points exist, and the CLI already decodes workspace.json into a BackupSnapshot with no Flutter dependency.

WHAT I VERIFIED IN THE CODE (corrects the candidate's problem statement)
1. There are two Git paths, with different leaks.
 - Per-collection git_sync (SecretFields.stripDoc, name-only): it blanks Authorization/Cookie/X-*-Key headers, secret query params, secret form fields, JSON string values under secret keys, URL userinfo and auth fields. A PAT in an ordinary header (X-Custom), a Slack token in a URL path or an AWS key in a non-JSON raw body goes out untouched. It does NOT sync saved examples at all (RequestDocMapper has no examples key; grep 'example' in git_sync returns nothing).
 - Workplace push of workspace.json (the "Git sync of a workspace JSON" path): this is the bigger hole. It carries the whole BackupSnapshot, including every request URL, header, body, extractor and saved example body. SecretSplitter only splits env/global variables flagged secret, collection variables with secret-looking names, and auth fields. Request headers, URLs, bodies and examples are never scrubbed, even with safety.keepSecretsLocal=true (the default). So the saved-example/real-access_token claim is true for workspace.json and for backup/export, not for per-collection git_sync.
2. SecretMasker (documentation feature) already has the value patterns (JWT, sk_, ghp_, github_pat_, glpat, AKIA/ASIA, xox, AIza, Slack/Discord webhook paths, URL password) plus body-format-aware masking. Its regexes are private statics.
3. Hook points exist: push_to_git.dart pushWorkplaceToGit() already runs a secrets warning dialog and then PushPreviewDialog (ToolDialog). WorkplaceRepositoryImpl.previewPush() already reads the local file text that will be pushed. ImportAnyDialog shows only a snackbar (summary.description). The collection context menu is a PopupMenuButton with 17 items. PaletteItems.tools() is a one-line registration. cli_main.dart has a fixed command set {run,list,mcp}.

DESIGN
Feature folder: lib/features/workspace_health/ (domain / data / presentation), same layering as mock_server.
- domain/services/secret_patterns.dart: move SecretMasker._knownToken and the onlyVariables regex into a public shared class (SecretMasker keeps calling it; test/documentation/secret_masker_test.dart guards the refactor). Add: Azure/Stripe-restricted/SendGrid/npm/PyPI/Twilio/Odoo-API-key-looking 40-hex under an odoo-ish name, URL userinfo, and Shannon entropy that only fires for values under SecretNames-looking names or in header values.
- domain/services/workspace_scanner.dart: input BackupSnapshot (pure Dart, same as the CLI). It walks "slots" (url, header, query, form/urlencoded field, raw/graphql body, auth field, collection/env/global variable, extractor/assertion value, request description, example body/headers) and emits Finding{rule, severity high|medium|low, location path, slot ref, masked preview, fingerprint}. The same slot walker also covers the health lints: undefined/unused {{vars}} (reuse AppConstants.variablePattern, DynamicVariables.names, extractor variableKey counts as defined), duplicate method+URL+body, repeated hard-coded host, env keys missing in some environments, Odoo (/jsonrpc, /xmlrpc legacy path; X-Odoo-Database only as info because JSON-2 does not always require it).
- Protection-aware flag: findings that the active sync policy already blanks (call the same SecretNames/SecretText predicates) are marked 'auto-protected' and do not block; otherwise a name-covered secret would be reported as a leak on every push (noise).
- domain/services/fix_planner.dart + usecases/apply_health_fixes_usecase.dart: all writes go through existing interfaces. RequestRepository.saveRequest (ids and uids stay stable, so ThreeWayMerger/EntityUidRegistry see an ordinary modified request, no spurious changes), CollectionVariableRepository.upsert, ResponseExampleRepository delete+add (there is no update). The same literal reused in 30 requests maps to ONE variable (dedupe by value).
- Generated variable names MUST satisfy SecretNames.looksSecretKey (always end in _token/_secret/_password, never a neutral tail). That is what makes them local-only on both paths (SecretFields blanks collection vars by name; SecretSplitter's cvar rule uses the same predicate). A unit test must assert this, otherwise the fix just moves the secret into a pushed variable.
- Examples get a Redact fix (replace with a mask), not a {{var}} (the mock server serves literal text).
- After applying, call the existing reopenReplacedRequests(shell, changedIds, deletedIds) from git_sync/presentation so open tabs are not stale.
- data/health_allowlist_prefs.dart: SharedPreferences, same style as SafetyPrefs; fingerprint = sha256(rule|value) (crypto is already a dependency), the value is never stored. For CI a location-based workspace.health-ignore.json beside the workspace (committed, contains no values).
- presentation: WorkspaceHealthDialog (ToolDialog + ToolSection + InfoBanner, Secrets / Health tabs via ToolTabs, per-finding 'Fix' + 'Fix all high'), HealthViewModel registered in injector.dart (and add to di_wiring_test). A small FindingStrip widget reused in: (a) PushPreviewDialog: add PushPreview.findings, computed in previewPush from the already-read local file text via BackupCodec.decode (in compute()), plus an acknowledge checkbox like the existing _overwrite one and a 'Review and fix' button (new PushAction that re-opens the preview after the dialog closes); (b) the per-collection GitChangesTab above the commit area, scanning one collection from CollectionLoader without examples; (c) ImportAnyDialog after a successful import, scanning summary.collectionIds; (d) palette command 'Check workspace health' in PaletteItems.tools() plus one menu entry in the collection PopupMenu (keep it to a single item, the menu is already long).
- CLI: add 'lint' (alias 'scan') to cli_main.dart: BackupCodec.decode(shared workspace.json WITHOUT merging workspace.local.json, because the question is what is committed), options --fail-on high|medium|low and an ignore file, exit 1 above threshold. Reporter prints rule + location + fingerprint, never the secret value. Add a case to test/cli/cli_test.dart.

PLATFORMS
Scanner, fix planner and rules are pure Dart, so they run on web and mobile. Only the CLI needs dart:io, and it stays under features/cli. Run the scan in compute() (BackupService already does) and cap each scanned body (about 256 KB, skip base64 blobs) for web/mobile. On web the workspace file comes through the existing WorkplaceStorage abstraction, so previewPush needs no conditional import.

EFFORT (corrected): M, upper end, about 2 to 3 weeks if everything listed ships at once. Phase 1 (about 1 week): scanner with patterns, push-preview and import hooks, fix-all for secrets, allow-list, CLI scan. Phase 2 (about 1 week): the non-secret lints (undefined/unused variables, duplicates, hard-coded host to {{baseUrl}}, env parity, Odoo). Phase 3 optional: have SecretSplitter also split detected literals into workspace.local.json. Do not change SecretFields.stripDoc to value-based blanking: the pull-time restore (DocValues._keepLocalValue) only restores values for name-based isSecret, so a value-pattern blank would be erased from the local copy on the next pull. Detect-and-replace avoids touching the merger.
- **Risks:** 1. False positives are the main product risk. Entropy and JWT hits in saved examples (UUIDs, hashes, ETags, base64 images, sample jwt.io tokens, Odoo ids) will erode trust, and a blocking gate amplifies that. Mitigation: provider-prefix, URL-password and name-gated findings are 'high' and ask for acknowledgement; bare entropy is warn-only; the gate is never a hard block; the allow-list is per value fingerprint. Unit-test against the repo's own templates and sample collections to prove zero high findings.
2. Fix correctness. A fix that replaces text inside a raw JSON/XML body or only the secret segment of a URL path (Slack webhook) must be an exact span replacement and idempotent (a second scan finds nothing). If the generated variable name is not secret-looking, the fix silently moves the secret into a pushed collection variable and gives false safety. With keepSecretsLocal OFF, secretsInWorkspace() and SecretSplitter would not hide those collection variables on the workspace path either, so the guard dialog must also count secret-named collection variables.
3. Open-tab race. If a request tab has unsaved or autosaving state, the editor can overwrite the fix with the old literal. Reuse reopenReplacedRequests and test it the way git_sync_concurrent_edit_test does.
4. The scanner itself can leak. Findings UI must show a masked preview only (prefix + bullets); CLI/CI output prints rule, location and fingerprint, never the value (CI logs are widely readable); never send findings to the AI assistant. A committed CLI ignore file must be location-based, because a hash of a low-entropy value can be brute-forced.
5. Performance/ReDoS: keep every regex linear like SecretMasker's (it documents this); run in compute(); cap body size; add a 5 MB pathological-input test. The workspace.json decode on every preview is heavy for large example sets.
6. Coverage gap to be honest about: per-collection git_sync pushes no examples, so the 'saved example' value is on the workspace.json push and on backup/Postman export, not on that path. Examples inside a backup export are already documented as plain-text, so the gate there is advisory only.
7. Legal/licensing: the project is MIT (Bista Solutions). Write own regexes or take them from MIT sources (gitleaks, with attribution); do NOT port TruffleHog detectors (AGPL-3.0). Do NOT add live key verification (calling provider APIs with a found key): it sends the secret to third parties, may breach provider ToS, and is unnecessary for the time-saving goal. No network, no telemetry, so no app-store or privacy-policy impact.
8. UI clutter: the collection menu already has 17 items. Add at most one entry, keep the rest in the palette and in the push/import dialogs, and keep nothing in the request/response views.
9. Odoo X-Odoo-Database check can be wrong (header is optional with a single database or dbfilter), so keep it info-level.
- **Strongest objection:** The 18 min/week claim (6 min x 3 uses) is inflated. It bundles one real but cheap fix with a lint suite most developers will not run weekly.

1. The secret gap is real but narrow, and it saves no time. I read the code. SecretFields.stripDoc blanks by NAME only: auth keys, secret-looking headers, query params, form fields and JSON keys, plus URL userinfo. The value patterns exist only in SecretMasker (documentation), as the candidate says. The best case is a prevented leak, not minutes saved. Pushes with no findings save 0 minutes.
   - The common cases are already covered: Authorization, Cookie, X-*-Key, api_key, password, token, user:pass@host.
   - GitHub is a second net. Its docs say push protection applies to the 'Create or update file contents' REST endpoint and returns a 409 when it finds a supported secret. PostPilot syncs through exactly that REST API. Known-provider tokens (ghp_, AKIA, xox, sk_live) are therefore blocked on repos with push protection. I could not confirm which repos have it on by default, and the WebSearch budget ran out, so I could not check demand.
   - The remaining hole is unknown-format secrets in oddly named fields, mostly in private repos. Closing it is an S-sized hardening: reuse the _knownToken regex inside SecretFields. It is a bug fix, not a feature.

2. One evidence claim fails. The candidate says a real access_token in a saved example (used by the mock server) can be pushed. I found no 'example' handling in git_sync, including request_doc_mapper.dart, so saved examples do not appear to be synced.

3. The health-check half is rarely used.
   - Undefined variables are already flagged red inline (variable_text_controller, variable_hover_card, 'Undefined').
   - Unused variables, duplicate method+URL+body and repeated hosts are occasional cleanup, maybe 10 minutes once a month. Nobody audits weekly, so the '15 min audit x 3/week' premise does not hold.
   - Entropy rules on API collections (UUIDs, ids, base64 bodies, Odoo session ids, fixtures) give false positives. A blocking gate plus an allow-list adds a maintenance cost and push friction that eats the gain.

4. The CI subcommand (postpilot lint --fail-on high) duplicates gitleaks, trufflehog, ggshield and GitHub secret scanning, which already scan the workspace JSON once it is in a repo. Postman's own CLI governance lints specs, not collections, so there is no strong competitor signal.

5. One part is genuinely useful: replacing a hard-coded host or token with {{baseUrl}} / {{token}} after an import. OpenAPI import already does baseUrl. HAR import and cURL paste do not. That chore happens on every DevTools cURL paste or HAR import. At about 30 seconds x 5 pastes plus an occasional import, it is roughly 3-4 min/week.

Recommendation: do not build the bundle as one feature.
- Make the value-pattern strip in stripDoc a small hardening ticket, not marketed as time saving.
- Fold 'parameterize host and token' into cURL/HAR import, where it automates a daily chore.
- Drop the entropy gate, the duplicate and unused-variable lint, and the CLI lint.
- **Platform notes:** Pure Dart string work, all platforms; CLI subcommand is desktop/CI only. Replacement must be idempotent and keep request order and uids stable so the three-way merger does not see spurious changes. Rules are deterministic and offline (regex plus entropy, no AI).

### 23. Environment overlays: combine a server env with a user/device env
- **Score 1.5** | effort S | ~1.5 min/week saved | persona all | lenses: pain-points | skeptic votes 2/3
- **Problem today:** Real projects need server x user (Staging/Prod x Admin/Customer), server x platform (iOS/Android) or Odoo database x API-key user. Today each combination is its own environment copy, so a shared variable change is repeated in every copy and a mistake leaks into the wrong server. PostPilot has a single active environment (getActiveVariables).
- **What PostPilot would do:** The environment selector gets an optional second 'overlay' pick (or an 'Extends' field in the Environments manager). Variables resolve overlay > environment > collection > globals, the tab shows 'Staging + Admin', and the PROD detection/red pill considers both. CLI gets --overlay. The dev maintains 3 + 3 environments instead of 9 and switches user in one click.
- **Where it lives:** Existing environment selector ('Add overlay...' entry) and the Environments manager dialog. No new screen.
- **Competitors:** Postman: multi-select environments requested since 2018 (issue #4968, still open), so there is no native support. Insomnia: base environment plus one selected sub-environment (inheritance, not combining two; from my own knowledge, not fetched here). Bruno: nested environments requested in issue #292 (open). I could not run extra web searches because the search budget was used up, so no further competitor implementations were checked.
- **Design in this codebase:** VERDICT: feasible without a rewrite, but the candidate's "Extends field" variant is NOT S. Build the per-device "overlay selection" variant (S, about 4-6 days). Do not build a persisted "Extends" relation shared through workspace.json (that is M-L).

WHY IT FITS THE CODE
- All variable resolution funnels through EnvironmentRepository.getActiveVariables(), called by BuildVariableResolverUseCase (send, runner, snippets, OAuth2, timing tab) and OdooStudioViewModel. VariableResolver.layered() is already first-scope-wins. If getActiveVariables() returns overlay-over-env (one flat map), every one of those callers gets the feature with no edits. Resolving {{x}} inside a value still works across all scopes.
- Only a few places read watchActive() directly and need an explicit change: ProductionGuard._activeProductionName, RunRequestScriptsUseCase._writeEnvironment, ListVariablesUseCase (hover and colouring), EnvironmentSelector, palette_items.environments, EnvironmentsManagerDialog.

DESIGN (feature folder lib/features/environments)
1. Persistence with NO schema change. Store the overlay env id in the existing key/value table setting_entries (key "environments.overlayId"). Do not add a column or table. The DB NOTE in app_database.dart says Migrator.addColumn reproducibly crashes the drift web worker on this project, and the C: drive has about 4 GB free, so avoid build_runner and regenerating app_database.g.dart. Add the methods to EnvironmentsDao by reading attachedDatabase.settingEntries (no codegen). Expose the overlay as a stream with customSelect(JOIN environments ON CAST(id AS TEXT) = setting value, readsFrom: {environments, settingEntries}).watchSingleOrNull(). No rxdart needed.
2. Domain. Add to EnvironmentRepository: Stream<EnvironmentEntity?> watchOverlay(); Future<void> setOverlay(int? id). EnvironmentRepositoryImpl.getActiveVariables() = {...overlayVars, over the active env's vars}, enabled rows only. Invariants live in the DAO or repo in one transaction: setActive(id) clears the overlay if id == overlay; clearActive() clears the overlay; deleteEnvironment clears it if it matches. Reads validate that the overlay id still exists. After a workplace switch or Git pull, clearWorkplaceData wipes environments but not settings, so a stale id is possible. Drift autoIncrement is AUTOINCREMENT, so ids are never reused and a stale id simply resolves to nothing.
3. Extractor write rule (RunRequestScriptsUseCase._writeEnvironment): write to the overlay if the overlay defines the key, otherwise to the primary environment. This mirrors the existing _rowTheResolverReads logic. Without this rule an extracted token would be silently shadowed by an overlay value of the same name.
4. ListVariablesUseCase: add one layer between the environment and collection layers, reusing VariableSource.environment with scopeName = overlay name. No enum or hover-card change.
5. ProductionGuard: check the primary AND overlay names; the label becomes "Staging + Prod DB" and is also the isSilenced key. Update the sentence "The active environment looks like production" in production_confirm_dialog.dart. EnvironmentSelector uses the same helper so the red PROD pill and the guard cannot disagree. Put the combined-label and isProduction logic in one small domain helper, not duplicated.
6. UI, no new screen. EnvironmentsViewModel listens to watchOverlay() and exposes overlay (VariableScope already refreshes on this view model). Selector: keep the single dropdown. Add a compact "+ Admin" chip with an x inside the same pill, shown only when an overlay is set or at least 2 environments exist. Tapping it opens a PopupMenu of environments plus "None". The top bar already scrolls on phones (shell_page.dart line 266), so no overflow work. Manager dialog: add "Use as overlay" and "Remove overlay" to each environment's existing PopupMenu, plus a small "overlay" badge. Update the Globals subtitle text. Palette: add "Add overlay: X" and "Clear overlay" items to PaletteItems.environments (same PaletteCategory.environments). ToolDialog migration of the manager dialog is optional polish, not required.
7. CLI and MCP (pure Dart, no dart:io change): add 'overlay' to _Args._valued and a RunOptions.overlay field. RunState merges env then overlay by name into the environment map, and throws the same ArgumentError listing the available environments if the name is unknown. In McpServer add an optional 'overlay' tool argument, include it in the session-reuse check (callOptions.environment == options.environment) and in _optionsFor. Update the usage text. SecretSplitter and workspace.local.json already walk every environment by name, so overlay secrets work in CI via the existing mechanism with no change.
8. No change to BackupCodec, workspace.json, Git sync, Postman export or SecretSplitter, because the selection is per-device like isActive (isActive is already not serialized).

PLATFORM: pure Dart/domain plus drift on SQLite and wasm; the SettingsDao pattern already works on web. No dart:io, no conditional imports, no CORS issue. Works on desktop, web and mobile.

TESTS: all EnvironmentRepository fakes in test/ use noSuchMethod -> UnimplementedError. Any caller that now invokes watchOverlay() will break production_guard_test, run_request_scripts_usecase_test and the list_variables tests (via InMemoryEnvironmentRepository) unless those fakes get a one-line watchOverlay() stub. Alternatively inject the overlay dependency as an optional constructor parameter in ProductionGuard, ListVariablesUseCase and RunRequestScriptsUseCase. Add new tests: resolver precedence, the three invariants, guard with overlay named Prod, extractor write target, and a CLI test with --overlay.
- **Risks:** LEGAL/STORE/SECURITY: none. No new network, permission, platform API or third-party code. Secret flags and the local-only secret split still apply because they are keyed by environment name.

REAL RISKS
1. Safety regression is the main one. If any production check looks only at the primary environment, a "Prod DB" overlay on top of "Staging" would send writes with no confirmation and no red PROD pill. The guard, the selector and the confirm dialog text must share one helper. A test is mandatory.
2. Stale-token ambiguity. A token extracted on login belongs to a (server, user) pair, but there is only one place to store it. The rule in design point 3 avoids shadowing, yet switching the overlay user can still reuse the previous user's token until the login request is re-run. Document it, or later offer "clear extracted variables on overlay switch".
3. Hidden state. Without the "+ Admin" chip and the hover-card source (design point 4), developers will not know why a value resolves the way it does.
4. Schema trap. Do not add an isOverlay column to environments: addColumn crashes the drift web worker on this project, and codegen is hard with the nearly full C: drive. Settings-key storage avoids both.
5. Scope creep. A shared "Extends" relation stored in workspace.json would touch BackupCodec (the environment key set and version), SecretSplitter, Git merge and conflict handling (environments are matched by name) and older clients that would silently ignore it. Keep that for a later M-L step. Per-device selection means teammates do not inherit your pairing, which is acceptable.
