6. Interface fan-out: new methods on EnvironmentRepository touch about 5 test fakes (see TESTS), a small but real chore.
7. Value is modest. About 5 min x 2 per week is realistic only for developers who really maintain 4 or more environment copies (server x user, Odoo db x key). For a single-server user it is dead UI, so show the chip only when 2 or more environments exist.
- **Strongest objection:** The claimed 5 min x 2/week = 10 min does not survive scrutiny, for four reasons.

1. Switching is not faster. Picking "Staging + Admin" is two clicks, and picking the env "Staging-Admin" is one. The "switch user in one click" saving is zero or negative. The only real saving is maintenance, which means editing a shared variable once instead of N times. That happens a few times a month and takes about 1-2 minutes, not 5 minutes twice a week.

2. The cross-product is mostly not factorable. The values that differ per user are credentials: API keys, passwords, tokens. They are almost always scoped to the server too. An Odoo API key belongs to one database, and Admin-on-Staging is a different key from Admin-on-Prod. So the "Admin" overlay still needs a per-server value, and the 3x3 matrix comes back. Only truly orthogonal variables (platform headers such as X-Platform=ios or an app-version string) factor cleanly, and those are set once and rarely edited.

3. Cheaper workarounds already exist in PostPilot. The usual pattern is that the environment holds baseUrl and the credentials. A Login request with an extractor then writes the token, so "the user" is just which credentials the environment holds, and nothing needs to be duplicated per user. Collection and global variables already serve as the shared base layer for anything common across environments. The Bruno #292 evidence is also a different feature: it asks for a base environment with sub-environments (Insomnia style), not a server x user matrix. In PostPilot, globals already cover the "base" part.

4. Demand is thin and the risk is real. Postman #4968 has been open since August 2018, about 8 years, and I could only confirm its description. The reaction count (~72) in the candidate could not be verified because the fetch returned no reactions or comments. Postman has not shipped it in that time, which suggests low priority even for a huge user base. The feature also adds a precedence concept that Git-synced workspaces, the CLI --overlay flag, the PROD lock and the red pill must all handle correctly. If an overlay silently overrides baseUrl, a developer can hit prod while the pill says staging. That is a new error mode, and it works against the feature's own "avoid leaking into the wrong server" claim.

I also found no existing env "duplicate" action in lib/features/environments. A cheap one-click "Duplicate environment" (S effort) would remove most of the real pain of copy-pasting an env.

My estimate is at most 1-2 min/week for a dev who has a server x user/platform matrix, and 0 for the majority who have one environment per server. It is a niche convenience, not a weekly time-saver. The web search quota was exhausted, so I did not search for further demand evidence.
- **Platform notes:** No platform limits. BuildVariableResolverUseCase already layers variable maps, so the core change is small; most work is persisting the overlay choice and the CLI flag.

### 24. Baseline and drift guard: record a baseline, flag breaking changes, 'Accept change' (replaces hand-fixing assertions)
- **Score 1.2** | effort M | ~3 min/week saved | persona all | lenses: backend-daily, flutter-daily, qa-testing, docs-collab, ai-agentic | skeptic votes 2/3
- **Problem today:** Writing assertions for 15 endpoints by hand takes 1-2 min each, so collections ship untested. Later a backend change renames/retypes a field and the Flutter app crashes in fromJson ('type Null is not a subtype of String') or a dozen assertions go red; the dev opens each request, compares JSON by eye and decides if it is real or harmless drift (15-30 min per incident). Today compare data is in-memory only (last 8 responses) and 'Update from OpenAPI' only diffs the spec side. Saved examples also go stale silently and a fresh OpenAPI import has no examples.
- **What PostPilot would do:** Collection/folder menu 'Record baseline': sends GETs (writes need confirm), sends each twice to learn volatile fields (ids, timestamps, tokens), then stores per request status, time ceiling, required paths, types and an inferred JSON Schema, plus the response as the saved example. Later runs (app and CLI --baseline) report drift per request: field removed, type changed, became nullable, new enum value, status changed, labelled breaking vs non-breaking for the generated Dart DTOs, using the existing diff view. 'Accept change' rewrites the baseline, example and affected assertions in one click. OpenAPI import/refresh can optionally seed examples from the spec's responses.
- **Where it lives:** Collection/folder context menu ('Record baseline', 'Check drift'), a Drift column/tab in the Collection Runner report, CLI flag. Request/response views untouched.
- **Partly exists already:** The per-request building blocks exist; the collection-level automation and drift workflow do not. Exists: (1) lib/features/response_tools/domain/services/json_schema_tools.dart has JsonSchemaTools.infer(samples) and validate(). Missing fields drop out of 'required', and a type seen with null becomes a type list. (2) lib/features/response_tools/presentation/widgets/schema_tab.dart has 'Infer from N responses' (current response, up to 8 in-memory previous ones, plus saved examples) and 'Save as test', which adds an AssertionType.jsonSchema assertion that runs in the collection runner and CLI via lib/features/scripting/domain/evaluator/assertion_evaluator.dart. (3) lib/features/response_tools/presentation/widgets/compare_tab.dart and json_diff.dart compare the current response with a previous one or a saved example, and its tooltip even says 'keep a baseline'. It has a manual 'Ignore volatile fields' chip with a hard-coded key list (updated_at, created_at, timestamp, request_id, write_date, date). Diffs are value-level only, by key and list position, with no breaking/non-breaking classification. (4) lib/features/ai_assistant/domain/ai_tasks.dart (testsSystem, parseAssertions) and ai_tab.dart let the AI propose up to 8 assertions for ONE response, so hand-writing assertions is partly addressed one request at a time. (5) lib/features/response_tools/domain/services/response_history.dart is in-memory only, 8 per request, as the candidate says.\n\nNot found anywhere in lib/, bin/ or README.md: any collection or folder 'Record baseline' or 'Check drift' action; sending a request twice to learn volatile fields automatically; stored per-request baselines (status, time ceiling, required paths, types); drift classification (field removed, type changed, became nullable, new enum value, status changed) or breaking vs non-breaking labelling for the generated Dart DTOs; 'Accept change' that rewrites the baseline, example and assertions together; a Drift column in the runner report (lib/features/request_builder/domain/services/collection_runner_service.dart drops the body via _withoutBody and never compares against examples or schema); a CLI --baseline flag (lib/features/cli/cli_main.dart only has --env, --bail, --report and --out, plus the repeat/data options); and seeding examples from OpenAPI responses (lib/features/import_export/domain/services/openapi_parser.dart never reads 'responses', and the refresh dialog only diffs added/removed endpoints).\n\nVerdict: pass, as a substantial extension and not a duplicate. It is best scoped as 'automate and batch the Schema tab + Compare tab + assertion flow, and add the drift classification and accept loop'. Reuse JsonSchemaTools and JsonDiff instead of rebuilding them. Notes for the owner: (a) the runner must keep the body or a schema-digest per result, since it currently discards bodies; (b) JsonSchemaTools.infer does not infer enums, and the CLI would need a schema-vs-schema diff (new code) for 'became nullable' and 'new enum value'; (c) 'Accept change' must rewrite the jsonSchema assertion's compact-JSON 'expected' string; (d) the Dart-DTO breaking label is new logic, because the Dart Studio generators do not expose their nullability rules as a reusable check.", "competitors": "Verified: Keploy self-healing API tests (https://keploy.io/docs/running-keploy/self-healing-ai-api-tests/) analyze the expected-vs-actual diff and propose updated assertions for review, but the page does not explain how real regressions are told apart from legitimate changes. Beeceptor contract drift detection (https://beeceptor.com/docs/openapi-contract-drift-detection/) flags schema and status-code mismatches in live traffic against an OpenAPI baseline and proposes a spec patch. The Dart null-cast article (https://startdebugging.net/2026/07/fix-type-null-is-not-a-subtype-of-type-in-dart/) exists and says missing or null JSON keys are the overwhelmingly common cause. Diffy does the noise-learning pattern with a primary and a secondary instance running identical known-good code, so their disagreement is the noise baseline. The candidate's Diffy link (thehackernews.com/search/label/Diffy) is wrong and should be replaced with https://github.com/opendiffy/diffy. oasdiff (https://github.com/tufin/oasdiff), Jest snapshots (https://jestjs.io/docs/snapshot-testing) and Verify (https://github.com/VerifyTests/Verify) are well-known tools I did not re-fetch. My web search budget ran out, so I could not check whether Postman, Bruno, Insomnia or Hoppscotch ship a baseline or snapshot or drift feature. Do not claim 'no GUI API client has this' without checking that. Postman and Bruno are known only for hand-written assertions plus schema validation."
- **Design in this codebase:** VERDICT: feasible without a rewrite. Most of the engine already exists. Effort stays M (about 1.5 weeks for the app side, plus 2-3 days for CLI and OpenAPI seeding). I recommend phasing it (see the end).

WHAT ALREADY EXISTS AND CAN BE REUSED (all pure Dart, so web, mobile and the CLI can all use it)
- lib/features/response_tools/domain/services/json_schema_tools.dart: JsonSchemaTools.infer(samples) and validate(). infer already merges several samples, treats a field missing from some samples as not required, and turns null plus a type into a type list.
- lib/features/response_tools/domain/services/json_diff.dart: JsonDiff.compare, with ignoreKeys and JSON paths.
- lib/features/scripting: the jsonSchema assertion type already runs in the app and in the CLI WorkspaceRunner (assertion_evaluator.dart). ScriptsJsonCodec and RequestScriptsRepository already persist assertions.
- The Schema tab (response_tools/presentation/widgets/schema_tab.dart) already does "infer, then save as test" for ONE request. The candidate is the bulk, collection-wide, drift-aware version of that.
- CollectionRunnerService (request_builder/domain/services/collection_runner_service.dart) already loops over requests, sends them, chains extractors and runs scripts.
- ResponseExampleRepository holds the examples (it has add and delete only, no update, so replacing an example means delete plus add).
- SecretMasker (documentation/domain/services/secret_masker.dart) can mask JSON string fields.
- OpenApiRefreshDialog plus RefreshOpenApiUseCase (plan/apply) is the exact pattern to copy: ToolDialog, a pure planner and a use case.

DESIGN
1. Where the baseline lives. Do NOT add a Drift table. A new table means schemaVersion 5, a build_runner regeneration, and Backup, Git doc mapper and CLI plumbing. The C: drive is at 99% (3.1 GB free), and the web worker is known to be fragile on schema upgrades (see the comment in app_database.dart).
   - The baseline is a set of generated assertions in the request's existing Tests, plus an example.
   - Add an optional `baseline: true` flag to AssertionEntity (scripting/domain/entities/assertion_entity.dart) and read and write it in ScriptsJsonCodec. Without the flag, saving the Tests tab would drop any extra key, because encodeAssertions rebuilds the list from entities.
   - This gives three things for free. It syncs through Git: tests.assertions already sync, DocValues.jsonList passes extra keys through, and the schema holds no response values, only keys and types. `postpilot run` and CI enforce the baseline with no new flag (they already evaluate jsonSchema assertions). The runner report goes red for baseline failures.
   - The response example stays local. Examples are not git-synced (grep of git_sync finds no examples). They only travel in Backup export.
2. New feature folder lib/features/api_baseline/ with domain/services, domain/usecases, presentation.
   - domain/services/schema_drift.dart (pure Dart, shared by the app and the CLI): compares the baseline schema with infer([newBody]) and returns findings {path, kind: removed | typeChanged | becameNullable | added | statusChanged, severity: breaking | nonBreaking}. Rules: a removed field, a changed type, or a null where the baseline was non-null is breaking (this is what crashes fromJson). An added field is non-breaking. A status change is breaking.
   - domain/usecases/record_baseline_usecase.dart: for each selected request it sends, runs infer, writes a statusEquals assertion and a flagged jsonSchema assertion, and saves one example named "Baseline". It uses SendRequestUseCase and the existing runner loop. It needs one small change to CollectionRunnerService: an optional hook or keepBody flag, because `_withoutBody` strips the body before anyone else can read it.
   - domain/usecases/check_drift_usecase.dart: runs the requests and returns findings per request. Pattern: plan, then apply, as in RefreshOpenApiUseCase.
   - "Accept change": a plan/apply step that re-infers and replaces only the flagged assertions and the "Baseline" example. See the corrections below for what it must NOT do.
3. UI. One new item in PaletteItems (command_palette/presentation/palette_items.dart, a one-line registration) and ONE collection menu entry, "API baseline & drift…". The collection popup in collections_sidebar.dart already has about 20 items, so do NOT add two ("Record baseline" and "Check drift"). The entry opens a ToolDialog (core/widgets/tool_dialog.dart) with two ToolTabs: Record and Check. The Check tab lists findings per request with breaking and non-breaking chips, a before/after view, and an "Accept" button per request or for all. The diff row widget in compare_tab.dart (_ChangeRow) is private, so extract it (S). The runner report shows a small Drift chip per result and does not touch the request/response views.
4. CLI. Because the baseline is a normal assertion, `postpilot run` already fails on it. Add only `--drift` (or print findings when a flagged assertion fails) in cli/reporters.dart, using the same pure SchemaDrift. cli_main.dart and workspace_runner.dart are already pure Dart with a dart:io sender, so no new conditional imports. For web and mobile no conditional import is needed except the existing downloadFile if a drift report is exported.
5. DI: register the use cases lazily in core/di/injector.dart and add a case to test/di_wiring_test.dart. New tests go beside test/assertion_evaluator_test.dart and test/collection_runner_test.dart.

CORRECTIONS TO THE CANDIDATE (things that would otherwise be wrong or unsafe)
a) ODOO PERSONA BREAKS WITH "GET ONLY". Odoo JSON-2 reads are POST requests (/json/2/<model>/search_read and similar), and ProductionDetector.changesData treats every POST as a write. A GET-only safe mode would skip every Odoo read. We need an explicit read-only allowlist for Odoo methods (search_read, read, search_count, fields_get, name_search, read_group, web_search_read, web_read, default_get), matched on the last URL segment under /json/2/, plus a per-request "safe to record" opt-in for other POST reads such as GraphQL queries and search endpoints.
b) ODOO RETURNS `false` FOR UNSET FIELDS. Inference would learn boolean on one record and string on another, giving false "type changed" drift. The inference step needs an Odoo normalisation: a boolean mixed with a non-boolean type becomes nullable of that type.
c) INFERENCE BUG TO FIX FIRST. JsonSchemaTools._finish emits `type: 'null'` for a field that is null in every sample. A later string value then fails with "Expected null" (a false breaking report). A field seen only as null must be left without a type.
d) "NEW ENUM VALUE" CANNOT BE DONE FROM RESPONSES. infer() does not produce enums, and from one or two samples a status string cannot be told from a free-text string. Doing it would flood users with false positives. Drop it from v1 and only report enum drift when the schema came from an OpenAPI spec.
e) THE "LEARN NOISE BY SENDING EACH GET TWICE" STEP SHOULD BE OPTIONAL. The baseline is a schema (shape), not values, so a second send adds little. It does help when the example is later value-diffed. Make it a checkbox, off by default. It doubles the traffic (rate limits, paid APIs, Odoo load) and the existing CollectionRunOptions.delay should apply.
f) THE TIME CEILING SHOULD NOT BE A DEFAULT. responseTimeBelowMs differs per machine, VPN and CI runner and would be the main source of flaky reds. Make it opt-in, or set it to something very loose (for example 5x the median, at least 1000 ms).
g) "ACCEPT CHANGE REWRITES AFFECTED ASSERTIONS" IS TOO BROAD. Auto-editing hand-written jsonPathEquals or jsonPathExists assertions needs rename detection, which is a heuristic and unsafe. Accept only rewrites assertions carrying the baseline flag. Hand-written assertions that now fail are listed with a "review" hint, never auto-edited.
h) OPENAPI SEEDING IS NOT FREE. openapi_parser.dart does not read response schemas at all today (only request bodies, examples and parameters), and ImportedRequest has no response field. It needs: read responses.2xx.content.*.schema, inline $ref with the existing _flatten and cycle guard (an assertion is per request, so it cannot point at components), then store a flagged jsonSchema assertion. Treat it as a separate follow-up (about 3-4 days), not part of v1.
i) Folder scope. The runner today only takes a collection (requestsIn(collectionId)). RequestSummaryEntity has a folderId, so a folder filter is easy, but it has to be added.
j) A response with `truncated == true` (cut off at the size limit) must be skipped. A partial JSON body would record a wrong baseline.
k) Chained flows. A login POST that fills a token variable is not a "safe" request. Offer "include requests that have extractors" with a confirm. Without it, collections whose auth comes from an extractor will 401 during recording.

SUGGESTED PHASES
v1 (about 1 week): the baseline flag, SchemaDrift, the inference fixes (c, b), recording with the safe-method rules (a), the dialog with Check and Accept, the palette entry, and the runner chip.
v2 (2-3 days): the CLI --drift output and the junit/json reporter fields.
v3 (3-4 days): OpenAPI response seeding and enum drift.
- **Risks:** LEGAL, TERMS OF SERVICE AND STORE POLICY: no blocker. Everything is local client-side processing of the user's own API responses and uses the existing ApiClient. No new third-party service, no new permission, no new dependency. Because it only sends ordinary HTTP requests to endpoints the user already configured, it raises no app-store issue beyond what PostPilot already has. The optional AI "why" text would reuse the existing Anthropic-key flow (BYO key, credentials masked before sending) and should send the finding list, never response bodies.

DATA AND SECURITY (the real risks)
1. Production data capture. Recording against a production-named environment stores real customer data in the local example (SQLite or IndexedDB), and Backup export carries examples. Mitigation: by default store the schema only. Save the example only on explicit opt-in. Never save an example when the active environment is a production one (ProductionDetector.isProduction). When saving, pass bodies through SecretMasker and trim arrays to a few items. Show a warning before Backup or Git push that examples are included.
2. The Git-synced part (flagged assertions) holds only keys, types and formats, no values. Key names can still be sensitive for a private API. State it in the dialog. The existing SecretFields policy does not blank assertions, which is fine for schemas.
3. Safety of sending. Reads are not always harmless: some GETs have side effects (counters, "mark as read", paid or metered APIs), and a baseline sends each request once or twice. Use the same ProductionGuard gate as the runner (collection_runner_dialog.dart calls guard.checkRun). Default to the safe set (GET/HEAD plus the Odoo read allowlist). Show a count and a preview of requests before the run. Respect the delay setting to avoid rate limits.
4. The runner currently keeps no body (`_withoutBody`). The hook to read the body must only live for the duration of the drift analysis and must not be added to CollectionRunResult, otherwise 1000 iterations of bodies sit in memory.

FUNCTIONAL RISKS
5. False positives destroy trust: pagination and empty lists (no items to infer from), dictionaries with dynamic keys, optional fields that appear only for some records, and the Odoo `false` value. Mitigations: fixes b and c, treat an empty list as unknown (no check), cap properties for dynamic-key maps, and let the user mark a path as "ignore" (it is stored in the flagged assertion).
6. Git merge conflicts: a compact schema string in one assertion conflicts when two developers accept different drift. It resolves in the existing conflict dialog, but pretty-print the schema (stable key order, as JsonSchemaTools.encode does) so diffs stay readable.
7. Performance: infer and drift on a multi-MB body on the UI isolate can freeze the window. Run it with compute (the same pattern as RefreshOpenApiUseCase). On web compute runs inline, so cap the body size analysed.
8. Web: CORS applies exactly as it does for normal sends. Recording and checking work on web, but only for APIs that allow the web origin. The CLI part (--drift) needs dart:io and is desktop and CI only.
9. Scope creep: "AI-ish" self-healing (rewriting hand-written assertions) is where trust is lost. Keep Accept deterministic and limited to flagged assertions.

OVERALL: acceptable. No rewrite, no schema migration, no new dependency, and the CLI and Git sync work through the existing assertion path. The two things that must be built right are the safe-send rules (Odoo POST reads) and the false-positive controls.
- **Strongest objection:** The claimed 24 min/week (12 min x 2 uses) does not hold up, for four reasons.

1. The "1-2 min per assertion" premise is already refuted by the code. The Schema tab (schema_tab.dart) already infers a JSON Schema from the last response and has a "Save as test" button that stores it as a jsonSchema assertion, and assertion types already include status, response time and path-exists. Covering 15 endpoints today costs about 10 seconds each after sending, so roughly 2-3 minutes in total, not 15-30. The real delta of this candidate is only the bulk version and the Accept loop. Also, devs who currently ship untested collections spend 0 minutes on this, so the saving is value created, not time saved.

2. The frequency is invented. Recording a baseline happens once per collection (maybe once a month), not twice a week. Drift only helps if something runs the check regularly. PostPilot has no scheduler or monitor. The check runs only when the dev clicks it, and at that point they usually already suspect a change. A Flutter 'Null is not a subtype' crash is typically diagnosed in 2-5 minutes by looking at the one failing response, not 15-30. Backend teams that own the API already have contract tests in CI (Schemathesis, oasdiff, Pact, or Postman monitors). Neither Postman, Insomnia, Bruno nor Hoppscotch ships a snapshot-style baseline feature, which suggests the demand is low.

3. The Odoo persona is mostly excluded. Odoo 19 JSON-2 is POST-only (/json/2/<model>/<method>; odoo_json2.dart confirms this), so the "GET/HEAD only, safe by default" rule skips every Odoo request. Supporting Odoo would need an allowlist of read methods (search_read, read, search_count, fields_get) and still a prod-lock confirm. That adds effort and shrinks the claimed 'all' persona.

4. There are real complexity and noise costs.
   - Sending each request twice to learn volatile fields only matters for value snapshots. Type and schema drift needs no second send.
   - Optional fields and variable-length arrays from one or two samples give false drift alarms, and devs turn noisy guards off.
   - Token expiry, pagination and environment-dependent data make baselines flaky.
   - 'Accept change' rewriting assertions adds a way to silently bless a real regression.
   - The Git-synced baselines add a leak and merge-conflict surface.

Salvageable S-sized pieces: a bulk "Save schema as test for every request in the last runner run", and a "Re-infer schema" button on a failing jsonSchema assertion. Together these would save about 3-5 min/week.
- **Platform notes:** Pure Dart, all platforms (CORS applies on web). Baseline JSON goes into the Git-synced workspace but response values stay local unless the dev opts in; reuse SecretMasker and warn before pushing prod snapshots. Safe by default: GET/HEAD only, production lock applies. Deterministic first; AI only for the optional 'why' text. CLI use of the drift check needs dart:io (desktop/CI).

### 25. Run triage: cluster failures by cause, re-run failed only, flaky/slow history, one-click GitHub issue
- **Score 1.2** | effort L | ~6 min/week saved | persona qa | lenses: competitors, qa-testing, productivity-ux, ai-agentic | skeptic votes 2/3
- **Problem today:** A runner pass with 9-14 red rows is read one by one; often most share one cause (expired token, unresolved {{orderId}} because an earlier extractor failed, wrong env, removed endpoint). After fixing the cause the dev re-runs the whole collection to see if those rows turn green. Run results vanish when the dialog closes, so flaky vs real and 'slower than yesterday' are guesses, and a real bug becomes a hand-written report (about 8 min: copy URL, headers, body, mask secrets).
- **What PostPilot would do:** The run report auto-clusters failures ('5 x 401 same auth source', '2 x {{orderId}} unresolved (extractor in Create order failed)', '1 x timeout', '1 x assertion data.total'), each with a suggested fix button (re-auth, open variable, open extractor). Header buttons: 'Re-run failed only' and 'Re-run from first failure', plus 'Skip in future runs'. Every run is stored (per-request pass-rate strip, Flaky and Slower-than-usual tags). 'Create GitHub issue' builds a masked repro (existing bug-report builder, cURL) and posts after a preview and explicit confirm. Optional 'Diagnose with AI' adds a root-cause sentence from a masked evidence pack.
- **Where it lives:** Existing Collection Runner results pane (cluster strip, two buttons, History tab); 'Create GitHub issue' on the Share tab of Response tools; sidebar status dot and 'last run failed' filter. CLI: --rerun-failed, --retry N with JUnit flakyFailure, cluster lines in console and report.
- **Partly exists already:** Only the substrate exists; none of the headline features do. Checked by reading lib/ and bin/ plus grep for retry, rerun, flaky, cluster, slower, rootCause and GitHub /issues; README has nothing either.

Present today:
- lib/features/collections/presentation/widgets/collection_runner_dialog.dart: setup and results pane with a summary strip (Requests, Passed, Failed, Tests, Total and Avg time) and a flat per-request list. The only buttons are Copy JSON, Copy CSV, Download, Edit setup, and Run/Run again, which re-runs the whole collection. "Stop on first failure" is a setup checkbox.
- lib/features/collections/presentation/view_models/collection_runner_view_model.dart: results live only in memory (results, _iterationList). Nothing is persisted; start() clears them.
- lib/features/request_builder/domain/services/collection_run_report.dart: summary and JSON/CSV export, with no grouping or cause analysis.
- lib/features/request_builder/domain/services/collection_runner_service.dart: run() always iterates every request in the collection. There is no id filter, no start-from-index and no skip flag.
- lib/core/database/tables/requests_table.dart: no enabled or skip column, so "Skip in future runs" has no home yet.
- lib/features/cli/cli_main.dart, reporters.dart and workspace_runner.dart: flags are only env, collection, folder, var, bail, timeout, delay, insecure, report and out. There is no --retry or --rerun-failed. The JUnit writer emits only skipped and failure, with no flakyFailure. The CLI's "skipped" means unsupported auth, not a user choice.
- lib/features/response_tools/domain/services/bug_report_builder.dart and share_tab.dart: a masked Markdown bug report for ONE request/response that the user copies by hand. No GitHub Issues API call anywhere (the only api.github.com calls are for Git sync in workplace_repository_impl.dart and github_http.dart). No run-level report or repro.
- lib/features/response_tools/domain/services/response_history.dart: in-memory, last 8 responses per request, used only for "compare with previous". lib/core/database/tables/history_table.dart: per-send log with no run id or pass/fail, so it can't give a pass-rate strip or a flaky tag.
- lib/features/ai_assistant/domain/ai_tasks.dart: AI explains a single request/response only. No evidence pack for a whole failed run.
- No sidebar status dot or "last run failed" filter in collections_sidebar.dart; the only dot is the environment pill.

Reusable plumbing: SecretMasker, BugReportBuilder, the Anthropic client, the GitHub token and HTTP helper, and CollectionRunResult.failures and error, which have enough detail to cluster on.
- **Competitors:** Verified by fetching:
- Postman, Jan 2026 (https://blog.postman.com/january-2026-product-updates/): the Collection Runner gets an Errors tab that separates test failures from network, script and timeout errors. Jira tickets can be created from monitor results. Agent Mode can create Jira and GitHub issues with context. So the Jira/GitHub claim is accurate but narrower than "from run results": it is monitor results plus Agent Mode.
- Postman docs (https://learning.postman.com/docs/collections/running-collections/intro-to-collection-runs/): the old URL in the candidate's evidence is stale. The page shows a per-collection Runs tab with past runs filterable by user, status and source, plus Run Again, New Run and Automate Run. It has no re-run-failed-only and no run comparison. Run history is therefore parity with Postman, not a differentiator. Failed-only re-run and cause clustering would be ahead of it.
- Checkly Rocky AI (https://feedback.checklyhq.com/changelog/rocky-ai-is-now-generally-available): GA on 2026-03-04, with root-cause analysis that categorizes and summarizes failures from monitors and checks. Confirmed.
- Bruno #1039 (https://github.com/usebruno/bruno/issues/1039): confirmed as "Flag a request to be exclude from test runner", opened 2023-11-23, labelled enhancement and module-collection-runner. The "17 reactions" figure could not be confirmed because the page showed no count, so drop or soften that number.
- Not re-fetched, because the WebSearch budget is exhausted: the Datadog flaky-tests and Maven Surefire flakyFailure URLs. They are well-known docs and plausible.

Recommendations:
- Pass, but split it. It is five features under one title, and the value is very uneven.
- Build first, and cheapest: failure clustering (a pure function over CollectionRunResult) plus "Re-run failed only" and "Re-run from first failure". Needs a request-id filter in CollectionRunnerService.run and a button in the dialog. Effort S to M. This is where the 6 min x 4 per week estimate is plausible.
- Re-run failed only: extractor-chained requests need the variables saved by earlier passed requests. Extractors write to environment or global scope, so those persist, but a failed-only re-run can still send stale values. "From first failure" is the safer default.
- Run history with flaky/slower tags: needs a new Drift table, a schema migration, and several runs before any tag means anything. Postman already has run history, so this part is parity. Do it second.
- One-click GitHub issue: the cheapest honest version is a run-level Markdown report built from the existing BugReportBuilder, copied rather than posted. Only add the Issues API if a PAT with Issues write is acceptable. Not worth it for solo users.
- AI diagnose is optional and a lower priority.
- CLI --retry and --rerun-failed: --retry on non-idempotent methods is risky, as the candidate itself notes.
- **Design in this codebase:** VERDICT: feasible without a rewrite. It bundles five features, though, and the "M" estimate only holds for the first slice. Corrected: L as a bundle (about 3-4 weeks), best shipped as M + M + S. Each slice is useful alone.

WHAT THE CODE LOOKS LIKE TODAY (read in lib/):
- The app runner is `request_builder/domain/services/collection_runner_service.dart`. It streams `CollectionRunResult` (summary, response without body, scripts, error, iteration).
- `_withoutBody` throws the response body away, and the result keeps no spec, no unresolved-variable info and no error kind, only `e.toString()`.
- `run(collectionId)` always runs the whole collection. `collection_runner_view_model.dart` clears the results on `start` and holds results only in memory.
- `collection_runner_dialog.dart` is a raw `Dialog` (720x640), not `ToolDialog`.
- The CLI has a second, separate engine: `cli/workspace_runner.dart` (`RequestOutcome`, `RunState`, pure Dart). It has no retry and no flaky concept; `cli/reporters.dart` has JUnit with `failure` and `skipped` only.

PHASE 1 (M, 1-1.5 weeks): cluster strip + "Re-run failed only" + "Re-run from first failure"
- New feature folder `lib/features/run_triage/`.
  - `domain/services/failure_clusterer.dart` is pure Dart with no Flutter import, so the CLI can use it too. It takes a neutral list of `TriageRow` (requestId, name, status, error kind, failed assertion names, failed extractor keys, `{{tokens}}` in the request template, auth-inherited flag). Both `CollectionRunResult` and the CLI `RequestOutcome` adapt to it, so there is one clusterer and not two. Put structured fields on `RequestOutcome`; do not parse its strings.
  - Cluster rules: group by (status + auth source), by error kind (timeout, connection), by assertion name, and by "failed extractor key K is used as {{K}} in a later request's template".
- Engine changes, all small and backward compatible:
  - Add an optional `onPrepared(ResolvedRequestSpec)` callback to `SendRequestUseCase.call`. It is currently the only place the resolved spec exists, and the runner needs it to see leftover `{{tokens}}` and to build a repro.
  - In `_sendOne`, store `NetworkException.kind`, the auth-inherited flag, and a masked 2 KB body snippet for failed rows only. Mask at capture time with `SecretMasker.maskBody` and never keep the raw body.
  - Give `run()` an optional plan of (iteration, requestId) steps. The view model builds the plan for "failed only" and "from first failure" and merges the new results over the old rows.
- UI: move the dialog onto `ToolDialog` (the shared frame the other tools use) and add a collapsible cluster strip above the results list. Fix buttons start as "Open request" (close the dialog, then `ShellViewModel.selectRequest(id)`) and "Open environment". Add "Re-auth" later.
- The re-run buttons MUST go through the same `ProductionGuard.checkRun(writes, ...)` + `confirmProductionSend` flow as `_run()` in the dialog. Count only the writes in the subset.

PHASE 2 (M): run history, "Unstable" / "Slower" tags, CLI retry
- Schema v4 to v5 using only `m.createTable` + `m.createIndex`. `app_database.dart` documents that `addColumn` crashes the drift web worker, so no ALTER TABLE.
- Two tables:
  - `run_records` (id, collectionId FK cascade, startedAt, environment name, completed, passed, failed, durationMs).
  - `run_request_stats` (runId FK cascade, requestId FK cascade to Requests like `response_examples`, passes, fails, avgMs, maxMs, lastStatus, clusterKey). Store per-request aggregates, not per iteration. Runs allow up to 1000 iterations, so per-pass rows could reach millions.
- Keep the last 50 runs per collection. Write in one transaction at `_onDone`. Mark stopped or disposed runs `completed=false` and leave them out of baselines.
- Request ids are stable across Git pulls (rows are updated in place; see `reopen_replaced_requests.dart`), so use the local id, not the Git uid.
- Like `HistoryEntries`, the tables stay OUT of `workplaceDataChanges()` and out of the backup/workspace JSON. Otherwise every run would trigger workspace autosave and make Git look dirty. Cascade handles `clearWorkplaceData`.
- UI: a "History" tab inside the migrated `ToolDialog`, shown only when at least one run is stored.
- Skip the sidebar status dot and the "last run failed" filter in v1. They touch `CollectionsViewModel.requestIdFilter` and add clutter.
- "Skip in future runs": do not add a column on `requests`. Add a `skipInRuns` key to `RequestSettings` instead (the JSON bag in `request_settings_table`). The typed entity must get the new field, because its `fromJson`/`toJson` drops unknown keys on the next settings save. This also syncs to the team through Git, which Bruno issue #1039 users want. Check that the CLI snapshot carries request settings so `postpilot run` honors the flag.
- CLI: `--retry N` (in-process, per request) and `flakyFailure` in the JUnit reporter. Cut `--rerun-failed` (see risks). Cluster lines in console/JSON output come for free from the shared clusterer.

PHASE 3 (S-M): GitHub issue and AI diagnose
- GitHub issue:
  - Add `createIssue` as a separate small interface (for example `GitIssueClient`) implemented on top of `GitHubHttp.post`, not by extending `GitHostClient`. Extending it would force changes to `FakeGitHost` and every implementer.
  - Reuse the PAT in `SecureGitCredentialsStore` and `BugReportBuilder`.
  - Ship it first as a button on the existing Share tab (S), because the Share tab has a real request and response in hand. Add it per runner row afterwards, using the masked snippet captured in phase 1.
  - Target repo: default to the collection's `GitLink` repo, with an override field parsed by `RepoRef.parse`. The workspace repo is often not the API's code repo.
  - Flow: `getRepo` to read `isPrivate`, then a full preview, then an explicit confirm. Never auto-post.
- AI diagnose: `AiClient.complete` plus a new task in `ai_tasks.dart`. It already masks and shows an exact "Sent to Anthropic" preview in `ai_tab.dart`. Keep it opt-in per click.

PLATFORMS
- All pure Dart. Drift runs on web (wasm) and the repo already uses `createTable` there. No `dart:io` is needed in the new code.
- `api.github.com` is CORS-friendly. `GitHubHttp` already restricts itself to the allowed request headers, and POST with a JSON `Content-Type` is within them.
- Web and mobile only know `NetworkErrorKind` (timeout / connectionError / badResponse / cancelled / other). The cluster says "connection error", never refused vs DNS.
- **Risks:** CORRECTIONS TO THE CANDIDATE
1. Stale variables hide the "{{orderId}} unresolved" case in the app. `RunRequestScriptsUseCase` writes extracted values to the environment/global tables in the DB, so after a failed extractor the next request resolves `{{orderId}}` to the previous run's value. Result: a wrong id and a 404, not a literal `{{orderId}}`. Only the CLI (in-memory `RunState`) shows literal unresolved tokens. The clusterer must link a failed extractor key to later requests whose template uses `{{key}}`. A scan for leftover tokens is not enough. Also scan only URL and headers; bodies can legitimately hold `{{ }}` (Mustache, Handlebars).
2. CLI `--rerun-failed` does not work as proposed. Chained variables live only in the process, so a failed-only second process loses the token and `orderId` that earlier passed requests produced. Cut it from v1, or add it later with automatic inclusion of the producer requests (found from extractor keys against templates).
3. Retry safety is not "GET only" for Odoo. Odoo JSON-2 read calls (search_read, read, search_count, fields_get, name_search) are POST. A method-based rule would block retrying them, and a blanket retry would hit real writes. Make retry opt-in per request, with an Odoo-read allowlist as the default for Odoo requests.
4. "Flaky" is an overclaim. Datadog's definition needs the same commit with differing results, and PostPilot has no commit or retry data. Use "Unstable (failed 3 of last 10)" and "Slower than usual". Record the environment name on every run and compute baselines per environment. Require at least 5 completed runs. Use a median-based threshold, for example above 2x the median and more than 300 ms over it.
5. Run rows carry no body and no spec today. "Create GitHub issue" from a runner row needs the masked failure snippet captured in phase 1. Otherwise only the Share tab can make the issue.
6. "Skip in future runs" cannot be a new column on `requests` because of the drift web-worker `addColumn` crash. Use the `RequestSettings` JSON key described in the design.
7. The usage numbers (6 min x 4/week) hold for QA and backend developers who run collections. Flutter and Odoo developers run them rarely, so expect closer to 2-3 min saved per week for them.

SECURITY AND PRIVACY (acceptable with mitigations)
- A GitHub issue posted to a public repo leaks. `SecretMasker` masks credentials by key name and known token shapes. It does NOT mask personal or business data in bodies, such as Odoo partner records, invoices or emails. Required mitigations:
  - Check `isPrivate` through `getRepo` and show a red banner for public repos.
  - Leave the response body OFF by default. The user turns it on and sees the exact text.
  - Always show a preview and require an explicit confirm.
  - Post only on a click, one issue at a time.
- Creating the issue needs a token with Issues: write. Fine-grained PATs scoped to Contents only will return 403. Map 403 to "Your token needs the Issues: Read and write permission", 410 to "Issues are disabled on this repo", and 422 to the secondary rate limit.
- GitHub docs say 410 is returned when issues are disabled, 403 for insufficient permission, and 422 may be a secondary rate limit when creating content too fast.
- ToS and store policy: a user-initiated REST call with the user's own token is ordinary API use, the same class as the existing Git sync. There is no new platform permission, so no new App Store or Play Store review risk. "Diagnose with AI" sends data to Anthropic with the user's own key. Keep it opt-in, with a preview of the payload, as `ai_tab.dart` does now.
- The `flakyFailure` JUnit element is a Maven Surefire extension. It is not in the core JUnit schema. Verify GitHub Actions and GitLab report parsers tolerate it before relying on it. The standard `<failure>` must stay as the fallback.

ENGINEERING RISKS
- Two engines exist (app runner on the DB, CLI runner on a snapshot). The shared pure-Dart clusterer plus a neutral adapter is the only way to avoid drifting behavior. Never duplicate the logic.
- The re-run buttons are a new "send" path. If they skip `ProductionGuard`, they could send writes to a production environment without a prompt. This is the most serious bug class in this feature, so add a test for it.
- The persisted history needs bounds: 50 runs per collection, per-request aggregates only, one batched write. Without bounds the wasm DB (IndexedDB-backed) will slow down on 1000-iteration data runs.
- Adding tables means regenerating `app_database.g.dart` with build_runner. Project memory notes that the C: drive was full and blocked builds, so check disk space first. Add DI registrations to `test/di_wiring_test.dart`. Extend `test/collection_runner_test.dart` for the plan and rerun logic.
- Dialog migration to `ToolDialog` is a visual change to an existing screen. Keep the setup and results behavior identical and do it as its own commit.
- Not a legal blocker. No rewrite is needed. Recommended order: Phase 1, then Phase 2, then Phase 3, with the Share-tab issue button and AI diagnose pulled forward only if wanted earlier.
- **Strongest objection:** The claimed 6 min x 4 uses/week = 24 min/week does not hold up. It is a bundle of five features, and each one is smaller than it looks once checked against the PostPilot code (web search was unavailable, so the demand evidence is not independently checked).

1. Clustering failures by cause. Each failed row in collection_runner_dialog.dart already shows the failed assertion names and the failed extractor ("variables: x/y saved - <error>"). The runner also already has a 'stop on failure' option, which prevents most of the cascade of 9-14 red rows where one early extractor failed. Clustering saves roughly 1-2 min, and only on runs that have many reds with a shared cause. That is maybe once a week, not on every run.

2. 'Re-run failed only' and 'from first failure'. A typical collection runs in 10-60 seconds, so skipping the green rows saves seconds. 'Failed only' is also unreliable for chained flows: the passing 'Create order' request that sets {{orderId}} is skipped, so the failed row breaks again. The dialog already has 'Run again' and 'Edit setup'. 'Re-run from first failure' is the only variant that is correct for chains, and it is a small change (S). Real saving: about 0.3-0.5 min x 2/week, about 1 min/week.

3. Run history, flaky and 'slower than usual' tags. This needs weeks of comparable runs. Manual desktop runs are not comparable (different env, data, time of day). Teams that care about flakiness already get it from CI (JUnit report plus GitHub Actions or Datadog). The cost is a new Drift table and its UI. Realistic saving is about 1 min/week, and it is a 'nice to have' rather than a chore removed.

4. One-click GitHub issue. The '8 min hand-written report' is outdated. PostPilot already has bug_report_builder.dart with masked cURL, headers and body, plus a Share tab. What is left is pasting that text into github.com/new, about 1 min. Most teams in the target personas (backend, Odoo agencies, QA) track bugs in Jira, Linear or Odoo tasks, not GitHub Issues. Posting also needs a second PAT with Issues:write and goes through a preview and confirm step, which is exactly the OAuth/login friction the owner has postponed. Real saving: about 2-3 min/week, and only for GitHub-tracker teams.

5. 'Diagnose with AI'. This duplicates the existing 'Ask AI' and error-explainer features. I count it as 0 min.

Total honest saving is about 5-7 min/week for a QA user and under 3 for a backend, Flutter or Odoo dev. That is below the 10 min bar, and nothing here removes a painful or error-prone chore. M effort for one table plus several surfaces is also poor value.

If anything is kept, take only the cheap, genuinely useful parts as separate small items. (a) Persist the last run, so closing the dialog does not lose it, plus 'Re-run from first failure'. (b) 'Unresolved {{var}} because extractor X failed' as a one-line hint on the failed row. That is the only non-trivial root-cause link, and it is S effort. (c) If GitHub reporting is wanted at all, open a prefilled github.com/OWNER/REPO/issues/new?title=&body= URL in the browser with no token (the URL limit of roughly 8 KB means a truncated body). Do not build flaky history or the AI step now.
- **Platform notes:** Pure Dart, all platforms. Needs one new Drift table (run id, request uid, status, duration, pass, failures; keep last 50 runs). Connection-refused vs DNS is only distinguishable on dart:io; web shows 'network/CORS error'. GitHub issue needs a token with Issues: write (ask separately, secrets masked first). Retry must not apply to POST/PATCH/DELETE by default. AI step is optional and uses the existing Anthropic key.

### 26. OpenAPI test generator: contract, negative, boundary and auth cases (also 'find crashes' on a plain JSON body)
- **Score 1.2** | effort L | ~6 min/week saved | persona qa | lenses: competitors, backend-daily, qa-testing | skeptic votes 2/3
- **Problem today:** After an OpenAPI import every request has only the happy path and no assertions. To really test an endpoint the dev duplicates the request 12-20 times by hand: missing required field, wrong type, null, too long, out of range, invalid enum, no token, bad id, then writes expected status and error-shape assertions (15-30 min per endpoint). Documented response schemas are checked by eye against Swagger UI (5-8 min per endpoint). Cases that return 500 instead of 400/422 are found by luck.
- **What PostPilot would do:** Right-click a request, folder or collection -> 'Generate tests'. A preview dialog lists counts per family: Contract (status documented, Content-Type, body validates against the response schema using the existing JSON Schema evaluator), Negative (missing/null/wrong type/empty/over-length/bad enum/extra field), Boundary (min/max edges), Auth (no/bad token), Not found. Dev unticks what they do not want and clicks Create: a 'Generated tests' folder of ordinary requests with status, not-5xx, no-stack-trace and schema assertions appears, runnable in the Runner and CLI. Without a spec it mutates the current JSON body. A 'Find crashes' quick-run sends the cases (2-4 parallel) and shows one table with 5xx/timeouts in red. 'Check against contract' can also be a runner toggle.
- **Where it lives:** Request/folder/collection context menu 'Generate tests...' (shared ToolDialog with preview) plus palette command; results are a normal sidebar folder, so runner, CLI and Git sync work unchanged. Runner dialog gets a 'Contract' toggle; CLI --contract.
- **Partly exists already:** The generator itself does not exist, only building blocks. I found no code, palette command, CLI flag or README text for negative, boundary or auth case generation, body mutation, contract checking or a "find crashes" run (searched lib/, bin/postpilot.dart and README.md). The parts that exist:
- lib/features/response_tools/domain/services/json_schema_tools.dart: a JSON Schema validator (JsonSchemaTools.validate, with $ref, enum, const and format) and an infer function.
- lib/features/response_tools/presentation/widgets/schema_tab.dart: the dev pastes a schema by hand, clicks Validate, then "Save as test" stores it as a jsonSchema assertion. This is a manual, per-response contract check that never reads the OpenAPI document.
- lib/features/scripting/domain/entities/assertion_entity.dart and lib/features/scripting/domain/evaluator/assertion_evaluator.dart: assertion types are statusEquals, statusIn2xx, bodyContains, jsonPathEquals/Exists, headerEquals/Exists, responseTimeBelowMs and jsonSchema. There is no 4xx-class status, no "body does not contain" and no "type is X", so the candidate's claim that 2-3 new assertion types are needed is correct.
- lib/features/ai_assistant/domain/ai_tasks.dart (testsSystem) and lib/features/ai_assistant/presentation/ai_tab.dart: "Suggest tests" proposes at most 8 happy-path assertions from one response. It does not create requests, and it needs an Anthropic key.
- lib/features/import_export/domain/services/openapi_parser.dart: OpenApiRequestItem has no schema, constraint or response fields. _exampleOf and _valueOf turn schemas into placeholder values (0, "string", true) and discard them. The caveat about retaining or re-reading schemas is accurate. openapi_refresh_planner.dart only matches endpoints by method and path.
- lib/features/request_builder/domain/services/collection_runner_service.dart: it runs requests sequentially with delay, iterations and stopOnFailure. There is no parallel send and no contract toggle. A request with assertions passes on those alone, so generated 4xx-expecting requests would run correctly in the Runner. workspace_runner.dart and the CLI would reuse them unchanged.
- **Competitors:** Verified:
- Schemathesis: https://schemathesis.readthedocs.io/en/stable/reference/checks/ lists not_a_server_error, status_code_conformance, content_type_conformance, response_schema_conformance, negative_data_rejection, positive_data_acceptance, ignored_auth and missing_required_header. It is a CLI/library with no GUI client and no saved requests, so there is a gap for a GUI client.
- Apidog: https://docs.apidog.com/generate-test-cases-1624860m0 generates positive, negative, boundary and security cases with AI, up to 80 at once, with Run / Accept / Discard. This is the closest product match, but it is AI-driven and tied to Apidog's cloud workspace. A deterministic, offline, spec-driven generator is a clear differentiator.
- OneUptime article (2026-08-24, "How to Generate Useful Negative and Boundary Tests from an OpenAPI Schema", https://oneuptime.com/blog/post/2026-08-24-openapi-negative-boundary-tests/view): real, and it matches the one-invalid-value-per-case idea. It covers required fields, types, numeric and string bounds, enum, array cardinality, unique items, additionalProperties and composition schemas.
- Postman: its Agent Mode docs say tests are written only when you ask in natural language. I found no automatic generation of negative or contract tests there (https://learning.postman.com/docs/tests-and-scripts/write-scripts/test-scripts/).

Not verified, so drop or soften it:
- The Thunder Client claim: https://docs.thunderclient.com/features/ai returns 404, and https://www.thunderclient.com/pricing lists no AI test generation (only an "MCP Server (AI)" row). The WebSearch budget ran out, so I could not check further.

Estimate: 15 min x 2 uses/week is plausible. A QA or backend dev does this per new or changed endpoint. 10-15 min x 1-2 per week is the safer number.

Risks to scope: negative cases that unexpectedly return 2xx create junk rows, so the production lock, a rate cap and a stop-after-N-5xx guard are essential. The first version should have the generator keep schemas from the OpenAPI document in the parser, then emit ordinary requests with the three new assertion types. Build parallel "Find crashes" later, since it is the biggest extra piece.
- **Design in this codebase:** VERDICT: feasible without a rewrite and with no new dependency. Corrected effort is L (about 4-5 working weeks) for the full pitch, not M. If phased it splits into an M (no-spec "find crashes" plus body and auth cases) and an M (spec-aware contract, boundary and negative cases).

WHY IT FITS (verified in code)
- The generated output can be ordinary requests with assertions, so Runner, CLI and Git sync work unchanged. features/cli/workspace_runner.dart already runs each request's saved assertions through the shared pure-Dart AssertionEvaluator, so new assertion types work in the CLI automatically.
- `postpilot run ws.json --folder "Generated tests"` already works (RunOptions.folder). So `--contract` and the Runner dialog "Contract toggle" should be dropped: the schemas live inside the assertions, and a toggle would need the spec stored somewhere.
- JsonSchemaTools.validate (features/response_tools/domain/services/json_schema_tools.dart) is pure Dart and covers type, nullable, 3.1 type arrays, required, enum, min/max, length, pattern, format, allOf/anyOf/oneOf and local $ref. The existing `jsonSchema` assertion takes a whole schema document and resolves `#/...` against it. So the generator only has to bundle each operation's response schema plus the transitively referenced `components/schemas` (or `definitions` for Swagger 2) into one JSON document. Do not embed the full components section: that would bloat every assertion on a large spec.
- Sending: SendRequestUseCase and PrepareRequestUseCase work on an in-memory ApiRequestEntity. Variables resolve through request.collectionId, and request settings and history key off the URL or id 0. So "Find crashes" can send unsaved cases directly.

DESIGN
1) New feature folder lib/features/test_generator/.
 - domain/services (pure Dart so the CLI and web can use it):
   - SpecContractExtractor: reuses OpenApiParser's _resolve/_flatten logic and returns per operation the bundled request-body schema, parameters (name, in, required, schema), responses (status -> bundled schema plus content types) and whether security is required.
   - SchemaBundler.
   - TestCaseGenerator: one-constraint-at-a-time cases for the families Contract, Negative, Boundary, Auth, NotFound. A GeneratedCase holds a name, family, an ApiRequestEntity and a List<AssertionEntity>.
   - BodyMutator: used when there is no spec, on the current JSON body.
 - domain/usecases: GenerateTestsUseCase with plan() (counts per family, for the preview) and apply() (creates the folder, requests and scripts).
 - application/presentation: GenerateTestsDialog (ToolDialog) plus a view model, registered in core/di/injector.dart. Add a line to di_wiring_test.
2) Parser change: OpenApiRequestItem and ParsedOpenApiDocument gain optional fields (contract data) with defaults. OpenApiRefreshPlanner and the import use case keep working, and the parser is already run via compute with a large test file (test/openapi_parser_test.dart). Match spec operations to existing requests with OpenApiRefreshPlanner.keyOf. Source of the spec: reuse the Spec URL, Download and paste UI from openapi_refresh_dialog.dart. Extract it into a shared widget and reuse the `openapi.source.<collection>` pref (also set it at import time). Do not add a DB table for the spec.
3) Persistence: apply() uses CollectionRepository.createFolder, RequestRepository.createRequest and saveRequest, and RequestScriptsRepository.save(ScriptsJsonCodec.encodeAssertions). ImportedCollectionWriter does not write scripts, so do not route through it. Make it idempotent: deterministic request names such as "[gen] POST /pets - missing name", skip names that already exist (same principle as the refresh planner), never overwrite edited ones. Contract assertions (status in documented codes, content-type, schema) are appended to the existing happy-path request as opt-in with a preview, not as new requests.
4) New assertion types (3, small): statusIn (expected "400,422" or "4xx"), bodyNotContains (stack-trace markers like Traceback, "at java.", "System.Exception"), headerContains (Content-Type). The enum switches in assertion_entity.dart are exhaustive, so the compiler finds every spot; the editor dropdown lists AssertionType.values automatically. Also fix ScriptsJsonCodec.decodeAssertions: an unknown type currently falls back to statusIn2xx, which would silently invert a negative test on an older app or CLI that reads a synced workspace. Make it an explicit "unsupported assertion" failure.
5) Find crashes: a CrashFinderService with a pool of 2-4 workers that sends the in-memory cases via SendRequestUseCase and evaluates them with AssertionEvaluator. It needs a cancel token (ApiCancelToken exists), a rate cap, and a stop-after-N-5xx guard. CollectionRunnerService is strictly sequential and strips bodies, so it cannot be reused as is. Results appear as a second tab in the same ToolDialog (ToolTabs): Preview, then Results with 5xx/timeouts in red and "Save failing cases as requests".
6) UI placement: 'Generate tests...' in the request, folder and collection popup menus (the collection menu is already about 20 items, so put it next to 'Update from OpenAPI') and one palette entry in palette_items.dart tools(). Nothing is added to the request or response views.
7) Platforms: pure Dart and Dio, so desktop, mobile and web all work. On web the spec URL fetch and the sends are subject to CORS; paste works. ToolDialog goes full screen on phones. No dart:io and no conditional imports are needed.

PHASING
- Phase 1 (M, roughly 2-3 weeks): no-spec body mutation, auth and not-found cases, the 3 assertion types plus codec fix, folder generation, crash finder.
- Phase 2 (M): parser contract retention, bundler, contract and boundary families, spec source widget.
- **Risks:** TECHNICAL RISKS (all fixable, none blocking)
1) False passes from unresolved variables. Negative cases on PUT/DELETE /pets/{{petId}} send the literal "{{petId}}". The server answers 404 and a "4xx" assertion passes without testing anything. SendRequestUseCase does not reject unresolved path variables (the CLI does). Fix: a preflight that lists unresolved {{vars}} and blocks or warns, body-validation cases asserting the documented 400/422 rather than any 4xx, and a separate NotFound family.
2) The shared cookie jar breaks the "no token" case on desktop. DioApiClient adds jar cookies to every request, and LenientCookieManager only prepends the request's own Cookie header, so session-cookie APIs return 200 and the Auth family gives false passes. Fix: a small skip-cookies flag on ApiRequestOptions or a Dio extra honoured by LenientCookieManager (S). On web the browser owns cookies, so the case cannot be controlled there.
3) Placeholders break body mutation. Bodies contain {{var}}, sometimes unquoted (`"id": {{petId}}`), which is not valid JSON. BodyMutator must swap placeholders for sentinels before jsonDecode and restore them after encoding, or skip unparseable bodies with a message. Form and urlencoded bodies need separate handling.
4) Contract false positives from sloppy specs: writeOnly or readOnly fields in `required`, non-nullable properties that return null, loose oneOf (the validator needs exactly one match). Mitigations: untick-able contract items in the preview, and optionally treating readOnly/writeOnly correctly. The validator lacks exclusiveMinimum, multipleOf, uniqueItems and discriminator, so contract checks are weaker than Schemathesis; document that.
5) Valid-edge boundary cases and a missing-auth case against an API with an auth bug really create, change or delete data, and sending garbage can create junk rows. Mitigations: default-off for POST/PUT/PATCH/DELETE boundary cases, DELETE excluded unless ticked, rate cap, stop-after-N-5xx, and a loud failure naming the created id when a mutated case returns 2xx.
6) Production safety: ProductionGuard is called from UI code only (collection_runner_dialog, request_builder_page), not from SendRequestUseCase. The crash finder must call it itself. For fuzzing, hard-refuse on a production-named environment instead of just confirming; this needs a small public method on ProductionGuard because _activeProductionName is private (S).
7) Workspace bloat: about 15 cases per endpoint becomes hundreds of requests in the Git-synced workspace JSON and sidebar, and body copies repeat any pasted real values. Mitigate with a default cap per endpoint, a deselect step, a 'Generated tests' folder that is easy to delete or collapse, and the ephemeral Find crashes path that saves nothing.
8) The spec is not retained anywhere today (the refresh dialog only remembers the URL in SharedPreferences, and only after a Download), so each generation needs a re-download or paste. A Drift table plus migration is possible later but not needed in v1.
9) Test and history noise: every send is recorded in History and the Console. Consider a 'do not record' option for generated runs.

LEGAL / POLICY / SECURITY
- No licensing issue: the idea is a general technique. Implement it from scratch rather than copying Schemathesis (MIT) or Apidog (proprietary) code or text.
- No store-policy concern: it is an HTTP client, with no code download or execution.
- Fuzzing a third-party API without permission can breach its terms or look like abuse. Add a one-time notice ("only run against APIs you own or are allowed to test"), defaults of 2 workers and a rate cap, and no live-production runs.
- Do NOT ship an injection or XSS payload corpus. Keep to schema-derived mutations; this keeps it a contract/negative testing tool, not a pentest tool, and avoids antivirus heuristics and reputational risk.
- Secrets: generated "bad token" cases use a literal fake token and "no token" uses AuthType.none, so real tokens are never copied. Mutated bodies copy the user's own body text, so any secrets pasted into a body get duplicated into generated requests that Git sync will push.

SCOPE CUTS (recommended): drop the Runner "Contract toggle" and the CLI `--contract` flag (redundant with `--folder`), and drop Not-found/Boundary at first if effort has to shrink. The extractor-style "attach contract assertions to existing requests" slice is the best value for effort (about S-M) and could ship first.
- **Strongest objection:** As written this is mostly NEW testing work, not automation of work developers already do, and the claimed 15 min x 2 uses/week is inflated.

1. Who actually does this chore. Writing 12-20 negative/boundary cases per endpoint is a QA habit. A typical Flutter dev consumes APIs and does not test the server's validation. A typical backend dev writes validation tests in their own repo (pytest/Jest/JUnit), where they run in CI against the code, not in a GUI client. Odoo devs have no OpenAPI spec to import (Odoo JSON-2 is model/method based; I could not verify any OpenAPI export because my web-search budget was exhausted), so the Odoo persona gets nothing from the spec-driven part. The real audience is QA plus the minority of backend devs who have an OpenAPI file and care about negative testing.

2. Frequency is wrong. This is a once-per-endpoint or once-per-spec-revision job, not 2x/week for every developer. A QA person onboarding a service might generate a whole folder once, then not touch it for weeks. Averaged over a normal week it is about 1 use x 10 min net, and that only for the QA/backend-with-spec minority. Averaged over all of PostPilot's personas it is roughly 5-6 min/week.

3. The problem statement is partly false. PostPilot already has data-driven runs (run_data_parser.dart plus the runner dialog). So a QA user does not have to duplicate a request 12-20 times; they can run one request against a data file of cases. The real manual cost is thinking up the cases and the expected status, which this does automate, but that is a smaller saving than claimed.

4. The 15 min/use is partly eaten by new overhead.
- Reviewing the preview and unticking families.
- Fixing false positives. Expected status for invalid input differs by framework (Django/Spring return 400, FastAPI returns 422, Express returns whatever the dev wrote), so a blanket '4xx' or 'not 5xx' assertion either passes everything or fails noisily and needs tuning.
- Regenerating after a spec change produces duplicates or stale folders. Fixing that needs diffing and merging logic, like 'Update from OpenAPI', which is effort the plan does not count.

5. Existing alternatives are already fast enough. Schemathesis does the same checks (status, content-type, response-schema conformance, negative-data rejection, ignored auth) with one CLI command and CI output (JUnit, GitHub Actions), and its property-based fuzzing is much deeper than static one-constraint mutations. Its own docs say the tool tests negative scenarios 'instead of me and much faster'. A dev who wants this already runs it. Apidog and Postman AI also generate tests. PostPilot would be a weaker, static copy and would not win a dev who is already in CI.

6. 'Find crashes' on a plain JSON body is a fuzz gimmick that is risky against real servers. It creates junk rows and can hit rate limits or trip prod. The mitigations in the proposal (prod lock, 5xx stop, rate cap) are needed because the feature is dangerous, which is a sign it is not a daily time-saver. Drop it.

What survives and is worth building (S effort):
- On OpenAPI import and 'Update from OpenAPI', auto-attach status-code and response-schema assertions, using the existing JSON Schema evaluator. This removes the real 5-8 min/endpoint 'check against Swagger UI by eye' chore for the whole folder at once, for every dev who imports a spec.
- Optionally a single 'Add missing-required-field / wrong-type / no-token' variant per endpoint as rows in a runner data file. That reuses the existing data-driven runner.

Drop the full Negative/Boundary/Auth/Not-found generator, 'Find crashes', and the CLI --contract flag as a standalone epic. Re-propose the narrow 'auto-assert on import' version instead.

Evidence: https://schemathesis.readthedocs.io/en/stable/ (one-command CLI, CI integration, negative testing, stateful testing). I did not verify the Odoo OpenAPI point online.
- **Platform notes:** Pure Dart, all platforms. OpenApiParser must retain response and request-body schemas ($ref, allOf, 3.0 nullable vs 3.1 type arrays) or re-read the spec via the Update-from-OpenAPI source URL. Needs 2-3 small new assertion types (status class 4xx, body does not contain, type is X). Negative cases can create junk rows: refuse/confirm on production via the existing lock, a 2xx on a mutated case fails loudly with the created id, rate cap and stop-after-N-5xx guard. Web fetch of spec URL limited by CORS (paste works).

### 27. Capture app traffic: reverse-proxy recorder for the Flutter/mobile app (no certificate, no proxy settings)
- **Score 1.2** | effort M | ~3 min/week saved | persona flutter | lenses: pain-points, backend-daily, flutter-daily, observability-debug | skeptic votes 2/3
- **Problem today:** To see what the Flutter app really sends, devs set up Charles/Proxyman/mitmproxy. Dart's HttpClient ignores the system proxy, so they add findProxy/HttpOverrides code, install a CA on the emulator/phone, then rebuild each captured call by hand in the API client (10-20 min per session, 30-60 min first time). Backend devs have the mirror problem: 'show me exactly what the app sent'.
- **What PostPilot would do:** A 'Record' mode starts a local forwarding server (reusing the mock server HttpServer and the Device helper's LAN address/QR) with the real upstream URL. The dev only changes the app's debug baseUrl to http://<LAN-IP>:port; PostPilot forwards (HTTPS upstream works) and lists every exchange live. Select rows -> 'Save to collection' (host to {{baseUrl}}, Authorization/cookies to variables, ids folded to :params), 'Open as request', 'Export HAR', or 'Create mock routes from these' (Replay when the backend is down). Alternative mechanism for the same tab: a ~25-line debug-only Dio interceptor snippet that posts each call to the listener with a one-time token.
- **Where it lives:** Record mode inside the existing Mock server / Device helper dialog (same server engine and log list) plus palette command 'Capture traffic from my app'. No change to request/response views.
- **Competitors:** The evidence URLs checked out. Proxyman's Flutter page says Flutter ignores the system proxy and needs code changes plus a certificate, but it also says Proxyman 6.4.0+ removes that for Android emulators through an automatic VPN. The sarunw.com post confirms the findProxy workaround. Postman proxy capture needs system proxy settings and a CA certificate. Mockoon's logs view has a Record button that auto-creates routes, and it also has a proxy mode. WireMock records stubs by proxying to a target URL. These competitors weaken the novelty, since they already do the forward and record-to-mock part, but none of them is built into an API client with save-to-collection, variable folding and a Flutter/Odoo workflow. The bigger threat to the claimed saving is Flutter DevTools' Network view. The Flutter docs (docs.flutter.dev/tools/devtools/network) say it records dart:io, HttpClient and Dio traffic with headers and bodies and needs no proxy, certificate or code change. It does not document a HAR export. That undercuts the "10-20 min per session" pain for Flutter devs on a plain Dio app. The realistic gain is the missing "save to collection / create mocks" step and the backend-side "what did the app send" case, so 8 min x 3/week is on the optimistic side. A closer estimate is 4-5 min x 2/week for Flutter devs. Also expect a Windows Firewall prompt when binding to the LAN. I could not check Postman, Insomnia, Bruno or Hoppscotch for a built-in reverse-proxy recorder, because the search budget ran out. Verdict: genuinely new relative to the PostPilot code, so it passes the novelty check, but its value rests on the save/mock step rather than the capture itself.
- **Design in this codebase:** VERDICT: buildable cleanly, no rewrite, desktop only. The existing architecture already contains almost every part. Corrected effort is M (about 1.5 weeks for the full scope). A tight v1 is S+ (about 1 week), covering forwarder, live list and detail, Save to collection and HAR export. v2 adds the Dio snippet ingest, a CLI `postpilot record`, and an Odoo RPC hand-off.

FIT WITH THE CODEBASE (checked in the source)
- The mock_server feature is the template: data/*_engine.dart plus _stub plus _io with a conditional import (`if (dart.library.io)`), a domain/ folder, and a session-singleton ViewModel registered in `_registerDevTools()` in lib/core/di/injector.dart. The engine test uses a real socket on port 0 (test/mock_server/mock_server_test.dart), so a recorder test can run against a second local HttpServer as a fake upstream.
- Correction to the candidate: the recorder cannot reuse the MockServerEngine class or MockServerViewModel. The engine interface is `start(config, MockRouteTable)` with a body-less `MockLogEntry`. The VM is a singleton holding one engine for one collection. Both servers must be able to run at the same time (mock on 3001, recorder on 3002).
- So add a new feature folder lib/features/traffic_capture/ and reuse the pieces:
  - `CapturedExchange` entity (method, url, req/resp headers, bodies, status, duration, truncated flag).
  - `TrafficCaptureEngine` interface with stub and io files, same conditional-import trio.
  - `TrafficCaptureViewModel`, a lazy singleton keeping the last 300 entries, as the mock VM does.
  - `TrafficCaptureDialog`, built with ToolDialog.
  - One line in `PaletteItems.tools()` (lib/features/command_palette/presentation/palette_items.dart), 'Capture traffic from my app'.
  - A link from the Mock server banner next to the existing DeviceHelperDialog icon.
- UI placement: the candidate's idea of a Record mode inside the Mock server dialog is wrong. The dialog is a single layout around routes plus a log, and the VM is single-engine. Use its own ToolDialog 'Traffic capture' (use `ToolTabs` or the same two-panel `LayoutBuilder` as mock_server_dialog.dart).
- Reused existing code:
  - `listLocalAddresses()` in device_helper/data/network_info.dart.
  - `DeviceTargets.rewrite` / `forEmulators` to print the exact baseUrl per device kind.
  - `DeviceHelperDialog.show(initialPort:)`.
  - `EmptyHint`, `InfoBanner`, `GradientButton` and `MethodBadge`.
  - `SecretMasker` (documentation/domain/services/secret_masker.dart) for masking.
  - `downloadFile` (core/utils/file_download.dart) for HAR export.
  - `ShellViewModel.selectRequest(id)` for Open as request.
  - `proxyDirectiveFor` / `ProxyConfig` plus `SettingsRepository.current` for the upstream leg, so a corporate proxy and 'Verify SSL' behave as in the rest of the app.
- Net-new pure-Dart code, all unit-testable:
  - `HarWriter`, the mirror of the existing `HarParser`; HAR import exists but nothing writes a HAR.
  - `PathNormalizer`, folding numeric, UUID, ObjectId and long-hex segments.
  - `CapturedToCollection`, with a `SaveCapturedTrafficUseCase` over `CollectionRepository`, `RequestRepository`, `CollectionVariableRepository`, `EnvironmentRepository` and `ResponseExampleRepository`.

FORWARDER (the io engine, about 250 lines)
- Use raw dart:io `HttpServer` plus `HttpClient`, as `ws_connect_io` and `cli/dart_io_sender.dart` already do.
- Do NOT reuse the injected ApiClient. `LoggingApiClient` would write into the Console log, and `DioApiClient`'s LenientCookieManager would inject stored cookies into forwarded requests and alter what the app really sent. `ApiClient` also buffers bodies.
- Concrete dart:io pitfalls:
  - Clear `server.defaultResponseHeaders`. dart:io adds x-frame-options, x-xss-protection, x-content-type-options and a text/plain content type that the upstream never sent.
  - Set `client.autoUncompress = false` and `followRedirects = false`. Pass gzip through raw and decode only for display. dart:io has no brotli, so force Accept-Encoding down to gzip or identity.
  - Rewrite `Location` headers that point at the upstream origin back to the proxy origin, otherwise the app follows a redirect straight past the recorder.
  - Forward Set-Cookie as separate values. cli/dart_io_sender.dart joins header values with ', ', which would corrupt cookies here.
  - Strip hop-by-hop headers (Connection, Keep-Alive, TE, Trailer, Transfer-Encoding, Upgrade). Set Host to the upstream host. Support an upstream path prefix.
  - Handle HEAD, 204 and 304 as no-body responses.
  - Stream request and response bodies, teeing at most about 2 MB into the log with a `truncated` flag.
  - An upstream failure becomes a 502 JSON body plus a log row. A WebSocket upgrade gets 501 in v1.
  - Answer CORS preflight locally (copy `_cors` from mock_server_engine_io.dart) so a Flutter-web app under test works.
- Binding:
  - Default to loopback only. That already covers Android emulator (10.0.2.2), iOS simulator, desktop and Flutter web.
  - Bind to all interfaces only when 'Allow other devices' is ticked (real phones).

SAVE / MOCK / EXPORT (corrections to the candidate)
- Fold ids to `{{userId}}`-style variables plus a collection variable holding the captured value. This matches `OpenApiParser`, which emits `{{name}}`. Do not use `:id`: the request builder has no `:param` substitution (a grep of request_builder found none), so it would be sent literally. `MockRouteTable` accepts `:id`, `{id}` and `{{id}}` anyway.
- Host becomes `{{baseUrl}}`, a collection variable.
- Authorization becomes a `RequestAuth` bearer `{{token}}`. Put the captured token ONLY into the active environment as `isSecret`, which stays local-only per the workspace rules, or leave it empty. Collection variables have no secret flag and are pushed by Git sync.
- 'Create mock routes from these' is free. Save each captured response with `ResponseExampleRepository.add`, then call `MockServerDialog.show(collectionId:)`. `BuildMockRoutesUseCase` already serves them. Do not change the engine. Use a dedicated use case, because `ImportedCollectionWriter.addItems` returns no request ids and `ImportedRequest` has no examples field.
- HAR export masks with `SecretMasker` by default; including secrets is an opt-in.

PLATFORMS
- Web: show the same `EmptyHint` 'Not available in the browser' as the mock dialog. A browser cannot listen. The Dio-snippet alternative also needs a listener, so web has no variant.
- Mobile: `MockServerEngine.isSupported` is true on Android and iOS, but gate the recorder to Windows, macOS and Linux (`defaultTargetPlatform`). A phone recording its own traffic makes little sense. This avoids new iOS Local Network permission and App Store review surface.
- macOS: macos/Runner/Release.entitlements has app-sandbox and network.client but NO `com.apple.security.network.server` (only DebugProfile has it). The existing mock server cannot bind in a sandboxed macOS release build, and neither could the recorder. Add that one key before shipping either on Mac (Windows and Linux are unaffected).

SCOPE CUTS I RECOMMEND
- Defer the Dio interceptor snippet. It needs an app-side dependency, adds a second ingest endpoint (spoofable on a LAN even with a token), and works against the 'only change baseUrl' pitch.
