- Optional cheap extras after v1: a CLI `postpilot record` (the engine file imports only dart:io and domain, so it can be pure Dart), and sending captured /web/dataset/call_kw and /jsonrpc calls straight into the existing Odoo 'migrate to JSON-2' converter. That hand-off is the Odoo-persona differentiator.

TESTS: `di_wiring_test.dart` must gain the new registrations. Add real-socket engine tests (fake upstream on port 0) covering gzip, redirect rewrite, multiple Set-Cookie, 204 and HEAD, upstream down giving 502, and the token prefix. Add unit tests for PathNormalizer, HarWriter (round-trip through `HarParser`) and the save use case.
- **Risks:** SECURITY (acceptable with the mitigations below)
1. LAN exposure. A 0.0.0.0 bind makes an open forwarder to ONE fixed upstream. If that upstream is only reachable over the developer's VPN, anyone on the café or office Wi-Fi can reach it through the laptop. This is not SSRF to arbitrary hosts, because the upstream is fixed and no credentials are injected. Mitigations: loopback-only by default; when LAN is on, require a random secret path prefix (baseUrl `http://IP:port/pp-<random>`, stripped before forwarding, 404 without it); optionally accept only private-range remote addresses; show a firewall note. Windows Firewall will prompt on first LAN bind, as the mock server already does.
2. Captured secrets. The list holds live Authorization headers, cookies and personal data in memory. The danger is the export path: a HAR file or a saved collection that Git sync later pushes to GitHub. Mitigations: mask by default with SecretMasker (opt-in to include), tokens go only to isSecret environment variables, a visible 'captured data may contain personal data' banner when the upstream is a public host, memory-only storage, a Clear button, and stopping the server clears nothing silently.
3. Legal, ToS and store policy: low. This is the same class as Mockoon proxy mode, WireMock record/playback and Charles or Proxyman, but as an explicit reverse proxy. The app under test must be deliberately pointed at it, so there is no TLS interception, no CA install and no way to snoop on someone else's app. The developer uses their own API keys against their own or their employer's backend. Desktop-only gating avoids new App Store and Play review surface.

ENGINEERING RISKS
- It is not a MITM. It sees only calls through the one baseUrl: no multi-host apps, no pinned or hard-coded hosts, no WebSocket in v1. HTTP/2 upstream is downgraded to HTTP/1.1 by dart:io. Secure or Domain cookie attributes can behave differently over http. Say so in the UI.
- The app-to-PostPilot leg is cleartext http. The app under test needs Android cleartext allowed in its debug build (network security config) and, on iOS, the Local Network permission plus possibly an ATS exception (verify per host form). Show copy-paste snippets for both. This is a setup step the developer still does by hand, but it is once and tiny.
- Subtle proxy bugs (gzip, chunked, Set-Cookie, redirects) can make the recorded behaviour differ from the real one. The pitfalls are listed in the design and each needs a real-socket test.
- Top-bar clutter: a second running-server chip next to 'Mock :port'. Show it only while recording, or merge both into one 'Servers' chip.

VALUE CAVEAT (important, the candidate's evidence overstates the pain)
- Flutter DevTools' Network view already shows request and response headers and bodies for dart:io, dio, cronet_http, ok_http and cupertino_http with no proxy at all (docs.flutter.dev/tools/devtools/network). The 'Flutter dev must set up Charles' framing is therefore weak for the pure 'see what my app sent' case. The docs page does not mention HAR export or cURL copy.
- Proxyman's page confirms Flutter ignores the system proxy (docs.proxyman.com/debug-devices/flutter). Mockoon's proxy-mode docs confirm the record-to-mock mechanism (mockoon.com/docs/latest/logging-and-recording/auto-mocking-and-recording/).
- Real, unserved value is: (a) one click from live traffic to a saved collection plus examples plus a replay mock, (b) the backend, QA and Odoo developer who does not run DevTools and asks 'what exactly did the app send', (c) Odoo Flutter apps (odoo_rpc style) feeding the JSON-2 migrator.
- Honest numbers: about 5 min x 2 uses/week for a typical Flutter developer, more for QA and backend. Reposition the persona as 'all' (backend, flutter, odoo, qa) rather than flutter only. The 8 min x 3 per week figure is optimistic.
- Verify before building: whether DevTools has since added export. The one DevTools page fetched did not mention it.
- **Strongest objection:** The claimed 10-20 min per session is measured against the Charles/Proxyman/CA-cert workflow, which almost nobody uses for Flutter today. The real baseline is already fast.

1. Flutter DevTools has a Network tab built into the IDE. Its docs say it shows request and response headers and bodies for dart:io HttpClient, Dio and http_profile-based clients, with no proxy, certificate or code change (docs.flutter.dev/tools/devtools/network). Its release notes also mention a "Copy as cURL" action (checked on the DevTools NEXT_RELEASE_NOTES page; the docs page itself does not mention it). I could not confirm HAR export. PostPilot's smart-paste cURL already accepts that output. So one captured call costs about 30-60 s: select the row, Copy as cURL, paste into PostPilot.
2. Mature in-app inspectors and loggers exist for Dio: Alice, chucker_flutter, talker_dio_logger, pretty_dio_logger, and cURL-printing interceptors. Teams usually leave one in their debug builds. The proposed "25-line Dio interceptor snippet" duplicates that. This is from my own knowledge; I did not find or cite sources, because the web search budget was exhausted.
3. The reverse-proxy route is not zero-setup:
   - The dev must change the debug baseUrl, which is often a const or flavor config, so it needs a rebuild or hot restart.
   - Android 9+ needs cleartext allowed, and iOS needs an ATS and local-network exception.
   - Windows prompts about the firewall when binding to the LAN.
   - The Android emulator, the most common case, already reaches the host at 10.0.2.2, and the rest of the setup is still needed.
   For one or two calls this costs about as much as DevTools Copy as cURL, and often more.
4. The proxy is fragile for real APIs. Absolute URLs in responses (pagination "next" links, Odoo image and attachment URLs, redirects with Location headers) bypass the recorder. Cookies with Domain or Secure attributes break. Odoo's dbfilter depends on the Host header, so the proxy must rewrite it. HTTP/2, pinned hosts, WebSocket and anything beyond the single base URL are missed. The first time one of these breaks, the dev falls back to DevTools. That is the opposite of a weekly habit.
5. The backend dev's mirror case is weak. They check server logs or ask the mobile dev for a DevTools cURL. Redirecting the mobile app through another dev's PostPilot is not a realistic workflow.
6. Direction of work: PostPilot's flow is spec to request to Dart code (OpenAPI import, Dart Studio). Starting from the app's actual traffic is a debugging niche, not a daily chore.

Honest numbers: about 1 use per week, saving only about 3-5 min over DevTools plus cURL paste. That is about 3 min per week at best, and the one-time 30-60 min Charles setup is not a weekly saving. The value is highest for a multi-call user-flow capture, but even then HAR import or repeated Copy as cURL covers it. "Replay when backend is down" is already covered by the existing mock server built from saved examples.

The useful kernel is not the recorder. HAR, cURL and Postman imports do not turn the host into {{baseUrl}}, tokens into variables, or ids into :params; only the OpenAPI import does that. A small "Parameterize imported requests" step on those existing import paths (effort S, no new server) would capture most of the real value and apply to every import. I did not verify that apart from grepping the import code. Recommend dropping the proxy recorder and, at most, proposing the parameterize step as a separate small candidate.
- **Platform notes:** Desktop only (dart:io HttpServer/HttpClient); stub on web, not offered on mobile (same split as the mock engine). App-to-PostPilot leg is plain http: Android 9+ debug builds need cleartext allowed, iOS needs an ATS/local-network exception, show both snippets. Not a MITM: sees only calls through the one base URL, no pinned/hard-coded hosts, no WebSocket in v1. Bind LAN only while recording, token-protect the in-app variant, mask Authorization/Cookie by default, warn on production traffic.

### 28. Mock from spec: schema-faked, stateful CRUD with scenarios (empty, 401, 500, slow, flaky, pagination)
- **Score 1.2** | effort L | ~6 min/week saved | persona flutter | lenses: competitors, pain-points, flutter-daily | skeptic votes 2/3
- **Problem today:** A Flutter dev waits for an unfinished or flaky backend. The mock serves only the newest saved example per route with one global delay (BuildMockRoutesUseCase), so every endpoint needs a hand-made example, POST/DELETE never change what GET returns, and loading/empty/error/timeout/page-2 states need Future.delayed hacks, json-server or Mockoon setup (20-60 min per feature), or asking the backend dev to force an error.
- **What PostPilot would do:** Mock dialog gets Mode: Examples / From spec. From spec builds responses from OpenAPI response schemas with realistic fake values (existing dynamic-variable generators, honour enum/format/example), keeps an in-memory store per resource so POST/PUT/DELETE affect GET and list routes honour page/limit/offset. Every saved example is also a named scenario; a per-route Behavior menu switches Normal / Empty / 401-once-then-OK / 500 / Slow (latency+jitter) / Flaky (x%) / Offline, and a 'Quick error cases' action creates 401/403/404/422/500 examples (Odoo error envelope on Odoo collections). The app or a test can pick a scenario per call with a Prefer: example=<name> header. Served on the LAN URL/QR from Device helper.
- **Where it lives:** Existing Mock server dialog: Mode selector, scenario/behavior dropdown per route row, 'Stateful' switch with Reset, latency slider. No other UI.
- **Partly exists already:** Only the basic examples-based mock exists. Nothing in the candidate's core is built.

What exists:
- lib/features/mock_server/domain/services/mock_routes.dart: MockRoute holds one status, headers and body. MockRouteTable.from() keeps the first request per method+path. match() prefers static segments over params.
- lib/features/mock_server/domain/usecases/build_mock_routes_usecase.dart: _pick() takes the first successful saved example, else the first example of any status. Requests with no example are skipped.
- lib/features/mock_server/data/mock_server_engine_io.dart: serves the stored body as-is. It has one global delay (_config.delay), a CORS toggle, an allow-other-devices toggle, a request log and a JSON 404/405 listing the mocked routes.
- lib/features/mock_server/presentation/mock_server_dialog.dart: collection, port, delay (ms), CORS and LAN chips, Reload examples, a read-only routes list, a request log, and a link to DeviceHelperDialog.
- README line 92 and the palette entry 'mock.server' describe it only as "serves saved examples as a live API".

Verified absent across lib/, bin/ and the README (no matches):
- scenarios, behaviors, flaky, chaos, jitter, offline, or any Prefer header handling;
- in-memory state, so POST/PUT/DELETE never change what GET returns;
- pagination, or honouring page/limit/offset;
- a per-route behavior field (MockRoute has no such field);
- mock tooling in the CLI or the MCP server (lib/features/cli has no mock reference);
- a "Quick error cases" generator, or an Odoo error envelope in the mock.

Parts of the candidate that build on existing code, with caveats:
- DynamicVariables (lib/core/utils/dynamic_variables.dart) has about 40 string generators: names, emails, uuid, dates, city and so on. They are reusable but need mapping from schema field name/format to generator, and values need typing (int, bool).
- OpenApiParser (lib/features/import_export/domain/services/openapi_parser.dart) has a schema-to-example walker (_exampleOf/_valueOf). It honours example, default, enum, oneOf and allOf, with $ref cycle cut-off. It only emits fixed placeholders ('string', 0, 2024-01-01) and is used for request bodies and params only. It never reads the 'responses' key (grep finds none).
- The spec is not stored after import. The refresh dialog only remembers a spec URL pref ('openapi.source.<collection>'). "From spec" mode therefore needs a new response-schema extraction and either persisting the schemas or re-fetching the spec. This is more work than the candidate's M estimate suggests, so L is plausible if spec persistence and the stateful store are both done.

Scope advice: this is really three features in one.
1. Per-route scenarios and behavior (Empty, 401, 500, slow, flaky, offline, Prefer: example=). It needs no spec, is the highest value, and is about S-M.
2. Stateful CRUD with pagination.
3. Faking responses from OpenAPI schemas.
Splitting them keeps each shippable. The "Quick error cases" action is cheap and does not depend on the others.

Estimate check: 10 min x 2.5 per week is plausible for Flutter devs who have an OpenAPI spec. For Odoo collections (JSON-2, no OpenAPI spec), only the scenario/error part applies.
- **Competitors:** Competitor claims, checked 2026-10-02:

- Insomnia 12: CONFIRMED. The heise article (published 2025-11-05) says users can describe an API in natural language, or provide a URL, JSON file or OpenAPI spec, and get a mock server.
- Mockoon: CONFIRMED on https://mockoon.com/features/. It lists CRUD operations on fake JSON databases, latency at environment or route level, rules-triggered or random responses, and OpenAPI import/export.
- mockd: CONFIRMED on the glama.ai page. The named chaos profiles are `slow-api` (200-800 ms latency), `flaky` (30% error rate) and `offline` (100% 503).
- Prism (stoplight.io/open-source/prism): PARTLY CONFIRMED.
  - The homepage says it generates dynamic, random examples from the API description.
  - The Prism mocking guide (raw.githubusercontent.com/stoplightio/prism/master/docs/guides/01-mocking.md) confirms `Prefer: example=<name>` and `dynamic=true`.
  - I could not confirm `code=` for status selection from what I fetched. The candidate's "by status" claim is plausible but not verified here.
- Postman #3777 (https://github.com/postmanlabs/postman-app-support/issues/3777): the issue is real and still open. It is "Emulate network connectivity with Postman", opened 2017-11-11. The GitHub API shows 68 thumbs-up reactions and 62 comments. The candidate's "62 reactions" is slightly wrong: it should read 68 +1 reactions and 62 comments.

Other implementations not in the candidate's list: json-server and WireMock (response templating, fault injection, delays, scenario states). Postman's own mock servers are examples-only like PostPilot's current one, so the proposal would be ahead of Postman here.

Web search budget was exhausted, so Prism `code=` and any WireMock/json-server URLs were not checked.
- **Design in this codebase:** VERDICT: feasible on top of the current architecture, no rewrite. The proposal's "M (1-2 weeks)" is too low because it bundles three features. Bundled it is L (about 3-4 weeks for one dev). Ship it as 3 slices, each releasable on its own.

WHAT THE CODE SHOWS (read, not assumed)
- lib/features/mock_server is already layered: domain/services/mock_routes.dart (pure Dart: MockRoute, MockSource, MockRouteTable with static-beats-param matching), domain/usecases/build_mock_routes_usecase.dart, data/mock_server_engine.dart (interface) + _io (dart:io HttpServer) + _stub (web) chosen by a conditional import, presentation/mock_server_view_model.dart (session-wide singleton, so the top-bar "Mock :3001" pill keeps working) and mock_server_dialog.dart (ToolDialog).
- Corrections to the proposal's premise. The single global delay lives in MockServerConfig and the engine, not in BuildMockRoutesUseCase. That use case does load all examples per request via watchByRequest(...).first, but _pick keeps only one (newest 2xx, else newest). So named scenarios from "every saved example" is a small additive change.
- Reusable today:
  - DynamicVariables (core/utils) is pure Dart, takes an injectable Random and clock, and already has email, uuid, names, city, ISO dates and so on. A field-name/format-to-generator map is thin new code. Seed Random per resource and index so GET /users/3 returns the same record after a restart.
  - OpenApiParser already has the hard part: $ref resolution, allOf flatten, cycle/depth/node caps, and Swagger 2 + OAS3 handling. But it only reads REQUEST side, and _resolve/_flatten/_exampleOf are private with a hard-coded leaf generator (0, 'string').
  - ParsedOpenApiDocument.baseUrl plus MockRouteTable.pathOfUrl give the server path prefix.
  - OdooErrorParser documents the JSON-2 error shape {name, message, arguments, debug}, so the Odoo error envelope is trivial.
  - OpenApiRefreshDialog already stores a spec URL per collection in SharedPreferences (key openapi.source.<collectionName>) and downloads via ApiClient. Reuse that key and pattern.
  - json_schema_tools.dart (schema infer) lets a later "fake from a saved example" mode work for Postman/hand-made collections.
- Pure-Dart domain means the same responder can also back a future `postpilot mock <spec>` CLI subcommand. The CLI is already dart:io and `dart run`-able.

DESIGN (folder: lib/features/mock_server)
domain/services (all pure Dart, web-safe, unit-testable with a seeded Random and fake clock):
- mock_behavior.dart: Normal | Empty | Unauthorized-once | Error(status) | Slow(latency+jitter) | Flaky(rate) | Offline | RateLimited(429+Retry-After). Add 429; it is cheap and Flutter devs test retry logic with it.
- mock_responder.dart: decide(route, request, state) returns MockReply{status, headers, body, delay, dropConnection}. It resolves the scenario from the dialog choice, then Prefer: example=<name> / code=<n>, then the route behavior.
- mock_store.dart: in-memory resources, lazily seeded with 5-10 deterministic items so list and item GETs agree.
- mock_schema_faker.dart: schema to value. Honour enum/example/default/format/min/max/readOnly/writeOnly.
- openapi_mock_reader.dart: operation to {status, schema} (OAS3 responses.2xx.content.json.schema, Swagger2 responses.200.schema).
- Extract the schema walker from OpenApiParser into a shared import_export/domain/services/openapi_spec.dart with a pluggable leaf callback. The existing 634-line openapi_parser_test.dart guards the refactor. Do NOT copy the walker.
- MockRoute/MockSource gain optional scenarios and a resource binding (additive, so mock_server_test.dart still compiles). Per-route behavior and store state live in the ViewModel/runtime keyed by "METHOD /pattern", NOT inside MockRoute. Otherwise "Reload examples" (which rebuilds the table) would wipe chaos settings and CRUD state.
data: the IO engine does only sockets and delegates to MockResponder. New work in the engine:
- read the request body with a size cap (it ignores bodies today);
- Offline via detachSocket().destroy();
- non-blocking delays;
- apply store mutations synchronously at arrival, then delay the reply, so ordering stays deterministic.
MockLogEntry gets an optional scenario/behavior field so the log shows "Flaky (failed)".
presentation: the dialog stays a ToolDialog. Add a SegmentedButton for Mode (Examples / From spec). Add a spec URL/paste row only in spec mode. Add a Stateful chip with a Reset button. Move latency/jitter/flaky% into one collapsed "Behavior" ExpansionTile so the already-dense top Wrap does not grow. Per route, the ListTile trailing becomes a small PopupMenuButton (scenario + behavior). Show a chip only when it is not Normal. Add palette commands "Mock: reset data" and "Mock: switch scenario" (palette_items.dart). The top-bar pill can show a dot when any behavior is active.

DEVIATIONS I RECOMMEND
- Make 'Quick error cases' VIRTUAL scenarios generated at serve time, not 5 persisted examples per request. ResponseExampleRepository only has add/delete, and persisting would add 5xN rows to the DB and to backups, which is clutter. Offer 'Save as example' only for customising one.
- Stateful CRUD v1 covers only the flat pairs /things + /things/{id}, with the id type taken from the schema (int autoincrement or uuid). Pagination v1 covers a top-level array, or a wrapper whose first array property is the list (data/items/results), filling total/count from the store. Page/limit/offset/per_page query names are honoured, plus X-Total-Count. Nested routes and Odoo search_read bodies stay static. Heuristics are where the time goes.
- Add a later 'fake from saved example' fallback via schema infer. Without it, From-spec does nothing for Postman-imported, hand-made and Odoo collections, because Odoo exposes no OpenAPI.

SLICES AND EFFORT
1. Scenarios and chaos (M, about 1 week): named scenarios from all examples, behaviors, Prefer header, virtual error cases including the Odoo envelope, jitter, log annotation, macOS entitlement fix. Needs no spec and no state. Probably the best value for least risk (about 5 min x 3 uses/week on its own).
2. From spec, read-only faker (M, about 1-1.5 weeks): spec reader extraction, faker, and a Preview-response action in the dialog that also works on web.
3. Stateful CRUD and pagination (M, about 1.5 weeks).
The proposal's 10 min x 2.5/week is plausible only for devs actively blocked on a backend, so treat it as an upper bound.

PLATFORMS
- Desktop (Windows, Linux, macOS): full support. Windows shows the Defender firewall prompt on LAN bind, as it does today.
- Web: a browser cannot listen. Keep the existing stub and the 'Not available in the browser' hint. Because the responder, faker and store are pure Dart they work in-process on web, so the Preview and 'copy N fake records' actions can be offered there. A service-worker mock would only intercept same-origin calls, so it does not help the app under test and is not worth building.
- Android/iOS as the host: dart:io HttpServer works while the app is in the foreground. Android needs no new permission, since INTERNET is already in the main manifest. iOS would need NSLocalNetworkUsageDescription for LAN use (not in Info.plist now; not verified further). The realistic pattern is desktop hosts, phone consumes, so call mobile hosting 'works, not a goal'.
- **Risks:** PRE-EXISTING DEFECT THAT BLOCKS THIS ON macOS: macos/Runner/Release.entitlements has app-sandbox + network.client but NOT com.apple.security.network.server (only DebugProfile.entitlements has it). CI builds `flutter build macos --release`, so the shipped macOS app cannot bind a port. The existing mock server is already broken there. Fix: add the network.server key (a standard sandbox entitlement) to Release.entitlements. Include it in slice 1 and note the proposal does not mention it.

TECHNICAL RISKS
1. The OpenApiParser refactor touches a well-tested 540-line file. Keep it behavior-neutral and run openapi_parser_test.dart before and after.
2. Stateful and pagination heuristics (id naming, envelope shape, nested resources) will be wrong for some specs. Mitigate by scoping v1 as above, showing which routes are stateful in the route list, and falling back to static generated data.
3. Request body handling is new in the engine. Cap the size (for example 1-2 MB, answer 413), answer 400 on invalid JSON, and cap store size so a looping client cannot grow memory.
4. State and chaos settings must be keyed by route key and kept in the ViewModel, not in the rebuilt MockRoute objects, or Reload wipes them. Tests: extend the real-socket tests in test/mock_server/mock_server_test.dart and add pure responder tests with a seeded Random.
5. Dialog clutter: the top Wrap already has 7 controls. Use the segmented Mode, one collapsed Behavior section and a per-route popup menu; do not add a new row of chips.
6. Persisting the spec: keep the URL only (the existing SharedPreferences key). Do not store spec text in prefs (can be MBs). Pasted specs are per session. Note that a URL containing a token query param would sit in cleartext prefs, as it already does for Update from OpenAPI.
7. Header-based scenario picking only helps clients that can set a header, so the dialog's per-route scenario must stay the default path.

SECURITY
- LAN exposure is opt-in ('Allow other devices'). With Stateful on, anyone on that network, or a web page that can reach localhost, can POST/PUT/DELETE and change the in-memory data. The data is fake and resets, so this is low severity. Do not add an unauthenticated HTTP admin/reset endpoint; keep Reset in the UI only.
- Existing CORS reflects Origin without allow-credentials, so no credential leak.
- Examples saved from real responses may contain PII or tokens and are served to the LAN if enabled. This risk exists today and is unchanged by this feature.
- Faker values come from built-in lists and example.* domains, so no real mailbox can be hit.

LEGAL / ToS / STORE POLICY
Low risk. It re-implements the common feature set of Mockoon (MIT), Prism (Apache-2.0) and Insomnia without copying any code, and uses original data lists. The Prefer header is RFC 7240; the example=/code= parameters are Prism's convention, which is fine to follow. The app does not download or execute code, so no store-policy issue. The only platform item is the macOS entitlement above, plus the iOS local-network usage string if mobile LAN hosting is ever advertised.

EVIDENCE NOTE: I did not re-verify the competitor URLs. They match my prior knowledge (Prism Prefer code/example, Mockoon stateful CRUD and latency), and nothing in this verdict depends on them.
- **Strongest objection:** The claimed 25 min/week (10 min x 2.5 uses) is inflated by about 4x, for four reasons.

1) Frequency. Mocking is episodic, not weekly. A Flutter dev sets up a mock once per feature or sprint, while the backend is unfinished. After that it is reused. Backend devs build the real server and rarely need a mock. Odoo devs have a live Odoo with demo data. Odoo JSON-2 is POST /json/2/<model>/<method> with array arguments, so path-based CRUD or stateful faking does not fit it, and Odoo has no OpenAPI spec to feed "From spec". QA usually tests against staging. So only the Flutter persona benefits, and only in some weeks. I estimate 0.3 to 0.5 mock setups per week for a typical Flutter dev.

2) The 20-60 min baseline is a straw man. The realistic alternative is one import. Mockoon takes OpenAPI import, stateful CRUD, latency and rules in about 5 minutes. Prism is one line (`prism mock spec.yaml`, with `Prefer: code=500` or `example=<name>`, plus dynamic faking). Both are free and already fast enough. Their features page and Prism are the candidate's own evidence for that. The saving over those tools is about 5 minutes, not 10 to 60. Only the single-tool and LAN/QR convenience is unique.

3) Error and loading states are not 10-minute chores. A temporary Future.delayed or a thrown exception takes 30 seconds to 2 minutes. Error-path tests normally use mocktail or http_mock_adapter in unit and widget tests, with no server at all. The scenario menu is nice, but it saves about 2 to 3 minutes per use.

4) Hidden cost, verified in the code. lib/features/import_export/domain/services/openapi_parser.dart never reads `responses` (zero matches). It only builds request-body skeletons. So "From spec" needs response schemas to be persisted: a new field, a DB migration, a Git workspace JSON format change, and changes to "Update from OpenAPI" refresh and diff. Or the spec has to be kept and re-parsed. That makes this L, not M. The faker would still produce wrong data for non-trivial APIs: custom pagination envelopes (page/limit, cursor, {data, meta}), validation, sorting, relations. The dev then hand-edits anyway. Stateful CRUD is the least needed part for UI development and the most error-prone to build.

Honest estimate: about 0.4 setups/week x 8 min (net of Mockoon or Prism as the real alternative) plus about 1.5 scenario uses/week x 2 min is roughly 6 min/week for a typical Flutter dev. A heavy user might reach 15 to 20.

This does not clear the bar of 10+ min/week or a painful chore, because the part that is a painful chore (hand-making an example for every route) has a much cheaper fix.

Suggested slim version, S to M. (a) At OpenAPI import or refresh, auto-create saved response examples from the spec's response schemas, including 200 plus the 4xx/5xx cases, reusing the existing mock engine and the faker already in openapi_parser. (b) A per-route Behavior override (status, slow, flaky) in the existing route rows. Drop stateful CRUD, pagination guessing and Prefer-header selection for now.

Limits of this review: WebSearch was out of budget, so the demand evidence rests on the candidate's URLs and general knowledge of Mockoon and Prism. I did not re-fetch those pages.
- **Platform notes:** Needs dart:io HttpServer: desktop (and possibly Android); web has a stub like today. LAN binding triggers the OS firewall prompt. Android 9+ needs a debug-only network_security_config for cleartext (Device helper can show the snippet). State is in memory and resets on stop. Header-based scenario picking only works if the app/test can add a header, so the dialog toggle stays the default.

### 29. Collection and folder defaults with inheritance: headers, auth, variables and tests
- **Score 1.2** | effort L | ~6 min/week saved | persona all | lenses: competitors, productivity-ux | skeptic votes 2/3
- **Problem today:** Folders have only a name; collection level has only auth and variables (no headers table). Content-Type, X-Odoo-Database, Accept-Language, X-Api-Version are copied into every request (PostPilot's own Odoo generator writes the same 3 headers into each of the 10 requests per model). Assertions/extractors are stored per request only (RequestScriptsEntity keyed by requestId), so a rule like 'no 5xx, under 2 s, JSON content type, no Odoo error object' is added 40 times (~1 min each) and edited 40 times when it changes. Imported requests arrive with zero tests.
- **What PostPilot would do:** Folder and collection settings get Headers, Auth (Inherit/Override/None), Variables and Tests tabs; the resolver merges collection -> folder -> request (request wins) and shows greyed 'inherited from Orders folder' chips with an override toggle. Collection/folder Tests apply assertions/extractors to every request below with a per-request opt-out and a one-click 'Healthy API baseline' template. 'Hoist common headers' scans a collection, finds headers/auth identical on 80 percent or more of requests and moves them up while deleting per-request copies. Runner and CLI/junit show inherited checks tagged by origin.
- **Where it lives:** Collection and folder context menus 'Settings...' next to the existing Auth, Headers and Variables dialogs, 'Hoist common headers...' action; inherited items shown as chips in the request Headers/Auth/Tests tabs.
- **Partly exists already:** PARTIAL, about 25% of the idea exists. The rest is genuinely absent.

ALREADY THERE (do not re-propose):
- Collection-level auth with inheritance. A request set to AuthType.inherit falls back to the collection's auth. Files: lib/core/database/tables/collection_auth_table.dart, lib/features/collections/presentation/widgets/collection_auth_dialog.dart (menu item 'Collection auth'), RequestAuth.resolveInherited in lib/features/request_builder/domain/entities/request_auth.dart:192, wired in prepare_request_usecase.dart:60-72, request_spec_builder.dart:40, cli/workspace_runner.dart:230, and the docs, OpenAPI and Postman exporters.
- Collection-level variables. collection_variables_dialog.dart and collection_variables_table.dart, resolved as a scope by BuildVariableResolverUseCase.

NOT THERE (the new part):
- Folder settings. The Folders table has only name, parentFolderId, orderIndex and collectionId (lib/core/database/tables/collections_table.dart). The folder context menu has only add request, add sub-folder, description & tags, rename, duplicate and delete (collections_sidebar.dart:359-364). There is no folder auth, headers or variables, and no collection -> folder -> request resolver. Auth resolution is two levels only.
- Collection and folder headers. There is no table, field, dialog or merge step. RequestSpecBuilder._buildHeaders reads only request.headers. The README line 57 claims "collection-level authentication and headers that propagate to child requests", but no code backs the headers part. Fix the README or build the feature.
- Collection and folder tests. RequestScripts is keyed by requestId only (request_scripts_table.dart). There is no baseline template, no per-request opt-out, and no origin tagging in the runner or JUnit report.
- Hoist common headers. There is no usage of hoist, dedupe or common-header analysis anywhere in lib/.
- Odoo duplication confirmed. create_odoo_workspace_usecase.dart:66 and :83 copy draft.headers (Authorization, X-Odoo-Database, Content-Type from odoo_json2.dart:64-66) into every generated request.
- Import currently flattens inheritance. postman_collection_parser.dart:62-107 copies folder auth down onto requests. insomnia_parser.dart:38 and :212 copy folder headers down onto requests. So imported Postman and Insomnia workspaces lose their structure and arrive with duplicated headers, which makes Hoist and real folder inheritance more valuable.
- Git sync does not carry it yet. folder_doc_mapper.dart stores only description and tags. The change needs a sync schema addition, as the candidate says.

Recommendation: keep it, but reword the scope as "folder level plus collection headers plus collection/folder tests plus hoist", since collection auth and collection variables are already shipped.

EVIDENCE CHECK:
- Hoppscotch changelog quote verified. 2026.5.0 (about 22 May to 2 Jun 2026) has "Collection-level pre-request and test scripts". It does not mention folder-level scripts, headers or auth.
- Bruno docs verified. The OAuth2 collection-level page states the precedence request, then folder, then collection.
- Bruno #2579 is real, closed, labelled enhancement and short-term-goal. The "37 reactions" figure is unverifiable because GitHub showed no count. Drop the number or cite only "closed enhancement".
- Bruno #334 is real and closed. It is a checklist of collection-level headers, auth, scripts, tests and variables. The "42 reactions" figure is unverifiable too. Bruno #334 is the best citation for the exact headers + tests + vars bundle.
- Could not run a further web search because the session search budget is exhausted.
- **Competitors:** Postman: collection and folder auth, plus collection and folder pre-request and test scripts; no collection-level headers (long-standing gap). Bruno: collection.bru and folder.bru carry headers, auth, vars, scripts and tests, with request > folder > collection precedence; docs.usebruno.com/auth/oauth2-2.0/collection-level-configuration.md; issues #334 and #2579 closed. Hoppscotch 2026.5.0: collection-level pre-request and test scripts (docs.hoppscotch.io/documentation/changelog.md). Insomnia: folder-level headers and auth, which PostPilot's own Insomnia importer flattens onto requests. None of them ships a "hoist common headers" refactor action or a one-click healthy-API assertion baseline, so those two parts are differentiators rather than parity.
- **Design in this codebase:** VERDICT: feasible without a rewrite; the architecture suits it. Effort is L (about 3 weeks) for the whole candidate, not M. I read about 25 files and estimate 25-30 touched. Phase 1 alone is M. Phase 1 = default Headers + Auth for collection and folders, the shared resolver, persistence in all three storage formats, the settings dialog, inherited chips, Hoist, and the Odoo generator. Phase 2 = Tests inheritance and the baseline template.

WHAT HELPS
- Send, code snippets and the cURL-script export already share one path, PrepareRequestUseCase -> RequestSpecBuilder.build(inheritedAuth). Adding an `inheritedHeaders` parameter next to `inheritedAuth` fixes all three.
- RequestSpecBuilder, BackupCodec and WorkspaceRunner are pure Dart, so one resolver can serve the app and the CLI.
- Git sync is generic. SyncDoc.data is a free key map and ThreeWayMerger compares per field key, so new `headers`, `auth` and `tests` keys on folder and collection docs need no merge-engine change.
- Notes are already stored as parallel maps (`BackupCollection.folderNotes`). Folder defaults can copy that pattern.
- ToolDialog, KeyValueEditor, AuthEditor(allowInherit), AssertionsEditor and ExtractorsEditor already exist, and tests are extensive.

DESIGN
1. Storage. Add schemaVersion 5 with TWO new JSON tables made by createTable: `folder_defaults` (folderId PK, FK cascade; headersJson, authJson, testsJson) and `collection_defaults` (collectionId PK, FK cascade; headersJson, testsJson). Keep collection_auth and collection_variables as they are. Do NOT use addColumn: app_database.dart documents that it crashes the drift web worker, and v4 already used createTable for 7 tables. Add both tables to clearWorkplaceData() and workplaceDataChanges(). Without the second, editing only a default would not refresh the workspace.json mirror or the Git push preview. Copy them in CollectionsDao.duplicateCollection and duplicateFolder.
2. Domain (features/collections). Add pure-Dart `ContainerDefaults` and `DefaultsResolver`. The resolver merges collection -> folders -> request and returns headers, auth and tests, each tagged with its origin. Rules:
   - Request wins, and header names are case-insensitive.
   - Auth walks up to the nearest non-inherit level.
   - A request row with the same name, even a disabled one, suppresses the inherited header. Today a disabled row is just skipped, so this rule is new.
   Register a ContainerDefaultsRepository in _registerCollections().
3. Wire-in.
   - PrepareRequestUseCase: fetch the chain, pass the merged headers and resolved auth.
   - RunRequestScriptsUseCase: merge the chain's assertions and extractors.
   - WorkspaceRunner: walk BackupCollection.folders using the existing _folderPath pattern.
   - Readers that bypass PrepareRequestUseCase and need the resolver or an explicit flatten: build_api_layer_usecase (Dart Studio), build_api_docs_usecase, openapi_exporter, postman_collection_exporter (flatten headers; Postman has no folder headers), and ai_tab.
4. Backup and Git.
   - BackupCollection gets `defaults` plus `folderDefaults` by file-local id.
   - Git: extend FolderDocMapper, CollectionDocMapper, DocValues and LocalCollectionStoreImpl (_load, _applyFolders, _applyCollection).
5. UI. One `ContainerSettingsDialog` on ToolDialog with tabs Headers / Auth / Tests (Variables stays on collections only at first), opened from a new "Settings…" item. It replaces the collection menu's 'Variables' and 'Collection auth' items, so that already-long menu does not grow. The candidate's "existing Headers dialog" does not exist; only the Variables and Auth dialogs do. Folders get a Settings item too.
   - The request Headers/Auth/Tests tabs get one collapsed line, e.g. "Inherits 4 headers from Orders folder, Odoo API", which expands to greyed chips.
   - "Hoist common headers…" goes on the collection menu and the palette. It shows a preview, then applies in one transaction.
6. Odoo. CreateOdooWorkspaceUseCase writes Authorization/X-Odoo-Database/Content-Type once, to collection defaults, instead of 3 headers x 10 requests per model. Also handle "save request into an existing collection that lacks the defaults".
7. Tests phase. Add 3 assertion types the baseline needs: status below 500, header contains, JSON path missing. Today there are only status equals / status 2xx, header equals (which fails on 'application/json; charset=utf-8') and path exists. Add an `origin` field to AssertionResult. Put the per-request opt-out in RequestSettings (a JSON-per-request store already carried by backup and Git), not in the request_scripts table.

PLATFORM: all logic is pure Dart and all tables use createTable, so web, mobile and desktop are the same. ToolDialog goes full-screen on a phone. No dart:io or conditional imports are needed; the CLI is desktop-only already.
- **Risks:** 1. Pass/fail semantics (silent regression). CollectionRunResult.passed and RequestOutcome.passed treat a request as "has its own assertions" if scripts.assertions.isNotEmpty; otherwise they fall back to HTTP 2xx. Once inherited tests are merged in, every request has assertions, so the 2xx fallback silently disappears. A 404 would then pass on a "no 5xx" baseline. The fallback must be keyed on the request's OWN assertions, and the opt-out must still work.
2. Bypass readers. Only send, snippet and cURL export go through PrepareRequestUseCase. Dart Studio, API docs, OpenAPI export, Postman export, the AI tab and the CLI read request.headers or auth directly. If any is missed, it silently omits inherited headers. Needs one shared resolver plus a test per consumer.
3. Secret leakage (the most serious one). Folder or collection default headers and auth can hold literal tokens, and nothing covers them yet:
   - SecretSplitter only walks collection and request auth fields.
   - SecretFields.stripDoc blanks variables only for the collection kind and headers only for the request kind.
   - SecretMasker for docs and exports would also need the new fields.
   Without extending all three, a hoisted Authorization literal could be pushed to GitHub in workspace.json or a collection repo. The same gap already exists for literal tokens in request headers in workspace.json, so fix that too.
4. Version skew. Older apps ignore unknown keys. The BackupCodec reader would silently drop defaults, and the requests would then lose their headers. Mitigation: bump the backup version only when defaults are present, as gitVersion=3 already does, so older apps refuse the file loudly. For Git team sync, an old client rebuilds docs via canonical() without the new keys and can push them away. Check whether the repo layout has a format-version marker; if not, add one. Also, ScriptsJsonCodec decodes an unknown assertion type as statusIn2xx, so new types would be misread by old apps.
5. Hoist at "80 percent" changes the other 20 percent. Moving a header to the collection adds it to requests that lacked it, such as a login call without Authorization. Default to 100 percent within the scope (folder first, then promote to the collection when all folders agree). The 80 percent mode must insert explicit suppress rows into the minority, with a preview and an undo.
6. DB migration. Regenerating drift .g.dart is required. The project memory notes the C: drive is full, which blocks build_runner. Migration needs a new test like schema_v4_test.dart and extra cascade coverage in database_cascade_test.dart.
7. Scope creep. Folder-level variables would touch BuildVariableResolverUseCase, ListVariablesUseCase, VariableScope.bindCollection and the hover card. Defer or drop them; collection variables already exist. OAuth2 at folder level needs the token write-back to go to the owning level. Digest and OAuth2 are already skipped in the CLI.
8. Postman fidelity. Folder headers must be flattened onto requests on export (state this in the dialog). Folder auth can now be imported faithfully instead of copied down, which is an optional improvement.
9. Legal, ToS and store policy: none; it is a local feature at parity with Postman, Bruno and Hoppscotch. I did not verify the candidate's evidence URLs.
- **Strongest objection:** The headline problem is mostly already solved or mis-stated, and what remains is a setup-time chore, not a weekly one. (1) Content-Type does not need copying: request_spec_builder.dart (line 114) sets it from the body type unless the user overrode it. (2) The Odoo generator's 3 headers are written by PostPilot itself (odoo_json2.dart) as {{odooApiKey}}, {{odooDb}} and a fixed Content-Type. The user types nothing, and a database or key change is one environment-variable edit, not 40. Authorization is already covered by collection-level auth with an 'inherit' request mode (collection_auth_dialog.dart, resolveInherited). (3) What is left is static custom headers (X-Api-Version, Accept-Language, X-Tenant-Id) and baseline tests. A developer adds those once per collection or occasionally to all requests, roughly once every 2-3 weeks. That is about 10 minutes by hand versus 1 minute with the feature, so roughly 3-4 min/week. Imported requests with zero tests is a real gap, but in practice a dev either skips tests or adds them to the 5 key requests, so the '40 x 1 min' figure overstates what is actually saved. 4 uses/week x 5 min = 20 is not credible, and 'Hoist common headers' is a one-time migration. A typical collection runs on a shared base URL, a collection bearer and variables, which PostPilot already has. The cost is high for the value: a Drift migration, a workspace.json schema change that old workspaces must still load, and one resolver shared by the builder, CLI, mock server, snippets and Dart Studio. That last point is the regression risk. Competitor evidence (Hoppscotch, Bruno issues #2579 and #334) shows parity demand, but I could not re-verify those URLs because the web-search budget was exhausted, and demand for parity is not the same as minutes saved. Suggested cheaper cut: a runner/CLI-level 'Baseline checks' option (no 5xx, under N ms, JSON content type) that needs no per-request storage, plus collection-level default headers only. That would capture most of the QA value at S effort and with much lower regression risk.
- **Platform notes:** All platforms. Needs Drift migration, git-sync doc mappers and workspace.json schema additions (old workspaces must keep loading), plus one shared resolver used by request_spec_builder, CLI runner, mock server, snippet and Dart Studio generators (main regression risk). Postman export has folder auth but no folder headers: flatten inherited headers on export.

### 30. 401/403 Doctor: deterministic 'why was I rejected' diagnosis
- **Score 1.2** | effort M | ~3 min/week saved | persona all | lenses: security-auth | skeptic votes 2/3
- **Problem today:** On a 401/403 the dev guesses: pastes the Bearer token into jwt.io to read exp/aud/iss/scope, checks if {{token}} resolved, whether a staging token hits the prod URL, clock skew, header name or missing 'Bearer ' prefix; for Odoo also X-Odoo-Database and API key expiry (Odoo 19 keys last at most 3 months). 3-8 min, several times a week. Ask AI helps partly but is slow, needs a key and guesses.
- **What PostPilot would do:** When a response is 401/403 (or has WWW-Authenticate) a compact banner above the response lists the top 1-3 ranked causes with a one-click fix each: parse WWW-Authenticate error/scope (RFC 6750); JWT exp/nbf vs the response Date header (expired by 3 min, skew 90 s); aud/iss host vs request host and active environment; required scopes from the imported OpenAPI security scheme vs token scope; unresolved/empty {{var}} or missing 'Bearer ' in Authorization; Odoo missing/wrong X-Odoo-Database or rejected key. Fixes: refresh the token, switch environment, open the exact field. No network or AI needed.
- **Where it lives:** Reuses the Odoo error-parser banner component, shown only on 401/403/WWW-Authenticate; a full claims table in a new 'Auth' tab of Response tools; palette command 'Explain last auth failure'.
- **Partly exists already:** The generic 401/403 diagnosis is not implemented; only fragments exist, mostly Odoo-specific or about other things. (1) Odoo only: lib/features/odoo/domain/services/odoo_error_parser.dart maps Unauthorized, Forbidden and AccessError to a static hint such as "key is wrong, expired or revoked". lib/features/odoo/presentation/widgets/odoo_error_banner.dart renders it, mounted at lib/features/request_builder/presentation/request_builder_page.dart:459. It fires only when the body is Odoo-shaped JSON, never for a plain 401/403 or a WWW-Authenticate header. It does no checks against the request, has no one-click fixes, and the banner is Odoo-specific so it would have to be generalised, not just reused. (2) JWT: response_tools/presentation/widgets/decode_tab.dart and domain/services/quick_decoder.dart decode only JWTs found inside the response body (JwtDecoder.findIn(data.json)), compared to the local clock (DateTime.now()). They never read the request's Authorization header, the response Date header, aud/iss or scope. (3) WWW-Authenticate is parsed only for Digest retry (request_builder/domain/services/digest_auth_challenge.dart, send_request_usecase.dart:78 and :125); there is no RFC 6750 error/scope parsing. (4) OAuth2 token expiry and a Refresh button exist in request_builder/presentation/widgets/auth_editor.dart (~lines 360-382), but nothing links a 401 to them. (5) Unresolved {{var}} shows as "Undefined" only on hover in the editor (variable_hover_card.dart, variable_scope.dart); there is no post-401 check. Not found anywhere in lib/bin/README: a missing "Bearer " prefix check, environment vs token host/aud/iss mismatch, clock-skew check, OpenAPI required scopes vs token scope, an "Auth" response tab, or an 'Explain last auth failure' palette command. The OpenAPI importer does keep scopes (openapi_parser.dart ~lines 406-473, into oauth2Scope), so the scope data is available. The Ask AI path (ai_assistant/domain/ai_tasks.dart, ai_tab.dart) takes status and body but needs a key and is not deterministic.
- **Competitors:** Could not check competitors: the WebSearch budget for this session was exhausted (200 of 200 calls), so I have no verified evidence either way on a deterministic 401/403 doctor in Postman, Insomnia or Bruno. Treat the "nobody else does this" angle as unverified. Evidence checked: the Odoo 19 claims are correct. The raw odoo/documentation 19.0 external_api.rst says an Authorization bearer API key is required, X-Odoo-Database is optional (only needed on multi-database servers without dbfilter), and keys cannot last more than three months. The first WebFetch of the odoo.com page returned only the table of contents. I did not re-fetch RFC 6750 or the Skycloak post; RFC 6750 is a well-known standard but was not checked here.
- **Design in this codebase:** VERDICT: feasible without a rewrite, but it is M (about 7-9 working days), not S. The pure-logic part is easy. Two plumbing gaps make it bigger.

GAP 1 (biggest): the sent request is not kept anywhere.
- SendRequestUseCase builds `prepared.spec` (resolved URL, resolved headers, auth type). It returns only ApiResponseEntity (statusCode, headers, bodyBytes, duration, truncated).
- RequestBuilderViewModel holds only the unresolved `request`. ResponseHistory holds responses only. ResponseToolsData has no sent request.
- Without the resolved Authorization header, JWT claims, leftover {{var}} and the host cannot be checked.
- Re-resolving afterwards with PrepareRequestUseCase would use the CURRENT environment. That is wrong exactly when the user switched environment after the 401.
- Fix: capture evidence at send time, only when status is 401/403 (or WWW-Authenticate is present). Add an optional `AuthEvidence? sent` to ApiResponseEntity. It has 3 construction sites; the runner's `_withoutBody` should leave it null.
- Keep derived facts, not the secret itself:
  - scheme (Bearer/Basic/none) and whether the token is empty;
  - whether the Authorization header came from the Auth tab, a manual header row, or the collection (inherited);
  - leftover {{vars}} found with AppConstants.variablePattern over the resolved strings;
  - JWT claims only: iss, aud, exp, nbf, iat, scope/scp, azp;
  - request scheme, host and port, with no query string;
  - active environment name, from EnvironmentRepository.watchActive().first, inside SendRequestUseCase.
- Nothing is persisted. History already stores no request headers. This is the same memory-only rule as ResponseHistory.

GAP 2: the redirect hop is invisible, and it is the best rule the candidate missed.
- DioApiClient._sendFollowingRedirects strips Authorization on http to https, example.com to www.example.com, or any port or host change. ApiHttpResponse does not report this.
- Add an optional `redirects` list (from, to, authorizationDropped) to ApiHttpResponse. It is built in only one place (dio_api_client.dart:104), so the change is cheap.
- Rule: "401 after redirect to https://www..., Authorization was dropped by design". This is common and fully deterministic.

FILES AND REUSE
- New folder: lib/features/auth_doctor/
  - domain/services/auth_failure_diagnoser.dart: pure Dart rules engine. Input is status, headers, body JSON, AuthEvidence, redirects, receivedAt/now. Output is a ranked list of AuthFinding (title, detail, confidence, fix kind).
  - domain/services/www_authenticate_parser.dart: Bearer params error, error_description, scope, realm, and the scheme the server wants. Dio joins repeated headers with ", ", so write a small tokenizer.
  - domain/services/http_date.dart: web-safe parser for IMF-fixdate. Do NOT use dart:io HttpDate; shared code avoids dart:io for web.
  - presentation/widgets/auth_doctor_banner.dart and auth_tab.dart.
- Reuse:
  - JwtDecoder.tryDecode and isExpired from quick_decoder.dart.
  - DigestAuthChallenge-style header parsing.
  - core/widgets/info_banner.dart (BannerKind).
  - ProductionDetector for the staging-vs-prod hint.
  - SecretMasker for any copy or share text.
  - ToolDialog and ToolTabs for the Auth tab.
  - PaletteItems.tools() plus ShellViewModel.selectedRequestId plus ResponseHistory.of(id).first for the palette command 'Explain last auth failure'.
- Keep the diagnoser pure so the headless CLI (workspace_runner.dart) can reuse it to annotate 401/403 failures in reports. No DI registration is needed for static services, so di_wiring_test is unaffected.

UI
- Mount in _ResponsePane (request_builder_page.dart ~line 459), directly where OdooErrorBanner sits.
- Show only on 401/403, as one collapsed line with a Details button.
- IMPORTANT: the candidate's claim that this "reuses the Odoo banner component" is wrong. OdooErrorBanner is hard-wired to OdooErrorInfo.
  - Build a small generic banner on InfoBanner.
  - Dedupe: an Odoo 401/403 already gets 'Invalid API key' or 'Not allowed' from OdooErrorParser, so two stacked banners would be clutter. When the Odoo parser matches, fold the generic findings into that single banner, or have the doctor defer to it.
- 'Auth' tab: add it to ResponseToolsDialog only for 401/403. Pass `AuthEvidence` through ResponseToolsData.from. ResponseToolsDialog.show needs an `initialIndex`; ToolTabs already supports it.
- Fixes that are cheap:
  - switch environment (EnvironmentsViewModel);
  - re-send (vm.send);
  - copy a masked diagnosis.
- Fixes that need extra plumbing:
  - 'open the exact field': the request tabs live in a DefaultTabController scoped inside _RequestPane, a sibling of the response pane. Add a `requestedTab` ValueNotifier on RequestBuilderViewModel.
  - 'refresh token': only feasible for OAuth2 auth type, through RequestOAuth2ViewModel.
  - For a {{token}} variable, offer 'run the request whose extractor writes {{token}}'. This is possible by scanning RequestScriptsRepository extractors. It is a stretch goal (M on its own), not v1.

RULES THAT WORK ON RELIABLE DATA (v1)
- Redirect dropped Authorization.
- Empty or unresolved {{var}} in the token, URL or headers.
- Manual Authorization header without a scheme prefix. Note the Auth tab 'bearer' type ALWAYS adds 'Bearer ', so this only applies to hand-written header rows.
- Server challenge scheme vs sent scheme (Basic vs Bearer vs Digest).
- RFC 6750 error and scope parsing.
- JWT exp/nbf vs the server's Date header, falling back to the local clock with a label.
- JWT iss/aud host vs request host. If another environment has that host in a non-secret variable, offer 'Switch to <env>'.

RULES TO CUT OR SHRINK
- Scope check vs OpenAPI: openapi_parser._authFromScheme keeps scopes only as RequestAuth.oauth2Scope, and only for oauth2 flows. For http-bearer and openIdConnect the scopes are dropped. OAuth2Token also stores no granted scope.
  - v1: compare the JWT scope/scp claim with oauth2Scope on OAuth2 requests only.
  - Anything more means persisting security metadata in the parser (extra M).
- Odoo extras: API keys are opaque, so expiry cannot be detected client-side, and the existing parser already explains 'Invalid API key'. The only genuinely new Odoo check is a missing or empty X-Odoo-Database header (odoo_json2.dart:57-65) plus an empty {{odooApiKey}}.

PLATFORM NOTES
- Everything is pure Dart. Desktop and mobile are fully supported.
- Web: Date and WWW-Authenticate are readable only if the server lists them in Access-Control-Expose-Headers. Otherwise skip the skew and challenge rules and say so in the UI (do not guess). JWT, variable, redirect-free and environment rules still work.
- Dio lowercases response header keys. Still do a case-insensitive lookup, as SendRequestUseCase._header does.

TESTS
- Rules are pure functions and easy to unit-test in the style of test/odoo/odoo_logic_test.dart.
- Existing SendRequestUseCase tests (4 files) keep compiling if the new dependency is an optional constructor param.
- **Risks:** 1. Hit rate is lower than the candidate implies. JWT rules only help for JWT bearer tokens. Odoo API keys, GitHub PATs and many opaque OAuth tokens cannot be decoded, so only the generic rules (empty var, redirect strip, scheme mismatch, WWW-Authenticate) apply. Realistic saving is about 2-3 min x 3 uses/week, not 4 x 4.
2. Wrong or over-confident diagnoses. JWT signatures are not verified, claims are untrusted, and clock skew is not always the cause. Word every finding as 'likely', rank by confidence, and show the evidence behind each. Never auto-apply a fix.
3. Secret handling. Keeping resolved Authorization headers in memory would be a regression. Mitigation: capture only on 401/403, store derived facts and JWT claims instead of the token, keep it in memory only, never include it in the Share/bug-report/Ask-AI text, and run any copied text through SecretMasker. JWT claims such as sub or email can be personal data, so keep them local.
4. Odoo double banner. If the generic doctor and OdooErrorBanner both render, an Odoo 401 shows two red banners. Merge into one slot.
5. Scope creep. The candidate's S estimate is not credible because of the two plumbing gaps (sent-request evidence and redirect reporting) plus the per-tab-controller 'open field' fix. Ship v1 without refresh-token and scope checks.
6. Web builds lose the Date and WWW-Authenticate rules unless the server exposes those headers. This is a documented limitation, not a blocker.
7. Legal, ToS and store policy: no issue. It is local analysis of the user's own traffic with no new network calls, permissions or third-party data flow.
- **Strongest objection:** The claimed 4 min x 4 uses = 16 min/week does not survive contact with the code or with how people really debug auth failures.

1) Most of it already exists in PostPilot.
- The Odoo case is covered by odoo_error_parser.dart. 'Unauthorized' already says "key is wrong, expired or revoked, create a new one in Preferences > Account Security", 'Forbidden' and 'AccessError' have hints, and an odoo_error_banner.dart is already shown. The Odoo half of the pitch adds almost nothing. X-Odoo-Database is the one new rule, and Odoo's own error text already points to it.
- Unresolved {{var}} is already tracked (VariableSource.unresolved, shown as red 'Undefined' in the variable hover card), and OAuth2 token expiry already shows 'Token expired' in the auth editor. The "did {{token}} resolve" and "is it expired" checks are therefore not a manual chore today.
- JWT decode with exp, relative time and a red 'Expired' banner already exists in decode_tab.dart.

2) The one real gap is small. decode_tab only scans the RESPONSE body for JWTs (JwtDecoder.findIn(data.json)), not the request's Authorization header, which is where the failing token sits on a 401. Fixing that is a one-evening change. It replaces the jwt.io paste and saves about 1 min, only on the minority of 401s where the token is a JWT.

3) The rest is rarely the real cause or is guesswork.
- Many real 401s use opaque or API-key tokens, not JWTs, so the exp/aud/iss rules never fire.
- aud/iss versus request host is a heuristic, since aud is often a logical API ID rather than a host, so it gives false positives.
- Clock skew from the Date header is rare, and on web the header is often hidden by CORS.
- The scope check needs an imported OpenAPI security scheme plus a scope claim in the token, which is a narrow combination.
- Most 403s are server-side RBAC or resource ownership. No client-side rule can diagnose those, so a ranked-causes banner would be confidently wrong or silent exactly when the developer is stuck.

4) Honest usage. A backend/Flutter dev hits a 401/403 about 2-4 times a week. For roughly half of those the body already says "token expired" or "invalid signature", or the cause is obvious (wrong environment, forgot to log in). The banner would genuinely help on about 1-2 of them, saving 1-2 min each. That comes to about 3 min/week, not 16.

5) It is not a painful or error-prone chore. The pain is 1-3 minutes of reading a decoded token, and a wrong auto-diagnosis is worse than none.

Counter-evidence I could not collect. The web search budget was exhausted, so I found no proof of demand beyond the RFC 6750 and Odoo docs the candidate cites. Those prove the error format, not that devs lose time on it.

Better version: do not build a 'Doctor'. Add 'decode the Authorization header JWT of the sent request' (exp countdown plus aud/iss/scope) to the existing Decode tab. Add a 'Date header skew' line only when the exp check is borderline. That is effort S, about 2-3 min/week, and carries no risk of wrong diagnoses.
- **Platform notes:** Fully client-side, all platforms. Date-skew check needs the server to send Date; on web Date and WWW-Authenticate are readable only if exposed via Access-Control-Expose-Headers. Scope check works only when an OpenAPI spec with security schemes was imported.

### 31. Environment matrix: compare, sync and bulk-edit environments (.env paste/export)
- **Score 1.2** | effort M | ~3 min/week saved | persona all | lenses: productivity-ux | skeptic votes 2/3
- **Problem today:** 'Works on staging, fails on dev' usually means an environment is missing a key or points at the wrong host (or prod points at localhost). Today the dev opens each environment and compares by eye; adding a variable to 4 environments is 4 edits; moving values from a .env or Slack message means typing key by key. Odoo devs repeat this per database (url, db name, API key).
- **What PostPilot would do:** Environments manager gets a 'Compare' view: one row per variable, one column per environment, highlighting missing keys, empty values, values identical between dev and prod, and keys used by requests but defined nowhere. Actions: 'Add missing keys to all environments (empty)', 'Copy staging values to dev', 'Paste .env / JSON to bulk-add', 'Copy as .env'. Warns when a prod-locked environment contains localhost, 10.0.2.2 or a staging host.
- **Where it lives:** A 'Compare' toggle inside the existing Environments manager (table view next to the single-environment list); palette command 'Compare environments'. Secrets stay masked.
- **Competitors:** No competitor implementation of a cross-environment compare/matrix was found or verifiable, because web search was exhausted. Related requests in other clients, all verified to exist: Hoppscotch #4719 (bulk editing env variables, open), Bruno #409 (.env file per environment, open), Bruno #3529 (import/export global environments, closed) and Bruno #4369 (inline env editing, closed). These cover only bulk edit, .env and import/export, not compare or missing-key detection.
- **Design in this codebase:** VERDICT: feasible without a rewrite, on all platforms, no schema migration, no new package, no legal/ToS/store issue. Corrected effort is M (about 1 to 1.5 weeks), not S, mainly because of the "keys used but defined nowhere" scan, unit tests, and a narrow-screen layout. Compare + .env paste/copy + prod/localhost warnings alone would be S (3 to 5 days), so ship that first and the usage scan second.

WHAT THE CODE LOOKS LIKE TODAY
- lib/features/environments is clean Provider + UseCase + Repository. EnvironmentsViewModel (lazy singleton, provided above MaterialApp, so dialogs reach it) already exposes `environments` and `watchVariables(id)`, which caches one subscription per environment. The compare view can watch every environment with no new data layer. The tables are Environments and EnvironmentVariables (key, value, isSecret, enabled).
- EnvironmentsManagerDialog is a plain `Dialog` of 640x480, not a ToolDialog. A matrix with 4+ environment columns does not fit in 640px, so the "Compare toggle inside the manager" in the proposal should become a separate ToolDialog.
- Reusable pieces: ToolDialog (becomes full-screen under 600px), AppConstants.variablePattern, DynamicVariables (to exclude `{{$guid}}`-style names), ProductionDetector.isProduction(name, extraWords: SafetyPrefs.extraWords), SecretNames.looksSecretKey, CollectionLoader.loadAll() (the palette already uses it for all requests), RequestScriptsRepository.get, `downloadFile()` (conditional import io/web), Clipboard, showConfirmDialog, and PaletteItems.tools() for a one-line palette entry.
- BulkEditText (request_builder/presentation/widgets/bulk_edit_text.dart) is the precedent for a pure-Dart text codec with tests.

CORRECTIONS TO THE CANDIDATE
1. "Uses the existing file picker" is wrong. There is no file_picker or file_selector dependency (pubspec checked). The app's pattern is paste textarea in, Clipboard/downloadFile out. Use that; do not add a package.
2. Compare belongs in its own ToolDialog (width about 1000), not a toggle in the 640px manager.
3. The environment entity has no "prod" flag. Prod is detected by name (ProductionDetector plus per-device extraWords). The localhost-in-prod warning must use that same detector and be advisory only, not blocking.
4. The evidence links are real: Hoppscotch #4719 (open, bulk edit of environment variables) and Bruno #409 and #3529 loaded and match. I did not open #4369.

DESIGN (all under lib/features/environments)
- domain/services/env_file_codec.dart: pure Dart. Parses .env (`export ` prefix, single/double quotes, `\n` escapes, `#` comments, inline comments only when unquoted, multi-line quoted values, BOM, CRLF, no `${}` expansion) and a flat JSON object. Encodes to .env with `includeSecrets=false` by default. Unit-tested like BulkEditText.
- domain/services/environment_matrix.dart: pure function (environments, variables per environment, globals, usedKeys, runtimeKeys) producing rows. Each cell is one of present / missing / empty / disabled / duplicate, using the same effective row the resolver reads (last enabled). Row flags: identical across prod-named and non-prod environments, defined in globals or collection variables (info, not an error), set at runtime by an extractor. A second function lists prod-environment warnings (localhost, 127.0.0.1, 10.0.2.2, 192.168.*, .local, "staging"/"dev" hosts).
- domain/usecases/find_used_variables_usecase.dart (UseCase<UsedVariables, NoParams>): CollectionLoader.loadAll(), then regex over url, headers, query params, raw/graphql/form body, auth fields, collection auth and variables, and environment values (nested references). Excludes DynamicVariables names. Adds extractor targets (RequestScriptsRepository.get(requestId) then ScriptsJsonCodec.decodeExtractors) to runtimeKeys. Register it as a lazy singleton in _registerEnvironments. It is lazy, so CollectionLoader being registered later is fine.
- domain/usecases/apply_environment_changes_usecase.dart: addMissingKeys, copyValues(from, to, keys), importPairs(envId, pairs, overwrite). It loops the existing EnvironmentRepository.upsertVariable (see risk 6 for why not a batch method) and returns counts for a snackbar.
- presentation/widgets/environment_compare_dialog.dart (ToolDialog): on open it calls vm.watchVariables for every environment and runs the usage scan once. Header shows filters (only problems, only differences) and a search box. Rows are keys, columns are environments. Secrets stay masked; the "identical in dev and prod" check compares raw values without showing them. Actions are in the footer: Add missing keys, Copy values from, Paste .env, Copy as .env.
- presentation/widgets/env_file_dialog.dart (ToolDialog): paste textarea, target-environment dropdown, a preview of new / changed / unchanged rows, and a "Mark likely secrets" checkbox that is on by default. It opens from a small header action in the existing _VariablesPane, so the manager stays uncluttered.
- Entry points: a "Compare" TextButton in the _EnvironmentList footer, plus PaletteItem id 'env.compare' in PaletteItems.tools() (run: (c) => EnvironmentCompareDialog.show(c)). Nothing is added to the request or response views.
- Narrow screens: under 600px render one card per key with environment chips instead of a wide table, with horizontal scroll as the fallback.
- Platforms: pure Dart plus Clipboard. downloadFile is already conditionally imported (io/web). No CORS and no dart:io outside the existing helper.
- Tests: codec and matrix as pure unit tests. The use cases run against test/support InMemoryEnvironmentRepository. One widget test for the dialog with a seeded drift in-memory DB, as the other tests do.
- **Risks:** 1. SECRET LEAK INTO GIT (highest risk, must handle). SecretSplitter keeps environment and global values out of workspace.json only when `isSecret == true`; there is no name heuristic for environment variables (only for collection variables). A pasted .env with DB_PASSWORD or API_KEY that is not flagged goes into workspace.json and gets pushed to GitHub. So importPairs must auto-flag with SecretNames.looksSecretKey, and "Copy values" must carry or OR the isSecret flag (secret wins) when copying into an existing row. Add a test that asserts this against SecretSplitter.split.
2. "Copy as .env" and clipboard. Secret values are blank by default; including them needs an explicit toggle plus a warning. The compare view never prints secret values and never lets a secret land in a snackbar or log.
3. Production confirm. ProductionGuard only covers sending requests. Add a confirm for bulk writes into a prod-named environment (Add missing, Copy values, .env import), via SafetyPrefs.extraWords. Do not change the existing per-row editing behaviour.
4. False positives: extractors write tokens (`access_token`) into the active environment at runtime, so those keys are legitimately absent elsewhere. Treat extractor targets as "set at runtime". Also treat globals, collection variables and a collection run's data-row columns as defining a key. Disabled rows are invisible to the resolver (show them as "off", not as present). Duplicate keys resolve to the last enabled row. Without these rules the "missing / undefined" column will be noisy and users will stop trusting it.
5. Usage scan cost. CollectionLoader.loadAll does N+1 reads (findById per request) and scripts need one more read per request. That is fine locally and the palette already does it, but run it once on open, off the UI path, with a spinner, and keep it optional. Large workspaces (thousands of requests) will take a moment.
6. Interface change trap. About 10 test fakes (test/support/in_memory_import_export_fakes.dart, send_request_usecase_test, variable_resolver_test, collection_runner_test, production_guard_test, and others) `implements EnvironmentRepository`. Adding a batch method such as upsertVariables would break them all. Keep v1 on per-row upsertVariable. The workspace autosave is debounced (800 ms) so dozens of writes only trigger one file write; each write does emit a stream update, which is acceptable at tens of keys. Add a batch method later only with a fakes update.
7. Dangling state. EnvironmentsViewModel never cancels the subscription of a deleted environment, so the compare view must iterate vm.environments and ignore stale entries in _variablesByEnvironment.
8. UI scale. A table of N environments by M keys is wide. Limit to the visible environments (a column picker) and virtualise rows with ListView.builder to avoid jank at 100+ keys.
9. .env edge cases (multi-line quoted values, `${VAR}` interpolation, duplicate keys, `export`) are the usual source of bugs. Do not expand `${}`; warn on duplicates; show the preview before writing.
10. Legal/ToS/store: none. It is local data handling with no network and no new permissions. Competitor issues are cited only as evidence of demand, nothing is copied from them.
- **Strongest objection:** The claimed 6 min x 2 uses/week (12 min) is inflated. Environments are set up once per project and then rarely change; the claimed uses are mostly a once-a-month onboarding event or an occasional debugging session.

What I checked in the code:
- The Environments manager (environments_manager_dialog.dart) is a basic 640x480 dialog. Each variable needs an "Add variable" click plus key and value typing, with no bulk paste, no .env import/export and no duplicate-environment action. The gap is real, but it is table-stakes polish, not weekly automation.
- A typical developer has 2-4 environments with 5-15 variables each. Eyeballing two such lists takes 1-2 minutes, not 6.
- The most common "works on staging, fails on dev" cause is a missing key. That is already caught at the point of use: variable_text_controller.dart and variable_hover_card.dart paint undefined variables red ("Not defined in the active environment...") as soon as you switch to the broken environment. The matrix would only add a cross-environment view.
- Git workspace sync already distributes environments to teammates, so non-secret values are not re-typed per developer. Secrets are local-only, so a .env paste helps only that one-time per-developer step.
- "Prod contains localhost" and "dev and prod values identical" are lint checks for rare misconfigurations. ProductionDetector and the prod-lock already cover the dangerous direction (write requests to prod).
- "Add key to all environments" is a rare action, perhaps once a month.

Realistic use per developer:
- Compare view: about 1 use every 2-3 weeks, saving about 3 minutes, so roughly 1 min/week.
- .env paste or clone-environment: about 1 use a month, saving about 4-5 minutes, so roughly 1-2 min/week.
- Total: about 2-4 min/week, well under the 10-minute bar.

Demand evidence is weak. Hoppscotch #4719 (bulk edit) is a single open issue and Bruno #409 is about Bruno's file format. Neither shows a weekly time sink, and I could not search for more evidence (the web-search budget was exhausted).

Verdict: not a headline time-saver. If anything ships, ship only the cheap parts: bulk paste/.env import and export, plus a "duplicate environment" action. Treat them as a 1-2 day polish item, and do not build a matrix view.
- **Platform notes:** Pure UI over existing environment tables, all platforms; .env import/export uses the existing file picker/download helper. Respect the local-only secret flag and the production lock when copying values into a prod environment (confirm first).

### 32. Run for each: bulk runner with data sources, concurrency, rate limit and resume
- **Score 1.2** | effort M | ~3 min/week saved | persona all | lenses: data-flows | skeptic votes 2/3
- **Problem today:** The Collection Runner runs a whole collection strictly one request at a time with one fixed delay; data is pasted into a text box (session only) and capped at 1000 iterations (CollectionRunOptions.maxIterations); there is no way to feed a request from another response. For a backfill or mass fix the dev exports ids, reformats to CSV, pastes them, puts the one request in a throwaway collection, and if row 312 fails (429, timeout) trims the CSV by hand and reruns, or writes a 15-20 min Python/Dart script.
- **What PostPilot would do:** Right-click a request or folder -> 'Run for each...'. Source: CSV/XLSX/JSON file or paste, a JSON array picked from the last response in the Explore tab ('Run for each item'), or an Odoo model + domain query. Set concurrency (default 1), max requests/second, retry on 429/5xx honouring Retry-After. A progress panel offers Pause/Stop, 'Retry failed rows only' and 'Export results CSV'. CLI gets --data, --iteration-count, --concurrency, --rps, --resume. One confirmation covers prod ('500 POSTs to PROD'), not 500 prompts.
- **Where it lives:** Sidebar context menu on a request or folder, a button in the Explore tab of Response tools, CLI flags; reuses the existing runner dialog and results table/export.
- **Partly exists already:** Already built (the sequential, data-driven runner): lib/features/collections/presentation/widgets/collection_runner_dialog.dart (Iterations, Delay ms, Stop on first failure, pasted CSV/JSON data box, Copy/Download JSON+CSV results, ONE production confirmation via ProductionGuard.checkRun before the run, Stop button); lib/features/request_builder/domain/services/collection_runner_service.dart (strictly sequential loop, one fixed delay, cancel token, per-row data variables fed to SendRequestUseCase/RunRequestScriptsUseCase); collection_run_options.dart (maxIterations = 1000, one delay); run_data_parser.dart (CSV/JSON array, delimiter auto-detect, rejects >1000 rows); collection_run_report.dart (JSON/CSV exporter); collection_runner_view_model.dart. So the candidate's "pasted data", "1000 cap", "one delay", "sequential" and "one prod confirmation" points are accurate or already solved. Not present anywhere in lib/bin/README: concurrency; requests/second limit; retry on 429/5xx or Retry-After (the only Retry-After code is in git_sync/data/github/github_http.dart for GitHub, not the runner); Pause; 'retry failed rows only'; resume state; a runner entry on a single request or folder (sidebar only has 'Run collection' on the collection menu, collections_sidebar.dart line 169); file picker (pubspec has csv but no file_picker or excel); a source from a response array or an Odoo model+domain query; CLI data support (cli_main.dart _valued/_boolean list has only env, collection, folder, timeout, delay, report, out, bail, insecure, no-color; workspace_runner.dart RunOptions has no data rows, iterations, concurrency or rps). Verdict: the data-driven loop is built, so this is an extension of it, not a new feature. The genuinely new value is: (1) run one request/folder for each item of a response array or Odoo query, (2) retry/Retry-After plus rps limit, (3) 'retry failed rows' after a partial failure, (4) CLI --data/--iteration-count. Practical advice: concurrency is the riskiest part, because extractors write shared environment/global variables (workspace_runner.dart RunState and the app's variable store), so parallel rows would race; keep default 1 and either disable extractors under concurrency>1 or scope them per row. Treat XLSX and the Drift resume table as optional (CSV export of failed rows plus 'retry failed only' in-session covers most of the saving). The claimed minutes (15 min x 1.5/week) are plausible but should be restated as the delta over the existing paste-CSV runner, since part of the saving is already available.
